# Production readiness audit: full findings (2026-11-20)

Appendix to `production-readiness-checklist.md`. Raw output of the read-only audit: 19 domain auditors, a second agent re-checking every blocker and high finding against the code, and a completeness critic. 373 findings before de-duplication; many repeat across domains. Severity shown is after the verifier's correction. Line numbers refer to the working tree on 2026-11-20.


## tier-core

Piggy Plan (core, $8/mo) promises four features. Two are genuinely built (BYO/default portfolio and withdrawal education), one is built but has production gaps (paycheck auto-invest), and one does not exist at all: automatic portfolio rebalancing. There is no rebalancing engine anywhere in the backend (no service, scheduler, controller, table or sell-and-rebuy logic), yet the tier card, the My Profile "Portfolio Rebalancing" card ("FRED automatically realigns it") and a "Next rebalance: <date>" line all tell the user it is happening. That is the single biggest promise-vs-reality gap in this tier and it also underlies the Plus "custom rebalancing schedule" feature, whose chosen schedule is only saved in localStorage. Tier gating: none of the four core features is gated by tier on the backend, and auto-invest keeps moving money for a user whose selectedTier is null (expired). The tier itself is a free-text string any authenticated client can set via PUT /api/user/profile, with no payment behind it (IAP is a TODO). Auto-invest itself is well hardened on the money path (idempotency keys, job locks, per-user transactions, batch claim before the ACH POST), but user notifications are all mocked (email disabled by default, push is a log line), small contributions silently drop the 5% slice, and a few trade states can strand an execution. Everything has only ever run against Alpaca sandbox and Plaid sandbox.


| Tier | Feature | Status | Tier gate | Gaps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| core | Paycheck-based automated investing | partial | no | Works end to end for a user-entered pay frequency and date (MANUAL mode). 'Paycheck-based' means the user types their pay schedule; real paycheck detection (DETECTED mode) is explicitly not processed. No tier or subscription check anywhere on this path. All user notifications (D-1 notice, success, failure) are mocked: email is disabled by default and push is a log statement. Slices under $1 are skipped silently, so contributions under $20 never buy VBR. Partially filled or expired orders can leave an execution stuck in TRADING_INITIATED. An execution with every slice skipped is marked COMPLETED with a success email. The 'Investment complete' D+0 notice fires at midnight, before the ACH has even settled. Only exercised against Alpaca sandbox / Plaid sandbox. |
| core | FRED default portfolio or BYO | implemented | no | Built and wired end to end, available to every tier (correct for a base-tier feature). Gaps: backend accepts any symbol string without checking it is a tradable, active Alpaca asset (only the frontend search filters), no cap on number of holdings, and changing the allocation only affects future buys because there is no rebalancer to move existing holdings. The word 'proven' in the copy is a marketing claim counsel should see. |
| core | Automatic portfolio rebalancing | missing | n/a | Nothing rebalances. No drift calculation, no sell/buy orders, no schedule stored server side. The My Profile card states 'When your allocation drifts, FRED automatically realigns it' and shows a 'Next rebalance' date that is a pure calendar heuristic in the browser. The schedule preference lives only in localStorage. settings.service has an unused autoRebalance: true default. Scheduled buys always split by fixed target percentages and never by drift, so even 'rebalancing by contributions' is not happening. |
| core | Portfolio withdrawal education | implemented | no | All four promised strategies exist as static, hardcoded slide decks on tab 2, open to every tier, with an 'Educational content, not investment advice' line. Gaps are small: the 'Stress-test this strategy' button sends a core user to the Monte Carlo simulator, which is locked for core; the content contains specific figures and claims (5% starting withdrawal, 40-60% annuitized, 'survived nearly every historical market') that have not been through compliance review as far as the repo shows; content cannot be updated without an app release. |


### [blocker] Automatic portfolio rebalancing is sold on every tier but no rebalancing engine exists

- Type: app-work, effort XL, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:97-102 (core promise), :152-157 (Plus custom schedule); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:178-204; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:60-80; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/WashSaleGuardService.java:26,70 ('any future rebalancer'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/ (no rebalance job among the 6 schedulers); backend/src/main/resources/db/migration V1-V9 (no rebalance table)
- Detail: The core tier card promises 'FRED periodically realigns your portfolio back to your target allocation; no action needed from you.' My Profile repeats it ('When your allocation drifts, FRED automatically realigns it. No trade approvals are needed.') and shows a concrete 'Next rebalance: <date>'. Nothing in the backend computes drift or places rebalancing trades. The only backend mentions of rebalancing are a reserved enum value and comments about a future rebalancer. For a paid investing product handling real money this is a false statement about how the account is managed, so it cannot ship to real users as written.
- Suggested fix: Decide between two paths. (A) Build it: a RebalanceService plus scheduler that, per user on their schedule, reads Alpaca positions, compares to PortfolioItem targets, applies a drift threshold, and places sells then buys through the WashSaleGuardService REBALANCE source, with an audit table, job lock, dry-run mode and tax-impact consideration (selling in a taxable account creates gains, which counsel should weigh in on). (B) For beta, remove the feature from all three tier cards, remove or relabel the My Profile rebalancing card and the 'Next rebalance' date, and mark it coming soon. A cheaper interim build is drift-aware contributions (direct each paycheck toward underweight holdings, no selling), but the copy would have to say that.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. The core tier card promises it at frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:98-101 and Plus sells a custom schedule at :152-156. My Profile repeats it at frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:185-186. A case-insensitive grep for 'rebalanc' across backend/src/main returns only WashSaleGuardService.java:26,70,91,107 (a reserved REBALANCE enum value and a 'any future rebalancer' comment), a narrative validator phrase list, an analytics topic label and a TLH comment. There is no rebalance service, no job among the 6 schedulers, and no table in V1 to V9. I found no profile gate or flag that hides the copy. Blocker stands because the fix can be copy-only (path B, roughly S/M effort), so the XL effort applies only if Andy chooses to build it. Whether the claim is acceptable in any form is also a counsel question, since it is a statement about how a managed account is run.

### [blocker] Subscription tier is a free-text field any logged-in client can set, with no payment behind it

- Type: decision, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:528-542 (PUT /api/user/profile sets selectedTier to any string), :618-633 (POST /user/subscription/confirm, same, no receipt validation); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:771-791 (IAP TODO, then updateUserProfile); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:716-718 (in-app upgrade is the same PUT); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.html:262-266 ('Try FRED free for 14 days. No charges, cancel anytime.'); frontend/src/app/services/dev/tier-mock.ts:29-34
- Detail: Picking Piggy Plan at $8/mo on the tier screen only writes the string 'core' to the user row. No charge, no trial clock, no product id, no receipt. The same endpoint lets a user set 'pro' for free, and it accepts any string (no whitelist). The card advertises a 14-day free trial and referral lifetime pricing ($5/mo with 3 referrals) with nothing behind either on the billing side. This is the known billing gap; reporting only what exists. Note that tier-mock.ts currently has 'plus' uncommented, which is harmless in deployed builds (gated on environment.local at auth.service.ts:233) but will overwrite the tier on every local launch.
- Suggested fix: Blocked on the counsel answer about per-paycheck billing. Independent of that outcome, app work that can start now: whitelist tier values server side, stop accepting selectedTier from the generic profile PUT in non-local profiles (make tier changes go through one subscription endpoint), and remove or soften the 14-day trial and price copy until a billing mechanism exists.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. PUT /user/profile accepts any string for selectedTier with no whitelist (UserController.java:528-542) and POST /user/subscription/confirm does the same with no receipt check (:618-633, SubscriptionService.java:21-35). The tier screen has an IAP TODO then writes the tier directly (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:771-791), and the in-app upgrade is the same PUT (my-profile.page.ts:716). The 14-day trial copy is at investmentconfirmation.component.html:262-266. tier-mock.ts:29-34 does have 'plus' uncommented and is gated on environment.local at auth.service.ts:233, as the auditor said. Extra details the auditor missed: (1) the two write paths set different columns, the profile PUT sets subscriptionStartedAt (UserController.java:537-538) while SubscriptionService sets subscriptionStartDate (:30-31), so there are two start-date fields to reconcile; (2) BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:13-28 states plainly that billing does not exist, that the fee is meant to be collected by ACH outside Apple, and its three events (trial started, billed, cancelled) have no caller; a 14-day TRIAL_LENGTH_DAYS constant exists (ServerAnalyticsEvent.java:105) but no trial record or clock; (3) the code comments still point at Apple IAP / RevenueCat, which conflicts with the per-paycheck ACH plan and should be cleaned up once counsel answers. Severity nuance: it is a blocker for charging anyone and for the App Store. For a free private beta where users carry the privateBeta flag it does not stop the beta itself, but the free-text tier lets any user self-grant 'pro' and so turn on real TLH trading (TlhController.java:272), which is a reason to do the whitelist and single-endpoint work before real money even without billing.

### [blocker] All auto-invest notifications are mocked: email is disabled by default and push is a log line

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:34,56-62 (app.email.enabled default false, records MOCKED); backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:47-49 (EMAIL_ENABLED:false, EMAIL_FROM default noreply@investingapp.com, provider 'logging'); only EmailSender implementation is service/LoggingEmailSender.java; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PaydayNotificationService.java:104-111 ('FCM push notification infrastructure: implement when Firebase project is configured'); callers InvestmentExecutionService.java:1175-1213 and :398-405
- Detail: The promise is 'so you never have to think about it', which makes failure notices essential. Today a returned ACH, a missing bank relationship, or a failed trade produces only a mocked email_log row. The day-before 'FRED will invest $X tomorrow' notice (which is also the user's chance to stop a debit) is never delivered by email or push. A user would only find out by opening the app and reading history.
- Suggested fix: Choose and wire a real EmailSender (the roadmap mentions Resend), set a real from address on a verified domain, and enable it for the beta environment. Implement APNs/FCM push or drop the push path. Add an in-app surface for the most recent failed execution so delivery problems do not hide failures. Confirm with counsel whether a pre-debit notice is required for recurring ACH.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. EmailService.java:34 defaults app.email.enabled to false and :56-62 records STATUS_MOCKED and returns. application.properties:47-49 sets EMAIL_ENABLED false, from noreply@investingapp.com, provider logging. The only EmailSender implementation is LoggingEmailSender (conditional on provider=logging), so even setting EMAIL_ENABLED=true would only log, and setting any other provider value would leave no EmailSender bean and likely fail startup. No profile file other than ci overrides these (application-ci.properties:80). Push is a log line at PaydayNotificationService.java:104-111, even though the frontend does register and store a device token (frontend/src/app/services/push-notification.service.ts:25-42). The D-1, D+0 and D+1 jobs are wired and run (InvestmentScheduler.java:147-180), so the plumbing is done and only delivery is missing. One softening point: failed executions are visible in app, since the frontend maps FUNDING_FAILED and TRADING_FAILED to labels and a failed state (frontend/src/app/services/investment.service.ts:237-276), so the user is not fully blind, but only if they open the app. The same mocked EmailService also carries the account status emails (AccountStatusService.java:167) and the market breakdown, so one real sender fixes all of them. Blocker stands for real money: a failed or returned ACH debit with no outbound notice is not acceptable, and whether a pre-debit notice is legally required is a counsel question. I did not check how the account recovery OTP email is sent; if it goes through this same service, recovery is also broken, and the lead should confirm.

### [high] My Profile shows a fabricated 'Next rebalance' date and a schedule that is only saved in localStorage

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:63-80 (nextRebalanceDate 'Heuristic: next calendar quarter/half/year boundary from today'), :729-747 (selectSchedule saves via goalsService.savePrefs, comment 'synchronous localStorage write'); frontend/src/app/services/goals.service.ts:177-200; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:199-205
- Detail: Plus and Pro users see 'Next rebalance: <date>', which is computed in the browser from the calendar and corresponds to no scheduled job. The Quarterly / Semi-annual / Annual choice is written only to localStorage keyed by user id, so it is lost on reinstall or a new device and the backend never sees it. Core users are told 'On Piggy, FRED uses the default quarterly schedule', which is also untrue because there is no schedule at all.
- Suggested fix: Tie this to whichever path is chosen for the rebalancing finding. If rebalancing is built, persist the schedule on the server (new column or prefs table plus endpoint) and have the server return the real next run date. If it is deferred, remove the date line and the default-schedule note.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. nextRebalanceDate is a calendar heuristic at frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:63-80 with no server input. selectSchedule at :729-735 calls goalsService.savePrefs, which is a localStorage write (frontend/src/app/services/goals.service.ts:192-195; the auditor's line range 177-200 is close enough). The date line and the Piggy 'default quarterly schedule' note render at my-profile.page.html:199-205. No backend field or endpoint for rebalanceSchedule exists. This is the same root cause as the rebalancing finding and should be one checklist item with it: if rebalancing copy is removed for beta, this card goes with it. The setting also emits a setting_changed analytics event with outcome 'applied' (:738-746), which records a change that had no effect.

### [high] Auto-invest has only run against sandbox brokers; there is no production path yet

- Type: external, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/application-dev.properties:39 (plaid.env=sandbox), :59-65 (Alpaca URLs from env); backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:19-23 (prod profile and BrokerEnvironmentGuard exist, plaid.env=production); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:646-653 (transfer pending over 7 days only logs a warning)
- Detail: The whole ACH and order flow depends on Alpaca Broker API and Plaid. Only sandbox has been used and there is no production backend. Real ACH timing, returns after settlement, and daily transfer limits will behave differently from sandbox. A transfer stuck pending for more than 7 days only writes a log line, with no alert or user message.
- Suggested fix: Blocked on Alpaca production approval and the production backend. App work that can be done now: turn the 7-day pending warning into a real alert, and write a go-live test script that runs one small real contribution through every state.
- Verifier (adjusted): Mostly right, with one correction and one addition. Correct: application-dev.properties:39 is plaid.env=sandbox with Alpaca URLs from env at :59-65; a prod profile exists with plaid.env=production and BrokerEnvironmentGuard refuses wrong hosts at startup, which is solid work; the stale-transfer branch at InvestmentExecutionService.java:646-653 only logs a warning (and its comment says 5 business days while the code checks 7 calendar days). 'Only sandbox has ever been used' is a claim about history that code cannot prove, so treat it as the known context rather than a verified fact. Addition the auditor missed: application-prod.properties contains no Alpaca keys at all (grep for 'alpaca' in that file and in application.properties returns nothing), while the dev file defines seven alpaca.* properties. The header comment listing the variables to set before first prod boot also omits Alpaca. I did not trace whether the Alpaca beans have defaults or rely on environment-variable binding, so confidence is medium, but as written the prod profile looks incomplete for Alpaca and this is app work that can be done now, not external. I would split the item: the Alpaca production approval and prod backend are external; adding the Alpaca block to the prod profile, alerting on stale pending transfers, and a go-live test script are app work at high severity.

### [medium] No backend tier enforcement on core features, and auto-invest keeps running with no active subscription

- Type: decision, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/ScheduledInvestmentProcessor.java:57-67 (eligibility checks account status and schedule only); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/InvestmentSchedule.java:356-365; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentScheduleService.java:293-301; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/SubscriptionService.java:37-47 (expireSubscription only nulls selectedTier); getSelectedTier is read only in TlhController.java:272, TlhExecutionService.java:142, GoalService.java:135, ReferralService.java:85, AnalyticsOutboxService.java:145; frontend/src/app/tabs/tabs.page.ts:86-89
- Detail: The only backend tier gates in the app are TLH (pro) and the multi-goal limit (TierLimitException). Auto-invest, portfolio customization and everything else in core have no subscription check. When selectedTier is null (the 'expired' state), the frontend shows a reactivation banner and locks swiping, but the 11:55 PM job still queues the user's schedule and the 11:59 PM batch still pulls money from their bank. Whether an unpaid or lapsed user should keep being invested is a product and legal decision (stopping contributions could itself harm the user), but today there is no decision encoded at all. I did not find any code that calls expireSubscription.
- Suggested fix: Decide the lapsed-subscriber policy with counsel as part of the per-paycheck billing question (keep investing, pause with notice, or grace period). Then encode it in one place: a server-side entitlement check used by ScheduledInvestmentProcessor and the controllers, instead of scattered string comparisons.
- Verifier (adjusted): The facts are right, but the severity is high only once billing exists. Confirmed: ScheduledInvestmentProcessor.java:57-67 checks mode, due date, accountStatus ACTIVE and isReadyForInvestment only; InvestmentSchedule.java:356-365 has no tier clause; getSelectedTier is read only in TlhController.java:272, TlhExecutionService.java:142, GoalService.java:135, ReferralService.java:78/85 and AnalyticsOutboxService.java:145. SubscriptionService.expireSubscription (:37-47) has no caller anywhere in backend/src/main. That last point is what lowers the severity: nothing in the system can put a user into the lapsed state today except a client sending selectedTier null through PUT /user/profile (UserController.java:530-533) or the local dev mock. Also missed by the auditor: users flagged privateBeta bypass the expired gate entirely on the client (frontend/src/app/tabs/tabs.page.ts:87-89, app.component.ts:571), so for the private beta cohort the lapsed state is not reachable in the UI at all. So for private beta this is a policy decision to record alongside the billing question, not a live defect. It becomes high the moment a billing mechanism can expire a subscription. Type 'decision' is correct.

### [medium] Small contributions silently skip portfolio slices, and an all-skipped execution is reported as a success

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:939-946 (slice under $1.00 is skipped with a log warning), :1067-1118 (empty trade list leaves allComplete true, sets COMPLETED and sends success notice); frontend/src/app/components/investment-schedule/investment-schedule.component.ts:75 (min: 5); backend dto investment schedule request line 21 (@DecimalMin 1.0)
- Detail: With the default 75/20/5 portfolio, any per-paycheck amount under $20 never buys VBR (5% of $19 is $0.95), so the user's real allocation never matches the advertised one and the skipped cash sits uninvested with no message. The app's minimum is $5, where VBR gets $0.25. A custom portfolio with many small slices is worse. The API minimum is $1, where every slice is under $1: no trades are created, yet the execution is marked COMPLETED and the user is told the investment 'has been successfully executed'.
- Suggested fix: Redistribute sub-$1 slices into the largest holding (or carry them forward) so the full amount is invested, raise the server-side minimum to match the app, and treat an execution with zero trades as failed or still-pending rather than completed.

### [medium] Partially filled, expired or otherwise unhandled order states can strand an execution forever

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:1077-1104 (only trades in SUBMITTED are re-polled; a trade set to PARTIALLY_FILLED at :1100-1102 is never checked again; only 'rejected' and 'canceled' are treated as failures); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AlpacaService.java:304 (time_in_force 'day'); InvestmentExecutionService.java:1008-1013 (TRADING_FAILED, no retry)
- Detail: Orders are day orders. If Alpaca reports 'expired' (or any status other than filled, rejected, canceled, partially filled), the trade stays SUBMITTED and the execution stays TRADING_INITIATED indefinitely with no alert. A trade that moves to PARTIALLY_FILLED is never polled again because the loop only looks at SUBMITTED trades. When some slices fail to place, the execution becomes TRADING_FAILED and the money for those slices stays as cash with no retry. Likelihood is low for liquid ETFs but real money would be left uninvested with nothing surfaced except a (mocked) email. I did not run this against Alpaca; this is from reading the code.
- Suggested fix: Poll trades in SUBMITTED or PARTIALLY_FILLED, treat expired/done_for_day/stopped/suspended as terminal failures, add a retry or a re-queue for failed slices, and add a staleness alert for executions sitting in TRADING_INITIATED or FUNDING_COMPLETED for more than a set time.

### [medium] Scheduled buy orders have no idempotency key, so a timeout can leave an order placed but recorded as failed

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AlpacaService.java:295-346 (placeBuyOrder sends no client_order_id; any exception returns FAILED), versus :589-599 (placeBuyOrderWithClientId exists and is used by TLH); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:968-990
- Detail: The paycheck buy path uses placeBuyOrder, which has no client order id. If the HTTP call times out after Alpaca accepted the order, the trade row is marked FAILED and the execution TRADING_FAILED, while the order actually fills. Books and broker then disagree. The safer variant with a client order id already exists in the same class but the scheduled path does not use it.
- Suggested fix: Use placeBuyOrderWithClientId with a deterministic id (for example execution id plus symbol) on the scheduled and lump-sum paths, and on an ambiguous error look the order up by client id before marking it failed.

### [medium] 'Investment complete' notice is sent at midnight, before the ACH has settled or anything is bought

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:159-169 (D+0 job at 00:00 ET for the previous day); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PaydayNotificationService.java:72-87 ('Investment complete', '$X invested. Your freedom date just moved closer.'); funding only starts at InvestmentExecutionService.java:283 (23:59 batch) and trading only after transfer completion at :578-605
- Detail: One minute after the batch ACH is merely initiated, the user is told the money is invested. Funding takes days and can still be returned, and trades are placed only after settlement during market hours. The D+1 message makes a similar claim. The notice does not check execution status at all, so a FUNDING_FAILED execution also gets 'Investment complete'. This is mocked today, but it becomes a false statement the moment email or push is switched on. I did not open the repository query behind findByScheduledDate, so exactly which rows match is unconfirmed.
- Suggested fix: Change D+0 copy to 'transfer started' and send the 'invested' message from the COMPLETED transition (notifyUserOfSuccess already exists at InvestmentExecutionService.java:1175), skipping failed executions.

### [medium] Backend does not validate custom portfolio symbols

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PortfolioService.java:57-89 (uppercases symbol, checks only that percentages total 100); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/dto/UpdatePortfolioRequest.java:21-41 (@NotBlank only); frontend/src/app/components/portfolio-customize/portfolio-customize.component.ts:204-209 (tradable/active filter is client side only); backend AlpacaService.java:351-403 (getAssetInfo exists)
- Detail: The tradable and active checks happen only in the app's search results. The API will store any string as a holding, and duplicates are not rejected. A bad symbol would then fail on every paycheck, turning each execution into TRADING_FAILED with part of the money left as cash. A real user is unlikely to hit this through the UI, but a symbol that is later delisted or made non-tradable has the same effect and nothing re-validates saved portfolios.
- Suggested fix: On PUT /portfolio/update, validate each symbol with the existing AlpacaService.getAssetInfo (exists, active, tradable), reject duplicates, cap the number of holdings, and handle a non-tradable saved symbol gracefully at buy time (redirect the slice and notify the user).

### [medium] Changing the portfolio only affects future buys; existing holdings are never moved

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PortfolioService.java:57-89 (update only rewrites target rows); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:931-938 (buys split by fixed target percentage); frontend/src/app/components/portfolio-customize/portfolio-customize.component.ts:634-641 ('Portfolio saved successfully')
- Detail: Because there is no rebalancer, a user who switches from the default portfolio to a custom one (or back) keeps all existing positions as they are; only new contributions follow the new targets. The save screen does not say this, and combined with the rebalancing promise a user would reasonably expect their holdings to be realigned.
- Suggested fix: Until rebalancing exists, add one line to the customize screen stating that changes apply to future investments only. Revisit once the rebalancing decision is made.

### [medium] Withdrawal education content and the word 'proven' need a compliance read

- Type: external, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/strategy-deck/strategy-deck.component.ts:46-164 (for example 'Start at 5%', '40-60% of portfolio annuitized', 'Guardrails survived nearly every historical market', 'Every major U.S. downturn has recovered'); frontend/src/app/components/strategy-deck/strategy-deck.component.html:70-73 (disclaimer only on slide 4 of 5); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:94 ("FRED's proven default portfolio"); frontend/src/app/components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.html:121 ('Four proven ways')
- Detail: The decks give specific withdrawal rates, annuitization ranges, SBLOC guidance and statements about historical outcomes. There is one 'Educational content, not investment advice' line, on the fourth slide only. 'Proven' is used for both the default portfolio and the four strategies. Nothing in the repo shows this copy has been reviewed by counsel.
- Suggested fix: Send the four decks and the tier card copy to counsel. Likely app work afterward is small: repeat the disclaimer on the first slide and the education landing, and replace 'proven'.

### [low] 'Paycheck-based' is a user-entered calendar, not paycheck detection

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/ScheduledInvestmentProcessor.java:51-56 ('Phase 0 knows MANUAL only ... a DETECTED row never queues from here'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentScheduleService.java:303-309 (DETECTED -> false); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/InvestmentSchedule.java:79 (default MANUAL)
- Detail: The copy 'FRED automatically invests your set portion of every paycheck' is delivered by the user telling FRED their pay frequency and next pay date. If a payday shifts or a paycheck is missed, FRED still debits the bank on the calendar date. That is a fair reading of the copy, but the label could be heard as real paycheck detection, which is the unmerged Paycheck Autopilot work. No code path creates DETECTED schedules in this tree, so there is no risk of a schedule silently never running.
- Suggested fix: Keep the copy but make sure onboarding states that investing follows the pay schedule the user entered. Decide whether Paycheck Autopilot is in or out of the beta.

### [low] Education 'Stress-test this strategy' button sends core users to a locked simulator

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/strategy-deck/strategy-deck.component.html:87-90; frontend/src/app/components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.ts:62-64 (isMonteCarloLocked for non plus/pro), :112-114 (role 'stress-test' switches to simulator and toasts 'Simulator ready. Tap the piggy'); frontend/src/app/components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.html:103-109 (lock overlay)
- Detail: The last slide of every education deck has a primary button that, for a Piggy Plan user, lands on the Monte Carlo section with an 'Available on Plus and Pro' lock overlay while a toast says the simulator is ready. The button carries no lock chip, unlike the Optimal Drawdown button beside it.
- Suggested fix: For core users, show a Plus lock chip on the button and open the Plus nudge instead of switching sections, or hide the button.

### [low] Dead autoRebalance setting implies a feature that does not exist

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/services/settings.service.ts:23,58 (autoRebalance: true default); no other reference in frontend/src/app
- Detail: settings.service declares and defaults autoRebalance to true but nothing reads it. Harmless today, misleading for whoever builds rebalancing later.
- Suggested fix: Remove it, or wire it to the real feature when that is built.

**Verified solid:**

- Nightly scheduling is idempotent: each schedule is processed in its own REQUIRES_NEW transaction with a per-schedule source key and a per-user night key, so a double run cannot queue two debits (ScheduledInvestmentProcessor.java:44-85).
- The 11:59 PM batch claims rows in a committed transaction before the Alpaca ACH POST, keeps the HTTP call outside any DB transaction, fails fast with no POST when the bank relationship is missing, and isolates each user's writes (InvestmentExecutionService.java:283-405).
- Both money jobs take a job lock and honor the investment.scheduler.enabled gate, which is off under the ci profile (InvestmentScheduler.java:45-63, SchedulerGate.java, application-ci.properties:27).
- A schedule only runs when the Alpaca account is ACTIVE, the schedule status is ACTIVE, an ACH request id exists and no blocking hold is set; new schedules start as PENDING_BANK (InvestmentSchedule.java:356-365, InvestmentScheduleService.java:176).
- Pause and resume work server side, resume is blocked unless the account is ACTIVE, and a bank change puts the schedule on hold rather than deleting it (InvestmentScheduleService.java:238-270, 326-420).
- Funding status is checked by Alpaca transfer id (not amount matching), batch siblings are settled together, and RETURNED / CANCELED / REJECTED transfers fail all sibling executions with a specific message (InvestmentExecutionService.java:569-642).
- Trades are held until market hours and picked up by the 15-minute trading job; non-fractionable assets fall back to whole-share orders (InvestmentExecutionService.java:848-857, 1040-1056, 1218-1258).
- Scheduled buys use the user's saved portfolio, with the 75/20/5 default as fallback, and respect TLH wash-sale windows by redirecting or skipping the slice (InvestmentExecutionService.java:931-962, 1136-1173).
- Default portfolio is exactly 75% VTI, 20% VXUS, 5% VBR on the backend, matching the tier card copy (Portfolio.java:99-113, PortfolioService.java:139-150).
- BYO portfolio is wired end to end: Alpaca asset search filtered to tradable and active, 100% total enforced on both client and server, PIN step-up before save when a PIN exists, reachable from onboarding and from tab 3 (portfolio-customize.component.ts:170-215, 574-650; PortfolioService.java:77-82).
- All four withdrawal education strategies named in the copy exist (dividends/yield, dynamic guardrails, SBLOC borrowing, annuity), each a 5-slide deck on tab 2, open to every tier, with an educational-content disclaimer (strategy-deck.component.ts:40-164, strategy-deck.component.html:72).
- The dev tier mock cannot leak into deployed builds: it is only consulted when environment.local is true, and prod, dev and test environments all set local: false (auth.service.ts:233, environments/*.ts).
- application-local.properties (which holds sandbox keys) is not tracked by git.
- Frontend minimum per-paycheck amount is $5 and the backend rejects amounts under $1 (investment-schedule.component.ts:75; schedule request DTO line 21).

## tier-plus-a

Piggy Plus ($15/mo) promises three things in my scope: Monte Carlo modeling, multi-goal tracking, and adding 401(k)/Roth IRA balances. Multi-goal tracking is the strongest: it is backend-backed (goals table, V9 migration, /api/goals) and is the only one of the three with a real server-side tier check. Monte Carlo is real and working, but runs entirely on the phone, is gated only in the UI, and answers a different question than the tier card promises (it tests whether money lasts in retirement, not the odds of hitting the Freedom Date). 'Add 401(k) & Roth IRA' is the weakest: it is only an optional step inside the Monte Carlo flow, stored in localStorage, limited to exactly two fields, and never feeds the Freedom Date projection that the copy says it does. Across all three, the tier itself is not trustworthy: any signed-in user can set selectedTier to any value through PUT /user/profile with no payment, and every 'Upgrade' button on these paywalls is a console.log stub. I did not run the app or tests; everything below is from reading the working tree.


| Tier | Feature | Status | Tier gate | Gaps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| plus | Monte Carlo modeling | partial | yes-frontend-only | 1) Output is 'will your money last' success rates for five withdrawal strategies, not a probability of hitting the Freedom Date. 2) 1,000 paths per strategy, copy says 'thousands'. 3) Gate is UI only (blur + lock overlay), all math is client side. 4) Plus users only get the Moderate scenario; scenarios, adjusters and the 90% spend estimate are Pro locked inside the Plus feature. 5) Upgrade buttons are console.log stubs. 6) History and last-used inputs are device-only. 7) Methodology sheet claims calibration to S&P 500 1926-2024 while the constants are round numbers. |
| plus | Multi-goal tracking | implemented | yes-backend | Feature works end to end from the code. Gaps are at the edges: the tier it checks can be self-assigned (PUT /user/profile), POST /api/goals/import skips the tier check, the upgrade CTA on the nudge is a stub, and the device pass for Goals v3 is still owed per project notes (I could not verify on device). Core users get one goal, so the Plus value is strictly the second and later goals. |
| plus | Add 401(k) & Roth IRA | partial | yes-frontend-only | 1) Balances never reach the Freedom Date or any projection outside the Monte Carlo sim, although the copy says 'for a full picture of your Freedom Date'. 2) 'Any other accounts' is not supported: exactly two fixed fields. 3) Stored only in localStorage, lost on reinstall or a new phone. 4) No dedicated screen; the feature is a toggle that is off by default on the simulator landing. 5) Gated only because the whole simulator is UI-gated. 6) Outside money is simulated as 100% stocks like the FRED portfolio. 7) Manual entry only, no account linking (the label 'Add' may read as linking). |


### [blocker] Any signed-in user can give themselves Plus or Pro for free through PUT /user/profile

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:528-541 (selectedTier taken straight from the request body, any string, no payment or entitlement check); :618-634 POST /user/subscription/confirm does the same; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/SubscriptionService.java:21-35 (just writes the fields); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:773-783 (TODO IAP, then updateUserProfile with the tier); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:705-726 (referral 'upgrade' writes the tier directly).
- Detail: The tier is a plain string on the user row that the client writes. Every tier gate in my scope (goal limit in GoalService, the Monte Carlo lock, outside accounts) trusts that string. So the backend check on multi-goal is real code but is not real enforcement until the tier can only be set by a verified payment or entitlement. This is known billing work that is waiting on the per-paycheck decision with counsel; I am reporting what exists, not designing it. The value is also not validated against core/plus/pro.
- Suggested fix: Once the billing model is decided, make selectedTier server-owned: remove it from the generic profile update, validate the value against the three known tiers, and set it only from a verified payment or entitlement event. Until then, treat all tier gating as honour-system.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. UserController.java:528-541 writes any string from the request body to selectedTier with no validation or payment check. POST /user/subscription/confirm (:618-634) only checks that tier is not blank, then SubscriptionService.java:21-35 writes it. I found no guard, profile gate or allow-list anywhere. The frontend never calls /subscription/confirm (grep found no caller), so it is an open endpoint with no client. Two things the auditor understated: (1) the same string gates Pro tax-loss harvesting, which places real sell orders (TlhController.java:271-273, TlhExecutionService.java:140-145), so self-granting Pro unlocks real-money trading behaviour, not just screens. (2) The my-profile referral card shows a price ('Upgrade to X for $10/mo', my-profile.page.ts:705-726) and then flips the tier with no charge, using labels 'Standard'/'Premium' that do not match the Plus/Pro names. Caveat on severity: if the private beta is deliberately free, the bypass costs no revenue, so it is a blocker for charging and for TLH exposure rather than for letting beta users in at all. The lead should decide whether beta is free; if yes, the minimum app work is validating the value and deciding who may be Pro.

### [high] Every Upgrade button on the Plus paywalls does nothing

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.ts:299-302 (upgradeToPlusClicked is a TODO + console.log), :253-256 (Monte Carlo lock overlay button calls it), :260-261 (tapping the orb while locked calls it); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:888-898 (Plus and Pro stubs); frontend/src/app/pages/goal/goal.page.ts:1175-1181 (TIER_LIMIT nudge upgrade is a console.log); frontend/src/app/components/monte-carlo-flow/monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:689-695 dismisses with {upgrade:true} but retirement-planning.component.ts:291-292 awaits onDidDismiss and ignores the result.
- Detail: A Core user who hits the Monte Carlo lock, the second-goal nudge, or the Pro nudge inside the simulator and taps Upgrade sees nothing happen. For the lock overlay there is not even a sheet: the button is silently dead. This is the visible half of the missing billing work, and it also means a Core user has no in-app way to reach Plus features after onboarding (other than the referral offer card).
- Suggested fix: When billing lands, route all of these to one upgrade flow. Before that, either hide the buttons or have them open a plain 'upgrades are coming' sheet so the tap is acknowledged.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. retirement-planning.component.ts:299-302 is a TODO plus console.log, reached from the lock overlay button (html:106 via :253-256), the locked orb (:261), the deck 'upgrade' role (:118-119) and the market-scenarios nudge (:326-327). my-profile.page.ts:889-898 are the same stubs, reached from :835 and :861. goal.page.ts:1175-1181 logs only. monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:689-695 dismisses with {upgrade:true} and retirement-planning.component.ts:291-292 awaits onDidDismiss without reading the result. Small correction: the taps are not fully inert, they do fire paywall analytics events (trackPaywallNudge), but the user sees nothing. The only working in-app upgrade is the referral offer card (my-profile.page.html:262-275), which needs at least one referral and charges nothing. High is right: it is a dead-button issue for App Store review and depends on the billing decision.

### [high] 'Add 401(k) & Roth IRA' balances never affect the Freedom Date projection the copy promises

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: Promise: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:147-150. Only consumers of the balances: monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:408-412 and :920-940, and drawdown-deck.component.ts:129-131. No outside-balance input in frontend/src/app/services/freedom-stats.service.ts or frontend/src/app/pages/freedom-plan/freedom-plan.page.ts (grep for outside/401/roth found nothing relevant). No backend storage at all.
- Detail: The card says outside balances are included 'in your projections for a full picture of your Freedom Date'. In the app they only raise the starting balance of one Monte Carlo run. The Freedom Date on the home tab, the profile card, the Freedom Plan page and the monthly update all ignore them. The balances also live only in localStorage (key fred.mcOutside.<userId>), so they disappear on reinstall or a new device. This also contradicts the Plus pitch 'Every account, every goal, one plan' (component.ts:123).
- Suggested fix: Decide between building it and narrowing the copy. Building it means a server-stored outside-balances record, an entry point outside the simulator (for example on the Freedom Plan page), and feeding it into the unified freedom estimate on both stacks. Narrowing means copy like 'Add your 401(k) and Roth IRA balances to your retirement simulations.'
- Verifier (confirmed): True. Copy at investmentconfirmation.component.ts:147-150 promises inclusion 'in your projections for a full picture of your Freedom Date'. The balances are signals inside the simulator only (monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:257-258), added to the starting portfolio at :408-412, :543 and :558, persisted only to localStorage under fred.mcOutside.<userId> (:920-940) and read once more in drawdown-deck.component.ts:131. My grep of freedom-stats.service.ts and freedom-plan.page.ts found no outside-balance input (the only 'outside' hits in freedom-plan are tap-outside pointer handlers at :427-597), and grep for mcOutside/k401Balance/rothBalance across the frontend found no other consumers. I did not find a backend field for these balances; I did not read every backend file that matched 'outside|roth', so that part is medium-high confidence. Also note the feature is opt-in per run via a toggle (retirement-planning.component.html:67-68), and only 401(k) and Roth are supported, not 'any other accounts' as the copy says. High stands as an over-promise on a financial product; narrowing the copy is a small fix.

### [high] Monte Carlo copy promises a Freedom Date probability, the feature shows retirement survival odds

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: Promise: investmentconfirmation.component.ts:135-138 ('real probability of hitting your Freedom Date'). Actual: frontend/src/app/components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.html:31-32 ('Will your money last?'), monte-carlo-flow.component.html:225 ('Five ways to withdraw. Here's what held across 1,000 markets.'), monte-carlo.service.ts:277-295 (success = portfolio never hits zero during withdrawal years).
- Detail: The simulator takes a portfolio value, a monthly spend and a number of years, then reports for five withdrawal strategies how often the money lasted. Nowhere does it output the chance of reaching the Freedom Date or freedom number. The accumulation phase is seeded from the freedom year (monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:752-757) but it is an input, not a result. On a financial product, a tier card that names a specific output the app does not produce is an over-promise that counsel may also care about.
- Suggested fix: Simplest path is to rewrite the description to match what exists, for example 'Stress-tests your retirement plan against 1,000 simulated markets to show how often your money lasts.' The alternative is to add an accumulation-only result (percent of paths that reach the freedom number by the Freedom Date), which the existing _accumulate code could support.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. Copy at investmentconfirmation.component.ts:135-138 says 'the real probability of hitting your Freedom Date'. The screens say 'Will your money last?' (retirement-planning.component.html:31) and 'Five ways to withdraw. Here's what held across 1,000 markets.' (monte-carlo-flow.component.html:225). Every percentage shown is successFor(strategy) (html:241-245, :295), which monte-carlo.service.ts:277-295 computes as the share of runs where the balance stays above zero through the withdrawal years. The accumulation phase exists (_accumulate :240, _phaseStart :264) but only produces the starting value for withdrawal; no output reports the share of paths reaching the freedom number by the Freedom Date. Also 'thousands of market simulations' is loose against the '1,000 markets' the UI states. Type 'decision' is right: copy fix is small, building the accumulation result is the alternative. High is fair for public launch; for a private beta it is a copy risk for counsel rather than a functional blocker.

Tier comes from userProgress$; when not logged in it can come from localStorage 'fred.selectedTier' (frontend/src/app/services/auth.service.ts:301, :313). No backend endpoint is involved in the simulation.

In a shipped iOS app this is hard for a normal user to bypass, so the practical risk is low compared with the self-assigned tier finding. Also note the blurred landing is still in the DOM and the scenario chips and toggle under the blur are still tappable elements beneath the overlay; I could not confirm on device whether the overlay blocks taps.

### [medium] Copy says 'thousands' of simulations and 'any other accounts'; the app does 1,000 and two fixed account fields

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: investmentconfirmation.component.ts:137 ('thousands of market simulations') vs monte-carlo.service.ts:108 (SIMULATIONS = 1000) and monte-carlo-flow.component.html:202, :215. investmentconfirmation.component.ts:149 ('401(k)s, Roth IRAs, and any other accounts') vs monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:74 and :714-726 (only k401 and roth, one balance each).

- Suggested fix: Change the card to '1,000 market simulations' and '401(k) and Roth IRA balances', or add a third 'Other accounts' field (small change in the flow, history entry and drawdown deck reader) and raise the simulation count.

- Evidence: monte-carlo-flow.component.html:253-316 (panel rendered for Plus with disabled steppers, blurred values, and a 'Deeper projections are Piggy Pro' veil); retirement-planning.component.html:52-58 (scenario chips locked unless Pro); mc-info-sheet.component.html:7 ('FRED tests your plan using three market scenarios, each simulated 1,000 times').

Worth a conscious product decision before real customers pay $15.

### [medium] Methodology sheet claims calibration to S&P 500 data 1926-2024; outside balances are simulated as 100% stocks

- Type: external, effort S, auditor confidence medium

This is the kind of factual claim counsel should clear before real users rely on the numbers.

## tier-plus-b

Piggy Plus ($15/mo) promises two features in this domain. Neither is deliverable to a real user today.  1) Custom rebalancing schedule: only a settings UI exists. The Quarterly / Semi-annual / Annual chips on My Profile save to localStorage on the device and the backend never sees the choice. More importantly, there is no rebalancing engine anywhere in the backend (no drift check, no rebalance job, no Alpaca rebalancing call), so the schedule controls nothing. The "Next rebalance: <date>" line shown to Plus users is a calendar heuristic computed in the browser for an event that will never happen. This also means the Core tier promise "Automatic portfolio rebalancing" that this feature builds on is not built.  2) Monthly market breakdown: the backend pipeline is genuinely built and well tested (real Alpaca monthly closes, one web-search-grounded Claude call, deterministic compliance gate, per-user deterministic numbers, idempotent email_log, 1st/2nd/3rd catch-up cron, plus/pro eligibility query). But nothing is ever delivered: sends are MOCKED (app.email.enabled defaults false, the only EmailSender is LoggingEmailSender), there is no in-app surface at all, the Unsubscribe link is a literal "#", there is no opt-out field, and the footer has no postal address. The narrative is also hardcoded to the FRED default portfolio (VTI/VXUS/VBR) even though users can build their own portfolio, so for BYO users "why your portfolio went up or down" would describe funds they do not hold.  Tier gating: the market breakdown is gated on the backend by users.selected_tier in ('plus','pro'). Custom rebalance is gated in the frontend only. In both cases the tier value itself is not trustworthy, because any authenticated user can set selectedTier through PUT /user/profile or POST /user/subscription/confirm with no payment check (billing not built, known).  All paths below are under /Users/andrewtimmer/PersonalTypeshit/FRED.


| Tier | Feature | Status | Tier gate | Gaps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| plus | Custom rebalancing schedule | missing | yes-frontend-only | No rebalancing engine exists at all, so the schedule controls nothing. Preference never reaches the backend (lost on reinstall or new device, cannot drive a server job). 'Next rebalance' date is shown for an event that never happens. No backend tier enforcement (nothing to enforce yet). The lock-tap upgrade nudge shows multi-goal copy instead of rebalancing copy. 'Based on your tax situation' has no supporting guidance in the UI. |
| plus | Monthly market breakdown | partial | yes-backend | Nothing is delivered to users: email is mocked and no real provider class exists. No in-app view of the breakdown. Unsubscribe link is '#', no opt-out flag, no postal address in footer. Narrative is hardcoded to VTI/VXUS/VBR so it is wrong for BYO portfolios. Scheduler has no job lock or enable flag and makes real Alpaca plus Claude calls monthly even while mocked. MOCKED rows count as sent, so months mocked before go-live are never re-sent. No alerting when all three daily attempts fail. Templates still contain em dashes. Default from-address is noreply@investingapp.com. Tier value it gates on is client-settable without payment. |


### [blocker] No rebalancing engine exists, so 'Custom rebalancing schedule' (and Core 'Automatic portfolio rebalancing') is not delivered

- Type: app-work, effort XL, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:97-102 and 152-157 (promises); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:184-185 ('When your allocation drifts, FRED automatically realigns it. No trade approvals are needed.'); backend grep for 'rebalanc' (case-insensitive) in backend/src/main returns only MarketNarrativeValidator.java:35, WashSaleGuardService.java:26,70,91,107 ('any future rebalancer', unused REBALANCE enum), TlhSummaryService.java:33, ConversationAnalyticsService.java:106; InvestmentExecutionService.java:939-946 and 1138-1175 only split new deposits by target percentages; FREDdocs/.stories/FRED-216.md:28
- Detail: There is no backend code that measures drift or places rebalancing trades. New contributions are bought at the target percentages, but existing holdings are never realigned. The Plus feature lets a user pick a schedule for a job that does not exist. Both tier cards and the My Profile card state in the present tense that FRED rebalances automatically. For a paid product handling real money this is a promise of a service that is not performed. Whether automatic rebalancing with no trade approval is allowed under FRED's regulatory setup is also a counsel question, and selling to rebalance creates taxable events and must respect the existing WashSaleGuardService.
- Suggested fix: Decide one of: (a) build the engine: persist the schedule on the user row, add a scheduler that compares Alpaca positions to target allocation on the user's cadence, routes sells and buys through WashSaleGuardService (the REBALANCE origin already exists), uses JobLockService, and records an audit trail; or (b) for beta, change both tier cards and the My Profile card to 'Coming soon' (the lockState 'coming-soon' already exists) and hide the schedule chips and the 'Next rebalance' line. Option (b) is small and removes the false claim.
- Verifier (confirmed): True in the working tree. A case-insensitive grep for 'rebalanc' across backend/src/main returns only MarketNarrativeValidator.java:35, ConversationAnalyticsService.java:106, WashSaleGuardService.java:26,70,91,107 (comment says 'any future rebalancer', REBALANCE enum has no caller) and TlhSummaryService.java:33. There is no scheduler, service or migration that measures drift or places realigning trades. InvestmentExecutionService.java:939-947 and 1138-1175 only split a new deposit by the saved percentages. The promises are real and present tense: investmentconfirmation.component.ts:97-102 (Core, 'Automatic portfolio rebalancing', unlocked), 152-157 (Plus, 'Custom rebalancing schedule', unlocked), my-profile.page.html:184-185 ('FRED automatically realigns it. No trade approvals are needed.') and 203-205 ('On Piggy, FRED uses the default quarterly schedule'). I found no profile gate or flag that hides any of this. Blocker stands because the claim is made on the paid tier card and on the free tier, but note the effort: XL applies only to building the engine. The beta-safe path (mark both features coming-soon, hide chips and the date line, reword the My Profile card) is S. I did not open FREDdocs/.stories/FRED-216.md.

### [blocker] Monthly market breakdown is never delivered: sends are mocked and no real email provider is implemented

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:34,56-62; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/LoggingEmailSender.java:13-25 (grep 'implements EmailSender' finds only this class); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailSender.java:5-11; backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:47-49; FREDdocs/backlog.md:185 ('Founder unblocks: choose email provider')
- Detail: With app.email.enabled=false (the default everywhere) EmailService writes a MOCKED row and returns without calling the sender. Even if the flag were flipped, the only sender logs a line and sends nothing. A Plus subscriber paying $15/mo would receive nothing. Known and intended for now, but it is the whole delivery channel for this feature. The same EmailService carries investment, payday and account-status emails (InvestmentExecutionService.java:1186-1214, PaydayNotificationService.java:67-96, AccountStatusService.java:167), so those are mocked too.
- Suggested fix: Pick the provider (memory notes say Resend, from fred@fredvested.com), add one EmailSender implementation with @ConditionalOnProperty, set EMAIL_ENABLED / EMAIL_PROVIDER / EMAIL_FROM in the environment, set up SPF/DKIM/DMARC for the sending domain, and do the go-live gates in the other findings first. Provider account and DNS are external; the adapter class is app work.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. EmailService.java:34 defaults app.email.enabled to false and lines 56-62 record a MOCKED row and return. LoggingEmailSender.java:13-25 is the only EmailSender implementation and only logs. application.properties:47-49 defaults provider to 'logging'. The other transactional emails go through the same service (PaydayNotificationService.java:67,81,96; InvestmentExecutionService.java:1186,1201,1214; AccountStatusService.java:167), so they are mocked too. I also checked for an in-app fallback: MarketBreakdownController.java:25-29 describes /api/market-breakdown/preview as a verification surface only, and the only frontend file mentioning market-breakdown is the tier card itself, so there is no in-app way to read the breakdown. Blocker is right for charging for Plus. If Plus is not sold in the private beta, or the feature is marked coming-soon, this drops to high. The fact that investment-failure and account-status emails are also silent is arguably the more important part for a real-money beta and deserves its own line in the checklist.

### [blocker] Tier used for market-breakdown eligibility can be set by any logged-in user without paying

- Type: decision, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:528-542 (PUT /user/profile accepts any selectedTier string) and 618-633 (POST /user/subscription/confirm takes tier from the request body with no receipt check); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/SubscriptionService.java:21-35; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:705-725 (upgrade just calls updateUserProfile) and 895-898 (TODO: trigger Pro subscription IAP)
- Detail: Billing is known to be unbuilt and waiting on counsel, so this is reported for completeness only. The effect in this domain: the plus/pro check in MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:31,68 and the frontend rebalance gate both trust a value the client can write freely, and the tier string is not validated against core/plus/pro. Until a payment source of truth exists, every tier gate is honour-system.
- Suggested fix: When the billing model is decided, make selectedTier writable only by the server-side billing path, remove it from the generic profile PUT, and validate the allowed values. For beta, decide whether everyone simply gets Plus for free and say so in the UI.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. UserController.java:528-542 sets selectedTier to any string sent on PUT /user/profile (null clears it) with no allowed-values check and no payment check. UserController.java:618-633 POST /user/subscription/confirm takes tier from the body, and SubscriptionService.java:21-35 saves it with no receipt validation. my-profile.page.ts:705-718 'Confirm upgrade' just calls updateUserProfile and toasts 'Upgraded', and 889-898 are TODO stubs for IAP. MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:31,68 and the frontend isPlusOrPro gate (my-profile.page.ts:199-202) trust this value. Blocker for charging anyone, and correctly typed as a decision since billing waits on counsel. Two extra details: no frontend code calls /user/subscription/confirm (grep found none), so that endpoint is currently dead but open; and the two write paths stamp different fields (profile PUT sets subscriptionStartedAt, SubscriptionService sets subscriptionStartDate), which should be unified when billing is built. Also note the confirm sheet tells the user they are upgrading 'for $X/mo using your referral discount' while nothing is charged, which is a false statement to fix or hide for beta regardless of the billing decision.

### [high] 'Next rebalance: <date>' is shown to Plus/Pro users for an event that will never happen

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:199-202; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:63-89 (comment: 'Heuristic: next calendar quarter/half/year boundary from today') and 247-249
- Detail: The date is computed in the browser as the next Jan 1 / Apr 1 / Jul 1 / Oct 1 boundary. Nothing on the server is scheduled for that date. The dates also fall on market holidays (Jan 1) and weekends. Showing a specific date for a trade action that is not scheduled is a misleading statement in a financial app.
- Suggested fix: Remove the line until an engine exists. Once it does, return the real next run date from the backend, computed on business days.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. my-profile.page.html:199-202 renders 'Next rebalance: {{ nextRebalanceDate }}' when isPlusOrPro; my-profile.page.ts:63-89 computes it client-side as the next Jan 1 / Apr 1 / Jul 1 / Oct 1 (comment: 'Heuristic'), and 247-249 exposes it. Nothing on the server corresponds to that date. No guard hides it. High is fair for a specific dated promise of a trade action in a money app. It should be fixed in the same small change as finding 1 option (b), so treat it as part of that item rather than separate work.

### [high] Unsubscribe link is a dead '#', there is no opt-out flag, and the footer has no postal address

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/MarketBreakdownEmailComposer.java:123 ('unsubscribeUrl', '#' with comment 'real provider substitutes its unsubscribe link'); backend/src/main/resources/templates/email/market-breakdown.html:30-40 (footer has disclaimer, help address, Unsubscribe link, no postal address); MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:68-80 (eligibility checks tier, email present, already sent, nothing else); grep for unsubscrib / opt-out fields in backend/src/main finds only those two lines
- Detail: This blocks turning on real sends, not the beta itself while email stays mocked. There is no unsubscribe endpoint, no per-user preference column, and the scheduler does not check any preference, so a user cannot stop the email. The comment assumes a provider will inject a link, but a transactional provider such as Resend does not do that automatically for API sends, and no List-Unsubscribe header can be set because EmailMessage is passed without headers. The plain-text body (composer lines 125-130) has no unsubscribe line at all. Whether this email counts as commercial or transactional under CAN-SPAM is a counsel call, but the template already advertises an Unsubscribe link that does nothing.
- Suggested fix: Add a market_breakdown_opt_out (or general email preference) column, a signed-token unsubscribe endpoint that needs no login, a toggle in app settings, a check in runForMonth, the real URL in both html and text bodies, a List-Unsubscribe header, and the company postal address in the footer. Ask counsel how the email should be classified.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts are correct. MarketBreakdownEmailComposer.java:123 passes unsubscribeUrl '#'; the template footer (market-breakdown.html:30-40) shows an Unsubscribe link and no postal address; the plain-text body (composer 125-130) has no unsubscribe line; EmailMessage has only to/from/subject/htmlBody/textBody, so no List-Unsubscribe header is possible; MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:70-83 checks tier, email present and already-sent only. The only opt-out fields in the backend are analytics and paycheck-autopilot ones. Severity lowered from blocker to high because, as the auditor says, nothing is sent while email is mocked, so no user can receive an email with a dead link today. It is a hard gate that must be closed before app.email.enabled is turned on for this email type, not a blocker for letting beta users in. Classification under CAN-SPAM is a counsel question.

### [high] Narrative is hardcoded to the default portfolio (VTI/VXUS/VBR), so it is wrong for users with a custom portfolio

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/MarketBreakdownService.java:36-40 (PORTFOLIO_SYMBOLS, PORTFOLIO_WEIGHTS) and 191-209 (prompt: 'The FRED default portfolio holds three ETFs', section 'Why your funds moved' with one item per VTI, VXUS, VBR); one shared narrative per month (lines 216-231); custom portfolios are real: frontend/src/app/components/portfolio-customize/portfolio-customize.component.ts:166-212 (asset search) and InvestmentExecutionService.java:1138-1175 (buys the user's saved allocation); tier copy 'FRED default portfolio or BYO' at investmentconfirmation.component.ts:92-95
- Detail: The promise is 'why your portfolio went up or down'. Every recipient gets the same text explaining VTI, VXUS and VBR under the heading 'Why your funds moved'. A user who built their own allocation is told about funds they may not own, next to their own real balance numbers. That is inaccurate personalised financial content.
- Suggested fix: Smallest fix: in the composer, detect a non-default allocation and switch the heading and intro to 'How the FRED default portfolio funds moved' (or omit that section) for those users. Fuller fix: generate per-holding explanations for the user's actual symbols, which needs per-symbol returns and a new compliance pass.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. MarketBreakdownService.java:37-38 fixes PORTFOLIO_SYMBOLS and PORTFOLIO_WEIGHTS; the prompt at 194-209 says 'The FRED default portfolio holds three ETFs' and requires a 'Why your funds moved' list with one item per VTI, VXUS, VBR; getOrGenerate (216-231) stores one narrative per month and its comment says every recipient gets identical text. Custom allocations are bought for real (InvestmentExecutionService.java:1138-1158 reads the user's saved Portfolio) and the Core tier card advertises 'default portfolio or BYO' (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:91-95). The scheduler and composer have no branch for non-default allocations. Like the unsubscribe item, it only bites once real sends are on, so treat it as a go-live gate for the email. I did not open portfolio-customize.component.ts to confirm the asset search lines.

### [high] Prod profile defines no email or Alpaca market-data settings; default from-address is noreply@investingapp.com

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:1-33 (whole file: datasource, JWT, Plaid, encryption, WebAuthn only); backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:47-49 (EMAIL_FROM default noreply@investingapp.com); alpaca.market.* appears only in application-dev.properties:61-65, application-local.properties:47-51 and application-ci.properties:61-65; MarketBreakdownService.java:53-60 defaults the keys to empty; PortfolioDashboardService.java:69 uses ${alpaca.market.key} with no default
- Detail: There is no prod backend yet (known), but when one is created the prod profile has no alpaca.market.key/secret/base-url. The market breakdown would call Alpaca with empty keys and fail every month, and PortfolioDashboardService has no default for the key so the app would likely fail to start unless the property is supplied some other way. I did not boot the app to confirm the startup failure. The fallback sender address is a domain that is not FRED's; EMAIL_FROM must be set or the default changed, or real emails go out from investingapp.com and fail SPF/DKIM.
- Suggested fix: Add alpaca.market.*, app.email.* and the market-breakdown flags to application-prod.properties as required env variables, list them in the header comment at lines 1-6, and change the default from-address to a fredvested.com address.

It is missing not just alpaca.market.* but also alpaca.api.key, alpaca.api.secret, alpaca.broker.base-url and alpaca.trading.base-url; those appear only in application-dev/ci/local/test properties. PortfolioDashboardService.java:63-73 injects alpaca.api.key, alpaca.api.secret, alpaca.market.key and alpaca.market.secret with no default, so a prod boot fails on an unresolved placeholder unless the values arrive as environment variables named for Spring relaxed binding (for example ALPACA_API_KEY and ALPACA_MARKET_KEY; note dev uses ALPACA_MARKET_DATA_KEY, which would not bind on its own). The broker and trading URLs default to sandbox and paper hosts in code (PortfolioDashboardService.java:75-79, AlpacaApiService.java:43-44, AlpacaService.java:32); BrokerEnvironmentGuard appears to exist to refuse that, but I did not read its checks. The header comment at lines 1-6 that says 'Every variable below must be set' does not list any Alpaca or email variable. The from-address default noreply@investingapp.com is confirmed at application.properties:48 and EmailService.java:35. Not booted, so the startup failure is by reading the code. High is right: it is a small fix that must be done when the prod service is created. Separately, application-local.properties holds literal Alpaca keys but is not tracked by git.

### [medium] Rebalance schedule choice is stored only in device localStorage and never sent to the backend

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:729-747 (comment at 736: 'savePrefs is a synchronous localStorage write'); frontend/src/app/services/goals.service.ts:20,177-199; no rebalance column in backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1..V9 (grep 'rebalanc' in backend/src/main/resources returns nothing)
- Detail: The user's Quarterly / Semi-annual / Annual choice lives under the key fred.profilePrefs.v<N>.<userId> in localStorage. It is lost on reinstall, on a new phone, or if WebView storage is cleared, and silently resets to Quarterly. A server-side rebalancer could never read it. Storage write failures are swallowed with no message to the user (goals.service.ts:194-198), while the analytics event still reports outcome 'applied'.
- Suggested fix: Add a rebalance_schedule column (Flyway V10 or later, check the paycheck-autopilot branch which also claims V10), expose it on GET /user/progress and PUT /user/profile with validation against the three allowed values, enforce plus/pro on the write in the backend, and have the page read and write the server value.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts are correct: my-profile.page.ts:729-747 calls goalsService.savePrefs, which is a localStorage write under fred.profilePrefs.v2.<userId> (goals.service.ts:20, 177-199), failures are swallowed at 194-198, and the analytics event always says outcome 'applied'. The value is read back at my-profile.page.ts:909-910. No backend column or endpoint exists. Severity lowered to medium because the setting controls nothing: with no engine (finding 1) losing the value has no real effect on the user's money, and this work only matters if option (a), building the engine, is chosen. It is a sub-task of finding 1, not a separate high. One correction to the suggested fix: V6__paycheck_autopilot.sql is already in the working tree and migrations run V1 to V9, so the next free version is V10 and the note about the paycheck-autopilot branch claiming V10 looks out of date.

### [medium] Market breakdown scheduler has no job lock and no enable flag, and makes real Alpaca plus Claude calls every month even while sends are mocked

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:50-62 (plain @Scheduled, no JobLockService, no SchedulerGate); compare InvestmentScheduler.java:48-61 which takes a JobLocks lock; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/config/SchedulerGate.java:10 (gate covers investment, ACH and Plaid jobs only); backend/src/main/resources/application-ci.properties:27,61-65 (CI survives only because market keys point at a dead host)
- Detail: If the backend ever runs more than one instance, both run the job at 9am ET. The unique keys on market_breakdown.period_key and email_log (V1__baseline.sql:122,201) stop duplicate rows, but both instances can still call Claude with web search and both can dispatch the same email before either row is written (send happens before record, EmailService.java:64-66). There is also no property to turn the job off, so every environment with the keys pays for a grounded Claude call monthly, including while mocked. Memory notes say this recurring cost has not been signed off.
- Suggested fix: Wrap runMonthlyBreakdownJob in JobLockService.tryAcquire like the other schedulers and add an app.market-breakdown.scheduler-enabled property (default false until go-live).

### [medium] MOCKED rows count as 'already sent', and a month that fails on days 1 to 3 is silently skipped with no alert

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:74-79 (hasBeenSent true for SENT or MOCKED); MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:50 (cron only on days 1,2,3), 57-61 (failure only logged), 88-94 (per-user failure only logged and recorded)
- Detail: Two effects. First, if real sending is switched on after the 1st, users whose row is already MOCKED for that period never get that month's email. Second, if generation fails on all three days (Alpaca outage, the model does no web search, the strict validator rejects the text), no one gets an email for that month and nothing notifies anyone. There is no backfill path in prod because POST /generate is disabled there (MarketBreakdownController.java:107-112) and it only generates the narrative, it does not send. The validator is intentionally strict (MarketNarrativeValidator.java:29-35 rejects any text containing 'guaranteed' or 'you should', even in a negated sentence), so rejected months are a realistic outcome.
- Suggested fix: Add an alert (Sentry or log-based) on a FAILED market_breakdown row and on failed counts above zero, add an admin-only backfill trigger that runs runForMonth for a given month, and at go-live either clear MOCKED rows for the current period or flip the flag before the 1st.

### [low] Possible double send when the audit write fails after a successful dispatch
