- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:63-66 (send, then record) and 92-116 (record swallows its own exceptions)
- Detail: If the provider accepts the email but the email_log save fails, no SENT row exists and the catch-up run on the next day sends the same email again. Memory notes show this was accepted knowingly. It only matters once real sending is on.
- Suggested fix: Accept for beta, or write a PENDING row before dispatch and update it after, so a missing confirmation does not trigger a resend without review.

**Verified solid:**

- Tier card copy for both features read exactly: investmentconfirmation.component.ts:152-163. Feature ids (custom-rebalance, market-breakdown) match their labels; no id/label mismatch in these two rows.
- Market breakdown eligibility is enforced on the backend: MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:31,68 uses userRepository.findBySelectedTierIn(['plus','pro']) (UserRepository.java:71).
- Market numbers are real, not hardcoded: MarketBreakdownService.java:79-152 fetches Alpaca 1Month bars and refuses to generate if any symbol is missing either month's close.
- Narrative grounding is enforced: zero web-search sources is treated as a generation failure (MarketBreakdownService.java:244-248); LLM call uses the web search tool (LLMService.java:167-204).
- Deterministic compliance gate exists and runs before anything is stored as GENERATED: MarketNarrativeValidator.java:45-107 (forbidden advice phrases on normalized and tag-stripped text, 7-tag allowlist, no attributes, href / on*= / javascript: scans, length bounds); a rejected narrative is nulled and marked FAILED (MarketBreakdownService.java:265-278).
- Per-user numbers are deterministic and never LLM generated: MarketBreakdownEmailComposer.java:53-83; a funded user whose history cannot be fetched gets a FAILED row and a retry instead of a wrong email (MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:81-94); unfunded users get a market-only variant (composer 54-56, 69-75).
- Idempotency is in place: unique key (email_type, user_id, period_key) on email_log and unique period_key on market_breakdown (V1__baseline.sql:122,201); FAILED rows are updated in place on retry (EmailService.java:92-111); one user's failure does not stop the batch.
- Email footer carries a not-investment-advice and past-performance disclaimer in both HTML and text (market-breakdown.html:31-35; MarketBreakdownEmailComposer.java:128-130).
- Manual generation endpoint is off by default and only enabled in local/dev (application.properties:52; application-dev.properties:72; application-local.properties:58; MarketBreakdownController.java:107-112); it refuses the current or future months (MarketBreakdownService.java:223-226).
- Email bodies are not stored in email_log (V1__baseline.sql:110-123 has no body column).
- Backend tests exist for the pipeline (@Test counts read, tests not run in this audit): MarketBreakdownSchedulerTest 6, MarketBreakdownServiceGenerateTest 6, MarketBreakdownServiceReturnsTest 6, MarketBreakdownPromptTest 2, MarketBreakdownEmailComposerTest 10, MarketBreakdownControllerTest 8, EmailServiceTest 7, plus MarketNarrativeValidatorTest.
- Rebalance schedule UI works as a UI: chips render, Core users see the lock chip and default note, Plus/Pro selection persists per user across app restarts on the same device, prefs are validated on read (goals.service.ts:179-190, 272-278; my-profile.page.html:171-205).
- tier-mock.ts is safe for production builds: it is only consulted when environment.local is true and forced null under Karma (frontend/src/app/services/dev/tier-mock.ts:9-13,36-40). Note the working tree currently has 'plus' uncommented at line 31, which only affects the local dev loop.

## tier-pro-a

Piggy Pro ($40/mo) audit of three features: Optimal drawdown walkthrough, Deeper projections and income simulation, and Tax loss harvesting (feature id "household"). Short version: none of the three is delivered as the tier card describes it. (1) Deeper projections is the closest: the three scenarios, live contribution and retirement-age steppers and a monthly spend estimate exist in the Monte Carlo flow, but everything runs in the browser and the Pro gate is frontend only. (2) The drawdown "walkthrough" is a static 2-slide education deck with balance chips. The copy promises a year-by-year strategy, a trigger "once you're within range of Freedom Date", and a plan that "updates dynamically"; none of that exists. (3) Tax loss harvesting has a large, real backend engine (controller, scheduler, lot ledger, wash sale guard, replacement pairs, tests, DB tables) that defaults to off in production config and dry-run on dev, but the frontend has zero calls to /api/tlh: no enroll screen, no status, no savings feed. The Pro card on my-profile shows a hardcoded "Saved this year: $0.00". The tier card correctly marks it "Coming Soon". Across all three, tier is not a real entitlement: any signed-in user can set selectedTier to "pro" through PUT /user/profile or POST /user/subscription/confirm with no payment proof, and every upgrade button in these flows is a TODO that only logs to the console. I did not run the backend or frontend test suites (read-only audit), so "tests exist" below means the test files exist, not that they pass.


| Tier | Feature | Status | Tier gate | Gaps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| pro | Optimal drawdown walkthrough | partial | yes-frontend-only | What exists is a generic 2-slide education deck (the order of accounts, and why) with three balance chips. Missing versus the copy: no year-by-year plan or schedule of any kind; no 'within range of Freedom Date' trigger (it is always available to Pro from the last slide of any strategy deck); nothing is 'sequenced' for the user's accounts beyond showing three balances; no plan that updates. The 401(k)/Roth chips come from localStorage on that one device, so they are empty after a reinstall or on a second device. Only reachable by swiping to the last slide of another strategy deck; there is no direct entry. Gate is a frontend check on a tier value passed as a component input. |
| pro | Deeper projections & income simulation | implemented | yes-frontend-only | All of it runs client side, so the Pro gate is a UI check only (no backend involvement at all). Scenario naming differs from the tier copy: the card says conservative/expected/aggressive, the app shows 'Conservative (8%)', 'Moderate (10%)', 'Optimistic (12%)'. An 8% stock return labelled 'Conservative' is a compliance question for counsel. Simulation history is per-device localStorage. The upgrade nudge for non-Pro users leads to a TODO stub. Not device-tested by me; I read the code only. |
| pro | Tax loss harvesting | partial | yes-backend | The backend can really trade (sell, replacement buy, re-entry) but has never been live: it is off by default and dry-run on dev. There is no user-facing way to use it: no enroll or consent screen, no status, no 'saved this year' feed, no event history, no wash-window explanation. The Pro card shows a hardcoded '$0.00'. The replacement pair list needs compliance sign-off (stated as a go-live gate in TlhCatalog.java:20-21). The feature id is 'household', which does not match the label. The card is honestly marked Coming Soon, so the gap is 'not shipped', not 'falsely claimed', but it is listed as a Pro feature on a $40/mo tier. |


### [blocker] Any signed-in user can make themselves Piggy Pro for free, so every Pro gate is bypassable

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:528-548 (PUT /user/profile accepts selectedTier and billingPeriod from the request body, no validation of the value and no payment proof); UserController.java:617-633 (POST /user/subscription/confirm does the same through SubscriptionService); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/SubscriptionService.java:21-35; frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:771-791 (selectTier writes the tier straight to the profile, IAP is a TODO at :776-781); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:705-725 (confirmTierUpgrade also just writes the tier); frontend/src/app/services/dev/tier-mock.ts:15-19 (the dev mock relies on this same write).
- Detail: The tier is a plain string on the user row that the client sets. There is no entitlement record, no receipt, no expiry, and no server-side source of truth for who has paid. The only backend Pro check in my domain (TlhController isPro, TlhHarvestService PRO_TIERS) reads that same client-writable string. Once TLH is turned on, a user could set themselves to pro, enroll, and have real sell orders placed on their account without paying. The value is also not validated, so any string up to 10 characters can be stored. This is expected while billing is unbuilt, but it has to be closed before paid tiers mean anything.
- Suggested fix: When the billing model is decided, remove selectedTier and billingPeriod from the client-writable profile fields and remove or lock down /user/subscription/confirm. Make tier a server-owned entitlement set only by the billing flow. Until then, at minimum validate the value against core/plus/pro. Do not design billing here; this is the list of write paths that must change.
- Verifier (confirmed): True in the working tree. UserController.java:528-548 stores any string sent as selectedTier (and billingPeriod) with no allow-list and no payment proof. POST /user/subscription/confirm (UserController.java:618-633) and SubscriptionService.java:21-35 do the same and only check that tier is not blank. I found no guard, profile gate or environment check on either path. The only length limit is the 10-character column (User.java:286-290). Frontend writes are as cited: investmentconfirmation.component.ts:771-791 (IAP is a TODO), my-profile.page.ts:705-725, tier-mock.ts. Extra details: (1) no frontend code calls /user/subscription/confirm, so that endpoint is currently dead but open; (2) the two paths write different start-date fields (subscriptionStartedAt in the profile PUT, subscriptionStartDate in SubscriptionService; User.java:111 and :304), which the billing work should unify; (3) the referral upgrade in my-profile.page.html:272 shows a '$20/mo' or '$10/mo' confirm sheet and then just flips the tier with no charge, so it is a user-visible fake purchase. Mitigation on the TLH risk: real sells also need TLH_MODE=live (default off, application.properties:83) and an enroll call that no app screen makes, and TlhExecutionService.java:139-145 re-checks the tier at submit time, but that re-check reads the same client-writable string. Blocker stands for any paid tier going live; it is the same work item as the unbuilt billing, not a separate one. If the private beta charges nothing, it is not exploitable for money on day one.

### [high] Drawdown copy promises a year-by-year, dynamic, account-sequencing plan; the app ships a static 2-slide deck

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:192 (copy); frontend/src/app/components/drawdown-deck/drawdown-deck.component.ts:29-52, :79, :102-137; frontend/src/app/components/drawdown-deck/drawdown-deck.component.html:18-72; grep for 'within range', 'year-by-year', 'yearByYear' in frontend/src/app returns only the tier copy line.
- Detail: The card says: year-by-year withdrawal strategy education, shown once you are within range of your Freedom Date, where FRED sequences your accounts in tax-optimal order and updates the plan dynamically as the portfolio grows. The deck is fixed text (taxable first, tax-deferred second, Roth last, and three reasons why) plus three balance chips. There is no per-year output, no trigger tied to the Freedom Date, no calculation, and nothing that changes over time except the FRED balance chip. For a $40/mo headline feature this is the biggest promise-versus-reality gap in the Pro tier. Wording such as 'tax-optimal order' is also a claim counsel should see.
- Suggested fix: Pick one: (a) rewrite the tier copy to match what exists, for example 'A walkthrough of the tax-smart order to draw down your accounts, shown with your own balances', or (b) build the promised thing: a year-by-year withdrawal table driven by the user's balances, target income and retirement age, surfaced when the user is near their Freedom Date. Option (a) is an S-size copy change; option (b) is L. Needs Andy's decision.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. The tier card copy at investmentconfirmation.component.ts:192 says year-by-year, shown once within range of Freedom Date, sequenced in tax-optimal order, updated dynamically, and it is marked lockState 'unlocked' (not coming soon). The deck is fixed content: DRAWDOWN_BUCKETS and DRAWDOWN_WHY constants (drawdown-deck.component.ts:29-52), SLIDE_INDICES = [0, 1] (:79), and the only personal data is three balance chips (:102-137). The FRED chip comes from the portfolio store; the 401(k) and Roth chips come from a localStorage entry and show a dash if absent. There is no per-year calculation and no Freedom Date trigger; the deck is reached only from the strategy deck's 'Discover the Optimal Drawdown' button (retirement-planning.component.ts:116-117, :129). The grep for 'within range', 'year-by-year', 'yearByYear' in frontend/src/app returns only the copy line. Note the deck content is marked 'verbatim from approved spec 2026-07-15' (:27), so the deck itself may be as intended and the tier card copy is the thing that is out of line. Type decision and severity high are right: it is point-of-sale copy for a $40/mo tier in a financial app. The copy fix is S.

### [high] Pro upgrade buttons in these flows do nothing (TODO stubs that only log)

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:887-896 (upgradeToPlusClicked and upgradeToProClicked are console.log TODOs); frontend/src/app/components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.ts:299-302 (same); retirement-planning.component.ts:118-119 (the drawdown Pro nudge 'upgrade' role is routed to upgradeToPlusClicked, the Plus handler); retirement-planning.component.ts:316-331 (scenario chip nudge also calls the Plus handler for a Pro-locked feature).
- Detail: A non-Pro user who taps 'Upgrade to Pro' from the TLH card, the drawdown lock, or the scenario chips sees the sheet close and nothing else happens. Two of the Pro gates also call the Plus upgrade handler, so when billing is wired they would send the user to the wrong tier unless fixed. This is tied to the unbuilt billing work, but the dead buttons are app work and are visible to every beta user.
- Suggested fix: Until billing exists, either route these buttons to a real 'change plan' screen or show a clear 'upgrades are not available yet' message. When billing lands, add a Pro handler in retirement-planning and point the drawdown and scenario nudges at it.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. my-profile.page.ts:889-898 and retirement-planning.component.ts:299-302 are console.log TODO stubs. The TLH nudge calls upgradeToProClicked (my-profile.page.ts:843-862). In retirement-planning there is no Pro handler at all: the strategy deck's 'upgrade' role, which comes from the Pro-only 'Unlock the Optimal Drawdown' nudge (strategy-deck.component.ts:329-359), goes to upgradeToPlusClicked (retirement-planning.component.ts:118-119), and the market scenario chips are gated on isPro (retirement-planning.component.html:58) but openChipNudge also calls upgradeToPlusClicked (:316-331). The nudge sheet does return data { targetTier } on dismiss (mc-info-sheet.component.ts:54) but these callers ignore it, so the wrong-tier routing is real once billing is wired. The same stub pattern exists in goal.page.ts:1177-1178 (outside this finding's list). Contrast worth noting for the lead: the referral offer button on my-profile (my-profile.page.html:272) is not dead; it grants the tier for free, which belongs with the first finding. High is fair for public launch; for the private beta it is a visible dead end that a short 'upgrades are not available yet' message would cover (S), with the real fix riding on billing.

### [medium] Tax loss harvesting has no frontend at all: no enroll, consent, status or savings screen

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:136-169 (only TLH surface; Pro branch at :161-166 shows a hardcoded '$0.00'; the comment at :160 says 'engine not built yet', which is out of date); grep of frontend/src/app for 'tlh' matches only my-profile, analytics-events.ts, tier-mock.ts and the tier card; backend endpoints that nothing calls: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/TlhController.java:74, :93, :121, :144, :174, :197.
- Detail: The backend exposes status, enroll, unenroll, summary, events and preview, and requires an explicit opt-in on top of the Pro tier before it will ever sell. The app has no screen that calls any of them. A Pro user cannot turn TLH on, cannot see the disclaimer the backend returns (TlhSummaryService.java:35-40), cannot see why a buy was redirected by a wash-sale window, and sees a fake '$0.00' saved figure. So even with TLH_MODE=live, no user could be enrolled through the app.
- Suggested fix: Build a TLH section for Pro: enroll and unenroll with a clear consent screen and the disclaimer, ledger status, saved-this-year from /api/tlh/summary, event history, and active wash-window notes. Replace the hardcoded $0.00. Until then keep the Coming Soon pill and consider hiding the '$0.00' line so it does not look like a live number.
- Verifier (adjusted): The facts are true. A grep of frontend/src/app for 'api/tlh' or '/tlh/' returns nothing, so none of the TlhController endpoints (status :74, enroll :93 and :121, summary :144, events :174, preview :197) is called. The only TLH surface is my-profile.page.html:136-169, where the Pro branch shows a hardcoded 'Saved this year: $0.00' and the comment at :160 says 'engine not built yet', which is out of date. Severity is lowered because the feature is honestly labelled as not available everywhere a user sees it: the tier card row is lockState 'coming-soon' with 'Coming Soon' (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:201-207) and the profile section carries a 'Coming soon' pill for all tiers (my-profile.page.html:142). So no promise is broken today, and the engine is off by default. It becomes high (a hard prerequisite, L effort) only at the moment TLH is meant to go live. Small items that are worth doing now: the fake '$0.00' line for Pro users, and the non-Pro CTA 'Want to save this money?' with a dollar estimate (my-profile.page.html:151-158) that upsells a feature that cannot be turned on. One small correction to the evidence: my case-insensitive grep for 'tlh' in frontend/src/app matched only my-profile files and tier-mock.ts; the tier card says 'Tax loss harvesting' in words and uses id 'household'.

### [medium] TLH engine has never run live; go-live gates are open (compliance sign-off, dry-run only, single-instance assumption)

- Type: external, effort L, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:75-83 (mode defaults to off); application-dev.properties:74-75 (dry-run on dev); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/TlhCatalog.java:18-21 ('Compliance sign-off on this list is a go-live gate'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/TlhScheduler.java:23-26 (single-instance assumption, in-memory ledger locks); TlhSummaryService.java:53 and application.properties:101 (savings estimate uses a flat 24% tax rate for everyone).
- Detail: The engine is real and carefully guarded, but it has only ever produced dry-run PROPOSED events. Turning it on means real automated selling in customer accounts, which needs counsel and likely Alpaca agreement, sign-off on the replacement ETF pairs, and a sandbox live-mode run end to end. The 'estimated savings' figure assumes a 24% rate for every user. The scheduler assumes one backend instance. None of this blocks the beta as long as TLH stays off and stays labelled Coming Soon.
- Suggested fix: Keep TLH_MODE unset (off) in the production environment and add that to the production deploy checklist. Before any live switch: counsel sign-off on automated selling and on the pair list, a full live-mode cycle in Alpaca sandbox, and a decision on the flat tax-rate estimate.
- Verifier (adjusted): The cited code facts are true: mode defaults to off (application.properties:83), dev and local are dry-run with manual runs enabled (application-dev.properties:74-75, application-local.properties:60-61), ci is off, TlhCatalog.java:18-21 names compliance sign-off as a go-live gate, TlhScheduler.java:23-26 states the single-instance assumption and that the kill switch needs a restart, and the estimate uses a flat 0.24 rate (application.properties:101, TlhSummaryService). The manual run endpoint is also config-gated and refuses when mode is off (TlhController.java:223-243). The claim that it has 'only ever produced dry-run events' is a statement about runtime history that I cannot confirm from the repo; the config is consistent with it. Severity lowered to medium for launch readiness because, as the finding itself says, nothing here blocks the beta while TLH stays off and labelled Coming Soon. The one concrete app-side action is a deploy checklist line: confirm the production environment does not set TLH_MODE and that the prod profile does not override app.tlh.mode (an application-prod.properties file exists; my grep found no tlh keys in it, so it inherits off, and manual-run inherits false). Type external is right for the go-live itself.

### [medium] Deeper projections and the drawdown deck are gated in the frontend only

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/monte-carlo-flow/monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:140-141 and :412-416 (isPro derived from an @Input tier string); monte-carlo-flow.component.html:267-311; frontend/src/app/components/strategy-deck/strategy-deck.component.ts:192, :329-334; frontend/src/app/services/monte-carlo.service.ts:108-126 (simulation runs in the browser). No backend endpoint is involved in either feature.
- Detail: Both features are pure client code and static content shipped in the app bundle to every user. The Pro lock is a CSS veil and disabled buttons. That is normal for a mobile app and low risk in practice, but it means there is no server-side enforcement to audit, and the real gate is whatever the server says the tier is (see the blocker above).
- Suggested fix: Accept frontend-only gating for these two features and make the tier value trustworthy on the server. No backend work needed for the features themselves unless Andy wants simulations moved server side.

### [medium] Scenario labels and return assumptions do not match the tier copy and need a compliance look

- Type: external, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:198 ('conservative, expected, and aggressive'); frontend/src/app/components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.ts:166-170 ('Conservative (8%)', 'Moderate (10%)', 'Optimistic (12%)'); frontend/src/app/services/monte-carlo.service.ts:62-64 (stock return 8% / 10% / 12%).
- Detail: The card names the scenarios one way and the app names them another. More importantly, calling an 8% annual stock return 'Conservative' and offering 12% is a performance-projection claim that counsel should review before real users see it.
- Suggested fix: Align the names in the tier copy and the app. Ask counsel to review the three return assumptions and their labels along with the other projection disclosures.

### [medium] Non-Pro TLH card shows a made-up savings estimate ('You could save ~$X/yr in taxes')

- Type: external, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:91-101 (estimate = 0.3% of portfolio value, rounded to $10) and :283-286; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:151-158; nudge copy at my-profile.page.ts:850 ('potentially saving you hundreds per year').
- Detail: Every Core and Plus user is shown a dollar figure for tax savings from a feature that is not available, based on a fixed 0.3% heuristic with no relation to their actual losses or tax situation, followed by a 'Want to save this money?' button that leads to a dead upgrade stub. This is a marketing claim about tax savings and should be cleared by counsel or removed for the beta.
- Suggested fix: For the beta, remove the dollar estimate and the CTA, and keep a neutral 'Coming soon' line. Bring it back only with counsel-approved wording once TLH is live.

### [medium] Drawdown deck's 401(k) and Roth balances live only in localStorage on one device

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/drawdown-deck/drawdown-deck.component.ts:129-137; frontend/src/app/components/monte-carlo-flow/monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:921 (same key written by the simulator).
- Detail: The 'your buckets today' personalization, which is the only user-specific part of the drawdown feature, depends on balances the user typed into the Monte Carlo flow and that are saved under fred.mcOutside.<userId> in the browser storage. Reinstalling the app, switching phones, or iOS clearing web storage wipes them, and the deck falls back to dashes. I did not check whether the Plus 'Add 401(k) & Roth IRA' feature has a server store elsewhere; in this deck it reads localStorage only.
- Suggested fix: Store outside account balances on the server (one small table or user fields) and read them in both the simulator and the drawdown deck.

### [low] Feature id 'household' is used for Tax loss harvesting, and related comments are stale

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:202-203; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:160 ('engine not built yet'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/TlhCatalog.java:14-16 ('the codebase has no migration tooling', but Flyway migrations exist in backend/src/main/resources/db/migration).

**Verified solid:**

- Tier card copy for the three features read directly: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:189-207. TLH is honestly marked lockState 'coming-soon' with a 'Coming Soon' pill (rendered at investmentconfirmation.component.html:237-243), and my-profile also shows a 'Coming soon' pill (my-profile.page.html:142).
- TLH is off by default for any environment that does not set TLH_MODE: application.properties:82-83. Dev and local are dry-run only (application-dev.properties:74-75, application-local.properties:60-61); CI is off (application-ci.properties:83).
- TLH mode gating is enforced inside the services, not just the scheduler: runCycle returns early when off and skips when the market clock is closed (TlhHarvestService.java:132-141); dry-run records PROPOSED events and creates no wash window (TlhHarvestService.java:316-340); replacement and re-entry orders only go out when mode is live (TlhExecutionService.java:418, :618).
- TLH requires both tier 'pro' and an explicit opt-in before any selling: scheduler query findBySelectedTierInAndTlhEnabledTrue (UserRepository.java:75, TlhHarvestService.java:146) plus ACTIVE account status; controller write endpoints check isPro (TlhController.java:100, :204, :234); manual run is config-gated and off by default (TlhController.java:225-228).
- Wash sale guard is wired into the real buy paths: scheduled buys and lump sum (InvestmentExecutionService.java:132, :898, :960-968) and DRIP (DripService.java:152-164).
- TLH replacement pairs cover the default FRED portfolio (VTI, VXUS, VBR) with different-index substitutes: TlhCatalog.java:44-53.
- TLH database tables and user columns exist in Flyway migrations: V1__baseline.sql:242-360 and :472-473; V3__catch_up_pre_flyway_schemas.sql:14-58.
- TLH backend has dedicated test files (TlhControllerTest, TlhSchedulerTest, TlhExecutionServiceTest, TlhHarvestServiceCycleTest, TlhEligibilityServiceTest, TlhCatalogTest, TlhSummaryServiceTest, WashSaleGuardServiceTest, TaxLotLedgerServiceTest, ReplacementSelectionServiceTest, DripServiceTlhGuardTest, InvestmentExecutionServiceTlhGuardTest under backend/src/test). I confirmed they exist; I did not run them.
- Deeper projections features all exist in code for Pro: three scenarios (retirement-planning.component.ts:166-170, monte-carlo-flow.component.html:303-308), live contribution and retirement-age steppers (monte-carlo-flow.component.html:267-282), and a monthly spend estimate including a 90%-confidence spend solved only for Pro (monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:412-416, .html:291-298). The simulation is a real 1,000-run Monte Carlo, not hardcoded output (monte-carlo.service.ts:62-64, :108, :615-622).
- Drawdown deck carries an 'Educational content, not tax advice' notice (drawdown-deck.component.html:62-65) and degrades safely when balances are missing (drawdown-deck.component.ts:109, :124-127).
- The dev tier mock cannot leak to deployed builds: it is only consulted when environment.local is true and is forced null under karma (frontend/src/app/services/auth.service.ts:232-234, frontend/src/app/services/dev/tier-mock.ts:36-40). Note it is currently toggled to 'plus' in the working tree (tier-mock.ts:31), which only affects the local dev loop.
- The old unauthenticated subscription webhook has been removed (UserController.java:668-671).

## tier-pro-b

Piggy Pro ($40/mo) support and early-access promises, plus the tier selection screen itself. Short version: neither "Priority support" nor "Early access to new FRED features" exists in code, and the tier screen is a price display with no billing, no trial and no entitlement control behind it.  What exists today: - Tapping "Join The Pig Leagues" calls selectTier(), which writes the tier to localStorage, PUTs { selectedTier, billingPeriod } to /user/profile, then finishes onboarding. There is no purchase step (IAP is a TODO comment) and no IAP plugin in the project. - The backend stores selectedTier as a free-text string that any signed-in user can set to any value through PUT /user/profile. Every tier gate in the app (TLH "pro" check, goal limit, market breakdown list) reads that same string, so the gates are real on the backend but the entitlement is self-service and free. - SubscriptionService has two methods. confirmSubscription is reachable through POST /user/subscription/confirm, which has no receipt check and no frontend caller. expireSubscription has no caller at all. There is no webhook, no trial record, no trial end date, no cancel or manage-subscription screen, and no way to change billing period after onboarding. - Support is a single "Contact Us" row in tab3 that opens mailto:help@fredvested.com for every user. Nothing identifies or prioritises Pro users, and nothing tracks a 24 hour response time. - There is no feature flag or beta mechanism of any kind. PostHog is used for analytics only.  The dev tier mock (frontend/src/app/services/dev/tier-mock.ts) is safely gated on environment.local, which is false in the dev, test and prod environment files, so it cannot leak into a shipped build.  Not verified: App Store Connect product setup (outside the repo), and whether the Pro-only features owned by other auditors (drawdown, deeper projections, TLH) work. I only noted the TLH id mismatch and the Pro pitch line because they sit on this screen.


| Tier | Feature | Status | Tier gate | Gaps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| pro | Priority support | missing | no | No support feature beyond a plain mailto link that is identical for all tiers. The email carries no tier, user id or subject, so the person answering cannot tell a Pro user from a Core user. No backend support endpoint, no ticket record, no SLA tracking, no in-app chat with a human. The 24 hour human response promise is purely an operational promise with nothing in the app to support or measure it. |
| pro | Early access to new FRED features | missing | no | No feature flag system, no per-tier or per-user rollout mechanism, no beta channel, and no backlog story for one. Nothing can currently be released to Pro users ahead of other users. The row is shown with a check mark as if it is live today. |


### [blocker] Tier selection takes no payment and there is no billing integration at all

- Type: decision, effort XL, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:771-791; frontend/package.json (no revenuecat, purchases or storekit dependency found by grep); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/SubscriptionService.java:21-47; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:614-633
- Detail: selectTier() saves the tier to localStorage, PUTs { selectedTier, billingPeriod } to /user/profile and then calls authorizeRecurringInvestment(). Lines 776-781 are a TODO describing an Apple IAP flow that was never built. No IAP plugin is installed. The backend has POST /user/subscription/confirm, described as 'post Apple IAP / RevenueCat', but it does no receipt validation and no frontend code calls it. There is no webhook for renewals, cancellations or expiry (grep for webhook in backend finds only Plaid). So a user who picks the $40 Pro plan gets Pro for free forever. The backlog marks FRED-174 (wire Apple subscription to tiers) and FRED-112 as done, but only the placeholder parts exist.
- Suggested fix: Hold the billing design until counsel answers the per-paycheck question, as planned. Once the model is chosen, the app work is: a purchase or authorisation step inside selectTier() before the tier is written, a server-side source of truth for entitlement, renewal and cancel handling, and removal of the unused confirm endpoint if the chosen model does not need it. For private beta, decide explicitly whether all beta users are free (privateBeta flag) and say so on the screen.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. selectTier() (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:771-791) writes localStorage, PUTs the tier and goes straight to authorizeRecurringInvestment(); lines 776-781 are an IAP TODO. frontend/package.json has no revenuecat, purchases, storekit or iap dependency. POST /user/subscription/confirm (UserController.java:614-633) does no receipt check and nothing in frontend/src calls it. The only webhook code in the backend is Plaid. One thing the auditor missed: BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:15-29 says the fee is meant to be collected outside Apple, by ACH from the Plaid-linked bank, by a separate billing session. That contradicts the IAP TODO in selectTier(), the 'post Apple IAP / RevenueCat' endpoint comment and the IAP stub in ReferralService.java:77, so the repo holds two opposite billing directions. Blocker only for charging money: users flagged privateBeta bypass the subscription gate (tabs.page.ts:86-89), so a free beta can run without billing if that is the explicit decision.

### [blocker] The 14 day free trial is copy only: nothing tracks, ends or bills a trial

- Type: decision, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.html:262-268; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:536-539; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:24-49; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/util/ServerAnalyticsEvent.java:13, 88, 105; backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__baseline.sql:465-469
- Detail: Every tier card says 'Try FRED free for 14 days. No charges, cancel anytime.' The only thing recorded is subscriptionStartedAt at the moment a tier is first chosen. There is no trial end date column, no scheduler that ends a trial, no conversion to paid and no reminder before the trial ends. The trial_started, subscription_billed and subscription_cancelled analytics events are defined but documented as not emitted, with a TODO waiting for a billing path. 'No charges' is true today only because nothing ever charges. Trial terms shown at the point of sale are also a disclosure matter for counsel and for App Store review.
- Suggested fix: Fold into the billing decision. Whatever model is chosen needs: a stored trial start and end, a job or provider event that ends the trial, the wording counsel approves for when the first charge happens and how much it is, and the three analytics emitters wired to the real events.
- Verifier (confirmed): Copy is at investmentconfirmation.component.html:262-268 on every tier card. The only stored value is subscriptionStartedAt set on first tier pick (UserController.java:536-539). V1__baseline.sql has subscription_start_date and subscription_started_at only (468-469), and no migration mentions a trial. BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:15-29 states outright that nothing creates a trial, charges or cancels. This is the same decision as the billing finding, so the lead should treat them as one item; it is a blocker for charging and a disclosure question for counsel, not a blocker for a free beta.

### [blocker] Terms of Service and Privacy Policy links on the consent step go nowhere, and consent is not recorded

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.html:133-138; frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:248, 317-318, 436-438, 687-690; frontend/src/app/tab3/tab3.page.ts:336-338, 392-394
- Detail: Step 1 of this same screen asks the user to tick 'I agree to FRED's Terms of Service and Privacy Policy'. Both links are href="#" with preventDefault, so the user cannot read either document. In tab3, 'Legal Information' shows a 'coming soon' toast. The agreement itself lives only in a component field: grep found no agreedToTerms or terms-accepted field in the backend or migrations, so there is no stored record of who agreed to which version and when. When the screen is restored at step 2 from localStorage, agreedToTerms is simply set to true. For a product that moves real money this needs to be fixed before any real user. The documents themselves are presumably with counsel.
- Suggested fix: Link both to the real documents (in-app page or hosted URL), store acceptance timestamp and document version on the user row via a migration, and wire tab3 Legal Information to the same documents.
- Verifier (confirmed): Both links are href="#" with preventDefault (investmentconfirmation.component.html:133-138). tab3 'Legal Information' only shows a coming soon toast (tab3.page.ts:336-338, 392-394). A grep of backend/src/main (Java and migrations) for agreedToTerms, termsAccepted, terms_accepted, tos or terms version finds nothing, so acceptance is never stored; the only consent sent to the server is agreedToMarketing. On restore at step 2 the flag is set to true from localStorage (component.ts:436-438). No other terms or privacy link exists anywhere in frontend/src/app. Blocker stands for real users with real money. The documents themselves are an external dependency on counsel; the linking, storage migration and tab3 wiring are app work.

### [high] Any signed-in user can give themselves any tier through PUT /user/profile

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:528-549 and 618-633; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/TlhController.java:266-273; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/GoalService.java:134-139; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/User.java:286-290; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:705-725
- Detail: The profile endpoint accepts selectedTier as any string and stores it with no validation and no check that the user paid. The same is true of POST /user/subscription/confirm. All backend tier gates read this one string: TLH requires exactly 'pro', goal limits allow 'plus' or 'pro', the market breakdown scheduler selects by tier. The gates are real, but the entitlement behind them is self-asserted. The frontend already relies on this: confirmTierUpgrade() in my-profile moves a user to Pro or Plus with one PUT and a success toast, with no payment. There is also no allow-list, so an unexpected value is stored as is, and a value longer than 10 characters would hit the varchar(10) column and return a 500. billingPeriod is likewise unvalidated.
- Suggested fix: Remove selectedTier and billingPeriod from the user-writable profile PUT (keep a dev-only path for the tier mock, guarded by profile). Make tier changes go through one server-side service that only runs after a verified payment or an admin grant. Validate tier against core, plus, pro and billing period against monthly, yearly.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts are all true. UserController.java:528-549 stores any string as selectedTier and any string as billingPeriod with no allow-list, and an explicit null clears the tier. SubscriptionService.confirmSubscription (lines 21-35) does the same. Gates read that one string: TlhController.java:271-273 needs exactly 'pro', GoalService.java:134-139 allows 'plus' or 'pro'. Columns are varchar(10) (User.java:286-290, V1__baseline.sql:465). my-profile confirmTierUpgrade (705-725) upgrades with one PUT. I did not run anything to confirm the 500 on an over-long value, but it is the expected result. Severity lowered to high because today every tier is free to pick in onboarding anyway, so self-granting gives a user nothing the tier screen does not already hand out. It becomes a blocker the moment any tier costs money, and must ship in the same change as billing. The missing allow-list is worth fixing now regardless.

### [high] Priority support is promised as live but nothing in the app delivers or prioritises it

- Type: decision, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:208-213; frontend/src/app/tab3/tab3.page.ts:146-152, 367-369
- Detail: The Pro card shows 'Priority support' with a check mark and the description 'A real human responds to your questions within 24 hours.' The only support path in the app is the tab3 'Contact Us' row, which opens mailto:help@fredvested.com with no subject or body. It is the same for every tier and tells the recipient nothing about who the user is or what plan they are on. There is no ticketing, no SLA tracking and no Pro routing. A 24 hour response time is a specific, measurable promise on a paid financial product.
- Suggested fix: Decide whether a 24 hour human response can be staffed at launch. If yes, the smallest app change is a Pro-aware contact path: prefill the mailto subject with a priority tag and the user's public id, or post to a small backend support endpoint that records tier and timestamp so response time can be measured. If no, mark the row 'Coming Soon' like tax loss harvesting or soften the copy.
- Verifier (confirmed): Pro card row at investmentconfirmation.component.ts:208-213 is lockState 'unlocked' with the 24 hour human response copy. The only support paths in frontend/src/app are tab3.page.ts:367-369 (bare mailto:help@fredvested.com, same for all tiers) and a mailto with a subject on the recovery page (recovery.page.html:40). No tier-aware routing anywhere. Staffing is a decision outside the repo; the app part is small.

### [high] 'Cancel anytime' has no cancel, downgrade or manage-plan path in the app

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.html:266; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/SubscriptionService.java:37-47; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:331-344, 705-725

### [high] A failed tier save during onboarding locks the user out of the app with a dead Reactivate button

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:783-790; frontend/src/app/tabs/tabs.page.ts:83-90; frontend/src/app/app.component.ts:567-571; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:17-25; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:403-405
- Detail: In selectTier() the profile PUT error is caught, logged and replaced with of(null), and onboarding completion still runs. If that one request fails (network blip, 500), the user ends up with investmentConfirmationCompleted true and selectedTier null. TabsPage and AppComponent treat that state as an expired subscription for non-beta users: tabs are gated and swiping is locked. The profile page then shows 'Subscription Inactive' with a Reactivate button whose handler only writes a console.log. The user has no way out without manual database help. The same dead end applies to anyone whose tier is ever cleared.
- Suggested fix: Do not complete onboarding unless the tier save succeeded: show an error toast and let the user retry. Separately, make reactivateSubscription() do something real, at minimum reopen the tier picker.
- Verifier (confirmed): selectTier() swallows the PUT error with of(null) and still calls authorizeRecurringInvestment() (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:783-790). tabs.page.ts:83-90 and app.component.ts:567-571 treat onboarded plus null tier plus not privateBeta as expired; the tab bar gates tabs and swipe is locked (tabs.page.ts:282). reactivateSubscription() only logs (my-profile.page.ts:403-405). The trigger is narrow (the PUT must fail while the following completeStep call succeeds), but the dead end is real for any null-tier user. Extra detail the auditor missed: my-profile.page.ts:473-475 computes isSubExpired without the privateBeta bypass that tabs and app.component use, so a private beta user with no tier sees the 'Subscription Inactive' banner and dead button even though their tabs are open. That inconsistency directly affects the planned beta cohort.

- Detail: upgradeToPlusClicked() and upgradeToProClicked() in my-profile, the same handler in retirement-planning and the goal page upgrade path are all TODO stubs that only log. It also labels the plans 'Premium' and 'Standard', names that appear nowhere on the tier screen.

- Verifier (confirmed): my-profile.page.ts:889-898 and retirement-planning.component.ts:299-302 are TODO stubs that only console.log; they are reached from real UI (retirement-planning 119, 255, 261, 327; my-profile 835, 861). goal.page.ts:1175-1179 logs only on the TIER_LIMIT upgrade role. confirmTierUpgrade (my-profile.page.ts:705-725) PUTs the tier and toasts 'Upgraded to X!' after quoting a price; offers are labelled 'Premium' and 'Standard' (lines 338, 341) and shown from my-profile.page.html:261-272. Note the referral offers only appear when referralCount >= 1, which today needs the 30 day scheduler credit, so early beta users will mostly hit the dead stubs.

### [high] Referral 'lifetime' pricing on every tier card is not implemented

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:73-79, 116-122, 171-177, 744-750; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/ReferralService.java:28-37, 64-81; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/ReferralValidationScheduler.java:22
- Detail: Each card shows a lifetime line (for Pro: '$20/mo lifetime with 1 referral', '$200/yr lifetime with 1 referral') and a savings chip. The backend counts referrals and flips referralRewardTriggered, but the reward itself is two TODO stubs that only log: a merch email for founders and 'IAP Promotional Offer downgrade' for everyone else. No discounted price is stored or applied anywhere. The 30 day qualifying window is measured from subscriptionStartedAt, which is set when a tier is merely selected, so it does not mean 30 days of paying after the trial as the backlog story (FRED-114) describes. processEligibleReferrals() also loads every user with findAll() on each run, which will not scale.
- Suggested fix: Either build the discount as part of billing (store the user's effective price and apply it at charge time) or remove the lifetime line and savings chip from the cards until it is real. Fix the qualifying clock to start at first successful payment. Replace findAll() with a query.
- Verifier (confirmed): Lifetime lines and savings chip are in investmentconfirmation.component.ts:73-79 and 744-750. ReferralService.java:64-81 flips referralRewardTriggered and then only logs in two TODO stubs. processEligibleReferrals (28-37) uses userRepository.findAll() and measures 30 days from subscriptionStartedAt, which is set at tier pick, not at first payment. Runs daily at 06:00 ET (ReferralValidationScheduler.java:22). No discounted price is stored anywhere. The findAll() scale point is low priority at beta size; the pricing promise is the real issue and belongs with the billing decision.

- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:659-667, 690; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:550-561
- Detail: 'Keep me updated with FRED tips and news' is saved to the user row when onboarding completes, but the backend only logs the opt-in, with a TODO to call a marketing provider. The frontend handler has a stale TODO saying the preference is not persisted, although it is persisted at line 690. Backlog FRED-176 is marked done.
- Suggested fix: Wire to the email provider once chosen, and remove the stale TODO. Harmless for private beta since the flag is at least stored.

### [low] Two duplicate subscription start fields and an unused confirm path

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/User.java:303-307; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:537-539; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/SubscriptionService.java:30-32; backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__baseline.sql:468-469
- Detail: The user table has both subscription_start_date (set only by the uncalled confirm endpoint) and subscription_started_at (set by the profile PUT and used by the referral scheduler). In practice only the second is ever populated. expireSubscription() clears the tier but leaves both dates and billing period behind.
- Suggested fix: When billing is built, keep one field with one meaning (first successful payment or trial start, stated explicitly) and delete the other path.

**Verified solid:**

- Tier card data and rendering: all three tiers render name, price, lifetime line, pitch, savings chip, expandable feature rows and CTA from PRICING_TIERS (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:67-222, .html:168-270). The Coming Soon chip renders for tax loss harvesting via the threshold field.
- Billing toggle UI works and persists across re-entry: setBillingPeriod writes fred.billingPeriod and ionViewWillEnter restores it (component.ts:425-445).
- Plus and Pro savings math is correct: Plus 60, 30, 80 and Pro 240, 80, 280 match the listed prices (component.ts:115-121, 170-176).
- selectTier() does persist the choice end to end when the request succeeds: PUT /user/profile stores selected_tier and billing_period and returns them in the progress response (UserController.java:69-70, 114-115, 528-549; V1__baseline.sql:417, 465).
- Dev tier mock cannot leak to shipped builds: AuthService only reads it when environment.local is true (auth.service.ts:232-234) and local is false in environment.dev.ts, environment.test.ts and environment.prod.ts. It is also forced null under karma (tier-mock.ts:36-40).
- Backend tier gates that exist do run server side: TLH requires exactly 'pro' (TlhController.java:271-272, TlhExecutionService.java:142), goal creation enforces a one-goal limit below plus (GoalService.java:134-139, surfaced via TierLimitException in GoalController.java:111). They are only as trustworthy as the selectedTier string, see the entitlement finding.
- Expired-subscription gate logic is consistent between TabsPage and AppComponent, and private beta users bypass it (tabs.page.ts:83-90, app.component.ts:567-571).
- Referral thresholds match the card copy: core 3, plus 2, pro 1 in both backend and profile page (ReferralService.java:23-25, 83-89; my-profile.page.ts:454-457). Only the counting is real, not the reward.
- A basic support contact does exist for all users: tab3 Contact Us opens mailto:help@fredvested.com, and the recovery page has its own support mailto (tab3.page.ts:367-369, recovery.page.html:40).
- Server analytics already defines trial_started, subscription_billed and subscription_cancelled with a ready emitter class, so billing analytics only needs call sites once billing exists (BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:31-75, ServerAnalyticsEvent.java:52, 88, 105).

## billing-entitlements

There is no subscription system in the repo, only a tier label. The whole entitlement model is one free-text column, users.selected_tier, which any signed-in user can set to any value through PUT /user/profile or POST /user/subscription/confirm. Nothing charges money, starts or ends a trial, expires a tier, or records a billing event. No IAP or payment SDK is installed (no RevenueCat, StoreKit or Stripe in frontend/package.json, the Podfile or backend/pom.xml).  What exists today: - Backend: SubscriptionService (two methods, set tier and null tier), TierLimitException (goals only), BillingAnalyticsEmitter (three events defined, nothing calls them), ReferralService plus a daily scheduler (rewards are log-line stubs), and User columns selected_tier, billing_period, subscription_started_at, subscription_start_date, private_beta, all in the V1 baseline. - Server-side tier checks exist in three places only: the multi-goal limit, Tax Loss Harvesting (pro), and the Monthly Market Breakdown recipient query (plus/pro). All three trust the self-writable column. - Frontend: the tier carousel on investmentconfirmation writes the tier straight to the profile and carries a TODO for the purchase. Every Upgrade and Reactivate button in the app is a console.log, except the referral upgrade in my-profile, which grants the tier for free. - The "expired" state (tier is null) is a frontend-only tab lock with a dead Reactivate button.  For workable tiers on any payment rail, the missing pieces are: a server-owned entitlement record, a trial clock, a state machine (trialing, active, past due, cancelled, expired), plan change and cancel API and UI, payment-failure and trial-end handling, billing history, yearly/monthly as real state, and the billing analytics hooks. Monte Carlo, Pro scenarios, the drawdown walkthrough and the custom rebalance schedule are gated in the browser only. I also found no rebalancing engine in the backend, although every tier promises automatic rebalancing.  I did not run any tests. The tier-related test files exist but their pass state is unverified.


### [blocker] Any signed-in user can give themselves any tier for free (PUT /user/profile)

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:496-541 (selectedTier written from request body with no validation or payment check; explicit null clears it); :543-548 (billingPeriod same); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AnalyticsOutboxService.java:140-142 (comment: 'The column is an unvalidated varchar that any client can write'); frontend/src/app/services/dev/tier-mock.ts:15-19 (dev mock relies on exactly this write)
- Detail: The profile PUT takes selectedTier from the body and saves it. It does not check the value against core/plus/pro, and it does not check payment. Every server-side gate reads this same column (GoalService.enforceTierLimit, TlhController.isPro, TlhExecutionService, MarketBreakdownScheduler), so every gate can be bypassed with one authenticated request. The same request stamps subscriptionStartedAt, which starts the referral 30-day clock.
- Suggested fix: Remove selectedTier and billingPeriod from the client-writable profile PUT. Move entitlement into a server-owned record that only the billing path (or an admin/beta grant) can write. Have onboarding record a 'chosen plan' intent that is separate from the entitlement. Keep a dev-only path for tier-mock behind the existing dev-tools guard.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. UserController.java:528-548 writes selectedTier and billingPeriod straight from the body with no allowed-value check and no payment check; explicit null clears it; :537-539 stamps subscriptionStartedAt. No guard or profile gate exists. Caveat on severity: today nobody is charged, and onboarding (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:783) legitimately uses this same write to let anyone pick Pro for free, so there is no loss right now. It is a blocker for turning on paid tiers, not for an unpaid beta, and must land with or before billing. The fix has to keep an onboarding 'chosen plan' write. Minor extra: the column is varchar(10) (V1__baseline.sql:465), so a longer string will fail at the database and return a 500.

### [blocker] No source of truth for entitlement: no subscription record, status, or period dates

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/User.java:286-293, :303-304 (selected_tier, billing_period, private_beta, subscription_start_date) and :110-111 (subscription_started_at); backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__baseline.sql:417,465,468,469; no subscription or billing table in V1-V9 (grep of db/migration for subscription/trial/billing returned only these columns)
- Detail: Entitlement is four loose columns on users. There is no status (trialing, active, past due, cancelled, expired), no trial end, no current period end, no next charge date, no cancel-at-period-end flag, no price or discount snapshot, no payment reference, and no history of changes. 'Paid', 'in trial' and 'just picked a card in onboarding' are indistinguishable. There are also two start fields that different code paths write: subscription_started_at (profile PUT, read by referrals) and subscription_start_date (SubscriptionService only, read by nothing).
- Suggested fix: Add a subscriptions table (user, tier, billing period, status, trial_started_at, trial_ends_at, current_period_end, cancelled_at, price and discount snapshot, rail reference) plus a subscription_events audit table, via a new Flyway migration. Add one EntitlementService.effectiveTier(user) that every gate calls. Collapse the two start-date columns into the new record.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. The only subscription-related schema in db/migration is four columns on users in V1__baseline.sql (billing_period :417, private_beta :452, selected_tier :465, subscription_start_date :468, subscription_started_at :469). No other migration mentions subscription, trial or billing. subscription_start_date is written only by SubscriptionService.java:30-31 (unreachable from the app) and read by nothing; subscription_started_at is written by the profile PUT and read by referrals. Blocker for paid tiers, not for an unpaid beta. Small correction: the auditor cited private_beta at line 465-469; it is at line 452.

### [blocker] Trial is promised but does not exist in code

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.html:262-267 ('Try FRED free for 14 days. No charges, cancel anytime.'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/util/ServerAnalyticsEvent.java:104-105 (TRIAL_LENGTH_DAYS = 14, a constant only); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:21-22 ('Nothing creates a trial, charges a fee or cancels anything'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/config/FredKnowledge.java:109-110
- Detail: There is no trial start, trial end, trial-ending reminder, or trial-to-paid conversion. Nothing runs on day 14. SubscriptionService.expireSubscription is the only expiry code and nothing calls it. A user who picks a tier keeps it forever.
- Suggested fix: Store trial_started_at and trial_ends_at on the subscription record when the plan is chosen. Add a scheduler that moves trialing to active (charge) or expired, and a pre-end reminder (push or email). The charge step depends on the rail counsel approves, but the trial clock and state changes can be built now.

BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:21-22 states nothing creates a trial. No scheduler or state exists. Note the copy also promises 'cancel anytime' while no cancel path exists in the app, which is a truthfulness problem even in an unpaid beta. Extra inconsistency found: FredKnowledge.java:109-110 tells the AI chat that FRED is 'A flat $8/month subscription' with 'First 2 weeks are free', which does not match the three-tier prices on the pricing screen.

### [blocker] No billing at all: no charge, no payment-failure handling, no receipts or billing history

- Type: external, effort XL, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:14-28; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:668-671 (old webhook removed, 'Billing is owned by a separate workstream'); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:776-781 (TODO: IAP purchase); no revenuecat/purchases/iap/storekit/stripe match in frontend/package.json, frontend/ios/App/Podfile or backend/pom.xml
- Detail: No code collects a fee. That means there is also no retry or dunning on a failed charge, no grace period, no downgrade on non-payment, no refund path, no receipt, and no billing history screen or endpoint. The backend comments assume ACH from the Plaid-linked bank, while the frontend TODOs and FredKnowledge assume Apple IAP. The codebase holds two conflicting plans. This is blocked on the counsel answer about the per-paycheck model.
- Suggested fix: Wait for counsel on the rail. Once chosen, build: charge job, ledger table (amount, period, status, reference), failure states with grace period, receipt email, and a billing history endpoint and screen. Remove the IAP TODOs or the ACH comments so only one plan is described.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:14-28 says billing does not exist in this repo; UserController.java:668-671 records removal of the old webhook; investmentconfirmation.component.ts:776-781 is an IAP TODO. No revenuecat, storekit, stripe or purchases match in frontend/package.json, frontend/ios/App/Podfile or backend/build.gradle (the backend uses Gradle; there is no pom.xml, so the auditor's pom.xml citation is wrong but the conclusion holds). The two conflicting plans (ACH in backend comments, Apple IAP in frontend TODOs and ReferralService.java:77) are real. Type external/decision is right: blocked on counsel.

### [blocker] Charging for digital subscription features outside Apple IAP (guideline 3.1.1)

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:16-17 ('The advisory fee is collected outside Apple, by ACH'); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:776-781 (IAP TODO); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.html:213-215 (in-app price display)

Whether that is allowed (for example as an advisory fee for a regulated financial service) or needs IAP is a question for counsel and Apple review. I am not resolving it here. The answer decides the whole billing build and whether prices can be shown as they are now.
- Suggested fix: Put this question to counsel alongside the per-paycheck model question, and get the answer in writing before building the charge path or submitting to App Store review.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed as a decision item. BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:16-17 states the fee is collected outside Apple by ACH; investmentconfirmation.component.html:213-216 shows $/mo and $/yr prices in-app; the selectTier TODO (ts:776-781) still describes IAP. I cannot resolve the Apple rule question from code. It blocks App Store submission and the billing build, not an unpaid TestFlight beta. No app work until counsel answers.

### [blocker] Expired or missing tier is a frontend-only lock with no way out, and a failed tier save in onboarding puts a new user in it

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/tabs/tabs.page.ts:83-90; frontend/src/app/components/floating-tab-bar/floating-tab-bar.component.ts:38-43, :77-79; frontend/src/app/app.component.ts:566-571; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:17-25; frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:783-790 (catchError returns of(null), then authorizeRecurringInvestment runs anyway)
- Detail: A fully onboarded user with selectedTier null has Portfolio, Planning and Chat disabled and sees a 'Subscription Inactive' banner whose Reactivate button only logs. In selectTier, a failed profile PUT is swallowed and onboarding still completes, so a network blip at that moment leaves a brand new user with no tier, locked out of their own portfolio, with no working fix in the app. The lock is also only in the UI. No backend endpoint (portfolio, chat, schedule) checks for an active subscription, and auto-investing keeps running for a 'locked' user. Whether a lapsed payer should lose sight of their own money is a product and counsel question too.
- Suggested fix: Make selectTier stop and show an error if the plan save fails. Decide the lapsed-user policy (likely: always allow viewing holdings, withdrawals and pausing; lock premium extras only), then enforce it on the server through the entitlement service and give the banner a working action.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. investmentconfirmation.component.ts:783-790 swallows a failed profile PUT with catchError(of(null)) and then calls authorizeRecurringInvestment anyway. The lock at tabs.page.ts:83-90 and app.component.ts:566-571 reads the server progress, where selectedTier would still be null; the localStorage copy 'fred.selectedTier' is only used for non-authenticated progress (auth.service.ts:296-314), so it does not rescue the user. Portfolio, Planning and Chat are then disabled (floating-tab-bar.component.ts:38-43, :77-79) and Reactivate only logs (my-profile.page.ts:403-405). A user with real money invested would lose sight of their portfolio with no in-app fix. The only bypass is privateBeta, and no code in the backend ever sets that flag (no setPrivateBeta call exists; it would need a manual database edit). No backend endpoint checks for an active subscription, so the lock is UI only. Fixing the swallow is a small task and should be done before any beta user onboards.

### [blocker] No rebalancing engine found in the backend, though every tier promises automatic rebalancing and Plus sells a custom schedule

- Type: app-work, effort XL, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: grep -i 'rebalanc' over backend/src/main/java matched only MarketNarrativeValidator.java:35, ConversationAnalyticsService.java:106, WashSaleGuardService.java:26, :70, :91, :107 ('any future rebalancer') and TlhSummaryService.java:33; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:67, :247-248, :735-736 (schedule saved by savePrefs, 'a synchronous localStorage write'); frontend/src/app/services/goals.service.ts:28-34, :192; frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:97-100, :151-155
- Detail: Outside my core domain, reported because it affects what the tiers sell. I found no service, scheduler or endpoint that rebalances a portfolio. The Plus 'custom rebalancing schedule' is a chip that saves to localStorage on the device, and 'Next rebalance' is a date computed in the browser. The server never sees the preference. The tier-feature auditor should confirm.
- Suggested fix: Either build the rebalancer and persist the schedule on the server (with its tier check), or remove or relabel the rebalancing promises on the pricing cards and My Profile before real users pay for them.
- Verifier (adjusted): Confirmed, and I would raise it. A case-insensitive grep for 'rebalanc' over backend/src/main/java matches only comments, a validator phrase, an analytics topic label and an enum value in WashSaleGuardService.java:70 that calls it 'any future rebalancer' (:26). No service, scheduler or Alpaca call rebalances anything. The pricing screen marks 'Automatic portfolio rebalancing' as unlocked on every tier with the text 'FRED periodically realigns your portfolio back to your target allocation; no action needed from you' (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:97-101) and sells 'Custom rebalancing schedule' on Plus (:152-156). My Profile shows a 'Next rebalance' date computed in the browser (my-profile.page.ts:67, :247-248) and saves the chip to localStorage only (:729-736). Telling real-money investors their portfolio is being rebalanced when it is not is a misrepresentation, so this should not go to beta users as is. The cheap path is to remove or relabel the copy and the Next rebalance date (small); building the engine is very large. Which path to take is a product decision.

- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:334-344 (offers: pro at $20 with 1 referral, plus at $10 with 2), :705-725 (confirmTierUpgrade PUTs selectedTier then toasts 'Upgraded to ...!'); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:261-275

### [high] Referral rewards are stubs and the '30 days as a paying subscriber' rule counts from tier selection, not payment

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/ReferralService.java:29-37, :64-80 (both rewards are TODO log lines); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/UserService.java:149-161; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:324-327 (user-facing copy about trial and paying subscriber), :537-539 (start stamp set on tier selection); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:75, :118, :173 (lifetime price promises); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:255-257 ('Reward unlocked!')

The user-facing copy at UserController.java:324-327 says 'stay a paying subscriber'. Lifetime price lines are at investmentconfirmation.component.ts:73-75, :116-118, :171-173. The referrer's count stops rising once the reward triggers (ReferralService.java:47). ### [medium] Premium features gated only in the frontend (no server enforcement)

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: Monte Carlo (Plus): frontend/src/app/components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.ts:62-67, :261 and .html:26, :103; Pro scenarios, adjust and income simulation: frontend/src/app/components/monte-carlo-flow/monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:140-141 and .html:254-311; Drawdown walkthrough (Pro): frontend/src/app/components/strategy-deck/strategy-deck.component.ts:192, :330; Custom rebalance schedule (Plus): frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:729-735; grep of backend/src/main/java for monte/simulat/drawdown found no feature endpoints
- Detail: These features run entirely in the browser, with the tier passed in as a plain input (userTier). There is no premium endpoint to protect, so the gate is cosmetic CSS and ngIf. The outside-accounts toggle (promised as a Plus feature, 'Add 401(k) & Roth IRA') has no tier check of its own in retirement-planning.component.html:67-68. It sits inside the Monte Carlo section, which is blurred for core. For a WebView app this is acceptable for beta once the tier itself cannot be self-written, but it is not real enforcement.
- Suggested fix: First fix the self-grant holes so the tier value can be trusted. Then decide per feature whether client-only gating is acceptable. Anything that must be enforced needs its data or computation behind a server check.
- Verifier (adjusted): The claim is true: Monte Carlo, Pro scenarios, drawdown and the rebalance chip are computed in the browser and gated by template checks; there is no backend endpoint for them. The outside-accounts toggle sits at retirement-planning.component.html:64-69 inside the wrapper that is blurred at :26, with no tier check of its own. Severity lowered to medium: these are local calculations on the user's own numbers, with no server data or cost to protect, and getting at them needs tampering with a native WebView build. This is normal for this kind of feature and is acceptable for beta and launch once the tier value itself is server-owned. I did not re-read every cited line in monte-carlo-flow and strategy-deck.

### [medium] Server-side tier checks that do exist all trust the self-writable column and are inconsistent

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/GoalService.java:134-140; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/GoalController.java:111-113; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/TlhController.java:74-240, :271-273; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/TlhExecutionService.java:140-145; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/TlhHarvestService.java:49, :146, :471; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:31, :68; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/ReferralService.java:84-89
- Detail: Enforced on the server today: multi-goal limit (create and reopen), all TLH write endpoints plus the scheduler query and an execution-time re-check, and Market Breakdown recipients. Each one compares raw strings in its own way: GoalService and TLH use case-sensitive equals, ReferralService uses equalsIgnoreCase, and the analytics code maps legacy 'piggy' to core. None of them considers trial state, payment state or privateBeta. Private beta users bypass the tab lock in the UI but get no server-side entitlement. GoalService.importLegacy (lines 114-130) does not call enforceTierLimit, so imported legacy goals can exceed the one-goal limit for core. I could not tell whether that is intended.
- Suggested fix: Route all of these through one EntitlementService with an enum tier and explicit capability checks (canMultiGoal, canTlh, getsMarketBreakdown). Decide what privateBeta grants on the server.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts confirmed: GoalService.java:134-140 and TlhController.java:271-273 and TlhExecutionService.java:140-145 use case-sensitive equals; ReferralService.java:84-89 uses equalsIgnoreCase; MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:68 and TlhHarvestService.java:146, :471 query by tier list; none considers trial, payment or privateBeta. GoalService.importLegacy (:114-130) does not call enforceTierLimit; it is only capped by MAX_IMPORTED_GOALS. Severity lowered to medium as its own item: the inconsistency causes no live bug today because every writer sends lowercase core/plus/pro, and the real risk (trusting a self-writable column) is already counted in the first finding. This is the refactor that falls out of building the entitlement record. The open product question of what privateBeta grants on the server is real, since nothing server-side reads it for entitlement.

### [medium] Self-granted 'pro' unlocks Tax Loss Harvesting, which sells real securities when TLH mode is live

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/TlhController.java:93-100, :223-234, :271-273; backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:82-83 (TLH_MODE default off, manual run default false); backend/src/main/resources/application-dev.properties:74-75 (dry-run, manual run enabled)
- Detail: TLH enroll and run only need selectedTier to equal 'pro', which a user can set themselves. The default mode is off, so this is not live today. It becomes a real-money risk the moment TLH_MODE=live is set without first closing the tier write hole. I did not read application-prod.properties for a TLH override.
- Suggested fix: Close the self-grant holes before TLH is ever switched to live, and add that as an explicit pre-flight item on the TLH go-live checklist.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts confirmed: TlhController.java:93-100 and :271-273 need only selectedTier equal to 'pro'. Guards the auditor did not fully check: application.properties:82-83 defaults TLH_MODE to off and manual run to false; application-prod.properties has no TLH line at all (I checked), application-ci.properties:83 is off, and only dev and local run dry-run. /run is also blocked when manualRunEnabled is false (TlhController.java:225) and when mode is off (:241). Severity lowered to medium: the user must explicitly enroll, it only ever touches their own account, and anyone can already pick Pro for free in onboarding, so the harm is an unpaid feature rather than unauthorised trading. Keep it as a pre-flight line on the TLH go-live checklist rather than separate work.

### [medium] Billing analytics events are defined but nothing emits them; plan changes are not tracked

- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:14-28, :45-77; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/util/ServerAnalyticsEvent.java:51-52, :63-67, :86-88 (AWAITING_BILLING); frontend/src/app/services/analytics/analytics-events.ts:335 (paywall nudge event props), :418-419

The paywall nudge event (shown, upgrade_tapped, dismissed) is wired on the frontend. There are no events for tier selected at onboarding, upgrade, downgrade, payment failed, trial ending or reactivated.

### [medium] Pricing and plan copy disagree across the app

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/config/FredKnowledge.java:109-110 ('A flat $8/month subscription', 'First 2 weeks are free'); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:67-79, :111-121, :166-176 (three tiers at $8, $15, $40); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:338, :341 ('Premium', 'Standard'); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:204 ('On Piggy'); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:130 ('Everything in Core')

The lowest tier is called Piggy Plan, Core and Piggy in different places, and the upgrade card uses Standard and Premium. 'No charges, cancel anytime' is shown with no cancel mechanism.
