### [low] My Profile 'Subscription Inactive' check ignores the private beta bypass

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:473-475 (no privateBeta term) versus frontend/src/app/tabs/tabs.page.ts:85-89 and frontend/src/app/app.component.ts:567-571 (both exclude privateBeta)
- Detail: Tabs and app landing treat private beta users as never expired. My Profile does not, so a private beta user with a null tier would see the 'Subscription Inactive' banner while their tabs still work. This is only reachable if the tier is null, for example after the swallowed save failure described above.
- Suggested fix: Compute isSubExpired in one shared place (AuthService or the future entitlement state) and use it in all three.

**Verified solid:**

- The old unauthenticated RevenueCat-style webhook is gone: UserController.java:668-671 documents its removal, and a grep finds no /subscription/webhook mapping.
- The multi-goal limit is enforced on the server for both create and reopen (GoalService.java:70, :87-92, :134-140) and returns a clean 403 TIER_LIMIT (GoalController.java:111-113). The frontend handles it with a nudge (goal.page.ts:1171-1180, my-profile.page.ts:785-790).
- TLH write endpoints (enroll, preview, run) check for pro on every request (TlhController.java:100, :204, :234). Execution re-checks the tier from a fresh DB read before selling (TlhExecutionService.java:140-145). The scheduler query filters on tier and opt-in (TlhHarvestService.java:146, :471). Lapsed users keep read access to their own TLH history (TlhController.java:81, :151, :181).
- TLH defaults to off, with manual run off, in the base config (application.properties:82-83).
- Market Breakdown recipients are filtered to plus and pro on the server (MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:31, :68), with a per-user, per-period duplicate-send guard (:79-82).
- BillingAnalyticsEmitter is deliberately inert, and its events carry no amounts or PII (BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:45-77). AWAITING_BILLING lists the three un-emitted events (ServerAnalyticsEvent.java:86-88).
- The analytics tier value is narrowed to core, plus, pro or null, so junk values in the column cannot pollute analytics (AnalyticsOutboxService.java:144-159).
- The tier dev mock only activates when environment.local is true and is forced null under karma (auth.service.ts:232-234, tier-mock.ts:36-40). I did not check the environment files to confirm local is false in deployed builds.
- privateBeta is not client-writable: there is no setPrivateBeta call anywhere in backend/src/main/java. It is only read into the progress response (UserController.java:116).
- /user/progress returns selectedTier, billingPeriod and privateBeta (UserController.java:114-116), and every frontend gate reads the tier from userProgress$ rather than localStorage (retirement-planning.component.ts:198-199, tabs.page.ts:83-89, my-profile.page.ts:459-461).
- Referral basics work: unique code generation, self-referral and double-apply guards (UserService.java:122-132), a daily validation scheduler at 6am ET (ReferralValidationScheduler.java:18), and thresholds that match the pricing cards (ReferralService.java:23-26 versus investmentconfirmation.component.ts:79, :122, :177).
- Paywall nudge analytics (shown, upgrade_tapped, dismissed) are wired at each gate (retirement-planning.component.ts:221, :254, :326; my-profile.page.ts:833, :859, :870; goal.page.ts:1207, :1283; monte-carlo-flow.component.ts:691, :1046; strategy-deck.component.ts:297, :357).
- All subscription columns the code uses exist in the Flyway baseline (V1__baseline.sql:417, 452, 465, 468, 469), so no migration is missing for the current fields.

## dev-mocks-flags

Dev scaffolding audit (working tree, read-only; no build was run).  Short answer: a production Angular build would NOT ship with mock data switched on, and the backend dev endpoints cannot exist under the prod profile. But the native iOS shell is still wired to the local dev tunnel, account recovery still prints its code to the server console instead of emailing it, and several smaller dev leftovers need cleanup.  Current value of every toggle (working tree, identical to HEAD, so these are committed): - frontend/src/app/services/dev/portfolio-mock.ts:34-40 MOCK_CHOICES = ['XLARGE'] (mock ~$2.4M portfolio ON for local builds) - frontend/src/app/services/dev/tier-mock.ts:29-34 MOCK_CHOICES = ['plus'] (ON; does a real PUT /user/profile on each launch of a local build) - frontend/src/app/services/dev/mfu-mock.ts:45 FORCE_MFU = true; :56 MILESTONE = 'DERIVED'; :59 QUARTERLY = false; :65 RETIRE = true (ON) - frontend/src/app/services/dev/goals-mock.ts:27-30 MOCK_CHOICES = [] (OFF) - frontend/src/app/services/first-time-tour.service.ts:18 FORCE_FIRST_TIME_TOUR = false (OFF) - frontend/src/app/components/authfinalize/authfinalize.component.ts:49 simulatePasskey = false (only flipped by the bypass button, which is shown only when env.local)  How the gating works: every mock consumer checks environment.local at runtime (portfolio.service.ts:89, monthly-freedom-update.service.ts:103 and :108, goals.service.ts:205, auth.service.ts:233). environment.local is true only in environment.ts (local) and environment.ci.ts; it is false in environment.dev.ts, environment.test.ts and environment.prod.ts. angular.json swaps the file per configuration and defaultConfiguration is production. So prod, dev and test builds run with all mocks inert. The mock modules are still statically imported by four core services, so their code most likely ships in the bundle as dead code; I could not confirm tree-shaking because I did not run a build (the existing frontend/www is a local build and does contain the mock strings).  Backend: DevAuthController, DevPlaidController, DevUserController, SimulatedAttestationService and DevToolsConfig are all @Profile({local,dev,test,ci}); DevToolsProdGuard aborts startup if any is registered under prod; a shared-secret header is a second lock on api-dev. This part is solid.


### [blocker] Native iOS shell loads the app from the local dev tunnel (capacitor server.url)

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9 (server.url 'https://local.fredvested.com'); generated copy frontend/ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json server.url same value (file is gitignored via frontend/ios/.gitignore:12); frontend/src/index.html:38-45 comment assumes prod loads from the local bundle
- Detail: The Capacitor config hardcodes server.url to the Cloudflare tunnel that points at Andy's machine. Any iOS build made from this config loads its web code live from local.fredvested.com instead of the bundled www folder. If the laptop or tunnel is down the app is a white screen, and whatever is being served there (a local build with mocks ON) is what the user gets. There is no per-environment Capacitor config and no build script that removes server.url for a release build.
- Suggested fix: Make server.url conditional (for example only when an env var like CAP_LIVE_RELOAD is set) so release and beta builds ship the bundled www. Add a release checklist step or script that fails if the synced capacitor.config.json contains a server.url.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9 hardcodes server.url 'https://local.fredvested.com' with no env condition, and the synced frontend/ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json carries the same value. I found no per-environment Capacitor config and no build script in frontend/ that strips it (frontend/scripts holds only icon, lint and docs scripts as far as I saw). The index.html comment at lines 38-45 assumes prod loads from the local bundle, which is not what this config does. One knock-on effect the auditor did not mention: removing server.url changes the web origin to capacitor://localhost, so backend CORS and WEBAUTHN_RP_ORIGINS must allow that origin. I did not check those values (they are env vars).

### [blocker] Account recovery code is printed to the server console and never emailed

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:45-56 (OTP generated, then System.out.println 'MOCK EMAIL TO' with the code), :61-97 (verify clears all passkeys and issues a JWT); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/config/SecurityConfig.java:113 (/api/auth/** permitAll)
- Detail: The recovery flow does not call EmailService at all. It writes the 6 digit code and the user's email to stdout. In production a real user can never receive their code, so the 'Already have an account?' path is dead, and the code plus email land in the hosting provider's logs. Separately, I saw no attempt counter or lockout in this controller: /verify is unauthenticated, the code is 6 digits and lives 15 minutes, and a correct guess wipes the user's passkeys and returns a login token. I did not find a rate limiter elsewhere that covers this path (grep for rate limit terms only hit PinController and unrelated models), but I did not trace every filter, so treat the brute-force part as medium confidence.
- Suggested fix: Send the code through EmailService with a real provider, delete the System.out lines, and add per-account and per-IP attempt limits with lockout on /verify and a resend throttle on /initiate.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. RecoveryController.java:51-56 prints the email and OTP with System.out.println and the controller does not inject EmailService at all (constructor at :29-33). Verify at :61-98 clears passkeys and returns a JWT. /api/auth/** is permitAll at SecurityConfig.java:113. Brute-force part also holds up: the only servlet filters in the backend are DevToolsKeyFilter, AuthTokenFilter and PlaidWebhookVerificationFilter, none of which rate limit, and the controller has no attempt counter (a wrong guess at :82-84 leaves the OTP in place for the full 15 minutes). So an attacker who knows an email can trigger /initiate and then guess up to 1,000,000 codes unthrottled to take over an account that holds real money. That raises the brute-force piece to high confidence, and it is a blocker on its own even once email works. Minor extras: nextInt(999999) at :46 can never produce 999999, and @CrossOrigin(origins = "*") sits on this controller at :20.

### [high] No real email sender exists; all transactional email is mocked, not only the monthly breakdown

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/LoggingEmailSender.java:13-25 (only EmailSender implementation, logs 'MOCK EMAIL'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:56-62 (disabled path records MOCKED); backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:47-49 (EMAIL_ENABLED default false, from default noreply@investingapp.com, provider default logging); callers InvestmentExecutionService.java:1186,1201,1214; PaydayNotificationService.java:67,81,96; AccountStatusService.java:167
- Detail: The known 'market breakdown email is mocked' gap is wider than that feature. Investment execution notices, payday notices and account status emails all go through the same EmailService, which has only a logging sender. The default From address is on investingapp.com, a domain that is not FRED's. Even with EMAIL_ENABLED=true nothing would leave the server until a provider class is written.
- Suggested fix: Implement one real EmailSender (the analytics spec names Resend), set the From default to a fredvested.com address, and decide which transactional emails must be live for beta. Provider account and domain verification are outside-party steps.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts confirmed: LoggingEmailSender is the only 'implements EmailSender' in backend/src (LoggingEmailSender.java:13-15), EmailService.java:56-62 records MOCKED when disabled, application.properties:47-49 defaults are EMAIL_ENABLED false, from noreply@investingapp.com, provider logging. Callers confirmed at InvestmentExecutionService.java:1186,1201,1214, PaydayNotificationService.java:67,81,96, AccountStatusService.java:167. One extra hazard: setting EMAIL_PROVIDER to anything other than 'logging' before a real sender class exists removes the only EmailSender bean (ConditionalOnProperty at LoggingEmailSender.java:14) and the app would fail to start. Severity: I keep it high as a standalone item, but note that the lead should treat it as a hard dependency of the recovery blocker, since recovery cannot work without a real sender. If execution-failure or account-status emails are the only way a user learns that a real-money transfer failed, this is arguably a blocker for beta; I did not verify whether push or in-app notices cover those cases.

### [high] Which build configuration ships to beta testers is undefined, and the environment files are stale

- Type: decision, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/environments/environment.prod.ts:7-11 (backend api.fredvested.com which does not exist yet, rpId app.fredvested.com, posthogKey empty); frontend/src/environments/environment.dev.ts:4-8 (production:false, rpId is a GitHub Codespaces hostname 'potential-engine-...app.github.dev'); frontend/angular.json build configurations (dev: optimization false, sourceMap true; production: fileReplacements to environment.prod.ts); frontend/package.json:11 ('watch' uses a 'development' configuration that does not exist)
- Detail: There is no production backend, so a production Angular build points at a host that does not answer. The only deployed backend is api-dev, and the build that targets it (dev) is an unoptimized build with production:false, which keeps the ?devPage login path compiled in and keeps non-production logging on. Its rpId is a leftover Codespaces hostname, so passkeys cannot work from that build as written. Someone has to decide what a beta build is: a real prod backend plus the production config, or a hardened beta config against a separate backend.
- Suggested fix: Decide the beta target. Then either stand up the prod backend and fill environment.prod.ts (PostHog key included), or add a 'beta' environment with production:true, local:false and correct rpId. Fix or delete the stale dev rpId and the broken watch script.
- Verifier (adjusted): Mostly true, with one wrong claim. Confirmed: environment.prod.ts:7-11 (api.fredvested.com, empty posthogKey), environment.dev.ts:4-8 (production:false, Codespaces rpId), angular.json dev config has optimization false and sourceMap true, package.json:11 'watch' uses a non-existent 'development' configuration, and app.component.ts:178 gates ?devPage only on !environment.production so it is compiled into a dev build. Wrong claim: 'Its rpId is a leftover Codespaces hostname, so passkeys cannot work from that build as written.' environment.rpId is not read anywhere in frontend/src outside the environment files. The app takes the rpId from the server's WebAuthn options (auth.service.ts:567, passkey-prompt.component.ts:270), which come from WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_DEV / _PROD (application-dev.properties:30, application-prod.properties:31). So the stale rpId is dead config to clean up, not a passkey breaker. Also note ?devPage in a dev build still needs the backend dev-tools key (devToolsKey is empty in environment.dev.ts:10 and /api/dev is behind DevToolsKeyFilter), so the exposure is smaller than implied. The core point stands: there is no build config that is both production:true and pointed at a backend that exists, and that is a decision plus app work.

### [high] Release entitlements do not match the production passkey domain and look malformed

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App/Release.entitlements (associated-domains is a single <string> 'webcredentials:api.fredvested.com' rather than an array; keychain group '$(AppIdentifierPrefix)com.fredvested.app'); frontend/src/environments/environment.prod.ts:8 (rpId 'app.fredvested.com'); frontend/ios/App/App.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:413,423 (Release uses Release.entitlements, bundle id com.fredvested.Fred); Debug.entitlements and Test.entitlements use an array
- Detail: Native passkeys need the app's associated domain to match the relying party id. The Release build declares api.fredvested.com while the prod frontend uses app.fredvested.com, so passkey sign-up and sign-in would fail in a release build. The value is also a string where Debug and Test use an array, and the keychain group name does not match the bundle id. None of the four entitlement files contains aps-environment even though the push notifications plugin is configured in capacitor.config.ts:28-30. I did not verify Xcode capability settings or the hosted AASA file, so confidence is medium.
- Suggested fix: Pick one prod relying party domain, use it in environment.prod.ts, WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD, the AASA file and Release.entitlements (as an array). Fix the keychain group and add the push entitlement if push is in scope for beta.
- Verifier (adjusted): Partly true. Confirmed: Release.entitlements has com.apple.developer.associated-domains as a single <string> 'webcredentials:api.fredvested.com' where Debug and Test use an <array> (the key must be an array, so this is malformed), the keychain group is '$(AppIdentifierPrefix)com.fredvested.app' while the bundle id is com.fredvested.Fred (project.pbxproj:413,423), and no entitlements file or the pbxproj contains aps-environment even though PushNotifications is configured in capacitor.config.ts:30-32. Not established: the 'mismatch with the prod rpId'. environment.prod.ts rpId 'app.fredvested.com' is never read by app code; the real relying party id is the backend env var WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD (application-prod.properties:31), whose value is not in the repo. The AASA file is served by the backend itself (AASAController.java:22-25, app id 9G59L97KRT.com.fredvested.Fred), so api.fredvested.com is a plausible and workable rp domain if WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD is set to it. Corrected finding: fix the string-vs-array format, the keychain group and the missing push entitlement, and make sure WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD equals the domain in Release.entitlements; then delete or correct the unused environment rpId values. Missing aps-environment means push registration fails in any signed build, which matters if payday or transfer notices rely on push.

### [high] Subscription tier can be set by the client with no payment check (the dev tier mock relies on this)

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:528-541 (PUT /user/profile writes selectedTier from the request body, null clears it), :618-632 (POST /subscription/confirm takes tier from the body); frontend/src/app/services/auth.service.ts:232-236 and frontend/src/app/services/dev/tier-mock.ts:15-19 (mock is 'a REAL WRITE' through the same endpoint)
- Detail: The tier is a plain string on the user row that any logged-in user can change through the normal profile endpoint. The dev tier mock works precisely because of this. Until billing exists, any user can give themselves 'pro' (which the backend uses to gate tax loss harvesting) or 'plus' for free. This is the billing gap seen from the dev-tooling side; I am not proposing a billing design.
- Suggested fix: When billing is decided, remove selectedTier and billingPeriod from the client-writable profile fields and make tier changes server-owned. The tier mock then needs a dev-only endpoint under /api/dev.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. UserController.java:528-541 writes selectedTier straight from the PUT /user/profile body with no validation of the value and no entitlement check, and POST /user/subscription/confirm (:618-632) passes the body tier to SubscriptionService.confirmSubscription (SubscriptionService.java:21-35), which just saves it. The comment says 'post Apple IAP / RevenueCat' but no receipt or payment verification exists. The tier gates real behaviour server side: TLH requires 'pro' (TlhController.java:272, TlhExecutionService.java:142), multi-goal requires plus/pro (GoalService.java:135-136), and the market breakdown email goes to plus/pro (MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:31). The frontend mock is gated on environment.local (auth.service.ts:233), so the mock itself does not leak into deployed builds; the hole is the endpoint. Note that tier-mock.ts currently has 'plus' uncommented in MOCK_CHOICES, so local builds (including the phone build loaded through server.url) overwrite the user's tier to plus on every launch. Severity high is right for a private beta where no one is charged yet; it becomes a blocker the day billing goes live. Tied to the counsel decision on billing.

### [medium] Dev mock toggles are committed in the ON position and guarded by one runtime flag

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/services/dev/portfolio-mock.ts:34-40; frontend/src/app/services/dev/tier-mock.ts:29-34; frontend/src/app/services/dev/mfu-mock.ts:45,65; static imports at portfolio.service.ts:6, monthly-freedom-update.service.ts:6-7, goals.service.ts:10, auth.service.ts:11; gates at portfolio.service.ts:89, monthly-freedom-update.service.ts:103,108, goals.service.ts:205, auth.service.ts:233; frontend/src/environments/environment.ci.ts:8 (local:true)
- Detail: Prod, dev and test builds are safe today because environment.local is false there. But the safety is a single boolean in a file that gets swapped at build time, the toggles themselves are committed ON (XLARGE portfolio, plus tier, forced MFU with the Retire card), and the mock modules are imported statically by four core services, so the fixtures probably ship as dead code in every bundle (not confirmed, no build run). Any build made with the local or ci configuration shows a fake $2.4M portfolio and rewrites the signed-in user's tier on the backend it talks to. Combined with the capacitor server.url finding, a phone build today serves exactly that.
- Suggested fix: Commit the toggles OFF by default, and add a CI or prebuild check that fails a production build if any MOCK_CHOICES entry is uncommented or FORCE_* is true. Optionally load the mock modules through a dynamic import inside the environment.local branch so they are not in release bundles.

### [medium] Sensitive values are written to the browser console and nothing strips console calls in production

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/linkplaid/linkplaid.component.ts:119 (Plaid public_token and metadata), :188 (link_token response), :198; frontend/src/app/change-bank-account/change-bank-account.page.ts:152,253; frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:357 (user email); frontend/src/app/components/authfinalize/authfinalize.component.ts:255-258 ([PASSKEY_DIAG] lines), :274 (registration options); frontend/src/app/services/auth.service.ts:619,628; angular.json production configuration has no console-drop setting
- Detail: There are 182 console.log/info/debug calls in non-spec app code. Several print Plaid tokens and bank metadata, the user's email, passkey registration options and schedule payloads. The production build configuration does not remove them, so they run on users' devices and show up in any attached log capture or future crash reporter breadcrumbs.
- Suggested fix: Delete the token, metadata and email logs outright. For the rest, route through a small logger that is a no-op when environment.production is true, or add an esbuild drop/pure setting for console in the production configuration.

### [medium] Backend logs full JWTs on validation failure and keeps debug prints

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/security/jwt/JwtUtils.java:226-234 (logger.error with 'for token: {}' and the raw token), :37-40 (banner logging), :47-48 (hardcoded fallback secret on an encoding error), :104-154 (info log with user email on every token issue); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/InvestmentSchedule.java:195 (System.out.println in an entity); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/config/WebAuthnConfig.java:52-56 (falls back to http://localhost:8100 origin instead of failing)
- Detail: Every expired or malformed token is written to the logs in full at error level; an expired token is normal traffic, so logs will fill with signed tokens that carry the user's email. The hardcoded fallback JWT secret is only reachable if UTF-8 encoding throws, which should not happen, but it is a known string in the repo and should not exist. WebAuthnConfig quietly falls back to a localhost origin if the origins property is empty, where a prod misconfiguration should stop the boot.
- Suggested fix: Log only the failure reason, never the token. Replace the fallback secret with a thrown exception. Make WebAuthnConfig fail under prod when origins are empty. Remove the System.out lines.

### [medium] Wildcard CORS annotations left from development on 19 controllers

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/PlaidController.java:28 ('For development, restrict in production'); AuthController.java:45 ('Allow all origins for now (dev)'); same annotation on AlpacaController.java:27, UserController.java:24, InvestmentController.java:33, TradingController.java:35, WebAuthnController.java:42, RecoveryController.java:20 and others; central list at backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/config/SecurityConfig.java:91
- Detail: Nineteen controllers carry @CrossOrigin(origins = "*") with comments saying to restrict before production. SecurityConfig also defines an allowed-origins list, so the two sources disagree. Auth is a bearer header rather than cookies, which limits the damage, but the unauthenticated endpoints (recovery, passkey, auth) are callable from any web origin. I did not test which setting wins at runtime.
- Suggested fix: Remove the per-controller annotations and rely on the single SecurityConfig origin list, set from an environment variable per profile.

### [medium] Prod profile property file does not list everything a prod boot needs

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:1-33 (header lists datasource, JWT, encryption, WebAuthn and Plaid variables only; no alpaca.* keys); backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:61-68 (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY, PINECONE_API_KEY, PINECONE_INDEX_NAME with no defaults), :47-49, :82-83 (TLH_MODE default off, manual run default false); AlpacaService.java:26 and PortfolioDashboardService.java:62,69 (@Value alpaca.api.key / alpaca.market.key with no default); BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:108-135
- Detail: Unlike the dev profile file, the prod file has no Alpaca block, so the Alpaca keys and base URLs must arrive as raw environment variables through Spring's relaxed binding, and the header comment that lists required variables does not mention them or the AI keys. BrokerEnvironmentGuard will correctly refuse to start on the sandbox defaults, so this fails loudly rather than silently, but the first prod deploy will be trial and error. Note also that prod defaults leave tax loss harvesting off and email off until someone sets TLH_MODE and EMAIL_ENABLED. I did not attempt a prod-profile boot.
- Suggested fix: Add explicit Alpaca, AI, email, analytics and TLH entries to application-prod.properties (all from env vars, no literals) and extend the header checklist so a prod boot has one authoritative list.

**Verified solid:**

- Backend dev endpoints are profile-gated at bean registration: DevAuthController.java:34, DevPlaidController.java:18, DevUserController.java:30, SimulatedAttestationService.java:30 and DevToolsConfig.java:24 are all @Profile({local,dev,test,ci}); prod is absent.
- DevToolsProdGuard.java:39-66 aborts startup if any dev controller, SimulatedAttestationService, DevToolsConfig, or any class named *DevAuth* / *Simulated* is registered under the prod profile. There is no property that re-enables them.
- Second lock on public api-dev: DevToolsKeyFilter.java:41-52 returns 404 without a matching X-Dev-Tools-Key; DevToolsKeyValidator.java:29-36 uses constant-time compare and an unset key denies everything; base default is empty (application.properties:110).
- Simulated passkey registration cannot work in prod: WebAuthnController.java:163-179 rejects SIMULATED_ATTESTATION when the service bean is absent, and the service also requires the dev-tools key (SimulatedAttestationService.java:57-62).
- No dev-tools key is shipped in any deployable frontend build: devToolsKey is '' in environment.ts:18, environment.dev.ts:10, environment.test.ts:9, environment.prod.ts:9; only environment.ci.ts:13 carries the throwaway CI literal for localhost.
- All four frontend mocks are inert when environment.local is false (portfolio.service.ts:89, monthly-freedom-update.service.ts:103 and :108, goals.service.ts:205, auth.service.ts:233), and environment.local is false in environment.dev.ts:5, environment.test.ts:5, environment.prod.ts:5. A production build does not show mock data.
- angular.json production configuration replaces environment.ts with environment.prod.ts and is the defaultConfiguration for build; main.ts:17-19 enables prod mode from it.
- ?devPage auto-login and ?devKey capture are compiled against !environment.production (app.component.ts:175-182) and need the backend dev key anyway.
- The passkey bypass button renders only when env.local (authfinalize.component.html:63).
- FORCE_FIRST_TIME_TOUR is false (first-time-tour.service.ts:18) and goals mock is off (goals-mock.ts:27-30).
- All mock toggles are forced null under Karma (IN_KARMA checks in each dev/*.ts file), so tests are not affected by toggle state.
- CI-only pieces are isolated to the ci profile: CiSeedConfig.java:26 (seed users facebook@gmail.com, hottie2@yn.con), CiStubConfig.java:37, CiBypassPlaidWebhookVerifier.java:14; BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:191-196 refuses prod if the webhook bypass bean exists.
- BrokerEnvironmentGuard refuses a prod start on sandbox or paper Alpaca URLs, loopback URLs, plaid.env other than production, and the Plaid sandbox secret that was once committed (BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:108-135, 154-165, 201-210). application-prod.properties:23 sets plaid.env=production with all secrets from env vars. Whether that old sandbox secret has actually been rotated at Plaid is an outside step I could not check.
- application-local.properties (which holds real local keys) is gitignored (.gitignore:44) and not in git ls-files. Tracked property files contain only env placeholders, apart from throwaway CI values.
- Dev verification switches default OFF outside dev: market breakdown manual generate (application.properties:52, enabled only in application-dev.properties:72) and TLH manual run (application.properties:83, enabled only in application-dev.properties:75).
- jwt.expiration.ms is back to 3600000 in the working tree (application.properties:19); the 5 minute debug value that is in HEAD has been reverted but not committed.
- Frontend analytics only sends from 'dev' and 'prod' builds (analytics.service.ts:273); local and ci builds send nothing; prod key is empty so it is a clean no-op until a prod PostHog project exists.
- Hibernate is ddl-auto=validate in every profile with Flyway owning the schema (application-*.properties, application.properties:118-121), so no dev auto-DDL can leak into prod.

## prod-config-infra

Production configuration and infrastructure. The backend is in better shape than expected: Flyway owns the schema with ddl-auto=validate in every profile, the prod profile reads every secret from the environment, and two startup guards refuse to boot prod with sandbox brokers, dev tools, loopback databases or the old leaked secrets. The money-moving nightly jobs have database job locks and claim-before-POST idempotency.  What is missing is everything that turns this into a real production environment. There is no deploy config in the repo and no prod backend (environment.prod.ts points at api.fredvested.com, which does not exist). The iOS shell is hard-wired to load the web app from the local Cloudflare tunnel (server.url = https://local.fredvested.com), so no build can be handed to a real user today. The Release entitlements file is malformed and names a different passkey domain than the frontend prod config. Account recovery prints the code to stdout and can be brute-forced. There is only a logging email sender, no push delivery, no rate limiting, no real health check, no error monitoring, and several scheduled jobs that place orders have no lock if two instances ever run at once.  I did not run a production build, boot the prod profile, or call any service, so anything about runtime behaviour under the prod profile is from reading code only.


### [blocker] iOS app is hard-wired to load from the local dev tunnel (Capacitor server.url)

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9 (server.url 'https://local.fredvested.com'); frontend/ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json:5-7 (generated copy, same value); FREDdocs/CLOUDFLARE_SETUP.md:16-26 (tunnel routes that hostname to localhost:8080 and localhost:8100)
- Detail: There is one Capacitor config and it always sets server.url to the Cloudflare tunnel that points at Andy's laptop. Any build installed on a phone (Debug, Test or Release) loads the web app live from that laptop instead of from the bundled www folder. If the laptop or tunnel is down the app is a blank screen. It also means the Angular 'production' build (environment.prod.ts) is never what a device actually runs, so the prod bundle has had no device testing. Apple also tends to reject apps that are just a remote web view.
- Suggested fix: Make the Capacitor config environment aware (for example only set server.url when an env var such as CAP_SERVER_URL is present), so a release build ships the bundled www with no server block. Add an npm script that does ng build --configuration production then cap sync ios. Then run a full device pass on the bundled build, since origin changes to capacitor://localhost and several things (passkeys, CORS, cache behaviour, live reload quirks) behave differently.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9 sets server.url to https://local.fredvested.com with no environment check, and the generated frontend/ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json:5-7 carries the same value. I looked for anything that would strip it for Release and found none: project.pbxproj has only the two CocoaPods shell script phases (lines 217, 232), and frontend/package.json:6-16 has no build-and-sync script. One detail to add: the generated JSON is gitignored (frontend/ios/.gitignore:12), so the fix belongs in capacitor.config.ts only, then cap sync. The Apple rejection remark is opinion, not something the repo proves, but the blocker stands without it.

### [blocker] Release entitlements are malformed and name a different passkey domain than the frontend prod config

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App/Release.entitlements:5-6 (associated-domains is a <string>, value webcredentials:api.fredvested.com); Debug.entitlements:5-8 and Test.entitlements:5-8 (correct <array> form); frontend/src/environments/environment.prod.ts:8 (rpId 'app.fredvested.com'); backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:31-33 (RP id and origins come from WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD / WEBAUTHN_RP_ORIGINS_PROD); backend/.../controller/AASAController.java:21-38 (AASA is served by the backend host); frontend/ios/App/App.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:413 (Release uses Release.entitlements)
- Detail: Passkeys are the only login. In the Release build the associated-domains entitlement is a single string, but Apple requires an array, so signing or passkey association will fail. On top of that the three places that must agree do not: Release entitlement says api.fredvested.com, environment.prod.ts says app.fredvested.com, and the backend takes the RP id from an env var with no documented value. The apple-app-site-association file is served by the backend controller, so the RP id has to be a host that serves that file. The environment.rpId field does not appear to be read anywhere in frontend/src/app (the RP id comes from the server challenge), so the frontend value is misleading rather than active. Release.entitlements also lists keychain group com.fredvested.app while the bundle id is com.fredvested.Fred (line 9 vs project.pbxproj:423); I did not confirm whether that group is usable.
- Suggested fix: Decide the one production RP id (it cannot change later without invalidating every passkey). Fix Release.entitlements to an array with webcredentials:<that host>, set WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD and WEBAUTHN_RP_ORIGINS_PROD to match, make sure that host serves /.well-known/apple-app-site-association, and correct or delete rpId in environment.prod.ts. Check the keychain group against the bundle id.
- Verifier (confirmed): Facts check out. Release.entitlements:5-6 has associated-domains as a <string> (webcredentials:api.fredvested.com) while Debug and Test use the <array> form; project.pbxproj:413 wires Release to that file; environment.prod.ts:8 says rpId app.fredvested.com; application-prod.properties:31-33 takes the RP id from env vars. I confirmed environment.rpId is read nowhere in frontend/src (the only rpId uses are publicKey.rpId from the server challenge at auth.service.ts:567 and passkey-prompt.component.ts:270), so the frontend value is dead and misleading. Two corrections. (1) I could not prove from the repo exactly how the string form fails (signing error versus the domain being silently ignored), so 'signing will fail' is unverified; either way passkeys would not associate in a Release build, so blocker holds. (2) The keychain group is probably a non-issue: KeychainSyncPlugin.swift never sets kSecAttrAccessGroup (it uses kSecAttrSynchronizable only, lines 86-119), so nothing depends on the com.fredvested.app group. It is stale and should be cleaned up, but it is not part of the blocker. Also note AASAController.java:25 hardcodes 9G59L97KRT.com.fredvested.Fred, which matches the bundle id and team, so that side is fine once the host is chosen.

### [blocker] No production backend exists and the repo has no deploy definition for one

- Type: external, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/environments/environment.prod.ts:7 (backendApiUrl https://api.fredvested.com/api); backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:1-33; no Dockerfile, railway.json, railway.toml, Procfile or nixpacks file anywhere in the repo (find at depth 3 returned only .env.local); backend/build.gradle:1-64 (no actuator, version 0.0.1-SNAPSHOT)
- Detail: The production frontend config points at api.fredvested.com, which is not stood up. Nothing in the repo describes how the backend is built or started on Railway (start command, Java version, health check path, replica count, profile), so the dev service's settings live only in the Railway dashboard. Standing up prod needs: a new Railway service and MySQL database, SPRING_PROFILES_ACTIVE=prod, DNS for the API host, live Alpaca Broker credentials, production Plaid credentials and webhook URL, and every env var listed in the next finding.
- Suggested fix: Create the prod Railway service and database, and commit a small deploy definition (railway.json or Dockerfile) with the start command, health check path and a single replica, so prod is reproducible and not dashboard-only. Write a one-page prod runbook in FREDdocs listing the env vars and first-boot steps.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. A find at depth 4 for Dockerfile, railway.*, Procfile, nixpacks.* and system.properties returned nothing outside node_modules; the only tracked infra is /infra/posthog and /infra/posthog-proxy plus .github/workflows/verify.yml and auto-pr.yml (CI, not deploy). build.gradle:1-64 has no actuator and version 0.0.1-SNAPSHOT. environment.prod.ts:7 points at api.fredvested.com. Type is mixed rather than purely external: creating the Railway service, DNS and live Alpaca/Plaid credentials are outside work, but the deploy definition and runbook are app work in this repo. Java 17 is at least pinned via the Gradle toolchain (build.gradle:10-14).

### [blocker] Account recovery code is printed to stdout, never emailed, and verification has no attempt limit

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/.../controller/RecoveryController.java:46-56 (6 digit code, System.out.println of email and code), 61-98 (verify has no attempt counter, wipes passkeys and issues a JWT on success); backend/.../config/SecurityConfig.java:113 (/api/auth/** is permitAll); FREDdocs/backlog.md:390-392 (already tracked as FRED-219)
- Detail: The recovery code goes to the server log in every profile, including prod, and is never sent to the user, so no real user can recover an account. The verify endpoint is unauthenticated and has no lockout, so anyone who knows an email can request a code and guess the 6 digits within the 15 minute window. A correct guess deletes the victim's passkeys and returns a valid login token. With brokerage accounts behind it this is an account takeover path. Already in the backlog as FRED-219 but not fixed in the working tree.
- Suggested fix: Send the code through EmailService with a real provider, remove the println, add a per-user attempt counter with lockout (the PinController pattern at lines 29-89 is a ready model), and throttle /initiate per email and per IP.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed by reading RecoveryController.java in the working tree (no uncommitted diff on it). Lines 52-56 System.out.println the email and the code with no profile gate; EmailService is not injected at all (constructor lines 29-33). Verify (61-98) has no attempt counter, does not clear the code on a wrong guess, wipes passkeys (line 91) and returns a JWT (96-98). SecurityConfig permits /api/auth/** and a backend-wide grep for rate limiting found only PinController. Extra points the auditor missed: (1) @CrossOrigin(origins = "*") on the controller (line 20); (2) the 1 second sleep runs only for unknown emails (line 41), so response timing reveals whether an account exists, defeating the enumeration comment; (3) /initiate can be called repeatedly, each call resets the code and the 15 minute window, so the guessing window is effectively unlimited; (4) FredKnowledge.java:369 tells users in chat that the recovery endpoint is 'strictly rate-limited', which is false today. Tracked as FRED-219 in FREDdocs/backlog.md:390-392, rated HIGH there; blocker is the right call for real money.

### [blocker] No real email provider: every email in the app is mocked, not just the market breakdown

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/.../service/LoggingEmailSender.java:13-25 (the only EmailSender implementation); backend/.../service/EmailService.java:56-62 (enabled=false records MOCKED and returns); application.properties:47-49 (EMAIL_ENABLED false, from noreply@investingapp.com, provider logging); callers: service/PaydayNotificationService.java:96, service/InvestmentExecutionService.java (failure notices, e.g. 1216), service/AccountStatusService.java, scheduler/MarketBreakdownScheduler.java
- Detail: There is one EmailSender and it only logs. That covers transfer and trade failure notices, account status emails and payday emails, as well as the known market breakdown. With real money moving, a user whose ACH or order fails would get no message. The default from address is noreply@investingapp.com, not a fredvested.com address. Choosing the provider is a founder decision (backlog line 185 mentions Resend or SendGrid), the adapter is app work.
- Suggested fix: Pick the provider, add one EmailSender implementation behind app.email.provider, set EMAIL_FROM to a verified fredvested.com sender with SPF/DKIM, and turn EMAIL_ENABLED on in prod only after a test send.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts confirmed: LoggingEmailSender.java:15 is the only EmailSender implementation, EmailService.java:56-62 records MOCKED and returns when disabled, and application.properties:47-49 defaults to disabled, from noreply@investingapp.com, provider logging. There are 19 emailService call sites outside EmailService. I am raising severity because the blocker recovery fix depends on it: passkeys are the only login and recovery is email OTP, so with no real sender a beta user who loses a passkey is locked out of a funded brokerage account. One softening fact for the failure-notice part: payday notices also go out as push (PaydayNotificationService.java:68, 84, 99, sendPushIfTokenPresent), so users are not entirely without messages, but I did not confirm that transfer or trade failure notices have a push path. Provider choice is a founder decision; the adapter is app work.

### [high] Several order-placing scheduled jobs have no job lock or idempotency key, so two instances would double execute

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/.../scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:45-63 (processScheduledInvestments takes a lock) versus 70-80 checkFundingStatus, 87-96 checkTradingStatus, 105-115 processDripDividends, 121-131, 147-181 payday notifications (no lock); backend/.../service/JobLocks.java:6-9 (only four lock names); service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:536-552 and 909-912 (funding check places buy orders through placeOrderWithFractionalCheck); service/AlpacaService.java:295 (placeBuyOrder has no client_order_id, unlike the TLH variants at 553-600); service/DripService.java:119-143 (check-then-insert dedupe, backed by the unique key at db/migration/V1__baseline.sql:106)
- Detail: Only four jobs use the job_locks table: nightly scheduling, the end of day ACH batch, the ACH relationship poll and Plaid item removals. The funding status check is the job that turns a settled transfer into buy orders, and it has no lock and sends orders without a client_order_id. If two backend instances run at the same time (a second replica, or the overlap during a Railway deploy) both can read the same FUNDING_INITIATED rows and both place the buys. DRIP is partly protected by the unique activity id. Payday emails and pushes have no dedupe that I could find. TLH is protected by its own claim constraint (TlhHarvestService.java:40). I did not trace every status transition, so the exact double-order window for the funding check is my reading of the code, not a reproduced bug.
- Suggested fix: Short term: pin the prod service to exactly one replica and say so in the deploy config. Proper fix: wrap checkFundingStatus, checkTradingStatus, the DRIP jobs and the payday notification jobs in JobLockService.tryAcquire, and pass a deterministic client_order_id (execution id plus symbol) on scheduled buys so Alpaca rejects a duplicate.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. InvestmentScheduler.java:70-80, 87-96, 105-115, 121-131 and 147-181 run without JobLockService; only four lock names exist (JobLocks.java:6-9) and tryAcquire is used at exactly four sites. checkFundingStatus (InvestmentExecutionService.java:536-555) reads FUNDING_INITIATED rows and the only guard is an in-memory status re-check (around line 598, 'Double check status...') inside one class-level transaction; there is no @Version or row lock on executions (grep found @Lock only in UserRepository.java:81, 90), so two instances can both pass it. placeOrderWithFractionalCheck (1223-1228) calls AlpacaService.placeBuyOrder (295) which sends no client_order_id, unlike the TLH variants (556-640). Payday emails use the legacy sendEmail path with no hasBeenSent check (PaydayNotificationService.java:67, 81, 96). Severity caveat: this only bites with two live instances, meaning a second replica or the overlap window of a deploy that lands on a cron tick, so with one pinned replica the likelihood is low; the outcome is a duplicated real buy, so high is fair. Already tracked as FRED-223 (backlog.md:406-408), rated MEDIUM there, and that entry is partly stale since locks and the batch claim now exist for the nightly paths. I did not reproduce a double order.

### [high] No rate limiting on public endpoints or on the AI chat

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/.../config/SecurityConfig.java:112-119 (/api/auth/**, /api/passkey/**, /api/user/should-prompt-reauth, /hello all permitAll); grep for RateLimit, bucket4j, 429 and attempts across controller, security and config found only PinController.java:29-89 and a chat action audit at service/FredChatActionService.java:82; backend/build.gradle:26-56 (no rate limiting library)
- Detail: The only throttles in the backend are the PIN lockout and a limit on chat action cards. Passkey registration and login start, recovery initiate and verify, and token refresh are open to unlimited calls. The Ask FRED chat calls a paid Claude model per message, and I found no per-user message cap in ChatController or the chat services (grep only, so a limit enforced another way could have been missed). api-dev is already on the public internet, and prod will be too.
- Suggested fix: Add a simple per-IP and per-account limiter (a servlet filter backed by Caffeine, which is already a dependency, or Cloudflare rate limiting rules in front of the API host) on /api/auth/**, /api/passkey/** and the chat endpoint, plus a daily chat message cap per user.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. SecurityConfig permits /api/auth/**, /api/passkey/**, /api/user/should-prompt-reauth and /hello without auth. A case-insensitive grep across the backend for ratelimit, bucket4j, 429, throttle, quota and similar found only PinController.java:57, 86 and the per-minute action cap in FredChatActionService.java:80-85. ChatController.java:37-60 passes POST /api/chat and GET /api/chat/stream straight to ChatService with no cap, and build.gradle has no limiter library. Mitigation the auditor did not state: chat requires a valid JWT (it falls under anyRequest().authenticated()), so chat cost abuse is limited to signed-in users, which in a private beta is a small known group. The unauthenticated part overlaps the recovery finding, which is where the real danger is. High for public launch is right; for private beta the chat cap alone would be medium. I did not read ChatService or LLMService line by line, so a limit hidden there is unlikely but not fully excluded.

### [high] No real health check, no error monitoring, no log configuration

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/.../controller/HealthCheckController.java:11-14 (/hello returns a fixed string, no DB check); backend/build.gradle:26-56 (no spring-boot-starter-actuator, no Sentry); no logback xml in backend/src/main/resources; no logging.* or management.* keys in any application*.properties; frontend/package.json and frontend/src/main.ts contain no Sentry; alert style log lines with no consumer, e.g. service/JobLockService.java:56 (alert.job_lock.unavailable) and service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:320 (alert.batch_claim_short)
- Detail: The only liveness signal is /hello, which answers even when the database is down. There is no actuator, so no readiness probe for Railway to gate a deploy on. The code writes lines such as alert.batch_claim_short and TLH_ALERT that are meant to page someone, but nothing collects or alerts on them. Neither the backend nor the app reports crashes anywhere. With real money, a failed nightly batch would only be found by reading Railway logs by hand.
- Suggested fix: Add actuator with only health exposed (include the db indicator) and point the Railway health check at it. Add Sentry or similar to backend and frontend with PII scrubbing, and an alert rule on log lines starting with alert. or containing TLH_ALERT.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. HealthCheckController.java returns a fixed string from /hello with no dependency check. grep for sentry, actuator and management. across build.gradle, every application*.properties and frontend/package.json returned nothing relevant; there is no logback file in backend/src/main/resources (only the properties files, db and templates); frontend/src/main.ts has no Sentry or captureException and no class in frontend/src/app implements ErrorHandler. The alert-style log lines exist with no consumer in the repo (InvestmentExecutionService.java:320 alert.batch_claim_short; TLH_ALERT around line 901). Missing log config is the weakest part: Spring Boot's default console logging is workable on Railway, so that piece is low on its own. The health check and alerting gaps carry the high rating, and for a beta with real money the lack of any alert on a failed nightly batch is close to blocker; I would do it before the first real deposit.

### [high] App Store privacy manifest does not yet declare KYC and banking data

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy:11-16 (the file's own comment says name, date of birth, SSN, address, email and bank details 'MUST be declared before submission'), 39-70 (only product interaction and the analytics user id are declared)
- Detail: The manifest covers analytics only. The data collected by the KYC and bank linking flows is not declared, and the file itself says so. An incomplete manifest and a mismatched App Store privacy label are common rejection reasons. FREDdocs/privacy-manifest-and-label.md is named as the list of what is owed; I did not read that document in full.
- Suggested fix: Add the collected data types for KYC and banking (linked to the user, purpose app functionality) to the manifest and fill in the matching App Store privacy label.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. frontend/ios/App/App/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy:11-16 states in its own comment that name, date of birth, SSN, address, email and bank details are not declared and must be before submission; lines 39-70 declare only ProductInteraction and UserID, both for analytics and both unlinked. FREDdocs/privacy-manifest-and-label.md exists and line 75 says the manifest and label still have to declare that data with purpose app functionality. The file also flags (lines 25-28) that the manifest must be revisited when billing lands, which ties into the subscription work. High is right: it gates App Store submission, not a private TestFlight beta. The matching App Store Connect label is outside the repo.

### [medium] Prod profile is missing the Alpaca block and its env var checklist is incomplete

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:1-6 (header lists DB, JWT, encryption, WebAuthn, Plaid only) and 7-33 (no alpaca.*, no email, no analytics keys); application-dev.properties:59-65 (dev maps alpaca.* from env explicitly); application.properties:61-68 (ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY, PINECONE_API_KEY, PINECONE_INDEX_NAME have no defaults); backend/.../config/BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:62-63,107-132 (missing base URLs fall back to sandbox defaults, which prod then refuses)
- Detail: The prod properties file never sets alpaca.api.key, alpaca.api.secret, alpaca.market.key, alpaca.market.secret or the three base URLs. They can still be supplied through Spring's relaxed env binding (for example ALPACA_BROKER_BASE_URL), but those names differ from the ones dev uses (ALPACA_API_BASE_URL, ALPACA_API_TRADING_URL), and nothing documents them. With the base URLs unset the guard sees the sandbox defaults and refuses to start, which is safe but will be a confusing first boot. The header comment also omits ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY, PINECONE_API_KEY, PINECONE_INDEX_NAME, EMAIL_*, POSTHOG_* and TLH_MODE. I did not boot the prod profile, so this is from reading the files.
- Suggested fix: Add an explicit Alpaca block to application-prod.properties using the same env var names as dev, and update the header to the full list of required variables. Consider a prod boot test (context test with the prod profile and dummy live-looking values) so a missing variable is caught in CI.
- Verifier (adjusted): The facts are right but the severity is high for what is a fail-closed documentation gap. application-prod.properties:1-33 has no alpaca.* keys, and the header (lines 1-6) omits Alpaca, ANTHROPIC_API_KEY, OPENAI_API_KEY, PINECONE_* (no defaults at application.properties:61-68). What actually happens: alpaca.api.key and alpaca.api.secret would resolve from ALPACA_API_KEY / ALPACA_API_SECRET through Spring's env mapping even with no properties line, so those two names happen to match dev. The ones that do not match dev are alpaca.market.key / alpaca.market.secret (dev uses ALPACA_MARKET_DATA_KEY / _SECRET, application-dev.properties:61-62; PortfolioDashboardService.java:69-72 has no default, so boot fails on an unresolved placeholder) and the base URLs (dev uses ALPACA_API_BASE_URL / ALPACA_API_TRADING_URL; unset in prod they fall to the sandbox defaults and BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:107-132 refuses to start). Every path fails loudly at boot, none silently trades against the wrong host, and the fix is a few lines. EMAIL_*, POSTHOG_* and TLH_MODE all have safe defaults (application.properties:33-49, 82), so they are not required for boot. There is a ProdPlaidPropertiesTest but it does not cover Alpaca keys (grep found no alpaca reference in it), so a prod boot test is still a fair suggestion. Not booted; from reading only.

### [medium] Emails and tokens are written to logs

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/.../security/jwt/JwtUtils.java:226-234 (logs the full JWT on every validation failure, including expired ones); service/WebAuthnService.java:83-115, 403-530 (email on every registration and login); service/InvestmentScheduleService.java:178-442 and service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:195-327, 750-765 (email per user per job); service/DripService.java:111 (email); controller/AASAController.java:23; frontend/src/app/components/linkplaid/linkplaid.component.ts:119,188 and change-bank-account/change-bank-account.page.ts:152,253 (console.log of the Plaid public token and link token response); investmentconfirmation.component.ts:357 (email); 179 console.log calls in frontend/src/app outside specs
- Detail: User email is the routine identifier in backend logs (about 80 logger lines matched email or similar patterns), and rejected JWTs are logged whole; an expired JWT still carries the email and public id claims. Once logs are drained to a third party this becomes PII held by another vendor. On the device, Plaid tokens and the user's email go to the web view console. The prod Angular build does not strip console.log. No SSN or bank number logging was found by grep.
- Suggested fix: Log user id or public id in place of email, log only the failure reason for bad JWTs, and remove or guard the frontend console.log calls that print tokens or email (a build-time drop of console.log in the production configuration is the quick version).

### [medium] CORS: dev origins are hard-coded for every profile and 20 controllers carry @CrossOrigin(origins = "*")

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/.../config/SecurityConfig.java:77-94 (localhost ports, a github.dev codespace URL and https://local.fredvested.com allowed with allowCredentials true, in all profiles); @CrossOrigin(origins = "*") at controller/AuthController.java:45, UserController.java:24, WebAuthnController.java:42, TradingController.java:35, PlaidController.java:28, InvestmentController.java:33, RecoveryController.java:20 and 13 more
- Detail: The global allow list is the same in prod as in local, so a prod backend would accept credentialed browser calls from localhost pages, a personal Codespace URL and the dev tunnel. The per-controller wildcard annotations say 'restrict in production' in their own comments (PlaidController.java:28). In practice the Spring Security CORS filter with the global list should run first, so the wildcards are probably not effective, but I did not test that and they are misleading either way. The bundled iOS app's origin, capacitor://localhost, is already on the list, so prod needs nothing extra.
- Suggested fix: Make the allowed origins a per-profile property (prod: capacitor://localhost only, plus any real web origin) and delete the @CrossOrigin annotations from all controllers.

### [medium] Secrets: old prod secrets in git history still need rotating, and weak keys only warn at startup

- Type: external, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: FREDdocs/backlog.md:394-396 (FRED-220); backend/.../config/BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:71-89, 205-241 (prod refuses the once-committed Plaid sandbox secret and dev JWT secret by digest); FREDdocs/plaid-sandbox-secret-rotation.md (tracked); backend/.../service/EncryptionService.java:45-62 (a 16 byte AES key only logs a warning outside local); backend/.../security/jwt/JwtUtils.java:42-56 (short secret only warns, and a hard-coded fallback key exists at line 47); application-ci.properties:31,72 (throwaway CI literals)
- Detail: application-prod.properties no longer contains literal secrets, and the guard blocks the two known leaked values, so the code side of FRED-220 is largely done. What remains is outside the repo: rotating the Plaid sandbox secret, the old datasource password and any other credential that was ever committed. Separately, prod will start with a 16 byte encryption key or a short JWT secret and only log a warning. The encryption key protects Alpaca account ids, Plaid access tokens and the SSN hash path, and it cannot be changed later without re-encrypting data, so the first prod value matters. I confirmed application-local.properties and .env.local are gitignored and have never been committed on any branch (git log --all returns nothing for either).
- Suggested fix: Rotate the historic credentials. Extend BrokerEnvironmentGuard so prod refuses an encryption key under 32 bytes and a JWT secret under 64 bytes, and remove the JWT fallback key. Generate fresh prod-only values for JWT_SECRET_PROD and ENCRYPTION_SECRET_KEY and store the encryption key somewhere recoverable.

### [medium] Push notifications are not wired end to end

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/services/push-notification.service.ts:22-25 (requests permission and registers); frontend/ios/App/App/Release.entitlements:1-12 and Debug.entitlements:1-10 (no aps-environment key); frontend/ios/App/App/AppDelegate.swift (no didRegisterForRemoteNotifications handler found by grep); backend/.../service/PaydayNotificationService.java:104-111 (logs '[PUSH] Would send', comment says FCM is not set up)
- Detail: The app asks the user for notification permission, but the iOS target has no push entitlement and the AppDelegate does not forward the APNs token to Capacitor, so no device token can reach the backend. The backend then only logs what it would have sent. Users would see a permission prompt for a feature that does nothing, which is also an App Review risk.
- Suggested fix: Either finish it (push capability and aps-environment entitlement, AppDelegate token callbacks, an APNs or FCM sender in the backend) or stop requesting permission until it is real.

### [medium] Production database setup: connection settings, time zone and backups are undefined

- Type: external, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:7-9 (whole JDBC URL comes from DATASOURCE_URL_PROD); application-dev.properties:8 (dev URL uses useSSL=true and serverTimezone=UTC); backend/.../BackendApplication.java:15-18 (JVM default zone pinned to America/New_York); application.properties:10-17 (Hikari pool 10, connection-timeout 60s 'to allow sleeping DBs to wake up'); no backup or restore document in FREDdocs (grep for backup found only backlog and review notes)
- Detail: The prod JDBC URL is free text, so TLS and the time zone parameter depend on whoever types it. The app pins the JVM to Eastern time while the dev URL declares the server as UTC; prod must use the same combination as dev or stored timestamps will mean something different between environments (I did not verify how each column type is converted, so treat this as a thing to test, not a confirmed bug). The 60 second connection timeout exists for a sleeping dev database and would make a real outage look like slow requests. Nothing documents backups, point in time recovery or a restore drill for the database that will hold account, schedule and execution records.
- Suggested fix: Document the exact prod JDBC URL template (same parameters as dev, TLS required), turn on Railway backups or scheduled dumps and do one restore test, make sure the prod database plan does not sleep, and lower the connection timeout for prod.

### [low] Production analytics is switched off by empty keys

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/environments/environment.prod.ts:10-12 (posthogKey empty 'until a production PostHog project exists'); backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:33-45 (publisher disabled by default, key and personal API key empty by default)
- Detail: A prod build sends no client analytics, and the backend outbox will fill but not publish, until a production PostHog project exists and its keys are set. This is deliberate and safe, but it means the first beta users produce no funnel data unless this is done before launch. The person deletion path also needs POSTHOG_PERSONAL_API_KEY and POSTHOG_PROJECT_ID or deletion requests stay pending.
- Suggested fix: Create the prod PostHog project, put its public key in environment.prod.ts, and set ANALYTICS_PUBLISHER_ENABLED, POSTHOG_PROJECT_KEY, POSTHOG_PERSONAL_API_KEY and POSTHOG_PROJECT_ID on the prod service.

### [low] Uncommitted change raises JWT lifetime from 5 minutes to 1 hour

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: git diff -- backend/src/main/resources/application.properties (HEAD has jwt.expiration.ms=300000 with the 3600000 line commented out; the working tree has jwt.expiration.ms=3600000 at application.properties:19)
- Detail: The only uncommitted backend change is the token lifetime going from 5 minutes to 1 hour for every profile. It may be a deliberate choice or a leftover from testing. It should be decided on purpose because it sets how long a stolen token works.
- Suggested fix: Confirm the intended production value and commit it, or set it per profile.

### [low] Small server hardening gaps: proxy headers, graceful shutdown, open-in-view, error text

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: no server.forward-headers-strategy, server.shutdown, spring.jpa.open-in-view, logging.* or management.* key in any backend/src/main/resources/application*.properties (grep returned none); controller/PortfolioDashboardController.java:61, MonthlyFreedomUpdateController.java:109, InvestmentScheduleController.java:84,163 (exception messages returned to the client); backend/.../config/SchedulingConfig.java:21-22 (scheduler waits 60s on shutdown)
- Detail: Behind Railway's proxy the app sees plain http, so Spring Security will not add HSTS and any redirect or URL building uses the wrong scheme unless forwarded headers are trusted. The web server does not shut down gracefully, so a deploy can cut in-flight requests even though the scheduler waits. open-in-view is left at its default (on), which the project memory already records as having caused a locking lesson. A few controllers return raw exception messages. None of these stop a beta.
- Suggested fix: Set server.forward-headers-strategy=framework, server.shutdown=graceful and spring.jpa.open-in-view=false (then test), and replace raw exception text in responses with fixed messages.

### [low] Stale and misleading config files

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json (root-level second iOS project, appId com.andrewtimmer.investingapp, appName InvestingApp); frontend/ios/App/'KeychainSyncPlugin 2.swift' and 'NativePasskeyPlugin 2.swift' (tracked duplicates); backend/database-migration-reminder.md (tells the reader to use ddl-auto=create-drop then update, which contradicts Flyway); backend/src/main/resources/application-test.properties:1-3,49 (says deployed from main, sets alpaca.api.base-url which no code reads); frontend/src/environments/environment.dev.ts:8 (rpId is a github.dev codespace host); frontend/ios/App/App.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:416,422 (version 1.0, build 1); frontend/ios/App/App/Info.plist:31-34 (armv7 capability, no ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption key)
- Detail: There is an old second iOS project at the repo root with a different bundle id, duplicate Swift files with ' 2' in the name, and a migration reminder that predates Flyway and would be harmful if followed. The test profile looks unused and half configured. None of these break anything today, but they are traps for whoever sets up prod or the release build.

**Verified solid:**

- Schema is owned by Flyway in every profile: spring.flyway.enabled=true with baseline-on-migrate at version 1 (application.properties:118-121) and ddl-auto=validate in prod, dev, local, ci and test (application-prod.properties:10, application-dev.properties:12, application-local.properties:4, application-ci.properties:20, application-test.properties:18). Migrations V1 to V9 plus repeatable views exist in backend/src/main/resources/db/migration. No uncommitted migration files in the working tree.
- application-prod.properties contains no literal secrets: datasource, JWT, Plaid, encryption key and WebAuthn values all come from environment variables (lines 7-33).
- BrokerEnvironmentGuard (config/BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:100-152) refuses to start when prod points at sandbox or paper Alpaca hosts, when non-prod points at live hosts, when plaid.env is wrong for the profile, when the prod Plaid webhook is not public https, when the CI webhook bypass bean is present, when the datasource is loopback, or when the once-committed Plaid sandbox secret or dev JWT secret is used.
- Dev tooling cannot run in prod: dev controllers and SimulatedAttestationService are @Profile local/dev/test/ci, DevToolsProdGuard (config/DevToolsProdGuard.java:39-66) aborts startup if any are registered under prod, and /api/dev/** also needs the X-Dev-Tools-Key header with an empty default key that denies everything (application.properties:105-110).
- application-local.properties and .env.local are gitignored (.gitignore:44 and :27) and have never been committed on any branch (git log --all is empty for both).
- The nightly money path is safe against double runs: processScheduledInvestments and the end of day batch take database job locks (InvestmentScheduler.java:48, InvestmentExecutionService.java:285), the ACH batch claims rows in a committed transaction before the Alpaca POST (InvestmentExecutionService.java:314-320, 385-390), and JobLockService uses a UTC lease with takeover after expiry (JobLockService.java:47-59).
- Tax loss harvesting is off by default (app.tlh.mode=off, application.properties:82), is idempotent through a unique claim constraint and client_order_id on its orders (TlhHarvestService.java:40, AlpacaService.java:553-600).
- DRIP dedupe has a database unique key on alpaca_activity_id (V1__baseline.sql:106) behind the existsBy check (DripService.java:120).
- Time zones are handled deliberately: the JVM default is pinned to America/New_York (BackendApplication.java:17-18), money crons name the zone explicitly (InvestmentScheduler.java:45-171), and job lock leases use UTC.
- Spring Data REST auto exposure of repositories is disabled (BackendApplication.java:10 excludes RepositoryRestMvcAutoConfiguration), and no actuator endpoints are exposed because actuator is not a dependency (build.gradle:26-56).
- Security filter chain is stateless with JWT, CSRF disabled appropriately for a token API, and everything outside a short permitAll list requires authentication (SecurityConfig.java:103-123).
- Hikari pool is sized with the scheduler in mind (max 10, max-lifetime 10 min, idle 5 min; application.properties:10-17).
- Frontend build configs are correct: the production configuration swaps in environment.prod.ts with output hashing and budgets (angular.json build.configurations.production), and no frontend source file outside environments hard-codes a local, dev or tunnel host (grep of frontend/src).
- Frontend dev mocks (services/dev/tier-mock, mfu-mock, portfolio-mock, goals-mock) are gated on environment.local, which is false in environment.prod.ts, and the ?devPage login shortcut is gated on !environment.production (app.component.ts:165-180).
- environment.prod.ts ships no dev tools key and no PostHog key (lines 9-12); CI builds carry no PostHog key either.
- iOS Info.plist has no App Transport Security exceptions and has the Face ID usage string (Info.plist:46-47). Debug and Test entitlements are well formed.
- The AASA endpoint is served by the backend without auth with the correct team and bundle id (AASAController.java:21-38, SecurityConfig.java:112), matching the bundle id in project.pbxproj:423.
- The analytics proxy config (infra/posthog-proxy/wrangler.toml) only routes m.fredvested.com and keeps Worker logging off; nothing in this repo touches the landing page or an lpapi host.
- CI (.github/workflows/verify.yml) runs the backend test suite against a MySQL service container and boots the app under the offline ci profile on every PR to develop.

## security-auth

Security and authentication audit of the working tree (backend security package, all 20 controllers, WebAuthn/JWT/PIN/recovery, Plaid webhook, frontend token storage and iOS config). The core design is sound: passkey login works through the Yubico library, almost every endpoint binds to the authenticated principal, the Plaid webhook is properly signature-verified, dev tools are profile-gated plus key-gated with a prod startup guard, and sensitive user columns use AES-GCM. But there are real blockers before real users and money: (1) three Alpaca endpoints let any logged-in user list every brokerage account at the firm and read any account or its ACH relationships by id; (2) account recovery never sends an email (the OTP is printed to stdout) and the OTP check has no attempt limit, so a 6-digit code can be brute-forced into a full account takeover; (3) beneficiary endpoints have a cross-user write hole and store third-party SSNs in plaintext; (4) the iOS shell still loads the app from the local dev tunnel. Beyond that: there is no rate limiting anywhere, session revocation stops working after the first token refresh, email can be changed with no re-auth or verification, tier is client-writable, step-up auth for withdrawals exists only in the frontend, and the prod passkey domain config (rpId vs entitlements) does not line up. I did not run the app; everything is from reading code. Items I could not fully confirm are marked with lower confidence.


### [blocker] Any logged-in user can list every brokerage account and read any account or its bank links by id

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AlpacaController.java:50-60 (GET /api/alpaca/account), :225-235 (GET /accounts/{accountId}/ach-relationships), :237-247 (GET /account/{accountId}/status); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AlpacaApiService.java:76-84 (calls broker GET /accounts with firm credentials), :559-569, :623-631; frontend/src/app/services/alpaca.service.ts:133,158,166
- Detail: GET /api/alpaca/account calls the Alpaca Broker API GET /accounts with FRED's firm-level key and returns the raw body. That is the list of all customer accounts, not the caller's. The two {accountId} endpoints pass the path variable straight to Alpaca with no check that the id belongs to the caller, so the ids from the list can be used to pull any customer's full account object and ACH relationships. Only a valid JWT is required, and anyone can get one by registering.
- Suggested fix: Delete GET /api/alpaca/account (or scope it to the caller's own decrypted alpacaAccountId). Remove the accountId path variable from the other two and resolve the account from the authenticated user, as /account/kyc already does. Update the three frontend callers. Return a trimmed DTO, not the raw broker body.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. AlpacaController.java:50-60 returns alpacaApiService.getAccountInfo(), which is a firm-credential GET {broker}/accounts (AlpacaApiService.java:76-84). The two {accountId} routes (AlpacaController.java:225-247) pass the path variable straight through with no ownership check (AlpacaApiService.java:559-569, 623-631). The controller has no class-level guard or profile gate (lines 25-28); SecurityConfig only requires a JWT. Extra detail: no page or component in frontend/src/app calls the three matching methods in alpaca.service.ts (only the service file defines them), so the endpoints look removable with no UI impact.

### [blocker] Account recovery OTP can be brute-forced: no attempt limit, no rate limit, and success gives a full-access token

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:35-59 (initiate), :61-99 (verify, no counter), :46 (6-digit code, 15 min), :91 (clears all passkeys), :96 (issues normal JWT); SecurityConfig.java:113 (/api/auth/** permitAll); FREDdocs/AUTHENTICATION_AND_JWT.md:143-157 (doc promises Persona face match, rate limiting, alert email)
- Detail: POST /api/auth/recovery/verify compares the code with no failed-attempt counter and no lockout, and nothing in the backend rate limits requests (no bucket4j/resilience4j, grep found none). A 6-digit code valid for 15 minutes can be guessed by scripting up to 1,000,000 requests, and initiate can be re-called to keep a code live. On success the endpoint deletes the victim's passkeys and returns a normal JWT that works on every endpoint (withdraw, liquidate, close account, change email), not a token limited to re-registering a passkey. Also: a missing user sleeps 1s while a real user does not, and verify returns 'Invalid request' vs 'Invalid or expired code', so emails can be enumerated. The design doc describes a Persona biometric check, rate limiting and a security alert email; none of that exists in code.
- Suggested fix: Add per-account attempt counting (for example 5 tries then invalidate the code and lock for a period), per-IP and per-email rate limits on initiate and verify, constant-time compare, identical responses and timing for unknown emails, single-use codes. Issue a short-lived recovery-scoped token that only /api/passkey/register/recovery/* accepts. Send an alert email on initiate and on success. Decide whether the Persona step in the doc is required for launch.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. RecoveryController.java:61-99 has no attempt counter or lockout, compares with String.equals, and line 96 issues a normal JWT with no scope after clearing passkeys (line 91). /api/auth/** is permitAll (SecurityConfig.java:113).
