A grep of the backend source for ratelimit, bucket4j, resilience4j and throttle returned no files at all. Enumeration differences are real (1s sleep only for unknown email at line 41; 'Invalid request' vs 'Invalid or expired code' at lines 66 and 70). Minor addition: nextInt(999999) at line 46 can never produce 999999. The Persona, rate limit and alert email claims in FREDdocs/AUTHENTICATION_AND_JWT.md:143-157 are not in code.

### [blocker] Recovery email is never sent: the OTP is printed to stdout

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:51-56 (System.out.println of the code under 'MOCK EMAIL'); application.properties:47-49 (app.email.enabled=false, provider=logging)
- Detail: A real user who loses their device cannot recover: the code only goes to the server log. It also means every recovery code sits in plaintext in Railway logs for anyone with log access. This is separate from the known mocked market-breakdown email; this is the only account recovery path.
- Suggested fix: Route the code through the EmailSender port with a real provider and a recovery template; remove the println. Needs the email provider decision (Resend is named in the analytics plan) and a verified sending domain, which is partly external.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. RecoveryController.java:51-56 prints the code with System.out.println and does not use any EmailSender; the controller's only dependencies are UserRepository, JwtUtils and EncryptionService (lines 25-33). application.properties:47-49 defaults email to disabled with the logging provider. Choosing the provider and verifying a sending domain is partly external, but the wiring is app work.

### [blocker] Beneficiary endpoints allow cross-user writes (submit by id, and create with a client-supplied id)

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/BeneficiaryController.java:164-170 (calls beneficiaryService.submitToAlpaca(beneficiaryId) without the user), :91-108 (@RequestBody Beneficiary entity); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/BeneficiaryService.java:204-260 (no ownership check, acts on the beneficiary's owner), :82-95 (setUser then save), :213 (error text includes owner email); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/Beneficiary.java:28-29 (id is a plain writable field)
- Detail: POST /api/beneficiaries/{id}/submit loads any beneficiary by sequential id and pushes that owner's beneficiary list to their Alpaca account and marks it APPROVED. Any logged-in user can do this to another user's draft beneficiary. The failure message can also return the other user's email. Separately, create binds the JPA entity straight from JSON, id included; saving an entity with an existing id is a merge, so a caller who sends another user's beneficiary id would overwrite that row and reassign it to themselves. I confirmed the code path by reading; I did not execute the merge case, so that half is medium confidence. update and delete do check ownership correctly (BeneficiaryService.java:118,159).
- Suggested fix: Pass the user into submitToAlpaca and reject when beneficiary.user != caller. Use a request DTO for create/update (no id, no status, no user) or force setId(null) on create. Return generic error text.
- Verifier (confirmed): Submit half is certain: BeneficiaryController.java:164-170 loads the caller but calls submitToAlpaca(beneficiaryId) without it, and BeneficiaryService.java:204-260 acts on beneficiary.getUser() with no ownership check; line 213-215 puts the owner's email in the exception text. Create half: the controller binds the JPA entity from JSON (BeneficiaryController.java:91-93), Beneficiary has a public setId (Beneficiary.java:271) with no READ_ONLY annotation on id, and createBeneficiary sets the user then calls save (BeneficiaryService.java:89-95), so a supplied id would go down the merge path. I also did not execute it, so that half stays medium confidence. One could argue high rather than blocker since the submit attack pushes the victim's own draft, but the email leak plus the likely row takeover justify blocker for a real-money beta.

### [blocker] iOS app shell loads the web app from the local dev tunnel

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9 (server.url = https://local.fredvested.com)
- Detail: With server.url set, the native app does not run its bundled code; it loads whatever local.fredvested.com serves (a Cloudflare tunnel to a dev machine). A build shipped like this only works while that machine is up, and whoever controls that host controls the code running inside the app with access to the stored JWT and the passkey bridge. This may overlap with the build/release auditor; I am flagging it here because it is a remote code loading risk.
- Suggested fix: Make server.url dev-only (env-driven Capacitor config) and ship release builds with bundled www. Confirm passkey rpId and CORS origins work from capacitor://localhost.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9 hard-codes server.url = https://local.fredvested.com with no environment switch, and the synced native copy frontend/ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json:5-6 has the same value, so a Release or TestFlight build as it stands would load from the dev tunnel. No build script in frontend/package.json or frontend/scripts overrides it.

### [high] No rate limiting or abuse protection on any endpoint

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: grep for ratelimit/bucket4j/resilience4j/throttle in backend/src/main and build.gradle returned only FredChatActionService.java:45-82 (6 chat actions per minute); SecurityConfig.java:112-118 (public routes); WebAuthnController.java:83-118, :231-270; config/CacheConfig.java:20-34 (challenge caches capped at 1000 entries)
- Detail: Unauthenticated endpoints (/api/passkey/register/start, /authenticate/start, /api/auth/recovery/*, /api/user/should-prompt-reauth) can be called without limit. register/start creates a users row plus an analytics outbox row per call for any unverified email. The two in-memory challenge caches hold 1000 entries, so a flood of start calls evicts real users' challenges and blocks login and signup. Authenticated chat has no per-user message quota or message length cap (ChatController.java:37-59, LLMService.java:54 allows 16000 output tokens per reply), so one account can run up the Anthropic bill.
- Suggested fix: Add a rate limit filter (per IP for public routes, per user for chat and money movement), or enforce at Cloudflare for the public routes and in-app for per-user quotas. Cap chat message length and daily messages per tier.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. My grep for ratelimit, bucket4j, resilience4j and throttle across the backend source returned no files (the auditor's one hit in FredChatActionService uses different wording; I did not re-open it). Public routes are as listed (SecurityConfig.java:112-118). register/start creates and saves a user row per call (WebAuthnService.java:81-114). Both challenge caches are capped at 1000 entries (CacheConfig.java:20-34). ChatController.java:37-59 has no length cap or quota and LLMService.java:54 sets 16000 max tokens. Whether Cloudflare in front of api-dev applies any limits is outside the repo and unverified. The recovery brute force is the part that makes this urgent and it is already tracked as its own blocker.

### [high] Session revocation stops working after the first token refresh; tokens can be refreshed forever; no server logout

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AuthController.java:152-194 (refresh issues generateTokenFromUsername with no sessionId, line 178); security/jwt/AuthTokenFilter.java:59-70 (revocation check only runs when the token has a sessionId); service/WebAuthnService.java:235 (registration token has no sessionId); RecoveryController.java:96; UserController.java:900 (update-email token), :935-959 (revoke); frontend/src/app/services/auth.service.ts:455-480 (refresh every session); no logout endpoint in backend (grep)
- Detail: The 'Active sessions / revoke' feature relies on a sessionId claim. Login puts it in the token, but /api/auth/refresh mints the replacement without it, and the filter skips the revocation check when the claim is absent. The app refreshes within the first hour of use, so in practice a revoked device keeps working. Tokens from registration, recovery and email change never had a session. There is also no absolute session lifetime: any valid token can be refreshed indefinitely, so a stolen token is permanent access. The 30-minute idle cap is enforced only in the client. Logout only clears localStorage; the token stays valid for up to an hour.
- Suggested fix: Carry sessionId through refresh (read it from the old token), create a session row for registration/recovery/email-change tokens, reject session-less tokens after a migration window, add a max session age, bump lastActive on refresh, and add POST /api/auth/logout that deactivates the session. Consider 'revoke all sessions' on recovery and email change.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. AuthController.java:178 calls generateTokenFromUsername(username, ns, publicId), a variant with no sessionId, and AuthTokenFilter.java:59-70 only checks revocation when the claim is present. Registration (WebAuthnService.java:235), recovery (RecoveryController.java:96) and update-email (UserController.java:900) tokens also carry no sessionId. Refresh has no max-age check. The only backend file mentioning logout is SecurityConfig, where it is disabled, so there is no logout endpoint. Token lifetime is 1 hour (application.properties:19). I did not re-read the frontend refresh timing at auth.service.ts:455-480.

### [high] Email can be changed with only a JWT: no passkey re-auth, no verification of the new address, no notice to the old one

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:869-908
- Detail: POST /api/user/update-email sets the new email immediately. Email is the recovery channel, so anyone holding a session (stolen token, unlocked phone) can point recovery at their own inbox and then take the account permanently. There is no format validation on newEmail and the brokerage record at Alpaca is not updated in this method.
- Suggested fix: Require a fresh passkey assertion, send a confirmation code to the new address before switching, notify the old address, and revoke other sessions. Validate format. Decide whether Alpaca contact email must be kept in sync.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. UserController.java:869-908 only checks that newEmail is non-empty and not already used, then saves it and returns a new JWT. No format validation, no confirmation step, no notice, no Alpaca update. Combined with the recovery flow this is a full takeover path from a single stolen token, so high is right and it should be fixed together with the recovery work.

### [high] Step-up authentication for withdrawals, liquidation, bank change and account closure is frontend-only

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/TradingController.java:108-159, :164-201, :206-258, :310-369 (JWT only); controller/PlaidController.java:82-120 (bank relink with JWT only); controller/InvestmentController.java:195-243 (lump sum up to $1,000,000, JWT only); frontend/src/app/services/pin.service.ts:40-73 (stepUpEnabled flag in localStorage); controller/PinController.java:32-47, :114-126; FREDdocs/AUTHENTICATION_AND_JWT.md:118-127
- Detail: The design doc lists step-up for withdrawals, lump-sum trades and settings. The server has no notion of it: every money endpoint accepts a plain JWT. The step-up/PIN gate lives in the client and is optional per user. A bearer token alone can relink the bank account to a different one and then withdraw to it, liquidate, pull up to $1M by ACH, or close the account. The PIN itself can be replaced or deleted with just the JWT (set and delete do not ask for the current PIN), which also clears the lockout. verify with a missing pin field likely throws a 500 (PinController.java:61, null passed to BCrypt matches).
- Suggested fix: Add a server-side 'recent strong auth' requirement for sensitive routes, for example a short-lived claim or session flag set by /api/passkey/authenticate/finish and checked on withdraw, liquidate, close, exchange_public_token (when replacing a bank), update-email and PIN change. Require current PIN or passkey to change or delete the PIN. Null-check the pin.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. TradingController.java has no step-up, re-auth, passkey or PIN reference anywhere (grep returned only the route mappings at 108, 164, 206, 310). PlaidController.java:82-100 and InvestmentController.java:195-243 (cap of 1,000,000 at line 239) need only a JWT. PinController.java:32-47 sets a PIN and 114-126 deletes it without asking for the current PIN, and both clear the lockout. verify passes request.getPin() to passwordEncoder.matches with no null check (line 61); the 500 on null is likely but I did not run it. The client marker is a localStorage boolean (pin.service.ts:40-58). Mitigation worth noting: withdrawals go to the linked bank, so theft needs the bank relink step first, which is also JWT-only.

### [high] Subscription tier is client-writable, so any user can grant themselves Plus or Pro

- Type: decision, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:528-549 (PUT /api/user/profile sets selectedTier and billingPeriod from the body), :618-633 (POST /subscription/confirm); service/SubscriptionService.java:21-35; controller/TlhController.java:265-267 (Pro check reads selectedTier); controller/GoalController.java:113-115 (TIER_LIMIT)
- Detail: No billing exists yet (known). What exists is two authenticated endpoints that write the tier straight from the request with no payment proof and no allowed-value check. Server-side gates that read selectedTier (tax loss harvesting, goal limits) are therefore honor-system. The unauthenticated webhook was already removed (UserController.java:668-671), which is good.
- Suggested fix: Until billing is decided, restrict selectedTier writes to a validated enum and decide what a self-selected tier should unlock during beta. Once the per-paycheck model is settled with counsel, make tier server-authoritative and remove it from PUT /profile.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. UserController.java:528-549 writes selectedTier and billingPeriod from the PUT /profile body with no allowed-value check, and :618-633 plus SubscriptionService.java:21-35 do the same via /subscription/confirm with no payment proof. The removed webhook comment is at UserController.java:668-671. For the private beta itself this is closer to medium because nothing is being charged yet and TLH is in dry-run on dev (application-dev.properties:73), but it must be closed before any paid tier exists, so high as a decision item is fair.

### [high] Beneficiary SSNs are stored in plaintext and returned in API responses

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/Beneficiary.java:68-71 (plain column, length 11), :294; backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__baseline.sql:32; controller/BeneficiaryController.java:37-108 (returns entities); contrast model/User.java:124 (user SSN is ENC: encrypted)
- Detail: The account holder's own SSN is encrypted with AES-GCM, but beneficiaries' SSNs are saved as raw 'XXX-XX-XXXX' text and the entity is serialized back to the client on every list, create and update call. A database leak or a stolen token exposes third-party SSNs.
- Suggested fix: Encrypt with EncryptionService before save (column needs widening via a Flyway migration plus a backfill), return only the last 4 digits in responses, and never echo the full value.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. Beneficiary.java:68-71 is a plain varchar(11) column with a format pattern, matching V1__baseline.sql:32, and nothing encrypts it before save (BeneficiaryService.java:82-95). getSocialSecurityNumber (line 294) has no JsonIgnore or write-only annotation; the only JsonIgnore in the entity is on user (line 33), so the full SSN is serialized wherever the entity is returned (for example BeneficiaryController.java:100). The user's own SSN is encrypted (User.java:123-124). Arguably a blocker for a real-money beta since these are third-party SSNs, especially next to the cross-user beneficiary bug; I left it at high because exposure through the API is limited to the owner once that bug is fixed.

### [high] Signup takes any email with no ownership check, and an abandoned signup locks that email

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/WebAuthnService.java:81-114 (user row created at register/start, before any passkey exists), :85-89 (existing email rejected); controller/WebAuthnController.java:109-112 (409 'Email is already taken'), :182-192 (5-minute challenge)
- Detail: The user row is created when registration starts. If the passkey step is cancelled or the 5-minute challenge expires, the email is taken and start will refuse it forever; the only way back is recovery, which does not send email today. Nobody verifies the email belongs to the person, so someone can register another person's address with their own passkey, and that address is then used for KYC and recovery. The 409 also confirms which emails have accounts.
- Suggested fix: Create the user at register/finish (or allow restart when the user has zero passkeys and no brokerage account), and verify the email with a code before or right after passkey creation. Clean up stale zero-passkey users.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. WebAuthnService.java:85-89 rejects any existing email and :92-114 creates and saves the user at register/start, before a passkey exists. WebAuthnController.java:109-112 returns 409 'Email is already taken'. I found no cleanup job or restart path for zero-passkey users in WebAuthnService or a scheduler (grep for stale, abandon, cleanup found nothing). The challenge lives 5 minutes (CacheConfig.java:20-24). Since the only way back is recovery and recovery email is not sent, a beta tester who cancels the Face ID sheet once is stuck, which pushes this toward blocker for the beta; fixing the recovery email blocker does not remove the lockout on first signup, so treat it as top of the high list.

### [high] Production passkey domain settings do not match each other

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/environments/environment.prod.ts:7-8 (backend api.fredvested.com, rpId app.fredvested.com); frontend/ios/App/App/Release.entitlements (associated-domains is a single string 'webcredentials:api.fredvested.com', keychain group com.fredvested.app); frontend/capacitor.config.ts:4 (appId com.fredvested.Fred); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AASAController.java:25; application-prod.properties:31-33; config/WebAuthnConfig.java:52-56, :62
- Detail: iOS only allows passkeys for a relying party id that appears in the app's webcredentials associated domains and serves the AASA file. The prod frontend uses rpId app.fredvested.com while the release entitlement lists api.fredvested.com, and the entitlement value is a string where Apple expects an array. The keychain group name does not match the bundle id. Dev rpId still points at a GitHub Codespaces hostname (environment.dev.ts:8). On the backend, an empty origins variable silently falls back to http://localhost:8100 instead of failing startup. Passkeys created against one rpId cannot be moved to another, so this needs to be settled before the first real user registers.
- Suggested fix: Pick the final rpId (ideally the registrable domain fredvested.com or one fixed host), align environment.prod.ts, Release.entitlements (array form), WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD/ORIGINS and the AASA host, and make an empty origins value a startup failure in prod. Needs the prod backend and Apple portal setup, which are partly external.
- Verifier (adjusted): Partly wrong in the details. environment.prod.ts:8 rpId (app.fredvested.com) is a dead field: nothing in frontend/src/app reads environment.rpId; the app takes the rpId from the server's options (auth.service.ts:567, passkey-prompt.component.ts:270). The real rpId in prod is the WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD env var (application-prod.properties:31), which is not set anywhere yet, so there is no proven mismatch, only an unset value that must equal the entitlement domain. The keychain-access-groups name differing from the bundle id is not a passkey problem. What is confirmed: Release.entitlements gives associated-domains as a single string where Debug.entitlements uses the array form, and the Release build config does use that file (project.pbxproj:413); the AASA app id 9G59L97KRT.com.fredvested.Fred (AASAController.java:25) matches the bundle id; WebAuthnConfig.java:52-56 falls back to http://localhost:8100 on empty origins and :62 allows untrusted attestation; environment.dev.ts:8 still has a Codespaces hostname (also unused). The action item stands: pick the final rpId, set the env vars, fix the entitlement to array form, and fail startup on empty origins before the first real user registers. The stale environment rpId values should be deleted to avoid confusion.

### [high] Decide where beta users live: the only backend runs the dev profile with dev tools reachable

- Type: decision, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/config/DevToolsConfig.java:23-25; controller/DevAuthController.java:89-166 (log in as any user), :172-202 (list all users); controller/DevUserController.java:44-58 (delete any user); service/EncryptionService.java:150-163 (dev profile accepts unencrypted legacy values); application-dev.properties:72-75; security/DevToolsKeyFilter.java:46-52
- Detail: Dev tooling is well fenced (profile-gated, constant-time key check, 404 on failure, prod startup guard). But there is no prod backend. If the private beta is pointed at api-dev, one shared DEV_TOOLS_KEY becomes a master key that can mint a session for any real user, list all users and delete them, and that key is also stored in testers' browsers via ?devKey. The dev profile also allows plaintext sensitive columns and manual TLH and market-breakdown triggers.
- Suggested fix: Stand up a prod-profile backend for the beta (prod properties also lack alpaca.* entries, see application-prod.properties vs application-dev.properties:59-65, so it will not boot as is). If beta must run on dev temporarily, unset DEV_TOOLS_KEY there so every dev endpoint is dead.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. Dev tooling is profile-gated to local/dev/test/ci (DevToolsConfig.java:23-25; @Profile on DevAuthController:34, DevUserController:30, DevPlaidController:18) and key-gated with a 404 on failure (DevToolsKeyFilter.java:46-52; empty key denies everything per application.properties:106-110). On the dev profile, EncryptionService.java:150-163 returns plaintext legacy values, and application-dev.properties:72-75 enables manual market-breakdown and TLH triggers. application-prod.properties has no alpaca.* entries while dev has them at lines 59-65 and the base application.properties has none, so a prod profile likely will not boot as is. I did not re-read the DevAuthController and DevUserController method bodies. If the beta really has to run on api-dev this becomes a blocker, so the decision should be made first.

### [medium] Investment history, dashboard and execution endpoints serialize the whole User entity

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/InvestmentExecution.java:18-20, :151 (public getUser, no @JsonIgnore; only schedule is ignored at :70-73); model/User.java:27-31 (@Getter, no @JsonIgnore anywhere), :86 pinHash, :124-130 ssn, ssnHash, recoveryOtp, :145 plaidAccessToken; controller/InvestmentController.java:66-71, :120-122, :419; model/PasskeyCredential.java:24-26 and User.java:135-141 (back-references with no ignore)
- Detail: These endpoints put InvestmentExecution entities straight into the response. Jackson will follow getUser() into the User entity, which has no ignore annotations: PIN hash, SSN ciphertext, the unsalted SSN hash, recovery OTP ciphertext, encrypted Plaid access token and Alpaca ids. User, UserProgress and PasskeyCredential point back at each other, so serialization probably ends in a recursion error after part of the body has been written. So for a user who has executions, these endpoints either leak the caller's own secret fields or fail with a 500. I did not run it, so the exact outcome is unconfirmed; the missing @JsonIgnore is certain.
- Suggested fix: Add @JsonIgnore on InvestmentExecution.user now, and move these three endpoints to response DTOs. Add @JsonIgnore to the sensitive User fields as a safety net. Add an API test that asserts the response has no 'user' key.
- Verifier (adjusted): The missing annotation is real: InvestmentExecution.java:18-20 and :151 expose getUser with no JsonIgnore (only schedule is ignored at :70-72), User is @Getter with no JsonIgnore on any field (User.java:27, 123-145), and PasskeyCredential is @Data with a back reference (PasskeyCredential.java:16-26). The entities are put straight into the response at InvestmentController.java:66-71, 120-122 and 419. Two corrections. First, the exposure is only the caller's own record (or a 500 from recursion), never another user's. Second, the app does not call these three routes at all: a grep of frontend/src/app for investments/history, investments/dashboard and investments/execution found nothing; the only /investments call is POST /execute (lump-sum-investment.page.ts:396). So no user-facing screen breaks. It is still a reachable endpoint that can return a PIN hash and encrypted secrets to a token holder, so fix it (one-line JsonIgnore or delete the unused routes), but medium fits better than high. Outcome at runtime was not executed by me either.

### [medium] JWT lives in localStorage with no Content Security Policy

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/utils/jwt-token.utils.ts:32-39, :127; frontend/src/index.html:5-26 (no CSP meta); frontend/src/app/services/keychain-sync.service.ts:28-58 (Keychain plugin exists but only stores the email); frontend/src/app/pipes/markdown.pipe.ts:23-27
- Detail: The session token, user id and email sit in WebView localStorage. Any script injection reads them. The chat markdown is sanitized with DOMPurify (good), but there is no CSP as a second layer, and several templates bind innerHTML from app-authored strings. The app already has a native Keychain plugin that could hold the token.
- Suggested fix: Store the JWT in the iOS Keychain (non-synced item) via the existing plugin and keep it in memory at runtime; add a CSP meta tag limiting script and connect sources to the app and API hosts.

### [medium] Passkey user verification is 'preferred', not required

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/WebAuthnService.java:139, :163, :386, :411; config/WebAuthnConfig.java:62-63
- Detail: With PREFERRED the server accepts an assertion made without Face ID or passcode if the authenticator allows it. For a money app, biometrics or device passcode should be mandatory at login and re-auth. allowUntrustedAttestation(true) is normal for consumer passkeys and is fine; the comment says to review it. Signature counter and lastUsedDate are not updated after login (finishAuthenticationFlow never saves the credential), which is low impact for iCloud passkeys.
- Suggested fix: Set UserVerificationRequirement.REQUIRED on registration and assertion and confirm the library enforces it; update lastUsedDate and signature count on successful assertion.

### [medium] Sensitive data and PII in logs

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/security/jwt/JwtUtils.java:226-234 (logs the full token on every validation failure); service/WebAuthnService.java:83 (email, target portfolio, income, monthly amount at INFO); controller/UserController.java:447-455 (IP and device id); service/AlpacaApiService.java:171, :436 (request bodies at DEBUG); service/PortfolioDashboardService.java:794 (full portfolio history body at INFO); controller/AuthController.java:79; frontend/src/app/components/linkplaid/linkplaid.component.ts:119 and change-bank-account.page.ts:152 (Plaid public token to console)
- Detail: Emails are logged on almost every auth and user action, financial figures are logged at signup, and rejected JWTs are written whole. Expired or bad-signature tokens are not directly usable, but logs should never carry bearer tokens. DEBUG lines would log KYC and ACH request bodies if debug logging is ever turned on.
- Suggested fix: Log user id (or publicId) instead of email, drop amounts, never log tokens or request bodies, remove System.out calls, strip the Plaid console logs from release builds.

### [medium] Error responses return internal exception messages

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AlpacaController.java:137-141, :201-205, :291-294, :369-373; controller/InvestmentController.java:79-82, :126-128; controller/AuthController.java:138-139; controller/PlaidController.java:77-78, :117-118; controller/MarketBreakdownController.java:100-103; controller/TlhController.java:212-214; service/WebAuthnService.java:251, :548
- Detail: Many catch blocks put e.getMessage() into the response body. That can expose broker error payloads, SQL or library text, and in the beneficiary case another user's email. Spring's default error page does not include stack traces (no server.error overrides found), so this is about the hand-written handlers. There is no global @RestControllerAdvice.
- Suggested fix: Add a global exception handler that returns a generic message plus an error id, and log the detail server-side. Replace e.getMessage() in response bodies.

### [medium] Crypto hardening: unsalted SSN hash and weak-key settings only warn

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EncryptionService.java:133-141 (plain SHA-256 of SSN), :40-61 (16-byte key only logs a warning outside local); security/jwt/JwtUtils.java:44-56 (short secret only warns; hard-coded fallback secret on encoding error); application-prod.properties:13,29
- Detail: SSNs have under a billion possible values, so an unsalted SHA-256 can be reversed in seconds if the ssn_hash column leaks, which defeats the encryption next to it. A too-short JWT secret or a 128-bit encryption key is allowed to boot in prod with only a log line. BrokerEnvironmentGuard already blocks the known dev JWT secret (config/BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:234-240), which is good.
- Suggested fix: Use HMAC-SHA256 with a server-side secret (pepper) for ssnHash and backfill; fail startup in prod if jwt.secret is under 64 bytes or encryption key is not 32 bytes; remove the fallback secret branch.

### [medium] Upload and request-size validation is thin

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AlpacaController.java:164-207, :250-264 (documentType, mimeType, content only @NotBlank); service/AlpacaApiService.java:331-340; application.properties:70-72 (multipart limits, but this endpoint is JSON base64, not multipart); controller/UserController.java:431-471 (unchecked Boolean casts)
- Detail: KYC document upload accepts any mime type, any document type string and any size of base64 content and forwards it to Alpaca. The 15MB multipart limits do not apply to a JSON body. It is correctly limited to the caller's own account and to ACTION_REQUIRED status. Several map-based endpoints cast values without type checks and will 500 on bad input.
- Suggested fix: Whitelist mime types (jpeg, png, pdf) and document types, cap decoded size (for example 10MB), validate base64, and set a max request body size. Replace Map bodies with validated DTOs.

### [low] Client can mark its own onboarding steps complete, including KYC

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:400-490 (PUT /api/user/progress sets kycVerificationCompleted, linkPlaidCompleted etc. from the body)
- Detail: These flags drive navigation. Money endpoints separately check for an Alpaca account and ACH relationship, so I found no way to trade without real KYC, but server state can be made to claim KYC is done.
- Suggested fix: Set kyc, plaid and schedule flags only from the server-side events that prove them; ignore them in the PUT.

### [low] Small leftovers on the public surface

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AuthController.java:67-82 (/api/auth/testuser), :105-143 (dead /login that always answers '2FA required'); controller/ChatController.java:110-113 (suggestions trusts userId param); controller/UserController.java:739-779 (public device-id check); config/SecurityConfig.java:77-86 (localhost and a github.dev origin in CORS), @CrossOrigin(origins="*") on most controllers; controller/WebAuthnController.java:236-248 (email-specific start reveals whether an account exists and its credential ids); FREDdocs/AUTHENTICATION_AND_JWT.md:51 (says HS512 and IP-based re-auth; code is HS256 and device-id based)
- Detail: None of these is a direct break. The chat suggestions IDOR only reveals whether another user has asked about FRED. Tokens are sent in headers, not cookies, so the open CORS annotations are not a CSRF hole, but they should be tightened. The auth doc is out of date in several places (recovery, step-up, algorithm).
- Suggested fix: Delete /testuser and /login, bind suggestions to the principal, trim CORS origins per profile and remove the per-controller wildcards, make authenticate/start usernameless only, update the doc.

**Verified solid:**

- Route protection: SecurityConfig.java:111-120 permits only AASA, /api/auth/**, /api/passkey/**, /api/plaid/webhook, /api/user/should-prompt-reauth, /api/dev/** and /hello; everything else requires a JWT. Stateless sessions, CSRF off (correct for bearer tokens), form login and basic auth disabled.
- Plaid webhook verification is solid: ES256 only, kid format check, 5-minute iat window, expired-key handling, constant-time SHA-256 body compare, 256KB body cap, 401 vs 503 split, filter runs before Spring Security, controller refuses requests without verified attributes (security/plaid/JwtPlaidWebhookVerifier.java, PlaidWebhookVerificationFilter.java, controller/PlaidWebhookController.java). CI bypass verifier is @Profile(ci) only.
- Dev tools gating: DevAuthController, DevUserController, DevPlaidController and SimulatedAttestationService are @Profile local/dev/test/ci; DevToolsKeyFilter requires X-Dev-Tools-Key with constant-time compare and 404 on failure, an unset key denies all; DevToolsProdGuard aborts startup if any are registered under prod. No dev key is shipped in dev/prod frontend builds (environment.dev.ts, environment.prod.ts).
- SIMULATED_ATTESTATION registration is rejected unless the dev-only bean exists and the dev key matches (WebAuthnController.java:163-180).
- Passkey ceremonies use the Yubico library with one-time challenges: challenge is removed from cache before verification on both register and authenticate finish (WebAuthnController.java:182-183, 296-297), 5-minute expiry, user is resolved from the credential's user handle, and step-up identity mismatch is rejected (WebAuthnController.java:312-327).
- Recovery re-registration start requires a valid JWT and takes the email from the token, not the body (WebAuthnController.java:120-151).
- Principal binding verified on: UserController (all authenticated routes), PinController, PlaidController, TradingController, InvestmentController, InvestmentScheduleController (findByIdAndUser), GoalController/GoalService (findByIdAndUser), PortfolioController, PortfolioDashboardController, MonthlyFreedomUpdateController, MarketBreakdownController, TlhController, DocumentsController, ChatController chat/stream/history/action (client userId ignored), AlpacaController create-account/kyc/upload/acats. Beneficiary update and delete check ownership. Session revoke checks ownership (UserController.java:951).
- Investment execution detail checks the execution belongs to the caller (InvestmentController.java:111-114); lump-sum has idempotency by client UUID.
- Duplicate brokerage account creation is refused and the account is saved against the JWT user, not the body email (AlpacaController.java:102-127).
- Plaid access token never leaves the server; primary-bank-account returns display fields only (PlaidController.java:134-143).
- User SSN, Plaid access token, Alpaca account id/number, ACH relationship id and recovery OTP are stored AES-GCM encrypted with a random 12-byte IV (EncryptionService.java:66-88); prod and test profiles refuse unencrypted legacy values.
- PIN is BCrypt-hashed with 5-attempt lockout and doubling lockout duration (PinController.java:29-91).
- JWT: HS256 signed, 1-hour expiry (application.properties:19), secret comes from environment in dev/test/prod, and BrokerEnvironmentGuard refuses the known old dev secret in prod. application-local.properties with real sandbox keys is gitignored (.gitignore:44) and not tracked.
- The old unauthenticated subscription webhook has been removed (UserController.java:668-671).
- Chat markdown is sanitized with DOMPurify and images are forbidden (frontend/src/app/pipes/markdown.pipe.ts). Chat agentic actions are rate limited to 6 per minute and audited (FredChatActionService.java:45-82).
- No actuator, Swagger or H2 console exposed; no server.error overrides that would include stack traces in default error responses.
- Manual TLH run and manual market-breakdown generation are config-gated and default to off outside local/dev (application.properties:52, 83).

## money-movement

Money movement and trading audit, read-only, against the working tree. I read the scheduler doc, all profile configs, BrokerEnvironmentGuard, InvestmentScheduler, ScheduledInvestmentProcessor, InvestmentExecutionService (all 1260 lines), InvestmentScheduleService, the InvestmentSchedule model, AlpacaService (transfer, order, sell, withdraw, clock parts), AlpacaApiService (ACH parts), AchRelationshipService, AccountStatusService, PlaidService, PlaidToAlpacaService, PlaidController, TradingController, InvestmentController, DripService, EmailService, PaydayNotificationService, and the sell-withdraw, lump-sum and change-bank pages. I did not run tests, did not call Alpaca or Plaid, and did not read TLH execution, the Plaid webhook internals or PortfolioDashboardService in depth. Statements about how live Alpaca behaves (late ACH returns, order states, the $50k ACH limit, raw bank numbers vs processor tokens) come from my own knowledge, not from the docs, and are marked with lower confidence.  Overall: the recurring path up to the ACH request is well hardened (startup environment guard, unique keys, claim before POST, job locks, bank change handling). The weak half is everything after the ACH request and everything user-initiated. Four things stop real money today: (1) the prod profile has no Alpaca config and cannot boot, (2) users are never told when a pull or trade fails (email is log-only, push is a stub, no screen shows execution status), (3) an ACH return does not stop the schedule and returns after settlement are never seen, (4) a timed-out ACH request is recorded as failed even though Alpaca may have pulled the money. After that: buy orders have no idempotency key and run inside one long DB transaction, several order states leave executions stuck or wrongly marked, withdraw and sell have no server-side checks or records, there is no reconciliation job, and automatic rebalancing (promised on the tier screen) has no backend at all.  All backend paths below are under backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend unless written in full.


### [blocker] Users are never told when a bank pull or trade fails, and no screen shows execution status

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/LoggingEmailSender.java:13-25 (the only EmailSender implementation, logs only); service/EmailService.java:56-61 (disabled means MOCKED); application.properties:47-49; service/PaydayNotificationService.java:104-111 (push is a log line); service/AccountStatusService.java:125,221 (push TODOs); service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:226-235 (batch ACH request failure marks FUNDING_FAILED with no notify call), 1192-1218 (all failure notices go through email); frontend/src/app/services/investment.service.ts:125,134,169 (history, execution detail and dashboard calls exist but a search found no page that calls them)
- Detail: Every money notification (funding failed, ACH returned, trades failed, investment completed, account rejected) is sent through EmailService, and the only sender in the codebase writes a log line. Push is a stub. On the app side, the endpoints that return executions and their status exist, but no page calls them, so a user has no way to see that a pull is pending, that it failed, or that money is sitting uninvested. Separately, when the 11:59 PM ACH request itself is rejected by Alpaca, the rows go to FUNDING_FAILED and the code throws without calling any notify method. Known context says market breakdown email is mocked; this finding is that the same mock covers all money alerts and there is no in-app fallback.
- Suggested fix: Build a real EmailSender (the roadmap names Resend), turn it on per environment, add the missing notify call on the batch failure branch, and add an in-app activity view that lists executions with status (pending, funding failed, trade failed, done) using the existing /api/investments/history endpoint.
- Verifier (confirmed): Verified. LoggingEmailSender is the only EmailSender implementation (LoggingEmailSender.java:15). EmailService.java:56-61 records MOCKED and returns when app.email.enabled is false, which is the default (application.properties:47-49). Push is a comment only (PaydayNotificationService.java:107; AccountStatusService.java:125 and 221). The batch failure branch at InvestmentExecutionService.java:226-235 marks FUNDING_FAILED and throws with no notify call. On the app side, getInvestmentHistory, getExecutionDetails and getDashboard are defined in investment.service.ts:125,134,168 and a grep of frontend/src/app (non-spec files) found no caller; the status label helpers at lines 235-276 are also unused by any page. I found no other screen that shows a pending or failed pull. For real money this stays a blocker: a user whose pull failed or whose cash is sitting uninvested has no way to find out.

### [blocker] An ACH return does not stop the schedule, and returns after settlement are never detected

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: model/HoldReason.java:7-10 and model/InvestmentSchedule.java:328-329 (ACH_RETURN_UNAUTHORIZED and ACH_RETURN_ACCOUNT are blocking holds); a repo-wide search for setHoldReason finds only BANK_CHANGE and MANUAL_REVIEW writers (InvestmentScheduleService.java:254, AccountStatusService.java:204, AchRelationshipService.java:111,172); service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:536-545 (only FUNDING_INITIATED rows are polled), 618-641 (failure branch marks rows failed and emails, nothing else); a search for balanceGet or accountsBalance in the backend returns nothing
- Detail: Three gaps in one area. (1) The hold reasons for ACH returns exist and are respected by the scheduler, but nothing ever sets them. After a returned or rejected transfer the schedule keeps its normal dates and pulls again next cycle, which can mean repeated bank NSF fees for the user and repeated returns against FRED's Alpaca relationship. (2) The poller only looks at transfers that are still FUNDING_INITIATED. Once Alpaca reports COMPLETE the shares are bought and the transfer is never looked at again, so a bank return that arrives days later (my understanding is that Alpaca can move a transfer to RETURNED after COMPLETE; I did not verify this against the docs) leaves a negative cash balance that FRED never sees. (3) There is no balance check before a pull, so an obviously underfunded account is still debited. FUNDING_FAILED is also terminal with no retry, so a failed paycheck investment is simply skipped.
- Suggested fix: On a failed or returned transfer, set the matching hold on the schedule by return reason and tell the user how to clear it. Keep polling settled transfers for a return window (for example 60 days, the same window UserDeletionService already uses) and alert on any late RETURNED. Decide with Alpaca what happens to the bought shares on a late return. Consider a Plaid balance check before the pull.
- Verifier (confirmed): Verified. ACH_RETURN_UNAUTHORIZED and ACH_RETURN_ACCOUNT exist (HoldReason.java:9) and are treated as blocking (InvestmentSchedule.java:329), but the only setHoldReason callers set BANK_CHANGE, MANUAL_REVIEW or null (InvestmentScheduleService.java:254, AccountStatusService.java:204, AchRelationshipService.java:111,172). The failure branch at InvestmentExecutionService.java:618-641 only marks rows FUNDING_FAILED and calls the mocked email. The funding poll selects only FUNDING_INITIATED rows (lines 541-544; repository query at InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:34-36), so a transfer is never looked at again after COMPLETE. No Plaid balance call exists in the backend. Nothing re-queues a FUNDING_FAILED row. The claim that Alpaca can move a transfer to RETURNED after COMPLETE was not checked against Alpaca docs by me either (the code's own comment at AlpacaService.java:176 says a return comes after initial processing), so the late-return part stays medium confidence. Severity split: the never-set hold (repeat pulls after a return) is the blocker part; late-return detection is high; the pre-pull balance check is an optional improvement, not a blocker.

### [high] Prod profile has no Alpaca configuration, so a production backend cannot start

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:1-33 (no alpaca.* keys at all); application.properties:1-121 (none either); service/AlpacaService.java:26-32 (alpaca.api.key and alpaca.api.secret have no default, base URL defaults to sandbox); service/AlpacaApiService.java:41-44; config/BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:107-132 (prod refuses a sandbox or paper URL)
- Detail: application-prod.properties sets datasource, JWT, Plaid, encryption and WebAuthn, but nothing for Alpaca. Under the prod profile alpaca.api.key and alpaca.api.secret are unresolved placeholders, and the broker and trading URLs fall back to the sandbox defaults, which BrokerEnvironmentGuard rejects in prod. So the prod profile fails at startup either way. The header comment listing required prod variables (lines 1-6) does not mention Alpaca. Email settings for prod are also only the global defaults (disabled, logging provider). Nothing in the repo has been run against the live Alpaca host.
- Suggested fix: Add alpaca.api.key, alpaca.api.secret, alpaca.market.key, alpaca.market.secret, alpaca.broker.base-url, alpaca.trading.base-url and alpaca.market.base-url to application-prod.properties as environment placeholders with no defaults, and list them in the header comment. Getting live Broker API keys from Alpaca is the external part.
- Verifier (adjusted): The facts are right: application-prod.properties (lines 1-33) has no alpaca.* keys and its header list of required variables does not mention Alpaca; AlpacaService.java:26-32 and AlpacaApiService.java:41-44 need alpaca.api.key and alpaca.api.secret with no default; BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:107-132 rejects the sandbox and paper defaults under prod. But "cannot start" is overstated. Spring resolves these placeholders from environment variables by relaxed naming, so a prod service that sets ALPACA_API_KEY, ALPACA_API_SECRET, ALPACA_BROKER_BASE_URL and ALPACA_TRADING_BASE_URL would boot with no file change. The trap is that dev uses different names for the URLs (ALPACA_API_BASE_URL and ALPACA_API_TRADING_URL, application-dev.properties:63-64), so copying the dev variables to prod would fail the guard at startup. That failure is loud and safe, not a money risk. It is a small config and documentation gap (effort S); the real gate is live Alpaca keys, which is external. Also true: email in prod falls back to the global defaults (application.properties:47-49, disabled, logging provider).

### [high] A timed-out ACH request is recorded as failed even though Alpaca may have pulled the money

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/AlpacaService.java:111-161 (initiateAchTransfer: any exception, including the 30 second read timeout set at line 69, returns status FAILED); service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:226-235 (FAILED response marks every row FUNDING_FAILED), 314-322 (claim committed before the POST); repository/InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:163-166 (claimed rows are excluded from every later batch); a search shows transferAttemptedAt is read nowhere else
- Detail: The ACH POST carries no idempotency key and its result is trusted as final. If Alpaca accepts the transfer but the response is lost (timeout, connection reset, deploy), FRED marks the investment FUNDING_FAILED. The money is then pulled from the user's bank, lands as cash at Alpaca, is never invested, and FRED has no transfer id to track it. The opposite case also has no recovery: if the process dies between the committed claim and the status write, the rows stay SCHEDULED with transfer_attempted_at set and are skipped forever by design, and no job or alert looks for them. The design correctly avoids double pulls; what is missing is the follow-up that finds out what actually happened.
- Suggested fix: After any non-definitive result (exception or 5xx), do not mark FUNDING_FAILED. Mark the rows as unknown, then list the account's recent incoming transfers and match on amount and time to adopt the transfer id or confirm absence. Add a sweeper that alerts on rows claimed more than an hour ago that are still SCHEDULED.
- Verifier (confirmed): Verified. initiateAchTransfer sends no idempotency key and returns status FAILED for any exception, including the 30 second read timeout (AlpacaService.java:69, 111-161). processBatchedInvestments then marks every row FUNDING_FAILED (InvestmentExecutionService.java:226-231). The claim is committed before the POST (lines 314-322) and claimed rows are excluded from later batches (InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:164, 180-181). transferAttemptedAt is referenced only in the model, that repository and the selection call at InvestmentExecutionService.java:360, so no sweeper or alert looks for rows that were claimed but never got a status. A definite 4xx from Alpaca also lands in the same catch, and for that case FAILED is the correct result; the gap is only the ambiguous cases (timeout, connection reset, 5xx, process death). Low likelihood per transfer, but the result is real money pulled, uninvested and untracked, so high is right.

### [high] Buy orders have no idempotency key and are placed inside one long database transaction, so a crash or redeploy can place them twice

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:35-36 (class-level @Transactional), 536-555 (checkFundingStatus loops all users in that one transaction), 848-1016 (orders placed inside it), 1021-1067 (same for checkTradingStatus and delayed trading); service/AlpacaService.java:295-305 and 409-419 (placeBuyOrder and placeBuyOrderWithQuantity send no client_order_id), compared with 553-567 and 588-600 where the TLH order methods do; scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:70-96 (neither poll takes a job lock)
- Detail: checkFundingStatus and checkTradingStatus each run as a single transaction covering every user, and the Alpaca order calls happen inside it. Status changes (FUNDING_COMPLETED, TRADING_INITIATED) and the trade rows only commit at the very end. If the process is killed mid-run (a Railway deploy during market hours is enough) or the commit fails, the orders already sent stay at Alpaca but the database rolls back to FUNDING_INITIATED or FUNDING_COMPLETED, and the next poll places the same orders again. In a cash account the second set will mostly be rejected for lack of buying power, which then marks a fully invested execution as TRADING_FAILED; if other cash is present, or margin was left on (see the margin finding), it is a real double purchase. The TLH code in the same file already uses client_order_id for exactly this reason.
- Suggested fix: Give each trade row a deterministic client_order_id (for example execution id plus symbol), commit the trade row before the POST, and send the id with the order, reusing the TLH helpers and the by-client-order-id lookup. Move each execution into its own short transaction so one user's failure or a shutdown cannot roll back another user's state. Add job locks to both polls.
- Verifier (confirmed): Verified. The class is @Transactional (InvestmentExecutionService.java:35-37). checkFundingStatus (536-555) and checkTradingStatus (1021-1045) are called from InvestmentScheduler.java:70-96 through the proxy with no NOT_SUPPORTED override and no job lock (unlike the 11:55 PM and 11:59 PM jobs at InvestmentScheduler.java:48 and InvestmentExecutionService.java:285), so each run is one transaction over all users, and the internal calls are self-invocations that share it. Orders are placed inside it (848-1016). placeBuyOrder and placeBuyOrderWithQuantity send no client_order_id (AlpacaService.java:295-305, 409-419) while the TLH methods do (567, 600, 633). A kill before commit rolls back TRADING_INITIATED and the trade rows while the orders stay live at Alpaca, and the next poll re-places them. Mitigation that keeps this at high and not blocker: accounts are configured cash-only (AlpacaService.java:1073-1078), so a duplicate is mostly rejected for buying power unless other cash is present; the side effect is then a wrongly TRADING_FAILED execution. The schedulerGate does not prevent this on the single active instance.

### [high] Several order outcomes leave an execution stuck forever or marked with the wrong result

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:1080 (only SUBMITTED trades are re-checked), 1100-1106 (a partially filled trade moves to PARTIALLY_FILLED and is therefore never checked again), 1092-1093 (only rejected and canceled count as failure; expired and done_for_day are not handled), 1008-1015 (one failed placement marks the whole execution TRADING_FAILED), 1031-1034 (the status poll only selects TRADING_INITIATED), 1118-1123 (an execution with zero trades is marked COMPLETED and sends a success email), 947-953 (slices under $1 are skipped; per-slice HALF_UP rounding)
- Detail: Verified by reading the status loop. (1) Partial fill: the trade is set to PARTIALLY_FILLED, and the next poll skips anything that is not SUBMITTED, so the execution stays TRADING_INITIATED forever. (2) An order that Alpaca expires stays SUBMITTED forever with no staleness alert. (3) If one symbol's order fails at placement, the execution becomes TRADING_FAILED immediately, the other symbols' orders are never polled to FILLED, the failed slice is never retried, and its cash just sits in the account. (4) If every slice is skipped (all under $1, or wash-sale skips), there are no trades and the execution is marked COMPLETED with a success email although nothing was bought. (5) With the default 75/20/5 portfolio any contribution under $20 leaves the 5 percent slice uninvested every cycle, silently. (6) Slices are each rounded half up, so they can sum to a cent or two more than the funded amount and the last order can be rejected for insufficient buying power.
- Suggested fix: Re-check PARTIALLY_FILLED trades, treat expired and done_for_day as terminal, keep polling sibling orders after a partial failure, retry or roll the failed or skipped remainder into the largest slice, give the last slice the remainder instead of rounding independently, never mark a zero-trade execution COMPLETED, and alert on any execution in TRADING_INITIATED for more than a day.
- Verifier (confirmed): All six points check out in the code. (1) Line 1080 re-checks only SUBMITTED trades; lines 1100-1105 move a trade to PARTIALLY_FILLED, after which it is never polled, and the execution stays TRADING_INITIATED (not complete, no failure). (2) Lines 1092-1093 treat only rejected and canceled as failure, so an expired order stays SUBMITTED. (3) Lines 1008-1015 set TRADING_FAILED on any placement failure and the poll at 1031-1034 selects only TRADING_INITIATED, so sibling orders are never updated to FILLED. (4) With an empty trade list, allComplete stays true and hasFailures false, so lines 1118-1123 mark COMPLETED and send the success notice. (5) Lines 947-953 skip slices under $1; the only amount floor I found is $1 (InvestmentController.java:233). (6) Per-slice HALF_UP rounding can exceed the total by a cent (for example $33.33 at 75/20/5 gives 25.00 + 6.67 + 1.67 = 33.34). Likelihood notes: partial fills and expiry are rare for notional day market orders on liquid ETFs, and the over-by-a-cent case needs specific amounts, so points 1, 2 and 6 are medium on their own. Points 3 and 4 (cash left uninvested, false success) justify high overall.

### [high] Withdraw and sell have no server-side checks, no duplicate protection and no record in FRED's database

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: controller/TradingController.java:206-258 (withdraw: only amount > 0 is checked, then the POST), 108-159 (sell by percentage), 164-201 (liquidate); service/AlpacaService.java:1401-1435 (withdrawal POST, no idempotency), 1278-1359 (sell, no client_order_id); frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.ts:315-324 and 389-398 (PIN step-up runs only in the app and only if the user set a PIN), 384 (the withdrawable-cash cap is client side only)
- Detail: The withdraw endpoint forwards any positive amount to Alpaca. It does not check withdrawable cash, the state of the ACH relationship, a daily limit, or decimal scale, and it has no idempotency key, so a double tap or a client retry after a timeout can create two withdrawals. Nothing is saved: FRED has no withdrawal or sell-order table, so there is no status tracking, no failure or return notice, and nothing to reconcile or show support. The PIN prompt before sell and withdraw exists only in the Angular page and is skipped when the user has no PIN, so the API itself accepts these calls with just a JWT. The symbol from the sell request is also placed into the Alpaca URL path after only an upper-case (line 1281), with no format check.
- Suggested fix: Add a withdrawals table and a sell-orders table (or one money-out ledger), a client UUID per tap with a unique key like the lump-sum path has, server-side checks against cash_withdrawable and relationship status, a symbol format check, a poll that follows each transfer or order to a final state, and a server-enforced step-up for money-out actions.
- Verifier (adjusted): The facts are true. TradingController.java:206-258 checks only amount > 0 and that a relationship id exists, then posts; initiateWithdrawal (AlpacaService.java:1401-1435) has no idempotency key and nothing is saved. Sell and liquidate (108-201) are the same. The PIN step-up is only in sell-withdraw.page.ts:315-324 and 389-398 and is skipped when no PIN is set; the cap at line 384 is client side. The symbol goes into the URL path after only toUpperCase (TradingController.java:138, AlpacaService.java:1281). Corrections to the impact: all three endpoints resolve the account from the authenticated user, so nobody can move another user's money; and Alpaca is the backstop for amounts, since it rejects an outgoing transfer above withdrawable cash, so "forwards any positive amount" does not mean an overdraft. The symbol path issue is limited to the caller's own account path with FRED's credentials; I rate that part medium. What remains high: a double tap or retry can create two withdrawals up to the available cash, FRED keeps no record or status of money leaving, there is no failure or return notice, and a stolen JWT alone is enough to sell and withdraw to the linked bank.

### [high] No reconciliation between FRED's records and Alpaca

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:642-659 (a transfer pending over 7 days only logs a warning; an UNKNOWN status retries forever with no alert); service/AlpacaService.java:164-170 and 191-246 (the transfer lookup searches at most 100 incoming transfers, then returns UNKNOWN); scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:45-181 (no reconciliation job among the crons); FREDdocs/INVESTMENT_SCHEDULER.md:96-97
- Detail: I found no job that compares FRED's executions, trades or cash expectations with Alpaca's transfers, orders, positions or cash. Each of the gaps above (lost ACH response, stuck trades, unrecorded withdrawals, late returns) therefore stays invisible. The existing safety nets are log lines: a 7 day pending transfer logs a warning, and a transfer that falls outside the first 100 results stays FUNDING_INITIATED indefinitely. Many alert.* log lines exist across the money code, but I did not find anything in this repo that routes them to a person (the roadmap mentions Sentry; I did not verify whether it is wired).
- Suggested fix: Add a nightly reconciliation job: for every user with activity, compare Alpaca transfers and orders for the last N days with FRED rows and flag anything on one side only, any non-terminal row older than a threshold, and idle cash above a threshold when no execution is pending. Route alert.* lines to a real channel.
- Verifier (confirmed): Verified. The 7 day pending case only logs a warning (InvestmentExecutionService.java:647-654) and UNKNOWN or ERROR just retries next cycle (655-659). checkTransferStatus searches at most 5 pages of 20 incoming transfers and then returns UNKNOWN (AlpacaService.java:183-240). InvestmentScheduler.java:45-181 has no reconciliation job; the only reconcile hits in the backend are TLH tax-lot code and a Plaid item-removal drain (SchedulingConfig.java:18), not transfers, orders or cash. On alert routing: I found no Sentry reference in backend/build.gradle, backend/src/main/resources or frontend/package.json, so the alert.* log lines (about 16 across the money code) go only to the log stream. That raises confidence from medium to high for the routing part. For a small private beta a manual daily check of the Alpaca dashboard could stand in, so high and not blocker is right.

### [high] Automatic rebalancing is promised on the tier screen but nothing executes it

- Type: app-work, effort XL, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:98-100 (Automatic portfolio rebalancing), 153-155 (Custom rebalancing schedule); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:171-201 and my-profile.page.ts:60-67,243-248 (schedule chips and a computed next rebalance date); a backend search for rebalanc finds only an unused REBALANCE enum value (service/WashSaleGuardService.java:70,107) and text mentions; service/PortfolioService.java:60-89 (changing the allocation saves percentages only)
- Detail: The tier screen says FRED periodically realigns the portfolio with no action from the user, and the profile page shows a next rebalance date. The backend has no rebalancing job, service or endpoint. Changing the target allocation only changes how future contributions are split; existing holdings are never sold or bought to match. The next rebalance date is calculated in the page from the chosen chip. I did not check whether the chosen schedule is saved server side.
- Suggested fix: Either build rebalancing (drift calculation, sell and buy orders with idempotency keys, wash-sale guard, tax and cost-basis handling, user notice) or remove the promise and the profile section before launch. The tier auditors should line this up with their feature table.
- Verifier (confirmed): Verified. investmentconfirmation.component.ts:98-99 lists "Automatic portfolio rebalancing" and 153-154 lists "Custom rebalancing schedule". my-profile.page.ts:60-67 and 243-248 compute the next rebalance date in the page from the chosen chip. A backend grep for rebalanc matches only MarketNarrativeValidator, ConversationAnalyticsService, WashSaleGuardService and TlhSummaryService; there is no rebalancing job, service or endpoint. One detail the auditor left open: the chosen schedule is saved through goalsService.savePrefs (my-profile.page.ts:735) and read back at line 910, so it persists as a preference, but nothing acts on it. If these tier claims are shown to real beta users, treat it as blocker-level for the tier screen copy (a promise about managing their money that is not kept); removing the claim is small, building it is XL.

### [high] Any logged-in user can read another account's ACH relationships and Alpaca account record

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: controller/AlpacaController.java:225-235 (GET /api/alpaca/accounts/{accountId}/ach-relationships) and 237-247 (GET /api/alpaca/account/{accountId}/status); both pass the path value straight to service/AlpacaApiService.java:559 and 623-631
- Detail: Both endpoints take an Alpaca account id from the URL and return Alpaca's raw response without checking that the id belongs to the caller. The second one returns the whole Alpaca account object, not just a status. The id is a UUID so it is hard to guess, but FRED's own positions endpoint returns the caller's account id in its response (TradingController.java:91), so ids are not treated as secret. I did not check exactly which bank and identity fields Alpaca includes in these responses.
- Suggested fix: Remove the path parameter and resolve the account from the authenticated user, as /my-account-status already does, or delete the two endpoints if the app no longer calls them.
- Verifier (confirmed): Verified. AlpacaController.java:225-235 and 237-247 take accountId from the path and pass it straight to AlpacaApiService.java:559-565 and 623-631, which return Alpaca's raw body for /accounts/{id}/ach_relationships and /accounts/{id} (the full account object, which carries contact and identity data). There is no ownership check, and SecurityConfig.java:119 only requires any authenticated user. TradingController.java:91 does return the caller's own account id in the positions response. Limits on the risk: the id is a random UUID, so an attacker needs to learn a victim's id from somewhere else (logs, support, a shared screen); I found no endpoint that lists other users' ids. The frontend still defines callers in alpaca.service.ts:158 and 166, but a grep found no page using them, so deleting the endpoints looks safe (check once more before removing). Fix is small; do it before real users.

### [medium] The Investment complete notice is sent at midnight for every execution, whatever actually happened

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/PaydayNotificationService.java:72-87 (every execution with that scheduled date gets an invested message; status is not read), 89-102 (same for the day-after message); scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:159-169 (runs at 00:00 ET for the previous day); service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:281-295 (the ACH request was only sent at 11:59 PM)
- Detail: One minute after the ACH request goes out, every execution scheduled that day gets a message saying the amount was invested. At that moment nothing is invested, the transfer takes days and may fail, and the query also picks up lump sums and rows already marked FAILED. Today this only reaches the log because email is mocked, but it becomes a false statement to users the moment a real provider is switched on.
- Suggested fix: Send the invested message from the COMPLETED transition only (notifyUserOfSuccess already exists at InvestmentExecutionService.java:1178) and change the midnight message to say the transfer has started, or drop it.

### [medium] Market-open and holiday logic is hand-rolled and incomplete

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:100-115 (isMarketOpen is weekday plus 9:30 to 16:00, no holidays or half days); model/InvestmentSchedule.java:234-280 (fixed-date holidays with no observed-day rule, no Juneteenth, no Good Friday); service/AlpacaService.java:766-782 (an Alpaca clock call exists and is used only by TLH); FREDdocs/INVESTMENT_SCHEDULER.md:164
- Detail: On a market holiday that falls on a weekday, or after an early close, the code believes the market is open and submits market orders. Alpaca will likely queue them for the next session, so this is mostly a timing and messaging problem, but the execution price is then the next open and I did not verify how Alpaca treats notional orders sent while closed. The schedule's holiday list misses Juneteenth and Good Friday and ignores observed dates (for example July 4 on a Saturday). The status poll also stops at 3:45 PM, so orders that fill after that are only marked done the next business morning.
- Suggested fix: Use the Alpaca clock and calendar endpoints for both the open check and business-day adjustment, and fail closed on error as TLH does.

### [medium] Transfer limits and input validation on money endpoints are loose or inconsistent

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: controller/InvestmentController.java:223-242 ($1 to $1,000,000, no scale check), 245-269 (type, symbol and fundingSource accepted as free strings); application.properties:56-57 and service/FredChatActionService.java:56-59 (investment.min.amount and investment.max.amount of $50,000 are read only by the chat action service); dto/CreateInvestmentScheduleRequest.java:20-25 (minimum only, frequency is just not-blank); model/InvestmentSchedule.java:189 (an unknown frequency falls back to weekly); service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:375-383 (one user's queued rows are summed into a single transfer with no cap)
- Detail: The lump-sum endpoint allows up to $1,000,000 while the configured maximum is $50,000 and is enforced only for chat-driven changes. The recurring amount has no maximum at all. My understanding is that Alpaca caps ACH at $50,000 per day per account (not verified), and because all of a user's queued rows share one transfer, one oversized lump sum would fail that night's recurring investment too. There is no per-day count or total cap. Amounts with more than two decimals are accepted. An unrecognised frequency string is stored and then treated as weekly pulls. A lump-sum type other than stock is treated as a portfolio buy, any fundingSource other than buying_power is treated as a bank pull, and the stock symbol is not checked against tradable assets before money is pulled.
- Suggested fix: One shared validator for every money-in path: configured min and max, two decimal places, per-day total cap under the broker limit, an enum for frequency, type and funding source, and a tradable-asset check on the symbol before queuing.

### [medium] The bank account that gets debited is picked by the server, not by the user

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: service/PlaidService.java:314-321 (first checking account, else the first account returned); controller/PlaidController.java:99 (only public_token is read from the request); frontend/src/app/change-bank-account/change-bank-account.page.ts:152-155 (Link metadata is logged, only the token is sent)
- Detail: After Plaid Link, the backend takes the first checking account on the Item, or the first account of any kind if there is no checking account. The account the user selected in Link is not sent to the server. A user with two checking accounts at the same bank can be debited from the one they did not choose. Whether this can happen in practice depends on the Link customization in the Plaid dashboard (single-account select), which I cannot see from the repo.
- Suggested fix: Send metadata.account_id from Link onSuccess with the public token, store that account, and reject the exchange if it is not a checking or savings account. Confirm the Link customization is set to single account select.

### [medium] ACH relationships are created from raw account and routing numbers pulled from Plaid, not a Plaid processor token

- Type: decision, effort M, auditor confidence low
- Evidence: service/PlaidToAlpacaService.java:96-100 and 127-153 (Plaid /auth/get, then account and routing numbers passed on); service/AlpacaApiService.java:428-433 (relationship created with bank_account_number and bank_routing_number), 436 (the request body is logged at debug level); a backend search for processor_token returns nothing
- Detail: The code fetches full account and routing numbers through Plaid Auth and posts them to Alpaca. The task brief mentions a processor token; none is used anywhere. I believe Alpaca's documented Plaid route for production is the processor token and that it also changes who carries which verification duty, but I did not check the docs or FRED's Alpaca agreement, so this is a question to confirm rather than a known defect. As written, FRED's server handles full bank account numbers in memory, and the debug log line would write them to logs if debug logging were ever enabled.
- Suggested fix: Ask Alpaca which method the production agreement requires. If processor tokens, switch to Plaid's processor token create call and pass the token. Either way remove the debug log of the request body.

### [medium] Account and ACH relationship status are never refreshed after approval

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: repository/UserRepository.java:62-63 (the status poller skips ACTIVE and REJECTED users); service/AchRelationshipService.java:100-101 and 210 (alpacaAchStatus is written only on adopt and on cancel), 104-121 (the schedule gets its ACH id and can go ACTIVE as soon as the relationship is created, whatever its status); controller/InvestmentController.java:211 (the lump sum only checks that ids exist)
- Detail: Once an account is ACTIVE it is never polled again, so if Alpaca later restricts, disables or closes it, FRED keeps scheduling pulls. The stored ACH status is whatever Alpaca returned at creation (often QUEUED) and is never updated to APPROVED or to a cancelled state, and nothing checks it before a transfer. If Alpaca cancels a relationship (for example after a return), FRED keeps trying each cycle and each attempt ends as FUNDING_FAILED. The lump-sum endpoint also does not check that the account status is ACTIVE.
- Suggested fix: Poll ACTIVE accounts on a slow cadence (or subscribe to Alpaca's account and transfer event streams), refresh the relationship status before each batch, and hold the schedule when the account or relationship is no longer usable.

### [medium] If turning off margin fails at account creation it is never retried

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/AlpacaService.java:1073-1101 (disableMarginTrading returns false on any error), 1107-1121 (setupCashOnlyAccount sleeps 2 seconds, tries once, logs Will retry later, and has no retry); callers at service/AlpacaApiService.java:196 and 305
- Detail: New accounts are meant to be cash only. The one attempt to set that happens two seconds after account creation, and a failure is only logged. No flag is stored and nothing retries, so an account can stay margin enabled without anyone knowing. That matters most in combination with the duplicate-order risk, because a margin account can fill orders that a cash account would reject.
- Suggested fix: Store a cash-only-confirmed flag per user, retry from the existing account status poller until it succeeds, and do not let trading start until it is set. Ask Alpaca whether the correspondent can be configured cash only by default.

### [medium] DRIP can reinvest old dividends in bulk and never checks available cash

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: service/DripService.java:44 and 304-317 (90 day lookback when a user has no DRIP rows; otherwise from the last processed dividend), 285-297 (eligibility is only the toggle and an account id), 177-178 (buy placed for the full dividend with no cash check), 190-200 (a failed reinvestment is final); 67-101 (processAllDividends loads every user and calls the per-user method directly)
- Detail: The first DRIP run for a user buys back every cash dividend from the last 90 days at once, and a user who turns DRIP off and later on again gets every dividend paid in between reinvested in one go. The cash may already have been withdrawn or used, in which case the orders fail, or it may be cash that just arrived for a scheduled investment waiting for the market to open, in which case DRIP spends it and the scheduled buy then fails. Failed reinvestments are never retried. The orders also carry no idempotency key, though the unique activity id prevents repeats in the normal case.
- Suggested fix: Start the window at the moment DRIP was enabled, skip dividends older than a few days, check available cash net of pending executions before buying, and retry failed rows a bounded number of times.

### [medium] Closing an account hard-deletes all of the user's execution, trade and dividend records

- Type: decision, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: controller/TradingController.java:333-344 (closes the Alpaca account, then calls UserDeletionService.delete); repository/InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:136-137 (deleteAllByUser); service/UserDeletionService.java:111-115 (deletes every child row, then the user); compare service/AccountStatusService.java:135-142, where the comment says no client record is hard-deleted without being asked first
- Detail: A user-initiated close removes every money record FRED holds for that person. The rejection path was deliberately changed to keep the row for recordkeeping, with a comment citing what an RIA is expected to be able to produce, but the close path still deletes everything. If a late ACH return, a dispute or a tax question arrives after the close, FRED has nothing to look at. What must be kept and for how long is a question for counsel.
