- Suggested fix: Ask counsel what transaction records must be retained after closure. Likely outcome: keep executions, trades and transfers (with personal fields minimised) and mark the user closed instead of deleting.

### [low] A queued or pending investment cannot be cancelled by the user

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: controller/InvestmentController.java:48-427 (endpoints are history, execution detail, schedule, execute, dashboard; none cancels); service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:151-158 (bank-funded lump sums wait in SCHEDULED until 11:59 PM)
- Detail: A lump sum sits queued until the nightly batch, which is a natural window to undo a mistake (wrong amount, wrong stock), but there is no cancel endpoint and no screen for it. Pausing the schedule does not remove a row that the 11:55 PM job already queued.
- Suggested fix: Add a cancel endpoint that only works while the row is SCHEDULED and unclaimed, and expose it on the activity view.

### [low] Smaller hardening items in the money path

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/AlpacaApiService.java:46 (RestTemplate built with no timeouts, used inside transactions and under a user row lock taken at service/PlaidService.java:166); service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:362-363 and service/ScheduledInvestmentProcessor.java:30-31 (the batch selects rows by text in error_message); service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:487-531 and 749-843 (unreachable immediate-funding path and a routine that parses a dollar amount out of an error message); model/InvestmentTrade.java:25-34 (quantity scale 6, average price scale 2); model/InvestmentSchedule.java:195-197 (System.out.println in date calculation); frontend/src/app/change-bank-account/change-bank-account.page.ts:152 (Plaid public token logged to the console); FREDdocs/INVESTMENT_SCHEDULER.md:59,239
- Detail: None of these loses money on its own. AlpacaApiService has no connect or read timeout, so a hung Alpaca call can hold a scheduler thread, a DB connection and a row lock. Whether a row is in tonight's batch depends on the phrase Queued for end-of-day being present in error_message, which a future edit could break silently. Two legacy code paths in the execution service are dead and one of them rewrites amounts by parsing text. Trade fills are stored with fewer decimals than Alpaca reports, so stored quantities and prices are rounded. The scheduler doc is out of date (it calls FUNDING_FAILED terminal with a user email, says Hibernate creates columns automatically, and does not describe claims, holds or job locks).
- Suggested fix: Add timeouts to AlpacaApiService, replace the error_message marker with a real column, delete the dead paths, widen the trade decimal columns in a migration, remove the println and console log, and refresh the doc once the fixes above land.

**Verified solid:**

- Startup environment guard: non-prod profiles must point at Alpaca sandbox or paper hosts and Plaid sandbox, prod must not, and prod also refuses a loopback database, a tunnel webhook URL, the leaked sandbox Plaid secret, the old dev JWT secret and the CI webhook bypass bean (config/BrokerEnvironmentGuard.java:101-152, 155-241)
- Recurring investments cannot be queued twice for the same day: unique source_key per schedule per date and unique night_key per user per night, each schedule in its own REQUIRES_NEW transaction, eligibility re-checked inside it (service/ScheduledInvestmentProcessor.java:44-85; db/migration/V6__paycheck_autopilot.sql:613-625)
- The 11:55 PM and 11:59 PM jobs take database job locks with UTC leases, so two instances or an overlapping run do not both proceed (scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:47-62; service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:281-295; service/JobLockService.java:47-68)
- The nightly ACH batch never sends two transfers for the same rows: rows are claimed in a committed transaction before the POST, already-claimed rows are excluded from selection, rows created after the batch started wait for the next night, a missing relationship fails fast with no POST, and each user's writes commit independently (service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:298-402; repository/InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:163-182)
- A missed night is caught up exactly once: a past-due schedule fires on the next run and the next date is calculated from today, not from the missed date (service/ScheduledInvestmentProcessor.java:88-91, 110-113)
- Lump-sum double taps are deduplicated: the app sends a UUID per tap and the server returns the first row for a repeat, backed by the unique source_key (controller/InvestmentController.java:271-291; frontend/src/app/lump-sum-investment/lump-sum-investment.page.ts:383-387)
- Funding status polling maps every documented Alpaca transfer status, looks the transfer up by id rather than by amount, and updates all executions that share one transfer together (service/AlpacaService.java:191-290, 995-1019; service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:562-660)
- Bank change flow: the exchange takes a no-wait row lock and rejects a second link within 60 seconds, the schedule is held with BANK_CHANGE, old relationships are cancelled (deferred while a transfer is in flight), and a new relationship is refused if it is the previous one, predates the link or does not match the Plaid mask (service/PlaidService.java:165-177, 199-212, 239-256; service/AchRelationshipService.java:83-136, 184-260)
- A user pause is never undone by the ACH machinery and a user resume never clears a system hold; status and hold are checked separately before money moves (model/InvestmentSchedule.java:356-365; service/InvestmentScheduleService.java:376-407; service/AchRelationshipService.java:110-120)
- Buying-power lump sums check Alpaca buying power before trading, and the withdraw screen caps the amount at Alpaca's cash_withdrawable (service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:453-481; service/PortfolioDashboardService.java:412-417; frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.ts:99-105, 384)
- Account close is refused while a transfer is in flight and for 60 days after the last settled transfer, checked before the Alpaca close call (service/UserDeletionService.java:98-109; controller/TradingController.java:325-331)
- Wash-sale guard is applied to scheduled buys, lump-sum stock buys and DRIP (service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:131-140, 955-972; service/DripService.java:151-170), and TLH is off by default (application.properties:82)
- DRIP will not reinvest the same dividend twice: each Alpaca activity id is stored and checked before any order (service/DripService.java:119-124, 141-143)
- Alpaca account id, ACH relationship id and Plaid access token are stored encrypted and decrypted only at the call site; the Plaid access token is never returned to the app (service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:199-201, 211; service/PlaidToAlpacaService.java:49-56; controller/PlaidController.java:134-143)
- AlpacaService (transfers and orders) has a 5 second connect and 30 second read timeout (service/AlpacaService.java:65-69)
- All money crons run in America/New_York and are switched off by investment.scheduler.enabled=false for CI (scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:45-181; config/SchedulerGate.java:19-38)
- Plaid production settings come only from environment variables with plaid.env fixed to production in the prod profile (backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:21-27)

## onboarding-kyc-lifecycle

Onboarding, KYC and account lifecycle audit, read from the working tree. The happy path is built end to end (get-started, survey, plan results, passkey, 2-step KYC, Plaid, schedule, confirmation, tabs) and resume-by-progress works. It is not ready for real users. The biggest gaps: (1) account recovery never emails the code (it is printed to the server log) and the code can be brute-forced, (2) the legal layer is placeholder (dead agreement, ToS and Privacy links, no stored record of consent, a margin agreement signed for a cash-only account, a fake trusted contact), (3) a rejected or pending applicant sees nothing in the app and all transactional email is mocked, (4) account closure is hidden, impossible for users without a brokerage account, and blocked for 60 days after any settled transfer, which is effectively every active investor, (5) no sign-out, no email verification anywhere, and SSNs sit in plaintext in localStorage (applicant) and in the database (beneficiaries). I did not run the app or call Alpaca, so anything that depends on Alpaca's actual responses is marked lower confidence.


### [blocker] Account recovery code is never emailed, is printed to the server log, and can be brute-forced

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:46-56 (OTP generated then System.out.println 'MOCK EMAIL'), :61-99 (verify has no attempt counter or lockout, clears all passkeys, issues a JWT); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/config/SecurityConfig.java:113 (/api/auth/** permitAll); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/LoggingEmailSender.java:15 (only EmailSender implementation)
- Detail: Recovery is the only path for a user who lost their passkey or changed phones without iCloud Keychain. Today the 6-digit code is written to stdout in plain text and no email is sent, so a real user can never recover. The verify endpoint is public and has no rate limit or attempt cap, so within the 15 minute window the code can be guessed by trying all values. A successful guess deletes the victim's passkeys and returns a full session token for a brokerage account. No rate limiting library or filter was found in the backend.
- Suggested fix: Send the code through EmailService with a real provider, remove the System.out lines, add a per-user attempt counter (for example 5 tries then invalidate the code), throttle /initiate and /verify per email and per IP, and compare codes in constant time. Consider a cooling-off period on withdrawals and bank changes after a recovery.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. RecoveryController.java:46-56 generates the code and prints it with System.out.println; it does not call EmailService at all. /verify (61-99) has no attempt counter, uses String.equals, clears all passkeys (91) and returns a JWT (96). /initiate also has no throttle. SecurityConfig.java:113 permits /api/auth/**. A grep of the backend for ratelimit, bucket4j, RateLimiter and attempt counters returned nothing. LoggingEmailSender is the only EmailSender implementation. Small extra: nextInt(999999) at line 46 can never produce 999999.

### [blocker] All transactional email is mocked, so the KYC rejection notice (and any future notice) is never delivered

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:56-62 (emailEnabled false records MOCKED and returns); backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:47-49 (EMAIL_ENABLED default false, provider 'logging', from noreply@investingapp.com); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AccountStatusService.java:167-171 (rejection email)
- Detail: This is wider than the known market breakdown mock. The rejection email in handleRejected goes through the same mocked facade, and the default From address is a placeholder domain. Combined with the missing in-app rejected state (separate finding) a rejected applicant is told nothing at all. An adverse action notice is likely a regulatory expectation; confirm wording with counsel.
- Suggested fix: Implement a real EmailSender (the analytics spec names Resend), set the From domain to fredvested.com with SPF/DKIM, and route recovery, rejection, action-required and account-closed notices through it. Wording of the rejection notice is a counsel item.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. EmailService.java:56-62 records MOCKED and returns when app.email.enabled is false; application.properties:47-49 defaults are enabled=false, from=noreply@investingapp.com, provider=logging. AccountStatusService.handleRejected sends the rejection notice through this facade. Only LoggingEmailSender implements EmailSender, so even EMAIL_ENABLED=true would only log. Blocker stands mainly because recovery depends on a real sender; note the recovery code does not even go through EmailService today.

### [blocker] Alpaca agreements: links are dead, margin agreement is signed for a cash-only account, and FRED keeps no record of consent

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/kyc-verification/kyc-verification.component.html:218-222 (three href='#' links with preventDefault and a TODO); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AlpacaApiService.java:256-262 (TODO, customer_agreement + margin_agreement + account_agreement all stamped with one server timestamp and IP), :303-305 (account then set to cash-only); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AlpacaController.java:91-95 (IP from CF-Connecting-IP, else remoteAddr); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/KycSubmissionService.java:78-109 (nothing about agreements persisted)
- Detail: The user ticks one box agreeing to three Alpaca documents they cannot open. The backend then tells Alpaca the user signed all three, including a margin agreement, even though the account is immediately switched to cash-only. The timestamp and IP are sent to Alpaca but FRED stores no local evidence (which documents, which version, when, from where). The IP capture itself is done correctly server side. If the prod backend is not behind Cloudflare the fallback getRemoteAddr will record the proxy IP.
- Suggested fix: Get the real agreement URLs from Alpaca and open them in an in-app browser. Ask Alpaca whether margin_agreement should be sent at all for cash accounts. Persist an agreements audit row per user (agreement name, version or URL, signed_at, IP). Have the request carry an explicit agreementsAccepted=true that the backend validates rather than signing unconditionally.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. kyc-verification.component.html:218-222 has three href='#' links with preventDefault and a TODO. AlpacaApiService.java:256-262 stamps customer, margin and account agreements with one timestamp and IP, then calls setupCashOnlyAccount (303-305). The create request built in kyc-verification.component.ts submitKycCreate does not carry the checkbox value, and CreateAlpacaAccountRequest has no agreement field, so the backend signs unconditionally. KycSubmissionService.persistSubmission stores nothing about agreements. IP capture at AlpacaController.java:91-95 is as described. Whether margin_agreement is required by Alpaca for cash accounts is an Alpaca question, not verified.

### [blocker] FRED Terms of Service and Privacy Policy do not exist in the app and acceptance is not recorded

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.html:124-137 (both links href='#' with preventDefault); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:675-687 (agreedToTerms is only a local boolean, only completeStep is sent), :433-439 (resuming at step 2 sets agreedToTerms = true without the user ticking it); frontend/src/app/tab3/tab3.page.ts:336-338 (Legal Information shows a 'coming soon' toast); FREDdocs/backlog.md:227-229 (FRED-138 sleeping)
- Detail: A user cannot read the documents they agree to, and the server never learns they agreed. No column or table for ToS acceptance was found in backend or frontend (grep for tosAccept/termsAccept and similar returned only the local boolean). Apple requires a reachable privacy policy inside the app. The same screen is where the recurring bank debit is authorized, and no authorization record is written either: the ach_authorizations table from V6 has no writer (UserDeletionService.java:35-36 says so). The documents themselves are a counsel deliverable; the wiring is app work.
- Suggested fix: Host ToS, Privacy Policy and the advisory agreement / Form CRS, link them from the confirmation screen and from Settings > Legal Information, and store acceptance server side (document version, timestamp, IP). Do not auto-tick the box on resume. Ask counsel what recurring ACH authorization language and record are needed.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. investmentconfirmation.component.html:135-137 links are href='#' with preventDefault; tab3.page.ts:336-338 shows a coming soon toast for Legal Information; no route or page for terms or privacy exists in frontend/src/app. Backend grep for tosAccept, termsAccept, agreedToTerms and similar found nothing, and ach_authorizations appears only in a comment (UserDeletionService.java:35). One detail is overstated: the auto-tick on resume (ts:436-438) only fires when the saved step is 2, and step 2 can only be reached after ticking the box (proceedToTrustStep returns if not agreed, line 318), so it restores an earlier tick rather than inventing one. Still worth removing once acceptance is recorded server side.

### [blocker] Rejected and pending applicants get no in-app state at all

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/services/account-status.service.ts:13-22 (accountStatus$ exists) but the only consumers are frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:28-30 (action-required banner), frontend/src/app/tabs/tabs.page.ts:73,82 (badge, one refresh in ngOnInit) and frontend/src/app/recurring-investments/recurring-investments.page.ts:479; frontend/src/app/components/kyc-verification/kyc-verification.component.ts:518-523 (after submit the user goes straight to /link-bank); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AccountStatusService.java:125,221 (push notification TODOs)
- Detail: Onboarding does not wait for or show the broker's decision. After KYC submit the user links a bank, sets a schedule, picks a tier and lands in the tabs. If Alpaca rejects them the backend marks REJECTED and holds the schedule (good), but the app shows a normal, empty dashboard forever with no explanation. A user who is still SUBMITTED or APPROVAL_PENDING also sees nothing saying their account is under review. Status is fetched once when the tabs page is created and never again, and there is no push. A grep for 'under review', 'pending' style copy found nothing.
- Suggested fix: Add an account-status surface on tab1 or app-wide: 'under review', 'action required' (with the upload CTA), and a terminal 'not approved' screen with support contact and a way to delete data. Refresh status on app resume and tab activation. Decide with counsel what a rejected user may still do in the app.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. accountStatus$ has no subscribers; only actionRequired$ is consumed (my-profile.page.html:28, tab3.page.ts:175, the tabs badge) and isAccountActive is used once in recurring-investments.page.ts:479. refreshStatus is called only in TabsPage.ngOnInit (tabs.page.ts:82) and after a document upload. No copy for under review or not approved exists in the frontend. After KYC submit the app navigates straight to /link-bank (kyc-verification.component.ts success handler). Backend handleRejected holds the schedule and sends a mocked email. Minor correction: tab3 is also a consumer of the action-required flag. For a very small hand-held beta the founder could contact rejected users manually, but with email mocked too the user gets nothing, so blocker is fair.

### [blocker] Alpaca endpoints take an arbitrary account id with no ownership check

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AlpacaController.java:50-60 (GET /api/alpaca/account), :225-235 (GET /accounts/{accountId}/ach-relationships), :237-247 (GET /account/{accountId}/status returns the raw Alpaca account)
- Detail: Any logged-in user can request another customer's Alpaca account record or bank relationships if they know or obtain that account's UUID. The ids are hard to guess, but the response is full broker account data. These endpoints overlap the security auditor's domain; listed here because they sit in the KYC controller. I did not find frontend callers for the path-parameter versions in this pass.
- Suggested fix: Delete these endpoints if unused, or resolve the account id from the authenticated user as the other methods in this controller do.
- Verifier (adjusted): Worse than stated. GET /api/alpaca/account (AlpacaController.java:50-60) takes no id at all: it calls AlpacaApiService.getAccountInfo, which does GET brokerBaseUrl + /accounts (AlpacaApiService.java:76-84), meaning it returns the broker's list of ALL FRED customer accounts to any logged-in user. That removes the 'ids are hard to guess' mitigation: a user can list every account id and then call /account/{accountId}/status (237-247) and /accounts/{accountId}/ach-relationships (225-235) for anyone. The controller has no role or profile gate and SecurityConfig only requires authentication. Anyone can become an authenticated user by registering a passkey. The frontend does have wrappers for all three (alpaca.service.ts:133, 158, 166); I did not trace whether they are still called. Must be fixed before any real customer data exists.

### [high] Account deletion does not meet Apple 5.1.1(v): hidden, unavailable to users without a brokerage account, and blocked for 60 days after any settled transfer

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.html:115-145 (close box only rendered inside the 'No Holdings Found' empty state); frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.ts:562-564, 640-647 (deferred close only shows a toast); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/TradingController.java:320-331 (404 when no Alpaca account; 409 DELETION_DEFERRED); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/UserDeletionService.java:43-44, 98-109 (60 day settlement window)
- Detail: The API and UI exist, with passkey step-up and a confirm sheet, but: (a) the button lives only in Sell and Withdraw and only appears once holdings are zero, so most users will never find it; (b) a user who signed up but never finished KYC, or was rejected before an account id was stored, gets 404 and cannot delete their FRED data; (c) any user whose last deposit settled within 60 days is refused, and since deposits recur every paycheck, an active investor can never close without first pausing for two months. The refusal is a toast and nothing is recorded, so the user has to remember to come back. Apple accepts delays for regulated accounts but expects the user to be able to start the request in the app. Open positions are handled by requiring the user to sell and withdraw by hand first; there is no guided flow and no check for an in-flight withdrawal beyond Alpaca refusing the close.
- Suggested fix: Add a clear 'Close account' entry in Account and Security. Store a deletion request (requested_at, status) so the request is accepted immediately and completed by a job once positions are zero, cash is withdrawn and the settlement window has passed; pause the schedule at request time. Support deletion for users with no Alpaca account. Show the pending-closure state in the app.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts are all true: the close box is only inside the positions.length === 0 empty state (sell-withdraw.page.html:115-145), reached through Sell and Withdraw (tab3.page.ts:95-98, 295); TradingController.java:320-323 returns 404 when there is no Alpaca account id; deferralReason (UserDeletionService.java:98-109) refuses for 60 days after the last settled funding, and the client only shows a toast (sell-withdraw.page.ts:640-647). Severity lowered to high because the cited rule is an App Store review requirement, which by the rubric is a must fix before public launch, not something that stops a private beta. Treat it as a hard gate for App Store submission (and likely for external TestFlight review).

### [high] Closing an account hard-deletes all client records (trades, executions, schedules, beneficiaries)

- Type: decision, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/UserDeletionService.java:142-165 (deletes trades, executions, drip, beneficiaries, schedules, sessions, portfolio, goals, user); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AccountStatusService.java:138-155 (comment: rejected rows are kept for RIA recordkeeping and SSN retention is an open counsel question as of 2026-10-24)
- Detail: The rejection path was deliberately changed to keep the row for recordkeeping, but the user-initiated close path still deletes everything, including the trade and transfer history. An adviser is normally expected to retain client records for years. This conflicts with the rule written in the code's own comments. Not confirmed with counsel; flagged as a decision.
- Suggested fix: Ask counsel what must be retained after closure and for how long. Likely outcome: soft-close (status CLOSED, credentials and sessions removed, PII minimised) with retained financial records, plus a retention purge job.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. UserDeletionService.java:142-165 deletes trades, executions, drip, beneficiaries, schedules, sessions, portfolio, goals and the user row. The handleRejected comment in AccountStatusService says rejected rows are now kept for RIA recordkeeping and that SSN retention is open with counsel. The class comment at UserDeletionService.java:27 still lists handleRejected as a caller, which is stale. Correctly typed as a decision for counsel.

### [high] Trusted contact sent to Alpaca is the account holder themselves

- Type: decision, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AlpacaApiService.java:264-268 (comment 'use the account holder's own info'), :275; grep for trusted contact across frontend and backend finds only this file
- Detail: Every account is opened with a trusted contact equal to the applicant's own name and email. That is false data on a brokerage application. FINRA 4512 requires a reasonable effort to collect a real trusted contact, and the customer may decline; it does not allow inventing one. There is no UI to add, skip or later edit a trusted contact.
- Suggested fix: Confirm with Alpaca whether trusted_contact can be omitted. Then either omit it or add an optional trusted contact step in KYC plus an edit screen in Account and Security.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. AlpacaApiService.java:264-268 builds trusted_contact from the applicant's own given name, family name and email, and it is always included in the payload (275). No trusted contact field exists in CreateAlpacaAccountRequest or the KYC form. Whether Alpaca allows omitting it was not verified; that is an Alpaca question.

### [high] KYC form has no age, citizenship or state eligibility checks and hardcodes US citizenship and birth country

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/kyc-verification/kyc-verification.component.ts:66-75 (DOB validator checks only that the date is real), :389-394 (max is today), :21-48 (all 50 states plus DC, no restriction list); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AlpacaApiService.java:243-245 (country_of_citizenship, country_of_birth, country_of_tax_residence all 'USA'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/dto/CreateAlpacaAccountRequest.java:45-49 (DOB and tax id only @NotBlank)
- Detail: A 15 year old can submit. A non-citizen (green card or visa holder) is declared to Alpaca as a US citizen born in the USA, which is a false statement on the application; the user is never asked. There is no employment status or employer question, which brokers normally collect. There is no server-side validation of DOB format, age, SSN format, state code or postal code, so the frontend is the only guard. No state restriction exists; whether FRED's RIA registration limits which states it can serve is a counsel question.
- Suggested fix: Add an 18+ check on both client and server. Ask citizenship and country of birth (or explicitly restrict to US citizens with a clear decline screen, and add permanent resident and visa fields if supported). Add server-side validation for all KYC fields. Confirm with Alpaca whether employment fields are required and with counsel which states are allowed.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. dateOfBirthValidator (kyc-verification.component.ts:66-75) only checks the date is real; the date sheet max is today. AlpacaApiService.java:243-245 hardcodes country_of_citizenship, country_of_birth and country_of_tax_residence to USA. CreateAlpacaAccountRequest.java has only @NotBlank on DOB, tax id, state and postal code (email has @Email). In practice Alpaca itself will likely reject a minor, so the age gap is mostly a wasted application; the false citizenship statement is the more serious part. The employment-field claim was not verified against Alpaca's requirements.

### [high] KYC resubmission dead end: the 'KYC done' flag is set by a second client call, and a retry hits a 409 the app does not handle

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/kyc-verification/kyc-verification.component.ts:515-538 (success then completeStep('kycVerification'); error shows err.error?.message || err.message); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AlpacaController.java:102-113 (409 with keys 'error' and 'code', no 'message'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/KycSubmissionService.java:99-109 (sets accountStatus but not the progress flag)
- Detail: If the app is killed, loses network, or the progress PUT fails after Alpaca accepted the account but before kycVerificationCompleted is saved, the next launch routes back to the KYC form. Submitting again returns 409 ACCOUNT_ALREADY_EXISTS. The error handler reads a 'message' field that the backend does not send, so the user sees Angular's raw 'Http failure response for https://... 409' text and cannot move on without support. The same wrong field is read for normal failures, so Alpaca validation errors (returned under 'error', with raw Alpaca JSON appended at AlpacaApiService.java:317) also show as raw HTTP text.
- Suggested fix: Set kycVerificationCompleted server side inside KycSubmissionService.persistSubmission. On the client treat 409 ACCOUNT_ALREADY_EXISTS as success and continue. Map backend errors to friendly, field-specific messages instead of passing through raw text.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. persistSubmission sets accountStatus SUBMITTED but not the progress flag; the client sets it with a separate completeStep call. AlpacaController.java:109-111 returns 409 with keys error and code only, and all other failures use the error key too (117-118, 139-141), while the client reads err.error?.message || err.message, so the user sees Angular's raw Http failure text. Narrowing: if the completeStep call fails the client still navigates on, and UserController.validateAndFixStepDependencies (836-845) auto-completes KYC once a later step is done, so the dead end needs the app to be killed or progress lost before link-bank completes. The wrong error field affects every KYC failure, which is the more common hit.

### [high] Applicant SSN and full identity are saved unencrypted in localStorage

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/kyc-verification/kyc-verification.component.ts:265-287 (saveStep1Draft writes step1Form.value, which includes taxId, DOB, name, address), :453 (called on Continue), :519 (cleared only on successful submit); FREDdocs/backlog.md:400 (already logged)
- Detail: The step-1 draft includes the full SSN and stays on the device until the account is created. A user who stops at step 2 leaves it there indefinitely. It survives app restarts and is readable by any script in the webview.
- Suggested fix: Exclude taxId (and ideally DOB) from the draft, or keep the draft in memory only. Clear any existing draft keys on app start.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. saveStep1Draft writes the whole step1Form.value (includes taxId) to localStorage key fred_kyc_step1_<userId> on Continue, and clearStep1Draft runs only in the create success handler. Already logged as FRED-221 in FREDdocs/backlog.md with the note that it must be fixed before any real user. Exposure is limited to the app's own webview storage, but the fix is small and should land before real users.

### [high] Beneficiary SSNs are stored in plaintext in the database

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/Beneficiary.java:68-71 (column length 11 with pattern XXX-XX-XXXX, so it holds the raw value); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/BeneficiaryService.java:188,219,299 (EncryptionService used only for the Alpaca account id); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AlpacaService.java:1177 (raw value sent to Alpaca)
- Detail: The account holder's own SSN is encrypted and hashed (KycSubmissionService.java:89-90) but third-party beneficiary SSNs are not. I also could not confirm that Alpaca's account PATCH accepts a 'beneficiaries' array in this shape; the code marks beneficiaries APPROVED purely on a 2xx response.
- Suggested fix: Encrypt the column with EncryptionService (migration to widen the column and re-encrypt existing rows), return only the last four digits to the client, and verify the Alpaca beneficiaries call in sandbox.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. Beneficiary.java:68-71 is a length 11 column with the XXX-XX-XXXX pattern, so it holds the raw value. BeneficiaryService uses EncryptionService only to decrypt the Alpaca account id (188, 219, 299), and AlpacaService.java:1177 sends getSocialSecurityNumber() straight to Alpaca. I did not check whether the API returns the full SSN to the client or whether Alpaca accepts this beneficiaries payload; both remain unverified.

### [high] No email verification at sign-up or on change; change-email has no server-side step-up and does not update Alpaca

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:869-908 (update-email: only checks non-empty and not in use, then saves and issues a new JWT); frontend/src/app/pages/change-email/change-email.page.ts:85-101, 158-165 (passkey or PIN check happens only in the client before the POST); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/WebAuthnController.java:83-97 (registration takes any email); grep for emailVerified / verifyEmail returns nothing
- Detail: Email is the recovery factor and the login identity, yet it is never proven to belong to the user. A typo at sign-up or in change-email silently makes recovery impossible. Anyone holding a valid session token can call update-email directly, point the account at their own inbox, then use recovery to replace the passkeys. The new address is not format-checked on the server and is not pushed to Alpaca, so broker notices keep going to the old address. Separately, the KYC edit form can change the Alpaca email without changing the FRED login email (kyc-verification.component.ts:543), so the two can drift either way.
- Suggested fix: Verify email with a code at sign-up and before committing a change. Require a fresh passkey assertion on the server for update-email. Validate format server side, notify the old address, and patch Alpaca's contact email in the same operation. Make email read-only in the KYC edit form.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. UserController.java:869-908 only checks non-empty, optional currentEmail match and not in use, then saves and issues a new JWT; no format check, no step-up, no Alpaca update. The passkey or PIN check in change-email.page.ts runs only in the client before the POST. Grep for emailVerified or verifyEmail in backend and frontend returned nothing. The takeover path needs a stolen session token first, and recovery email is not delivered today, so exploitation depends on the recovery fix landing; severity high is right.

### [high] Document upload (ACTION_REQUIRED) flow is incomplete

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/document-upload/document-upload.component.ts:38-43 (fixed list of four types, no read of what Alpaca asked for), :115-119 (success clears flag then refreshStatus); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AlpacaController.java:164-199 (upload does not change accountStatus; no size or mime validation); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AlpacaApiService.java:336-340 (document_sub_type omitted); frontend/ios/App/App/Info.plist:46-47 (only NSFaceIDUsageDescription present)
- Detail: The user is told 'additional verification needed' but not which document Alpaca wants or why; nothing reads Alpaca's kyc_results. After a successful upload the local status is still ACTION_REQUIRED, so the banner and badge come straight back after the re-fetch and the user cannot tell the upload worked. The file input accepts image/* and offers the camera, but Info.plist has no camera or photo library usage strings, which normally crashes the app when the camera is chosen (not device-tested). The backend accepts any mime type and size up to whatever the server allows as a base64 JSON body. The banner only lives on my-profile, and status is only fetched once per tabs page creation.
- Suggested fix: Read the requested document types from Alpaca's account details and show them. Track an 'uploaded, in review' state locally. Add NSCameraUsageDescription and NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription. Validate mime type and size on the server. Verify in Alpaca sandbox with an ACTION_REQUIRED fixture account.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. document-upload.component.ts:38-43 is a fixed list of four types; nothing in the backend reads kyc_results (grep empty). The upload endpoint (AlpacaController.java:164-199) does not change accountStatus, and clearActionRequired immediately re-fetches status (account-status.service.ts:58-62), so the banner returns. The request DTO has only @NotBlank; the 10 MB limit is client side only and the multipart limits in application.properties do not apply to a JSON body. Info.plist has only NSFaceIDUsageDescription. The input is accept='image/*,.pdf', and iOS normally offers Take Photo for that, which needs NSCameraUsageDescription; the crash was not device tested. The photo library picker normally does not need a usage string.

### [medium] No sign-out anywhere in the app

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/tab3/tab3.page.ts:69-161 (settings menu has no log out item), :416-420 (onLogout is a console.log stub); frontend/src/app/services/auth.service.ts:197-205 (logout only clears local state, no server call); FREDdocs/backlog.md:412 (already logged)
- Detail: A user cannot sign out, for example before handing over or selling a phone. The existing logout() also does not revoke the server session row, so the token stays valid until it expires.
- Suggested fix: Add a Log out item in Account and Security that revokes the current session on the server, clears local storage and the keychain email hint, and returns to get-started with a visible sign-in path.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts are true: tab3.page.ts:415-419 onLogout is a console.log stub, the settings menu has no log out item, no template contains log out or sign out, and AuthService.logout (197-205) makes no server call. Security settings can revoke other sessions but not the current one (security-settings.page.html:130). Severity lowered: it does not put money at risk, the app has passkey auth and an optional app lock, and it is not an App Store requirement. The backlog rates it LOW. Medium is a fair middle; fix it alongside the sign-in path work.

### [medium] Returning user on a new or wiped phone has no direct 'Sign in' path

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/get-started/get-started.component.html:180 (only action is the set-up CTA); frontend/src/app/components/authfinalize/authfinalize.component.html:67-70 and authfinalize.component.ts:413-415 ('Already have an account?' goes to /recovery); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:739-763 (re-auth prompt only for a device id that already completed auth-finalize); frontend/src/app/services/keychain-sync.service.ts:17-37 (iCloud Keychain email hint, iOS only)
- Detail: Automatic passkey re-auth depends on the device id having been seen before or on an email hint synced through iCloud Keychain. A user without Keychain sync, or after a fresh install where the hint is missing, must click through the intro, survey and plan screens to reach 'Already have an account?', which leads to email recovery (which wipes passkeys and is currently non-functional) rather than a simple passkey sign-in. I did not trace every branch of AppLockService, so the exact behaviour when the hint is present was not verified on device.
- Suggested fix: Add 'I already have an account' on get-started that runs the usernameless passkey sign-in first and offers email recovery only as the fallback.
- Verifier (adjusted): Partly overstated. There is an automatic sign-in attempt on a device with no known user: auth.service.ts:138-150 calls promptForPasskeyReauth, which reads the iCloud Keychain email hint (514-531) and starts passkey authentication for that account. It silently does nothing when the hint is missing, and then the only manual route is 'Already have an account?' on auth-finalize, which goes to /recovery (authfinalize.component.ts:413-415); get-started has a single set-up CTA. So the gap is real but narrow: passkeys themselves normally travel by iCloud Keychain, so a user whose hint did not sync usually has no passkey on the new phone either and needs recovery anyway. A visible sign-in button is still a small, worthwhile fix. Not device tested.

### [medium] Broker account status is never re-checked once ACTIVE

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/repository/UserRepository.java:60-63 (poller excludes ACTIVE and REJECTED); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/AccountStatusScheduler.java:3-4 (TODO to move to Alpaca SSE events); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AccountStatusService.java:97-107
- Detail: If Alpaca later restricts, disables or closes an active account (for example ACCOUNT_UPDATED after a KYC edit, DISABLED, ACCOUNT_CLOSED), FRED keeps treating it as ACTIVE and the scheduler keeps trying to move money. The poller is also a single transaction over all pending users with one HTTP call each, which is fine for a beta but will not scale. No job lock on the status poll, unlike the ACH poll.
- Suggested fix: Subscribe to Alpaca account status events or add a slower re-poll of ACTIVE accounts, and handle the non-happy statuses (pause schedules, show a banner).

### [medium] Onboarding progress flags are fully client-writable

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:400-481 (PUT /user/progress sets any flag from the body), :790-856 (later flags auto-complete earlier ones)
- Detail: Any authenticated client can mark KYC, bank link and confirmation complete without doing them, and the dependency fixer will fill in the rest. Money movement is still safe because the scheduler requires an ACTIVE Alpaca account and an ACH relationship, but the app can be put into a 'fully onboarded' state with no account, and funnel analytics built on these flags can be wrong.
- Suggested fix: Derive kycVerificationCompleted, linkPlaidCompleted and investmentScheduleCompleted on the server from real state (Alpaca account id, Plaid token, schedule row) and ignore them in the PUT.

### [medium] Security Settings calls an endpoint that does not exist, so the account number never shows

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/security-settings/security-settings.page.ts:151-171 (POST /alpaca/sync-account-number); grep for 'sync-account-number' in backend/src returns nothing
- Detail: The brokerage account number row in Account Security will always be empty because the call 404s and the error is swallowed. Users need this number for ACATS transfers and support.
- Suggested fix: Add a GET endpoint that returns the decrypted account number for the current user (AlpacaApiService.getAccountNumber already exists at line 633) or remove the row.

### [medium] Passkey registration and recovery leave the user stuck on some failures

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/authfinalize/authfinalize.component.ts:375-385 (finish failure sets errorMessage but never resets isLoading); frontend/src/app/pages/recovery/recovery.page.ts:76-113 with backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:86-92 (passkeys are deleted at verify; if the OS passkey prompt is then cancelled the user has a token but no passkey and the code is already consumed); frontend/src/app/components/authfinalize/authfinalize.component.html:17 (a debug 'Try loading saved data' button is user-facing)
- Detail: If the finish-registration call fails the Create button stays in its loading state. In recovery, cancelling the passkey prompt after the code was accepted leaves an account with zero passkeys; the toast says 'try again' but the code no longer works, and once the token expires the user must recover again. An already-registered email at sign-up shows 'Could not start registration: Email is already taken.' with no pointer to sign in.
- Suggested fix: Reset isLoading on every failure branch. In recovery, delete old passkeys only after the new one is registered, and let the user retry passkey creation with the token they already hold. On 'email taken', offer sign-in.

### [low] Onboarding screens show raw technical text and log sensitive data

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/linkplaid/linkplaid.component.ts:118 (console.log of the Plaid public token and metadata), :142-146 ('Link flow exited: ...', 'Link flow closed by user.'), :165-169 ('Error: ...'); frontend/src/app/components/document-upload/document-upload.component.ts:123 (reads err.error?.message but backend sends 'error'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AuthController.java:67-143 (legacy /testuser and password /login endpoints still exposed under permitAll)
- Detail: Status lines on the bank link screen read like developer messages. The Plaid public token and account metadata are written to the console. Document upload errors show raw HTTP text for the same wrong-field reason as KYC. The old password login and test endpoints are dead code on a public path.
- Suggested fix: Replace status strings with user copy, strip sensitive console logs in production builds, fix the error field, and remove the legacy auth endpoints.

### [low] Marketing email consent and pre-auth survey data handling

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:665-667 (TODO: persist marketing preference), :691 (agreedToMarketing sent via updateUserProfile only at final submit); FREDdocs/PROGRESS_TRACKING_SYSTEM.md (pre-auth progress in localStorage, device-specific)
- Detail: Marketing consent is sent once at the end with a fire-and-forget call whose failure is ignored. Pre-auth progress (get-started, survey, plan results) is localStorage only, which is by design and works for resume on the same device. The progress doc still describes behaviour accurately at a high level but predates the JWT next-step hint and the localStorage fallback hold in app.component.ts:300-304.
- Suggested fix: Confirm the marketing flag is persisted by PUT /user/profile and handle failure. Refresh the progress doc.

**Verified solid:**

- Route order and resume: app.component.ts:325-349 routes by server progress in the documented order, and a logged-in user whose progress load failed is held rather than ejected to get-started (app.component.ts:300-304).
- Duplicate brokerage accounts are prevented: a second POST /create-account is refused with 409 before calling Alpaca (AlpacaController.java:97-113).
- KYC persistence is transactional and tied to the JWT user, not the request email; SSN is encrypted and hashed, Alpaca account id and number are encrypted (KycSubmissionService.java:65-115).
- Agreement timestamp and client IP are captured server side from CF-Connecting-IP, not trusted from the client (AlpacaController.java:91-95, AlpacaApiService.java:224, 258-262).
- The four regulatory disclosure questions (control person, FINRA affiliation, PEP, family PEP) are asked and passed through to Alpaca (kyc-verification.component.html:162-204, AlpacaApiService.java:248-254).
- New accounts are configured cash-only after creation (AlpacaApiService.java:303-305).
- KYC rejection keeps the user row, records the decision, holds every schedule with MANUAL_REVIEW, and stops re-polling (AccountStatusService.java:157-207).
- Scheduled investing only runs for users whose accountStatus is ACTIVE, and resume is refused otherwise (InvestmentScheduleService.java:293, 379-383).
- Account status poller and ACH retry run on a schedule behind SchedulerGate, with a job lock on the ACH poll (AccountStatusScheduler.java:39-73).
- Document upload endpoint is restricted to the caller's own account and to ACTION_REQUIRED status (AlpacaController.java:164-185).
- Close-account API refuses while funding is in flight, calls Alpaca close before deleting, removes the ACH relationship, queues Plaid item removal and analytics person deletion, and deletes child rows in one transaction (TradingController.java:310-369, UserDeletionService.java:116-167).
- Close-account UI requires zero positions and zero withdrawable cash, a passkey step-up and a destructive confirm sheet, then clears local storage and analytics identity (sell-withdraw.page.ts:562-632).
- Session list and per-session revoke exist and revocation is enforced on every request for tokens that carry a session id (UserController.java:910-959, AuthTokenFilter.java:58-70).
- Simulated passkey bypass is gated: button only when env.local (authfinalize.component.html:63) and the backend bean exists only in local/dev/test/ci profiles and requires the dev-tools key (SimulatedAttestationService.java:29-62).
- KYC edit mode (Security Settings > Update) prefills from Alpaca, hides SSN editing, and patches Alpaca (kyc-verification.component.ts:176-263, 541-572; AlpacaController.java:340-375).
- Plaid link in onboarding handles the OAuth return, clears stale link tokens, and still advances if the progress write fails (linkplaid.component.ts:70-209).
- Back navigation is defined for each onboarding screen (survey to get-started, plan results to survey, auth-finalize to plan results, KYC to auth-finalize, link-bank to KYC).
- Beneficiaries have full CRUD plus submit endpoints and a UI (BeneficiaryController.java:37-229, routes beneficiaries, beneficiaries/add, beneficiaries/edit/:id).

## ios-appstore

The live Capacitor project is frontend/ios (Capacitor 8.4.1, bundle id com.fredvested.Fred, team 9G59L97KRT, version 1.0 build 1, iOS 15.0 minimum, iPhone and iPad). The root ios folder is a stale May 2025 copy and is not used.  The project today is a dev shell, not a shippable build. Nine things stop a TestFlight or App Store build: 1. The WebView loads https://local.fredvested.com (the laptop tunnel) instead of bundled assets. 2. Passkey registration only works through the WebView's WebAuthn, which will stop working once the app loads from capacitor://localhost. 3. The Release entitlements file is malformed and names a different domain (api.fredvested.com) than the prod passkey rpId (app.fredvested.com). 4. The scheme archives with the Debug configuration. 5. Push is not wired at any layer: no aps-environment entitlement, no AppDelegate token forwarding, and the backend only logs "Would send". 6. NSCameraUsageDescription is missing while the document upload input can open the camera, which crashes the app. 7. The iPad orientation setup is expected to fail upload validation. 8. Terms, Privacy Policy and Legal Information are dead links or a "coming soon" toast. 9. The privacy manifest does not yet declare the KYC and banking data.  I did not build or archive the project; findings come from reading config and source. The prod backend env values (WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD and similar) are not in the repo, so I could not check them.


### [blocker] App loads its UI from the dev tunnel (server.url = https://local.fredvested.com), not bundled assets

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9; frontend/ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json:5-7 (generated copy); backend/src/main/resources/application-local.properties:36-38 (Spring serves frontend/www for the Capacitor app); frontend/src/index.html:41-45 (comment assumes prod loads from the local bundle)
- Detail: capacitor.config.ts hardcodes server.url to the Cloudflare tunnel that points at Andy's laptop. There is no per-environment config and no script that strips it for release. A build made today shows a blank loading cover for any user whenever the laptop is off. App Review would also reject it as a remote-loaded web wrapper (guidelines 2.1 and 4.2). The bundled web assets in frontend/ios/App/App/public are dated Aug 29, while frontend/www is dated Nov 18, so the bundled copy is stale as well.
- Suggested fix: Make capacitor.config.ts environment-aware: only set server.url when an env var such as CAP_DEV_SERVER is present. Add a documented release script (ng build --configuration production, npx cap sync ios, archive). Then run the full app from capacitor://localhost on a device; that run will surface the passkey, Plaid and CDN findings below.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9 hardcodes server.url with no env switch, and the generated frontend/ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json:5-7 carries the same value. frontend/package.json:6-16 has no release or cap sync script. The bundled copy in frontend/ios/App/App/public is dated Aug 29 while frontend/www is dated Nov 18, so it is stale. backend application-local.properties:36-38 confirms the laptop serves the UI. I found no guard or profile gate that changes this for a Release build.

### [blocker] Passkey registration will break in a bundled build (only assertion is native; creation uses WebView WebAuthn)

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App/NativePasskeyPlugin.swift:17-19 (only an 'authenticate' method), :109-110 (assertion request only); frontend/src/app/components/authfinalize/authfinalize.component.ts:20 and :402 (create from @github/webauthn-json); frontend/src/app/pages/recovery/recovery.page.ts:17 and :89 (same); frontend/src/app/services/auth.service.ts:573-580 (login uses the native plugin)
- Detail: Sign-up and account recovery create the passkey with navigator.credentials.create() inside the WKWebView. That works today only because the page origin is https://local.fredvested.com, which matches the rpId. In a bundled build the origin is capacitor://localhost. WebAuthn requires the rpId to match the page origin, so creation should fail with a SecurityError and no new user could finish sign-up. I have not run this on a bundled build; confirm on device. Login is not affected because it already goes through ASAuthorizationController.
- Suggested fix: Add a native 'register' method to NativePasskeyPlugin using ASAuthorizationPlatformPublicKeyCredentialProvider.createCredentialRegistrationRequest. Return attestationObject and clientDataJSON in the JSON shape the backend expects. Use it in authfinalize and recovery when NativePasskeyService.isAvailable. Also move change-email (pages/change-email/change-email.page.ts:22) and passkey-prompt off the web 'get' if they do not already branch to native.
- Verifier (confirmed): Facts check out. NativePasskeyPlugin.swift:17-19 exposes only 'authenticate' and :109-110 builds only an assertion request. authfinalize.component.ts:20 and recovery.page.ts:17/:89 call create() from @github/webauthn-json with no native branch. Login (auth.service.ts:573) and passkey-prompt (passkey-prompt.component.ts:275) already branch to native. Correction to the fix text: change-email.page.ts:140 calls web get() with no native branch, so it has the same problem; passkey-prompt does not. The failure itself is not device-verified and only appears once finding 1 is fixed (origin becomes capacitor://localhost), so confidence stays medium. It still blocks sign-up in any bundled build if it fails as expected.

### [blocker] Release entitlements are malformed and point at the wrong passkey domain

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App/Release.entitlements:5-6 (associated-domains is a <string>, value webcredentials:api.fredvested.com); frontend/src/environments/environment.prod.ts:7-8 (rpId 'app.fredvested.com', API at api.fredvested.com); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AASAController.java:21-36; backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:31-33 (rp id comes from env WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD, value not in repo)
- Detail: Three problems in one file. (1) com.apple.developer.associated-domains must be an array; here it is a single string, which signing or the OS will reject or ignore. (2) The domain is api.fredvested.com, but the prod frontend rpId is app.fredvested.com. Native passkeys only work when the rpId domain is listed under webcredentials and that same domain serves the AASA file. (3) The AASA file is served by the Spring backend, so it exists on the API host only. Nothing in this repo serves it on app.fredvested.com. As written, prod passkey login fails.
- Suggested fix: Pick one prod rpId. The simplest is api.fredvested.com, since the backend already serves the AASA there; the parent domain fredvested.com also works if the landing site can host the file. Set environment.prod.ts rpId, WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD and the entitlement to the same value, and write the entitlement as an <array>. The decision is sticky: passkeys created under one rpId cannot be moved to another.
- Verifier (adjusted): Point 1 is confirmed: Release.entitlements:5-6 uses a <string> where Debug and Test use an <array>. Point 2 needs correcting: the frontend never reads environment.rpId (grep of src/app finds no use; auth.service.ts:567 and passkey-prompt.component.ts:270 take rpId from the server's options). So the real rpId is whatever WEBAUTHN_RP_ID_PROD is set to (application-prod.properties:31), which is not in the repo. The mismatch with environment.prod.ts:8 is a stale value, not the live cause. The entitlement domain must match that backend env var and that host must serve the AASA (AASAController.java:21 serves it from the backend host only). Extra problem the auditor missed: Release.entitlements:9 sets keychain-access-groups to $(AppIdentifierPrefix)com.fredvested.app, but the bundle id is com.fredvested.Fred (project.pbxproj:423), and Debug.entitlements has no keychain group at all. KeychainSyncPlugin.swift uses synchronizable keychain items, so keychain behaviour may differ between Debug and Release; not device-verified.

### [blocker] Xcode scheme archives with the Debug configuration

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App.xcodeproj/xcshareddata/xcschemes/App.xcscheme:71-74 (ArchiveAction buildConfiguration = "Debug"); frontend/ios/App/App.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:385 (Debug uses App/Debug.entitlements), :395 and :399 (DEBUG flags), :413 (Release uses Release.entitlements)
- Detail: Product > Archive would build the Debug configuration. The uploaded binary would carry Debug.entitlements (webcredentials:local.fredvested.com only), DEBUG compile flags and no optimisation. Prod passkeys would not work in that binary.
- Suggested fix: Set ArchiveAction (and ProfileAction) to Release in the shared scheme. Decide what the Test configuration is for (it points at api-test.fredvested.com, which may not exist) and delete it if unused.
- Verifier (confirmed): App.xcscheme:71-72 has ArchiveAction buildConfiguration = "Debug"; ProfileAction is Debug too (:55). project.pbxproj:385 maps Debug to Debug.entitlements (local.fredvested.com only) and :413 maps Release to Release.entitlements. An archive made today would be a Debug build that can only do passkeys against the laptop tunnel. One-line fix, but nothing in the repo overrides it (no fastlane or xcodebuild script found), so it stands. Apple will accept a Debug-config upload, so this is a correctness blocker, not an upload rejection.

### [blocker] Terms of Service, Privacy Policy and Legal Information are dead links or a 'coming soon' toast

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.html:135-137 (href="#" with preventDefault); frontend/src/app/tab3/tab3.page.ts:154-158 and :336-337 (legalInformation calls showComingSoon); :392-393
- Detail: The user must tick a box agreeing to the Terms and Privacy Policy before authorising recurring investments, but neither link opens anything. The Legal Information row in the profile tab shows a "coming soon" toast. Apple requires an in-app privacy policy link (5.1.1). For a money app, consent to documents the user cannot read is also a legal problem. The documents themselves are with counsel; opening them is app work. There is no in-app browser plugin installed (@capacitor/browser is not in the Podfile).
- Suggested fix: Host the two documents at stable URLs. Open them from both places, either as an in-app page or with @capacitor/browser. Replace the Legal Information toast with a real page that also carries the brokerage disclosures.
- Verifier (confirmed): investmentconfirmation.component.html:135-137 has both links as href="#" with preventDefault, directly beside the required agreedToTerms checkbox (:128, gating the button at :300). tab3.page.ts:153-158 shows a live menu row 'Legal Information / Terms of Service & Privacy Policy' whose action hits showComingSoon at :336-337. A grep for terms/privacy across all templates found no other place the documents are shown. Podfile has no CapacitorBrowser pod. Consent to unreadable documents before authorising recurring real-money transfers is a fair blocker.

### [blocker] No universal links or URL handling, so the Plaid OAuth bank return cannot come back into the app

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App/Release.entitlements:5-6 and Debug.entitlements:5-8 (webcredentials only, no applinks); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AASAController.java:28-36 (applinks deliberately left out); frontend/src/app/components/linkplaid/linkplaid.component.ts:76-80 and :157; frontend/src/app/change-bank-account/change-bank-account.page.ts:240-241 (reads oauth_state_id from window.location); frontend/src/app/utils/plaid-link-session.ts:2-3 (comment says a universal link brings the user back); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PlaidService.java:69-70 and :126; grep for appUrlOpen in frontend/src/app returned nothing; frontend/ios/App/App/Info.plist (no CFBundleURLTypes)
- Detail: The Plaid code assumes a universal link returns the user to the app with ?oauth_state_id in the URL. The app declares no applinks associated domain, the AASA file has no applinks section, there is no custom URL scheme, and no JS listener for appUrlOpen. For banks that use OAuth (Chase, Capital One, Wells Fargo and others), the redirect will open Safari instead of the app and linking will not complete. In a bundled build window.location is capacitor://localhost, so the current window.location check will not see the return either. Not verified on device.
- Suggested fix: Add applinks:<redirect host> to the entitlements. Add an applinks section to the AASA for the redirect path. Listen for App.addListener('appUrlOpen') and route the returned URL into the Plaid resume code as receivedRedirectUri. Register the prod redirect URI in the Plaid dashboard. Consider Plaid's native iOS Link SDK, which Plaid recommends over web Link inside a WebView.
- Verifier (adjusted): All cited facts confirmed: no applinks in any entitlements file; AASAController.java:28-36 returns webcredentials only; no CFBundleURLTypes in Info.plist; grep for appUrlOpen in frontend/src/app is empty; linkplaid.component.ts:157-159 only looks at window.location; the backend always sends a redirectUri (PlaidService.java:126), which locally is https://local.fredvested.com/link-bank (application-local.properties:26). I raised the severity. This is broken today, not only after bundling: with no applinks the bank's redirect opens Safari, where sessionStorage has no stored link token, so the link cannot finish inside the app. In Plaid production the largest banks (Chase, Capital One, Wells Fargo and others) require OAuth, so many real users could not link a bank and therefore could not fund. Sandbox testing does not show this. Not device-verified, so confidence stays medium; confirm with one OAuth institution before treating it as settled.

### [blocker] Subscription purchase is a TODO; payment method will decide App Review outcome (guideline 3.1.1)

- Type: decision, effort XL, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:771-791 (selectTier: TODO for the Apple IAP sheet, then just saves selectedTier and proceeds); FREDdocs/backlog.md:351 and :793 (App Store Connect subscription products referenced); no StoreKit or RevenueCat code or pod (grep of frontend/src/app, Swift sources, package.json)
- Detail: Picking a tier saves a string on the profile and continues. No purchase happens and no native purchase code exists. Apple generally requires In-App Purchase for subscriptions that unlock app features. A per-paycheck charge collected outside IAP is a real rejection risk unless counsel confirms an exemption applies. Shipping paid tier screens with no working purchase or restore flow would also be rejected. This is reported for the decision only, per the brief.
- Suggested fix: Ask counsel and decide before building: IAP, or an outside payment with a written basis for why 3.1.1 allows it. For a free private beta, hide prices and the tier picker (or make every beta user a fixed tier) so TestFlight review sees no unbuyable paid plans.
- Verifier (confirmed): investmentconfirmation.component.ts:771-791: selectTier writes localStorage, has a TODO block for the IAP sheet, then calls updateUserProfile({selectedTier, billingPeriod}) and goes straight to authorizeRecurringInvestment. A failed save is swallowed by catchError and the flow continues. Podfile has no StoreKit or RevenueCat pod. Any user can pick Pro for free. Blocker is right for a paid launch and for App Store review; for a free private beta it is a decision item, as the auditor says. The 3.1.1 outcome is a counsel and Apple question, not something the code can settle.

### [high] Push notifications are not wired end to end; nothing is ever delivered

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PaydayNotificationService.java:104-111 (logs "[PUSH] Would send", no sender); no APNs or Firebase dependency or config found by grep across backend/src/main and application*.properties; frontend/ios/App/App/AppDelegate.swift:1-124 (no didRegisterForRemoteNotificationsWithDeviceToken or didFailToRegister handlers); frontend/ios/App/App/Debug.entitlements, Release.entitlements, Test.entitlements (no aps-environment key); frontend/src/app/services/push-notification.service.ts:17-32; frontend/src/app/app.component.ts:92-104; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:582-602
- Detail: Each layer is missing a piece. (1) No aps-environment entitlement, so APNs registration fails. (2) AppDelegate does not forward the device token to Capacitor, which the push plugin requires, so the 'registration' event never fires. (3) The JS listener is added after register() is called, and comments still assume Firebase, which is not installed. (4) The backend stores a token via PATCH /user/push-token but the only sender is a log line. (5) The permission prompt fires at cold launch, before login. The token PATCH then needs a JWT that may not exist yet, and a prompt with no context hurts opt-in. Net effect: users are asked to allow notifications and none are ever sent. Payday emails use the same service and are a separate path.
- Suggested fix: Either build it or remove it for beta. To build: add the Push Notifications capability (aps-environment), add the two AppDelegate forwarding methods, register the listener before calling register(), move the permission request to after onboarding, and add a backend APNs sender (token-based .p8 key) that clears dead tokens. If deferring: remove the registerDeviceToken call so no prompt appears, and drop any copy that promises push.
- Verifier (confirmed): All five points hold. No aps-environment key in any of the four entitlements files. AppDelegate.swift has no didRegisterForRemoteNotificationsWithDeviceToken or didFailToRegister method (function list at :28-117). push-notification.service.ts:22-29 requests permission, registers, then adds the listener. app.component.ts:103 calls it on platform.ready with no login check. PaydayNotificationService.java:104-111 only logs. No APNs or Firebase code in backend/src/main. One softening detail: tab3 has a 'notifications' case but no menu row for it (tab3.page.ts menu actions at :77-157), so I found no settings UI promising push. For a private beta the real harm is a pointless permission prompt at first launch; high is fair, and removing the call is a small fix if push is deferred.

### [high] NSCameraUsageDescription is missing while document upload can open the camera

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/document-upload/document-upload.component.html:42 (<input type="file" accept="image/*,.pdf">); frontend/ios/App/App/Info.plist:1-49 (only NSFaceIDUsageDescription at :46-47)
- Detail: A file input that accepts image/* in WKWebView shows the iOS menu with Take Photo, Photo Library and Choose File. Choosing Take Photo without NSCameraUsageDescription in Info.plist makes iOS kill the app. This is on the KYC document upload path, so real users will hit it. App Review rejects crashes under 2.1. The photo library picker does not need a usage string.
- Suggested fix: Add NSCameraUsageDescription, for example "FRED uses the camera so you can photograph an ID or document for identity verification." Add NSPhotoLibraryUsageDescription for safety. Test Take Photo on a device.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts confirmed: document-upload.component.html:42 is a file input with accept="image/*,.pdf" and Info.plist has only NSFaceIDUsageDescription (:46-47). The page is a standalone route (app.routes.ts:148-149) reached from my-profile (my-profile.page.ts:399-401), not a step in the main onboarding or KYC form, so calling it 'the KYC path' overstates reach. The crash happens only if the user picks Take Photo, and it has not been reproduced on device. Lowered to high for that reason. It is still a one-line plist fix that should be done before any external tester gets a build.

### [high] iPad is a supported device family with a partial orientation list; upload validation is expected to fail

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:403, :429, :514 (TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY = "1,2"); frontend/ios/App/App/Info.plist:35-43 (iPad orientations portrait and upside down only; no UIRequiresFullScreen key)
- Detail: The app declares iPad support but lists only two iPad orientations and does not set UIRequiresFullScreen. App Store Connect normally rejects such uploads because iPad multitasking requires all four orientations. Keeping iPad also means iPad screenshots are required and App Review tests the phone-shaped UI on an iPad.
- Suggested fix: Decide: iPhone only (set TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY = 1 in all three configurations, the simplest for beta), or keep iPad and add UIRequiresFullScreen = true plus an iPad layout pass. Note that an app released with iPad support cannot drop it later.
- Verifier (confirmed): project.pbxproj:403, :429, :514 all set TARGETED_DEVICE_FAMILY = "1,2". Info.plist:39-43 lists two iPad orientations and there is no UIRequiresFullScreen key. This matches the known App Store Connect validation error for iPad multitasking. Not proven by an actual upload, so medium confidence is right. Type 'decision' is right.

### [high] Privacy manifest and App Store label do not declare KYC, contact and financial data

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App/PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy:11-16 (states the gap) and :39-70 (only ProductInteraction and UserID declared); FREDdocs/privacy-manifest-and-label.md:60-79 (table of undeclared types and "Before the first submission, someone has to add those entries")
- Detail: The manifest covers analytics only. Name, date of birth, address, SSN, email, phone, bank account details and other financial info are collected but not declared in NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypes. The file and the doc both say so. A label that understates this for a finance app is a rejection and trust risk. If billing lands, purchase history must be added too.
- Suggested fix: Add entries for Name, EmailAddress, PhoneNumber, PhysicalAddress, OtherFinancialInfo, sensitive or government id data (SSN) and any payment or bank info. Mark each linked = true, tracking = false, purpose AppFunctionality. Update the doc table and answer the App Store Connect label to match. Have counsel check the wording against the privacy policy.
- Verifier (confirmed): PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy:11-16 states in its own comment that KYC and banking data is not declared and must be before submission. NSPrivacyCollectedDataTypes (:39-70) holds only ProductInteraction and UserID, both for analytics. I did not open FREDdocs/privacy-manifest-and-label.md, but the manifest alone proves the gap. High is right: this is a submission item, not a private beta blocker.

### [medium] Placeholder 'coming soon' features are visible to users

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/tab3/tab3.page.ts:299 (Investment Goals), :331 (Help Center), :337 (Legal Information), :392-393; frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:142 ("Coming soon" pill); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:206 (tier feature threshold 'Coming Soon')
- Detail: App Review guideline 2.1 rejects apps with placeholder content or buttons that do nothing. Several rows in the profile tab toast "feature coming soon!", and the tier screen lists a feature as Coming Soon. I did not trace whether the tab3 rows at :299 and :331 are reachable from the current menu; confirm on device.
- Suggested fix: Hide or remove every row that ends in showComingSoon before submission. Remove 'coming soon' items from paid tier lists or mark them clearly as not included in the price.
- Verifier (adjusted): Partly refuted. The tab3 menu only defines these actions: recurringInvestments, lumpSumInvestment, portfolioAllocation, sellWithdraw, security, changeBankAccount, taxDocuments, beneficiaries, faq, contactSupport, legalInformation (tab3.page.ts:77-157). So the 'Investment Goals' (:299) and 'Help Center' (:331) cases are dead code and no user can reach them. The only reachable toast is Legal Information, which is already covered by the legal links finding. What remains real: the 'Coming soon' pill on Tax Loss Harvesting in my-profile.page.html:142, and the Pro tier listing tax loss harvesting as 'Coming Soon' (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:201-206, feature id is mislabelled 'household'). Selling a paid tier that lists an unbuilt feature is a review and trust risk, but this is medium, not high.

### [medium] Ionicons and Plaid Link are loaded from CDNs at runtime

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/index.html:28-29 (unpkg.com ionicons 7.2.1 scripts), :95 (cdn.plaid.com link-initialize.js as a blocking script); frontend/src/app/beneficiaries/beneficiaries.page.html:163 and frontend/src/app/faq/faq.page.html:32 (ion-icon in use); frontend/package.json (ionicons already a dependency)
- Detail: The ionicons scripts come from unpkg at every launch. If unpkg is slow, down or blocked, every ion-icon is blank, and it is third-party code executing inside a finance app with no integrity check. The Plaid script tag is a synchronous script at the end of body on every launch, even for users who never link a bank again; a slow network delays page load. Plaid requires loading from their CDN, so that one should be lazy, not bundled.
- Suggested fix: Remove the two unpkg tags and register icons from the installed ionicons package with addIcons. Remove the Plaid tag from index.html and rely on the on-demand loader that already exists (change-bank-account.page.ts:103).

### [medium] No offline or no-connection state

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: grep for navigator.onLine, 'offline', @capacitor/network and 'no connection' in frontend/src/app returned nothing; frontend/src/main.ts:22-31 (provideHttpClient with no interceptors); frontend/src/app/services/auth.service.ts:356 (status 0 treated as transient for progress only); frontend/ios/App/App/AppDelegate.swift:53-66 (overlay removed after a 10 s fallback)
- Detail: There is no global handling for a device with no network. Launching in airplane mode gives the loading animation, then whatever each screen does with a failed request. Apple reviewers do test this; a hang or blank screen is a 2.1 rejection risk. I did not trace every screen's error state.
- Suggested fix: Add a small connectivity service (Capacitor Network plugin or online/offline events) and one shared "You're offline" state with retry. Use it at cold start and on the main tabs.

### [medium] Production environment values are placeholders

- Type: external, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/environments/environment.prod.ts:7-12 (api.fredvested.com backend, empty posthogKey); frontend/src/environments/environment.dev.ts:8 (rpId is an old GitHub Codespaces hostname); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/config/SecurityConfig.java:77-86 (CORS list includes a Codespaces origin and local.fredvested.com for every profile)
- Detail: The prod build points at api.fredvested.com, which does not exist yet (known). Analytics is a no-op in prod until a PostHog key is set.
