The dev environment's rpId is a dead Codespaces hostname, so a dev-config build cannot do passkeys. CORS already allows capacitor://localhost, which the bundled app needs, but it also allows dev origins in every profile.
- Suggested fix: When the prod backend exists, set the real API host, rpId and PostHog key. Fix or delete environment.dev.ts rpId (a TestFlight beta against api-dev needs a correct one). Make dev-only CORS origins profile-specific.
- Verifier (adjusted): The values are as described: environment.prod.ts:7-11 (api.fredvested.com, rpId app.fredvested.com, empty posthogKey), environment.dev.ts:8 (Codespaces hostname), SecurityConfig.java:77-86 (Codespaces and local.fredvested.com origins allowed in every profile). But one claimed consequence is wrong: environment.rpId is never read by app code. The rpId comes from the server's WebAuthn options (auth.service.ts:567, passkey-prompt.component.ts:270), so the dead dev rpId does not break passkeys in a dev-config build. What a TestFlight build against api-dev really needs is an entitlement listing the api-dev rpId host, and none of Debug, Release or Test entitlements has one (they list local, api and api-test). The extra CORS origins are hygiene; capacitor://localhost is already allowed. The missing prod backend is known and external. Net: stale config to clean up plus one real gap (no entitlement for the api-dev rpId), medium overall.

### [low] In-app review prompt can never fire: the plugin is not installed

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/services/review.service.ts:13-28 (dynamic import of '@capacitor/rate-app', silent catch); frontend/package.json dependencies (no rate-app package); frontend/ios/App/Podfile:11-22 (no such pod); frontend/src/app/components/monthly-freedom-update/monthly-freedom-update.component.ts:172
- Detail: ReviewService imports '@capacitor/rate-app' at runtime and swallows the failure. The package is not in package.json or the Podfile, so the StoreKit review sheet never shows. The hasSeenFirstMFU flag is still set, so the one chance is used up silently. I could not confirm that a package with that exact name exists.

### [low] Export compliance key (ITSAppUsesNonExemptEncryption) is not set

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App/Info.plist:1-49 (key absent)
- Detail: Without it, every upload sits in "Missing Compliance" until the question is answered by hand in App Store Connect. The app only uses standard HTTPS and OS crypto, which is normally exempt.

### [low] Version, signing and release hygiene items

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:388/394, :416/422 (build 1, version 1.0, no bump script); frontend/ios/App/App/Release.entitlements:7-10 (keychain group com.fredvested.app vs bundle com.fredvested.Fred at project.pbxproj:423); frontend/ios/App/App/KeychainSyncPlugin.swift:26 and :85-86 (account email stored in iCloud-synced keychain); frontend/ios/App (stray 'KeychainSyncPlugin 2.swift', 'NativePasskeyPlugin 2.swift', .m files and duplicate Swift files outside App/, tracked in git); ios (stale second project); frontend/ios/App/App/Info.plist:31-34 (armv7 capability); frontend/src/app/app.component.ts:150 (?testing=true skips startup navigation in every build, not gated on production); 179 console.log calls in frontend/src/app (grep count)
- Detail: None of these blocks a build alone. The build number must be raised by hand for every upload. Debug has no keychain group and Release has one, so the synced email saved by a debug build will not be visible to a release build (test recovery on a release build). The duplicate plugin files and the old root ios folder invite editing the wrong file. The email in iCloud Keychain should be mentioned in the privacy policy.
- Suggested fix: Add a build number bump step to the release script, delete the stray files and the root ios folder after confirming nothing references them, gate ?testing=true on !environment.production, and strip console.log in production builds.

**Verified solid:**

- Live iOS project is frontend/ios/App (Capacitor 8.4.1, Podfile.lock matches package.json plugin versions). The root ios/ folder is an untouched May 2025 copy.
- Bundle id com.fredvested.Fred is consistent across capacitor.config.ts:4, project.pbxproj (all three configurations) and the AASA app identifier 9G59L97KRT.com.fredvested.Fred (AASAController.java:25). Automatic signing with team 9G59L97KRT.
- App icon set is complete for modern Xcode: single 1024x1024 universal icon plus dark and tinted variants, all without an alpha channel (checked with sips).
- Launch screen storyboard exists, is wired in Info.plist:27-28, and uses the FREDLogo vector image set on a white background.
- NSFaceIDUsageDescription is present (Info.plist:46-47) and matches real use: LAContext biometric unlock in NativePasskeyPlugin.swift:27-46.
- PrivacyInfo.xcprivacy is well formed, registered as a bundle resource (project.pbxproj:18 and :195), declares NSPrivacyTracking false, analytics data types and UserDefaults reason CA92.1. The app's own Swift code uses no other required-reason APIs.
- Custom plugins NativePasskey, KeychainSync and LoadingOverlay are compiled into the target (project.pbxproj:243-246) and registered in MainViewController.swift:13-15. Main.storyboard uses MainViewController.
- Native passkey assertion (login and re-auth) is implemented with ASAuthorizationController, handles user cancel, and returns the WebAuthn JSON shape (NativePasskeyPlugin.swift:96-184).
- AASA endpoint for webcredentials exists and is public (AASAController.java:21, SecurityConfig.java:112).
- Backend CORS already allows capacitor://localhost and ionic://localhost (SecurityConfig.java:81-82), which a bundled build needs.
- No cleartext or App Transport Security exceptions: Info.plist has no NSAppTransportSecurity key and capacitor.config has no cleartext or allowNavigation entries.
- Dev mocks (services/dev/*-mock.ts) are consulted only when environment.local is true, and the devPage auto-login and devKey capture are gated on !environment.production (app.component.ts:165-181). environment.prod.ts sets production true, local false and an empty devToolsKey.
- Angular production configuration exists and swaps in environment.prod.ts with output hashing (angular.json:51-71). Fonts and the Lottie cover are self-hosted.
- No advertising or attribution SDK is in the Podfile or package.json, so no App Tracking Transparency prompt is needed.
- Account closure and deletion exists in app and backend (sell-withdraw.page.html:141-144, TradingController.java:310-343), which covers Apple's in-app account deletion rule for funded users. I did not check the path for a user with no brokerage account.
- App snapshot privacy: AppDelegate covers the WebView with the loading overlay on resign-active so the app switcher does not show balances (AppDelegate.swift:38-43).

## legal-disclosures-inapp

Legal, disclosure and regulated-content surfaces are the least finished part of the app. All paths below are under /Users/andrewtimmer/PersonalTypeshit/FRED.  What is missing outright: there is no Terms of Service or Privacy Policy anywhere in the app (the two links on the consent checkbox are dead `href="#"`, and Settings > Legal Information shows a "coming soon" toast). The three Alpaca agreements the user "agrees" to at KYC are also dead links, yet the backend signs all three (including a margin agreement) with the user's IP and a timestamp. FRED's own ToS/Privacy acceptance is a frontend-only boolean: nothing is stored on the server (no timestamp, no version). There is no recurring ACH debit authorization language, no fee or auto-renew disclosure next to the prices and the "free for 14 days" claim, and no standard Alpaca/SIPC/FINRA brokerage disclosure block.  What is partly there: disclaimers exist on some projection surfaces (survey, freedom plan, MFU, lump sum, Monte Carlo info sheet, strategy and drawdown decks, market breakdown email) but are missing on the highest-claim ones (get-started slides, "Fast Forward to {year}. Your portfolio hits $X", Monte Carlo results, TLH "You could save $X in taxes", AI chat UI). The AI chat has a solid system prompt and a keyword safety gate, but no on-screen "educational, not advice" notice, and its knowledge base contains false product facts (flat $8/month, Persona biometric recovery, email trade confirmations).  What is real and solid: the Documents page pulls real Alpaca tax forms and statements, the analytics opt-out toggle is built and server-owned, account closure with deletion propagation exists, and the market breakdown email carries a disclaimer and a compliance validator.  I did not run the app or call any service. Everything is from reading the working tree. Whether Alpaca itself emails trade confirmations and statements to customers could not be confirmed from the repo.


### [blocker] Terms of Service and Privacy Policy do not exist in the app (dead links and a 'coming soon' toast)

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.html:135-137; frontend/src/app/tab3/tab3.page.ts:153-159, 336-338, 392-394; FREDdocs/backlog.md:227-229
- Detail: The consent checkbox says "I agree to FRED's Terms of Service and Privacy Policy" but both anchors are `href="#"` with `(click)="$event.preventDefault()"`, so the user cannot read what they agree to. In Settings, the 'Legal Information / Terms of Service & Privacy Policy' row calls `showComingSoon('Legal Information')`, which shows the toast 'Legal Information feature coming soon!'. A grep of all frontend .ts/.html for http(s) URLs found no ToS or privacy URL anywhere. Backlog FRED-138 tracks this as sleeping. Apple also requires a reachable privacy policy inside the app.
- Suggested fix: Add a single constants file for legal URLs (ToS, Privacy Policy, and later Form CRS / advisory agreement if applicable). Open them in an in-app browser from the consent checkbox and from a real Legal Information page in Settings. Counsel supplies the text and hosting URL; the app work is the slots and the page.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. investmentconfirmation.component.html:135-137 has both anchors as href="#" with preventDefault. tab3.page.ts:154-158 defines the Legal Information row, 336-338 routes it to showComingSoon, and 392-394 shows the toast. A grep of frontend/src/app .ts/.html for terms/privacy found no other ToS or privacy link, and app.routes.ts has no legal/terms/privacy route. Backlog FRED-138 (backlog.md:227-229) is still sleeping. No guard or alternate path found.

### [blocker] ToS / Privacy consent is never stored on the server (no timestamp, no version)

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:248, 318, 436-439, 675-690; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:550-553; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/User.java:315-319
- Detail: `agreedToTerms` is a component field only. On confirm the app calls `completeStep('investmentConfirmation')` and then sends only `agreedToMarketing` to the backend. A grep of backend Java, SQL and properties for agreedToTerms, termsAccepted, tosVersion, terms_accepted and agreed_to_terms returned nothing, so there is no record of who accepted which version and when. The flag is also set to true automatically when the user re-enters at step 2 from localStorage (line 436-439), which is fine for UX but means the only evidence of consent is a local flag.
- Suggested fix: Add a migration with terms_accepted_at, terms_version, privacy_version (and ideally the accepting IP) on users or a separate consent table. Record it server-side in the same call that completes the investmentConfirmation step, and reject the step if the consent fields are absent. Add a re-consent path for when the version changes.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. authorizeRecurringInvestment (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:675-690) only checks the local agreedToTerms field, calls completeStep('investmentConfirmation'), then sends agreedToMarketing only. ionViewWillEnter (436-439) sets agreedToTerms = true from a localStorage flag. A backend grep over .java/.sql/.properties for agreedToTerms, termsAccepted, terms_accepted, tos version and similar returned nothing; the only consent columns are analytics opt-out (V4) and plaid_transactions_consented (V6). UserController.java:550-553 handles agreedToMarketing only. Blocker is fair for real money: there is no evidence of assent beyond a client flag.

### [blocker] Alpaca Customer, Margin and Account agreements are signed server-side but the user cannot read them

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/kyc-verification/kyc-verification.component.html:206-220; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AlpacaApiService.java:256-262; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AlpacaController.java:91-95
- Detail: The KYC step 2 checkbox reads "I agree to Alpaca's Customer Agreement, Margin Agreement, and Account Agreement". AlpacaApiService.java:256-262 submits customer_agreement, margin_agreement and account_agreement with signed_at and the client IP for every user, with a matching TODO. The checkbox is enforced client-side only (kyc-verification.component.ts:154 Validators.requiredTrue). I found no agreement or signed_at field in the backend model package, so the acceptance does not appear to be stored in FRED's database (not exhaustively traced). Whether margin_agreement is needed for this account type is a question for Alpaca.

A grep of pages/ai-chat for advice/disclaim/educat/AI-generated found nothing user-facing. The 'must not' rules at FredConstitution.java:272-278 are prompt-level only. I did not verify the claim that chat can propose account changes.

### [high] Account deletion is hard to find and unavailable to users without a brokerage account

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.html:114-147; frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.ts:562-600; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/TradingController.java:310-323
- Detail: The only user-facing deletion path is the 'Want to close your account?' box, which is nested inside the 'No Holdings Found' empty state of Settings > Sell & Withdraw. A user with positions never sees it, and nothing in Settings is labelled close or delete account. The backend returns 404 when the user has no Alpaca account id, so someone who registered but did not finish KYC, or whose application was rejected, cannot delete their account or data from the app at all. Apple requires that any app with account creation offers in-app account deletion that is easy to find. There is also no data export or data request path in the app (grep for export|download my data in security-settings found nothing); the only route is the general mailto:help@fredvested.com.
- Suggested fix: Add a 'Close account' row under Account & Security that always shows, explains the sell and withdraw prerequisites, and links to the existing flow. Add a backend path that deletes a user with no brokerage account. Add a 'Request my data' action (a prefilled email is enough for beta) once counsel confirms the process.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. The close-account box sits inside the *ngIf="positions.length === 0" empty state (sell-withdraw.page.html:115-146), so a user with holdings never sees it. tab3.page.ts settings rows (71-157) have no close or delete row; the only entry is 'Sell & Withdraw / Sell stocks and withdraw money'. A grep for close/delete account across the frontend finds only sell-withdraw. TradingController.java:319-322 returns 404 when alpacaAccountId is null, and the only other caller of UserDeletionService is the dev-profile DevUserController. So a user who registered but has no brokerage account has no deletion path. The deletion itself is solid once reached (passkey step-up, confirm sheet, deferral check at TradingController.java:327-331). High is right for App Store; it does not block a private beta.

### [medium] Documents page shows 'No Documents Yet' when the load fails, and only looks back 3 years

A safety check runs before every chat call (ChatService.java:143, 281).

- No lorem ipsum, TBD or example.com text found in any user-facing template (grep across frontend/src/app .html and .ts, excluding specs).
- Kid cost figures cite real public sources with URLs (frontend/src/app/constants/kid-costs.ts:75-93).

## notifications-email

Notifications and email are the least production-ready part of FRED. Nothing in the system can deliver a message to a real user today. Email: there is one facade (EmailService) and one provider port (EmailSender), but the only implementation is LoggingEmailSender, which writes a log line. No mail library is in backend/build.gradle, app.email.enabled defaults to false in every profile, and application-prod.properties has no email settings at all. The account recovery OTP does not even use the facade: it is printed to stdout with System.out.println. Push: the frontend asks for permission and tries to register, but the iOS app has no aps-environment entitlement and AppDelegate does not forward the APNs token, and the backend push sender is a log line. In-app: the Monthly Freedom Update and toasts exist, but there is no in-app surface for a system hold on a schedule, a failed transfer, or a rejected account.  Email map (class, state): 1. Recovery OTP: RecoveryController, stdout only, never reaches EmailService. Broken. 2. Change-email verification: does not exist. UserController.updateEmail swaps the address immediately, no email to old or new address. 3. Welcome / email verification: does not exist (no emailVerified field). 4. KYC approved: no email (TODO push comment). KYC rejected: plain-text email via EmailService (mocked). KYC action required: nothing (TODO comment). 5. Payday D-1, D+0, D+1: PaydayNotificationService via legacy plain-text EmailService path (mocked). D+0 and D+1 copy is wrong for the real flow (see findings). 6. Investment success / failure (funding failed, ACH returned, trading failed): InvestmentExecutionService.notifyUserOfSuccess/Failure, plain text, mocked. 7. Withdrawal, sell, lump-sum initiation, deposit settled, bank change, passkey reset, email changed, account deletion: no notification of any kind. 8. Monthly market breakdown: MarketBreakdownScheduler + composer + 4 HTML templates, the only templated email. Mocked, unsubscribe link is "#". 9. Referral founder reward email: TODO stub in ReferralService. 10. Marketing list opt-in: TODO stub in UserController. 11. ACH authorization copy, authorization revoked, reconnect bank, skip notices, ops alerts (paycheck autopilot spec section 10): not built (no sendOnce, NotificationGate, OpsAlertService in code).  The plumbing that exists (email_log audit table, idempotent period sends, market breakdown composer and tests) is solid and can be kept. The work is: add a real provider, move the OTP onto it, fix misleading copy, decide the notification matrix, and either finish push end to end or remove the permission prompt for beta.


### [blocker] No real email provider exists: every email in every profile is mocked

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/LoggingEmailSender.java:13-25 (only EmailSender implementation, logs 'MOCK EMAIL'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailSender.java:5-11; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:56-62 (emailEnabled=false records MOCKED and returns); backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:47-49 (EMAIL_ENABLED default false, EMAIL_PROVIDER default logging); backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:1-33 (no email settings, EMAIL_* not in the required env list); backend/build.gradle (grep for mail|resend|firebase returned nothing)
- Detail: EmailSender is a clean port, but the only class implementing it logs and returns. There is no spring-boot-starter-mail, no Resend/SES/Postmark client, and no HTTP sender. Even if EMAIL_ENABLED=true were set, LoggingEmailSender would run and the email_log row would say SENT while nothing left the server. That means recovery codes, failed-transfer notices, KYC rejection notices and the Piggy Plus market breakdown all go nowhere. The provider choice (Resend vs other) and the sending domain DNS (SPF, DKIM, DMARC for fredvested.com) are outside steps; the sender class is app work. Tracked as FRED-101 (sleeping) in FREDdocs/backlog.md:181-193.
- Suggested fix: Pick the provider, then add one EmailSender implementation with @ConditionalOnProperty(app.email.provider=<name>), API key from env, and a timeout. Add EMAIL_ENABLED, EMAIL_PROVIDER, EMAIL_FROM and the API key to the documented required prod env list in application-prod.properties. Add a startup guard so the prod profile refuses to boot with provider=logging or enabled=false (same pattern as BrokerEnvironmentGuard). Make LoggingEmailSender unable to produce a SENT row.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. LoggingEmailSender.java:13-25 is the only class implementing EmailSender (grep for 'implements EmailSender' across backend/src returns one hit). build.gradle has no mail, Resend, SendGrid, SES or Postmark dependency. application.properties:47-49 defaults EMAIL_ENABLED=false and EMAIL_PROVIDER=logging; application-prod.properties:1-33 sets no email keys and its required-env comment does not list any EMAIL_* variable. The claim that EMAIL_ENABLED=true would write SENT rows while nothing is delivered is correct: EmailService.java:63-66 calls emailSender.send then records STATUS_SENT, and the only sender just logs. One extra detail the auditor did not mention: the default from address is noreply@investingapp.com (application.properties:48, EmailService.java:35), not a fredvested.com address, so EMAIL_FROM must also be set. No guard or profile gate found that changes this.

### [blocker] Account recovery OTP is printed to stdout and never emailed

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:45-58 (System.out.println of email and OTP, comment 'Mock email sending'); no EmailService dependency in the constructor at lines 25-33; frontend/src/app/pages/recovery/recovery.page.ts:64 ('Recovery code sent. Check your email.'); FREDdocs/backlog.md:390-392 (FRED-219, still open)
- Detail: Recovery is the only way back into an account for a passkey-only user who loses their device. The code is generated and stored, then written to the server console in plaintext in every profile including prod. It never goes through EmailService, so no user can ever receive it, and any log drain captures live OTPs. The app tells the user the code was sent. FRED-219 also notes verify has no attempt limit (lines 61-84 confirm: no counter or lockout), which belongs to the auth audit but must ship in the same change.
- Suggested fix: Inject EmailService, send the code through it with an email type such as RECOVERY_OTP, delete the println block, and never log the code. Add attempt limiting and lockout on /verify and a resend cooldown on /initiate. Depends on the real provider finding.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. RecoveryController.java:51-56 prints the user's email and the plaintext OTP with System.out.println under a 'Mock email sending' comment, with no profile check. The constructor (lines 29-33) takes only UserRepository, JwtUtils and EncryptionService, so there is no EmailService path at all. The app still shows 'Recovery code sent. Check your email.' (recovery.page.ts:64). /verify (lines 61-84) has no attempt counter, lockout or rate limit visible in the controller; I did not check for a global rate-limit filter elsewhere, so that sub-claim is confirmed only at controller level. Successful verify wipes all passkeys (line 91). Matches FRED-219 in FREDdocs/backlog.md:390-392, still open.

### [high] Push notifications cannot work on iOS: no APNs entitlement, no token forwarding, no backend sender

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/ios/App/App/Release.entitlements, Debug.entitlements, Test.entitlements, App.entitlements (none contain aps-environment; grep across ios/App returned nothing); frontend/ios/App/App/AppDelegate.swift:28-117 (no didRegisterForRemoteNotificationsWithDeviceToken or didFailToRegister methods, which the Capacitor push plugin needs to emit 'registration'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PaydayNotificationService.java:104-111 (sendPushIfTokenPresent only logs '[PUSH] Would send'); frontend/src/app/services/push-notification.service.ts:17-32 (comments say FCM and GoogleService-Info.plist still needed); backend/build.gradle (no firebase or APNs dependency)
- Detail: Three separate pieces are missing. (1) Without the aps-environment entitlement and the Push Notifications capability on the App ID, PushNotifications.register() fails. (2) Without the two AppDelegate methods that post capacitorDidRegisterForRemoteNotifications, the JS 'registration' listener never fires, so no token is ever stored. (3) The backend has no sender: no APNs or FCM client, no credentials config. Net effect today: the user sees the iOS permission prompt and nothing ever arrives. The decision of APNs direct vs Firebase, and creating the APNs key in the Apple developer account, are outside steps.
- Suggested fix: Decide APNs direct (simplest for iOS only) or FCM. Add the capability and aps-environment to each entitlements file, add the two AppDelegate forwarding methods, build a PushSender port with one real implementation plus a logging default (mirror EmailSender), and handle invalid-token responses by clearing the stored token. If push is not going into the private beta, remove the registerDeviceToken call so users are not asked for a permission that does nothing.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. None of the four entitlements files contain aps-environment (grep over frontend/ios/App/App and the xcodeproj returned nothing; Release.entitlements holds only associated-domains and keychain groups). AppDelegate.swift has no didRegisterForRemoteNotificationsWithDeviceToken or didFailToRegister method (function list at lines 28-117; grep for RemoteNotifications returns nothing). Backend sendPushIfTokenPresent only logs (PaydayNotificationService.java:104-111) and no APNs or Firebase code or dependency exists. One point makes this slightly worse than stated for beta: registerDeviceToken is called unconditionally on every native app start, before login or onboarding (app.component.ts:103), so every beta user gets the iOS notification permission prompt at first launch for a feature that does nothing. The plugin itself is installed (package.json:33 @capacitor/push-notifications ^8.1.1), so the 'npm install' comment in the service is stale. High is right: push is not required for a private beta if email works, but the dead permission prompt should be removed or deferred before beta (a small change).

### [high] D+0 email says 'Investment complete, $X invested' one minute after the ACH pull is started, for every execution including failed ones

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PaydayNotificationService.java:72-87 (subject 'Investment complete', body '%s invested', no status check); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/repository/InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:128-129 (findByScheduledDate filters on date only, no status or type filter); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:45 (executions created 11:55 PM ET), 159-166 (D+0 job at 00:00 ET for yesterday), 70 (funding status only checked 8-17 weekdays); FREDdocs/specs/2026-10-19-paycheck-autopilot-design.md:574 (spec copy: 'FRED started your $300 transfer. It's invested once it clears, usually 1 to 3 business days.')
- Detail: The recurring job creates executions at 11:55 PM ET and the D+0 notice fires at midnight for all executions scheduled the day before. At that point the ACH transfer has at most been initiated; money has not settled and no trade has been placed. Telling a customer of an investment adviser that money is invested when it is not is a real accuracy problem, and the same email goes out for rows that are FUNDING_FAILED, and for lump-sum rows that share the date. The approved spec already has correct copy for this moment; the code was not updated. The emails also contain emoji in the subject and are sent at midnight.
- Suggested fix: Replace D+0 with the spec's PAYDAY_D0 copy and restrict it to recurring executions in FUNDING_INITIATED. Send the 'invested' message only from the COMPLETED transition (notifyUserOfSuccess already sits there). Add a test that a FUNDING_FAILED row gets no success copy.
- Verifier (adjusted): Substance confirmed; small factual corrections. The gap is 5 minutes, not one: executions are created by the 23:55 ET job (InvestmentScheduler.java:45) and the D+0 job runs at 00:00 ET for the previous day (lines 159-165). PaydayNotificationService.java:72-87 sends 'Investment complete' / '%s invested' with no status check, and findByScheduledDate (InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:128-129) filters on date only. The lump-sum claim is also true: InvestmentController.java:294-300 creates one-off executions with scheduledDate = EasternTime.now(), so they are swept in too, as are cash-funded and failed rows. Two things the auditor missed: (1) sendDayAfterNotifications (lines 89-102) has the same flaw, telling every user with any execution yesterday 'You just got closer to financial freedom' regardless of status; (2) a separate success email already fires at the real COMPLETED transition (InvestmentExecutionService.java:1123, 1178-1190), so a successful user gets a false 'invested' notice at midnight and then a true one later. Today all of this is mocked, so no user sees it yet; it becomes live the moment a real provider is switched on, so it should be fixed in the same change as the provider. High stands (arguably must-fix before enabling email in beta because it is a false statement about client money).

### [high] Email change takes effect immediately with no verification and no notice to the old address

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:869-908 (setEmail and new JWT issued in one call, no token, no email sent); frontend/src/app/pages/change-email/change-email.page.html:21 ('You will be asked to verify your identity', which is the passkey or PIN check, not email ownership); grep for emailVerified|email_verified in backend returned nothing; FREDdocs/backlog.md:190-191 (emailVerified is an unbuilt FRED-101 criterion)
- Detail: Email is the login identifier, the recovery channel and the address every money notice goes to. Today a typo in the new address silently redirects all of that to an address nobody controls, and recovery then sends the code there. There is no confirmation link or code to the new address, and no 'your email was changed' notice to the old one, which is the standard account-takeover tripwire for a financial app. Sign-up email is also never verified, so the same risk exists from day one: a mistyped sign-up email means recovery can never work.
- Suggested fix: Add an emailVerified flag and a pending-email flow: send a code to the new address, switch only after it is confirmed, and email the old address that a change happened. Verify the sign-up email at or shortly after registration. Depends on the real provider.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. UserController.java:869-908: /update-email checks only that the new address is non-empty and not already in use, then setEmail, save and a new JWT in one call. No code sent to the new address, no notice to the old one, and no email format validation in that method. No emailVerified field exists anywhere in backend/src/main (grep empty). The passkey or PIN re-check lives only in the frontend (change-email.page.ts:97-144); the backend endpoint itself requires just a valid JWT, so the re-auth is not enforced server side (that part belongs to the auth audit). Because recovery sends the OTP to whatever email is on file and then wipes passkeys, an unverified email change is a full account takeover path for anyone holding a live session. High is reasonable; for a small invited beta the practical risk is mostly typos, but the fix depends on the email provider anyway.

### [high] KYC outcomes: approved and action-required send nothing; rejection email is a bare plain-text line that is mocked

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AccountStatusService.java:124-134 (handleActive: 'TODO: push notification to frontend'), 213-222 (handleActionRequired: TODO only), 160-180 (handleRejected sends plain text via EmailService); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AlpacaController.java:145-155 (status endpoint exposes only status and hasActionRequired); frontend/src/app/services/account-status.service.ts:30-53 (app only reacts to actionRequired and ACTIVE)
- Detail: KYC review can take minutes to days, so the user is usually not in the app when the result lands. Approved: no email or push, so the user does not know they can start investing. Action required (Alpaca needs a document): nothing is sent; the only signal is a badge on the settings tab the next time they open the app, so applications will stall. Rejected: the email text exists but goes to the mock sender, has no reason, no adverse-action style wording, and says 'contact our support team' without an address. I found no dedicated in-app screen for a REJECTED account (the frontend only branches on ACTIVE and actionRequired); I did not trace every route guard, so confidence on the in-app part is medium. What a rejection notice must legally say is a counsel question.
- Suggested fix: Add emails (and push when available) for ACTIVE and ACTION_REQUIRED, add a clear in-app state for REJECTED with the support address, and have counsel review the rejection wording.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. AccountStatusService.java:120-130 (handleActive) and 213-222 (handleActionRequired) contain only TODO comments for notification. handleRejected (160-180) sends a two-sentence plain-text email with no reason and no support address through the mocked EmailService. /my-account-status returns only status and hasActionRequired (AlpacaController.java:145-155). I can raise confidence on the in-app part: a grep of frontend/src/app for REJECTED finds only beneficiary status code and an analytics type, so there is no rejected-account screen or banner. The frontend consumes account status only for the action-required badge (tabs.page.ts:73, tab3.page.ts:175, my-profile.page.ts:365) and an isAccountActive check when resuming a schedule (recurring-investments.page.ts:479). A rejected user therefore sees a normal-looking app with no explanation. I did not trace route guards either. Legal wording for rejection is a counsel question (external).

### [high] A system hold on a schedule is silent: no email, no push, and the app never reads holdReason

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AchRelationshipService.java:169-176 (MANUAL_REVIEW hold set, only a log line); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentScheduleService.java:241-256 (BANK_CHANGE hold, log and analytics only); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AccountStatusService.java:204 (MANUAL_REVIEW on rejection); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PaydayNotificationService.java:59-63 (a blocking hold suppresses the D-1 notice and sends nothing in its place); grep -rl 'holdReason|hold_reason' frontend/src/app returned no files; FREDdocs/specs/2026-10-19-paycheck-autopilot-design.md:540 (designed MANUAL_REVIEW card copy, not built)
- Detail: When the system stops a schedule (bank change in progress, ACH relationship refused, manual review), the user's recurring investing stops and nothing tells them. The frontend has zero references to holdReason, so the Recurring Investments page will keep showing a next investment date that will not happen. The only effect of a blocking hold on notifications is that the D-1 reminder quietly disappears. For real money this is the 'I thought I was investing for three months' failure.
- Suggested fix: Expose holdReason on the schedule response, render a status card per hold reason on Recurring Investments and the dashboard (copy already drafted in the spec table), and send one email per hold via the idempotent path.
- Verifier (confirmed): True, and the UI effect is concrete. Holds are set at AchRelationshipService.java:169-176 (MANUAL_REVIEW), InvestmentScheduleService.java:254 (BANK_CHANGE) and AccountStatusService.java:204 (MANUAL_REVIEW) with log lines only. InvestmentScheduleResponse has no hold field (grep for 'hold' in dto and controller returns nothing) and the frontend has zero references to holdReason. The isPaused value sent to the app is derived from status alone (InvestmentSchedule.java:336-346: status != ACTIVE), and holds deliberately do not change status, so a schedule under a blocking MANUAL_REVIEW hold renders as 'Active, Your investments are running' with a next date (recurring-investments.page.html:19-25, 48) while isReadyForInvestment refuses it (InvestmentSchedule.java:356-362). Nuance: BANK_CHANGE is treated as an informational, self-clearing hold (PaydayNotificationService.java:31-38), so the silent-stop risk is mainly MANUAL_REVIEW. Also, only BANK_CHANGE and MANUAL_REVIEW are ever set in code; ACH_RETURN_* holds are never written (grep of setHoldReason). High stands; in a beta with real money a MANUAL_REVIEW hold would be invisible to the user.

### [high] Failed transfers and failed orders have an email (mocked) but no push and no in-app surface

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:401, 525, 639, 879, 891, 1013, 1130 (notifyUserOfFailure call sites), 1192-1219 (email only, no push); frontend/src/app/services/investment.service.ts:125 (getInvestmentHistory has no callers in frontend/src/app), 237-276 (FUNDING_FAILED and TRADING_FAILED labels and isStatusFailed exist but are not used by any template or page)
- Detail: The backend does try to tell the user when ACH funding fails, when the bank returns a transfer, and when trades fail. But the only channel is email, which is mocked, and the app has no screen or banner that shows a failed execution: the history API client and failed-status helpers are dead code. So today a returned ACH is invisible to the user. Also, the returned-transfer path does not set an ACH_RETURN_* hold (no setHoldReason with those values anywhere), so the schedule would pull again next payday; that part belongs to the money-movement audit but changes what the notice should say.
- Suggested fix: Add an in-app notice for the latest failed execution (dashboard banner plus an activity list using the existing history endpoint), add push to the failure path, and replace the generic copy with the classified copy in the spec (NSF, unauthorized, account invalid).
- Verifier (confirmed): True. notifyUserOfFailure is called at InvestmentExecutionService.java:401, 525, 639, 879, 891, 1013, 1130 and both overloads (1192-1219) only call emailService.sendEmail, which is mocked. On the frontend, getInvestmentHistory (investment.service.ts:125) has no callers, and FUNDING_FAILED / TRADING_FAILED / isStatusFailed appear only inside investment.service.ts itself; no template or page uses them, and recurring-investments.page.html has no failure state. So a failed or returned transfer is invisible in the app. The side claim that no ACH_RETURN_* hold is ever set is also correct (setHoldReason is only called with BANK_CHANGE, MANUAL_REVIEW or null). Extra detail: the failure email body passes raw internal 'Reason/Details' strings to the user (lines 1195-1199), which may expose broker error text. Given that with email mocked there is literally no channel telling a user their money did not move, this is at the top of high and could be argued as a beta blocker once paired with the provider finding; I leave it at high because fixing the provider restores the email channel.

### [high] No notification for withdrawals, sells, lump-sum deposits, bank changes, passkey reset or email change

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/TradingController.java:108 and 206-250 (sell and withdraw endpoints, no EmailService use); full call-site list of emailService in backend: MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:84, InvestmentExecutionService.java:1186/1201/1214, AccountStatusService.java:167, PaydayNotificationService.java:67/81/96 (grep result, nothing else); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:86-98 (passkeys cleared, no notice); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentScheduleService.java:241-256 (bank change, no notice)
- Detail: Money leaving the account (withdrawal to bank), a sale of holdings, a change of linked bank account, all passkeys being wiped by recovery, and a change of login email are the events a financial app must confirm out of band, because they are the events an attacker causes. None of them sends anything. FRED-101 in the backlog lists deposit and withdrawal confirmations as intended. Which trade confirmations and statements Alpaca sends on FRED's behalf versus what FRED must send is a question for Alpaca and counsel.
- Suggested fix: Add transactional emails for: withdrawal requested and completed, sell placed, lump-sum started and invested, bank account changed, passkeys reset, email changed, account closed. Confirm with Alpaca which regulatory documents (trade confirms, statements, tax forms) they deliver and whether FRED must send 'document available' notices.
- Verifier (adjusted): Mostly confirmed, one overstatement. The full list of emailService.sendEmail call sites is MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:84, PaydayNotificationService.java:67/81/96, InvestmentExecutionService.java:1186/1201/1214 and AccountStatusService.java:167. TradingController (sell/percentage :108, liquidate :164, withdraw :206, account/close :310) has no EmailService use; RecoveryController.java:86-98 wipes passkeys with no notice; UserController.java:869-908 changes email with no notice; resetForBankChange (InvestmentScheduleService.java:241-256) sends nothing. Correction: lump-sum deposits are not entirely without notice. One-off executions go through the same execution pipeline, so they get the generic success email at COMPLETED (InvestmentExecutionService.java:1123) and the failure email, and they are also swept into the D+0 and D+1 payday emails because findByScheduledDate has no type filter. Those are generic and mocked, but they exist. Withdrawals do get an in-app toast at request time (sell-withdraw.page.ts:409) but nothing out of band and nothing on completion. Severity high is fair for public launch; for the private beta the security-relevant ones (withdrawal, bank change, passkey reset, email change) matter most. What Alpaca delivers itself (trade confirms, statements) is an external question and was not verifiable from the repo.

### [high] Market breakdown email: unsubscribe link is '#', there is no opt-out flag, and the send is gated only by selectedTier

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/MarketBreakdownEmailComposer.java:119-123 ('unsubscribeUrl', '#' with comment that the real provider substitutes it); backend/src/main/resources/templates/email/market-breakdown.html:37-38 (Unsubscribe anchor, no postal address in footer); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:31 and 68 (eligible = selectedTier in plus, pro; no opt-out or marketing consent check); grep for unsubscribe in backend returned only the composer line; frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:158-162 (Piggy Plus promises 'Monthly market breakdown')
- Detail: This is the one tier feature in my domain. The content pipeline, templates, idempotent monthly send and catch-up runs are built, but the email is never delivered (mocked), so the Piggy Plus promise is not met today. When a provider is switched on, the email will go out with a dead unsubscribe link, no List-Unsubscribe header (EmailMessage has no headers field, dto/EmailMessage.java:15-21), no physical address, and no per-user opt-out stored anywhere. Whether this recurring content email counts as commercial under CAN-SPAM is a counsel call, but a working unsubscribe is expected either way. Eligibility is whatever tier string the user picked, and since billing is not built, anyone who selected plus or pro would receive it without paying.
- Suggested fix: Add a per-user email preference (at least market_breakdown opt-out), a signed unsubscribe endpoint, a List-Unsubscribe header on the message, and the company postal address in the footer. Have the scheduler check the preference, and move eligibility to the real entitlement once billing exists.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. MarketBreakdownEmailComposer.java:119-123 passes unsubscribeUrl '#'; the template footer (templates/email/market-breakdown.html:31-39) has the disclaimer, help@fredvested.com and the Unsubscribe anchor but no postal address. EmailMessage (dto/EmailMessage.java:15-21) has only to, from, subject, htmlBody, textBody, so no headers can be set. No email opt-out or preference column exists (the only opt-out columns are analytics_opt_out and paycheck_autopilot_opt_out). MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:31 and 68 select users purely by selectedTier in (plus, pro). The Piggy Plus promise is at investmentconfirmation.component.ts:158-162. Severity note: this is not a private beta blocker because nothing is delivered today, but it is a go-live gate for the moment a real provider is enabled, and the tier promise is unmet until then. One more thing the auditor did not flag: the scheduler has no schedulerGate or job-lock check (lines 49-61), unlike InvestmentScheduler, so it runs in every profile and on every instance; duplicates are limited by the hasBeenSent check and the unique index, but narrative generation can still be triggered wherever the backend runs. Whether CAN-SPAM applies is a counsel question.

### [medium] Push permission is requested on cold start before login, and the token is lost when there is no session

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/app.component.ts:92-104 (registerDeviceToken called once inside platform.ready, the only call site); frontend/src/app/services/push-notification.service.ts:22 (requestPermissions immediately), 35-44 (storePushToken sends 'Bearer ${jwtToken}' with whatever getValidJwtToken returns, error only console.warn); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:581-611 (single token per user, overwrite); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/User.java:310-313
- Detail: A brand new user gets the system notification prompt on first launch, before any context about why. If they are not logged in yet, the PATCH goes out with no valid token and fails, and nothing retries after login, so the token is only stored on a later cold start while already signed in. There are no listeners for registrationError, pushNotificationReceived or pushNotificationActionPerformed, so a tap on a notification cannot route anywhere. The token is never cleared on logout or account switch, so a second user on the same phone would leave the first user's row pointing at that device. One column means one device per user. Apple review tends to push back on permission prompts with no context.
- Suggested fix: Move the permission request to a moment with context (after first schedule is created, with a pre-prompt), register the token after login and on token refresh, clear it on logout, add the received and action listeners with deep links, and consider a device-token table if more than one device matters.

### [medium] D+1 'You moved closer to freedom' email is sent regardless of outcome, and one investment produces up to four overlapping emails

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PaydayNotificationService.java:89-102 (no status check, same findByScheduledDate query); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:1123 and 1178-1190 (separate 'Investment Executed Successfully' email on COMPLETED); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PaydayNotificationService.java:67, 81, 96 (all use the legacy sendEmail with no periodKey); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:43-45 and 88-91 (legacy path has null userId and periodKey, so always inserts, no dedupe)
- Detail: A user whose transfer was returned still gets 'You just got closer to financial freedom' the next morning, possibly alongside a 'Funding Failed' email. For a successful cycle the user gets D-1, D+0, D+1 and a separate success email, written in two different voices. None of these use the email_log idempotency key, so a job that runs twice (restart, or two instances; the three notification crons take no job lock, unlike the 11:55 job at InvestmentScheduler.java:48) sends duplicates. The legacy path also records no user id, so email_log cannot answer 'what did we send this user'.
- Suggested fix: Define one notification matrix (the spec's section 10 is a good base), route every send through a sendOnce(type, userId, periodKey) path, gate D+1 on a non-failed status, and delete the duplicate success email or the D+1 email. Add job locks to the three notification crons.

### [medium] Failure emails can carry raw broker error text and unformatted amounts

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:525 (passes transferResponse.errorMessage straight into the email), 394-401 (details string passed to user), 1194-1199 and 1210-1214 ('$%s' with a BigDecimal, 'Reason: %s Details: %s')
- Detail: When the ACH call fails at initiation, the Alpaca error message is placed in the email body as 'Details'. That can expose internal ids or confusing API wording to the user. Amounts are formatted with %s on a BigDecimal, so the user can see '$300.0000' style values. Subjects such as 'Investment Execution Failed' do not match FRED's voice, and the body tells the user to 'ensure sufficient funds' even for causes that are not about funds.
- Suggested fix: Map failures to a small set of user-safe messages, keep the raw text in execution.errorMessage only, and format money with a currency formatter.

### [medium] Default from-address is noreply@investingapp.com, a domain FRED does not appear to own

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:48 (app.email.from=${EMAIL_FROM:noreply@investingapp.com}); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:35 (same default in code); support address elsewhere is help@fredvested.com (backend/src/main/resources/templates/email/market-breakdown.html:37)
- Detail: If EMAIL_FROM is not set when a provider goes live, mail is sent from a domain that is not fredvested.com. It would fail SPF and DKIM and land in spam or be rejected, and it presents a brand that is not FRED. There is also no display name ('FRED <...>') and no reply-to. Domain authentication records are a DNS task outside the repo.
- Suggested fix: Change the default to a fredvested.com address with a display name, add a reply-to of help@fredvested.com, and make the prod profile require EMAIL_FROM. Set up SPF, DKIM and DMARC for the sending domain with the chosen provider.

### [medium] Transactional emails are plain text with no templates, no branding and no required disclosures

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/templates/email/ (only market-breakdown.html, market-breakdown-numbers.html, market-breakdown-change-rows.html, market-breakdown-empty.html); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:42-45 (legacy path: htmlBody null); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PaydayNotificationService.java:24-28, 81-83, 96-98; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/AccountStatusService.java:167-171
- Detail: Every email except the market breakdown is a one or two sentence plain-text string built inline in a service class. There is no shared layout, no footer, no support contact, and no disclosure language. Statements such as 'Your Freedom Date moves closer' and 'closer to financial freedom' in automated emails from an adviser should be reviewed by counsel. The copy also lives in five different classes, which makes a compliance review hard.
- Suggested fix: Create a shared transactional template (header, body slot, footer with support address and disclosure), move each email's copy into a template file or a single catalog class, and send the catalog to counsel for one review pass.

### [medium] No notification preferences exist: the settings model is a local stub with no UI and no server storage

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/services/settings.service.ts:7-13 and 46-52 (notifications object hardcoded to all true, held in a BehaviorSubject); frontend/src/app/tab3/tab3.page.ts:321-322 and 360-365 (handler only shows a toast with a count; grep of tab3.page.html for 'notifications' returned nothing, so no row triggers it); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:550-560 (agreedToMarketing saved, 'TODO: call marketing email provider API')
- Detail: Users cannot turn any email or push category on or off, and the backend has nowhere to store such a choice. The only consent field is agreedToMarketing from onboarding, which is saved but not acted on (the list sync is a TODO) and is not consulted by any sender. Apple expects push to be controllable, and the unsubscribe work in the market breakdown finding needs somewhere to write.
- Suggested fix: Add a small server-side preferences record (transactional always on; payday reminders, market breakdown, marketing as toggles), a Notifications page in settings, and have senders check it. Decide whether to build or drop the marketing list sync.

### [medium] Plaid bank link breaking produces no user notice

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PlaidService.java:220 (the only write of plaidItemStatus in the backend, always OK); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/PlaidItemStatus.java:5 (LOGIN_REQUIRED and others defined but never set); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/HoldReason.java:8 (ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED defined, never set: setHoldReason call sites are InvestmentScheduleService.java:254, AccountStatusService.java:204, AchRelationshipService.java:111 and 172 only); FREDdocs/specs/2026-10-19-paycheck-autopilot-design.md:586 (RECONNECT notice designed)
- Detail: The enums for a broken bank connection exist, but nothing ever sets them, so there is no 'reconnect your bank' email, push or in-app prompt. I confirmed this by searching for writers of the status and hold; I did not read the Plaid webhook filter code path end to end, so there may be handling I did not see, hence medium confidence.
- Suggested fix: When the Plaid webhook or an API error reports ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED or pending expiration, set the item status, show a reconnect card in the app and send the RECONNECT notice once per incident.

### [medium] Paycheck autopilot notification layer from the approved spec is not built

- Type: decision, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: FREDdocs/specs/2026-10-19-paycheck-autopilot-design.md:566-592 (section 10: NotificationService.sendOnce, NotificationGate, 20 notice types), 371 (ACH_AUTHORIZATION email copy of the authorization), 640 (OpsAlertService), 682 and 696 (real EmailSender and ops alert provider are Phase 2 gates); grep for sendOnce|OpsAlertService|NotificationGate|AUTHORIZATION_REVOKED|ACH_RETURNED in backend/src/main returned nothing
- Detail: The spec makes a real email provider a gate for turning on detected-paycheck pulls, and says the user is emailed a copy of their ACH authorization. None of the notification classes it names exist in the working tree, and there is no operator alert channel, so money-path alarms such as an unauthorized ACH return are only log lines (for example 'alert.ach.adopt_refused' at AchRelationshipService.java:170). If the private beta runs MANUAL schedules only, most of this can wait, but the authorization-copy email and an ops alert channel are worth deciding on now because the per-paycheck billing idea would lean on the same authorization record.
- Suggested fix: Decide the beta scope: MANUAL-only needs PAYDAY_D1, PAYDAY_D0, INVESTED, ACH_RETURNED, RECONNECT and an ops alert channel. Build sendOnce on top of the existing email_log unique key first, since every other notice depends on it.

### [low] Recovery screen says the code expires in 3 minutes; the server uses 15

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/recovery/recovery.page.html:46 ('It expires in 3 minutes.'); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:48 (plusMinutes(15))
- Detail: The user-facing copy and the real expiry disagree. Minor, but it should be settled when the OTP email is written, because the email will state an expiry too.
- Suggested fix: Pick one value and use it in the server, the screen and the email.

### [low] Dead legacy login endpoint claims 'OTP sent to your phone' with no sender

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AuthController.java:105-127 (otpSent hardcoded true, returns '2FA required. OTP sent to your phone.')
- Detail: There is no SMS provider anywhere in the backend. This password login path looks like a leftover from before passkeys and always answers that an SMS code was sent. It is misleading if anything can still reach it. Whether it is reachable is for the auth audit.
