### [low] House-style and polish issues in the email layer

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/templates/email/market-breakdown.html:16 (&mdash; in the header line); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PaydayNotificationService.java:82-84 (emoji in subject and push title); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:50-51 (no schedulerGate check, unlike InvestmentScheduler.java:149); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:605 and backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:57 and 68 (user email addresses written to logs)
- Detail: The market breakdown header uses an em dash, which the house style bans. Emoji in subjects hurts deliverability and tone for a financial sender. The market breakdown job does not check the scheduler gate that the other jobs honor, so any environment with a real provider and plus or pro users would send. Email addresses are logged at info level in several places, which matters once logs go to a third-party drain.
- Suggested fix: Replace the em dash, drop the emoji, add the scheduler gate to the breakdown job, and log user ids instead of addresses.

### [low] Referral founder reward email and marketing list sync are TODO stubs

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/ReferralService.java:72-79 (TODO: sendMerchSelectionEmail, and TODO: IAP promotional offer); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:555-558 (TODO: marketing provider)
- Detail: When a founder hits the referral threshold, referralRewardTriggered is set to true and only a log line is written, so the reward is marked as triggered but the person is never contacted. Because the flag blocks a second trigger (line 65), anyone who crosses the threshold before the email exists will need a manual backfill. The non-founder branch depends on billing, which is undecided.
- Suggested fix: Either send the founder email through EmailService once a provider exists and add a backfill for already-triggered rows, or hide the referral reward promise for the beta.

**Verified solid:**

- EmailService facade is sound: every attempt is written to email_log with status MOCKED, SENT or FAILED, bodies are never stored, and it never throws into callers (backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailService.java:52-117).
- email_log has a unique key on (email_type, user_id, period_key) and the market breakdown uses it for idempotent monthly sends with catch-up runs on the 2nd and 3rd (backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__baseline.sql:112-122; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:50-98).
- The EmailSender port is correctly shaped for a drop-in provider: one interface, selected by app.email.provider, LoggingEmailSender as the default (backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/EmailSender.java:12-16; LoggingEmailSender.java:13-15).
- Market breakdown email has real HTML templates, a plain-text alternative, a not-investment-advice disclaimer in the text body and a support address (backend/src/main/resources/templates/email/*.html; MarketBreakdownEmailComposer.java:119-130). Unit tests exist: EmailServiceTest, MarketBreakdownEmailComposerTest, PaydayNotificationServiceTest (backend/src/test/java/com/investingapp/backend/service). I did not run them.
- Email is explicitly disabled in the ci profile (backend/src/main/resources/application-ci.properties:79-80), and defaults to disabled everywhere else, so no environment can send by accident today.
- D-1 payday notice copy matches the approved spec for MANUAL schedules and correctly skips schedules under a blocking hold; the date is computed once in America/New_York (PaydayNotificationService.java:24-70; InvestmentScheduler.java:147-157).
- Recovery initiate returns the same response whether or not the account exists, and the OTP is encrypted at rest with a 15 minute expiry (RecoveryController.java:37-49). Delivery and rate limiting are the broken parts, not storage.
- KYC rejection keeps the user row, records the decision, holds the schedule and attempts an email in one transaction (AccountStatusService.java:160-180, 204).
- Push token endpoint is authenticated and scoped to the caller: PATCH /user/push-token reads the user from the security principal, not from the body (UserController.java:581-611). The device_push_token column exists in the baseline migration (V1__baseline.sql:423).
- The Capacitor push plugin is installed on a matching major version with the rest of Capacitor 8 and is in the Podfile (frontend/package.json:27-36; frontend/ios/App/Podfile:18), so the old peer-dependency conflict (DEV-190) is gone in the working tree.
- ACTION_REQUIRED does have an in-app signal: the status endpoint returns hasActionRequired and the tabs page shows a badge on settings, with document upload clearing it (AlpacaController.java:145-155; account-status.service.ts:16-22, 58-62; tabs.page.ts:73-82).
- In-app Monthly Freedom Update has a backend controller and frontend store and presenter services, and ToastService is the single toast entry point (MonthlyFreedomUpdateController.java:23; frontend/src/app/services/mfu-store.service.ts, mfu-presenter.service.ts, toast.service.ts). I confirmed they exist; I did not audit their behavior, which belongs to another domain.

## frontend-ux-debt

Scope: main-app frontend (tabs, my-profile, security-settings, freedom-plan, goals, ai-chat, recurring-investments, lump-sum, sell-withdraw, change-bank-account, beneficiaries, faq, tax-documents, change-email, recovery), plus the app shell (routes, main.ts, app.component, auth/app-lock services, angular.json, capacitor config). All findings are from the working tree, read-only. Nothing was built or run, so bundle size against the budget is not measured.  Overall: the screens are mostly real and wired to the backend, with good skeleton loading states and a consistent toast system. The gaps that matter for a beta with real money are in four groups.  1. Shell and build: the iOS app is still configured to load its web code live from the dev laptop tunnel (capacitor server.url), there is no HTTP interceptor, no global 401 handling, no route guards, no offline handling, no crash reporting, and no Sign out button. 2. Paid tier plumbing: every Upgrade and Reactivate button is a console.log stub, and one live path lets a user set their own tier for free, with hardcoded prices and wrong tier names. 3. Broken or misleading screens: Change Email cannot succeed (request sent with no auth header), "Hi, Firstname" is hardcoded on the Profile tab, Legal Information shows a "coming soon" toast, rebalancing copy and "Next rebalance" date have no engine behind them, and several pages show a false "empty" state when a load fails (one of which enables Close My Account). 4. Money actions: one-time invest, sell and withdraw fire immediately on a single tap with no confirmation step unless the optional PIN is on.  Meaningful TODOs found (all listed in findings): app.routes.ts:69, recurring-investments.page.ts:600, kyc-verification.component.html:217, retirement-planning.component.ts:300, investmentconfirmation.component.ts:665 and :776, my-profile.page.ts:890 and :896. There are no FIXME or HACK comments. There are 182 console.log/info/debug calls and 122 console.warn/error calls in non-spec app code, with nothing stripping them in the production build.


### [blocker] iOS app loads its web code from the dev laptop tunnel, and no shippable build configuration exists

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9 (server.url 'https://local.fredvested.com'); frontend/ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json:5-7 (same value synced into the iOS project); frontend/src/environments/environment.prod.ts:7-8 (api.fredvested.com and rpId app.fredvested.com, which do not exist yet); frontend/src/environments/environment.dev.ts:5-9 (production:false, rpId is a GitHub Codespaces hostname); frontend/angular.json dev configuration (optimization false, sourceMap true, no budgets)
- Detail: The native app does not ship its own web bundle today. It points at local.fredvested.com, which is the Cloudflare tunnel to the dev machine. A beta user's app would be blank whenever that laptop is off. The only config that points at a real backend (dev, api-dev) is unoptimized, ships source maps, has production:false (so the devPage and dev login code paths are live), and carries an rpId that is a Codespaces hostname. The production config points at hosts that do not exist yet. So there is currently no configuration that produces a bundled, optimized build against a live backend.
- Suggested fix: Remove server.url for release builds (keep it only in a dev-only capacitor config). Add a beta build configuration: production:true, optimization on, no source maps, budgets on, backendApiUrl and rpId pointing at the backend the beta will really use. Confirm passkeys work with the bundled capacitor:// origin and that rpId. Then run cap sync from that build.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9 and frontend/ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json:5-7 both set server.url to https://local.fredvested.com, and there is only one capacitor config file. angular.json:81-92 (dev) has optimization false, sourceMap true, no budgets; production (angular.json:51-71) swaps in environment.prod.ts, which points at api.fredvested.com (no prod backend exists). Three corrections to the detail: (1) environment.rpId is not read anywhere in frontend/src/app (grep finds only publicKey.rpId from the backend's options at auth.service.ts:567 and passkey-prompt.component.ts:270), so the Codespaces rpId in environment.dev.ts:9 is a dead value, not a live bug. The real passkey gap is the iOS entitlements: Release.entitlements:6 has webcredentials:api.fredvested.com, Test has api-test, Debug has local. None covers api-dev, the only live backend. (2) The 'test' configuration (angular.json:93-100) inherits default optimization, so it is an optimized build, but it targets api-test.fredvested.com with production:false and I could not confirm that host exists. (3) 'devPage and dev login are live' in a dev build is overstated: app.component.ts:175-182 does run when production is false, but dev builds ship an empty devToolsKey (environment.dev.ts:11) and a bundled native app has no easy way to receive a ?devPage query. Still should be production:true for beta.

### [blocker] Referral 'Upgrade Your Plan' card lets a user set their own tier for free, with hardcoded prices and wrong plan names

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:333-344 (offers: 'Premium' $20 normally $40, 'Standard' $10 normally $15, hardcoded), :705-725 (confirmTierUpgrade writes selectedTier via PUT /user/profile and toasts 'Upgraded to ...!'); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:260-277 (card shown when referralCount >= 1); frontend/src/app/services/auth.service.ts:390-393 (updateUserProfile accepts selectedTier from the client)
- Detail: Any user with one redeemed referral sees an offer to upgrade to Pro 'for $20/mo'. Tapping Confirm changes the tier on the server immediately. No payment happens, yet the copy tells the user they are being charged $20 a month. The names (Standard, Premium) and prices do not match the Piggy / Piggy Plus / Piggy Pro tiers used everywhere else. More broadly, the client can PUT any selectedTier to /user/profile, so tier gates are not enforceable until the server stops accepting that field from the client.
- Suggested fix: Remove or hide this card until billing exists. On the backend, stop accepting selectedTier from the general profile endpoint outside onboarding and dev tools (flag for the backend/billing auditor).
- Verifier (confirmed): True. my-profile.page.ts:334-344 hardcodes 'Premium' $20/$40 and 'Standard' $10/$15. my-profile.page.ts:705-725 confirms with 'Upgrade to X for $N/mo' and then PUTs selectedTier. my-profile.page.html:260-277 shows the card whenever referralCount >= 1 and the tier is not pro. The backend accepts any string: UserController.java:528-541 sets selectedTier from the request body with no validation or payment check, and POST /user/subscription/confirm (UserController.java:618-633) does the same. Blocker stands for two reasons: the app tells a user they will be charged $20 a month when nothing is charged, and a self-set 'pro' passes the real TLH gates (TlhController.java:272, TlhExecutionService.java:142), which is a feature that places trades. In a founder-heavy beta the card is likely to show, because a privateBeta referral counts at once (UserService.java:140-146). Hiding the card is small; the server-side fix belongs with the billing work.

### [blocker] Legal Information row shows 'coming soon'; no Terms of Service or Privacy Policy is reachable inside the app

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/tab3/tab3.page.ts:153-158 (row 'Legal Information: Terms of Service & Privacy Policy'), :336-338 (showComingSoon('Legal Information')), :392-394 (toast 'Legal Information feature coming soon!'); frontend/src/app/components/kyc-verification/kyc-verification.component.html:217 ('TODO: replace with real Alpaca agreement URLs'); a grep of all http(s) URLs in app templates and code finds no terms or privacy link
- Detail: A live settings row promises the Terms and Privacy Policy and answers with a yellow 'coming soon' toast. The App Store requires a reachable privacy policy, and a brokerage app needs its agreements and disclosures to be viewable after sign-up. The documents themselves are a counsel deliverable, but the row, the viewer and the KYC agreement links are app work.
- Suggested fix: Build a simple Legal page (or open hosted URLs in the in-app browser) for Terms, Privacy, and the Alpaca customer agreement. Replace the KYC placeholder links. Until the documents exist, remove the row rather than ship 'coming soon'.
- Verifier (adjusted): True, and understated. tab3.page.ts:153-158, :336-338 and :392-394 are as described. The auditor missed the worse part: the required consent checkbox at the end of onboarding links to nothing. investmentconfirmation.component.html:135-137 has 'Terms of Service' and 'Privacy Policy' as href="#" with preventDefault, and the Continue button is gated on that checkbox (:300-301). The Alpaca agreements at account opening are the same (kyc-verification.component.html:217-220, three dead links plus a TODO). A grep of every http(s) URL in app code finds no terms, privacy or agreement link. So a real user would open a brokerage account and agree to documents they cannot open. That cannot go to real users with real money, so blocker. The documents are a counsel and Alpaca deliverable (external); the links and a viewer are small app work.

### [high] Change Email can never succeed: the request is sent with no Authorization header

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/change-email/change-email.page.ts:158-164 (http.post to /user/update-email with payload only, no headers); frontend/src/main.ts:30 (provideHttpClient() with no interceptors); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/UserController.java:869-873 (endpoint reads Authentication principal); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/config/SecurityConfig.java:112-119 (anyRequest().authenticated(), update-email is not in the permitAll list)
- Detail: Every other call in the app attaches the JWT by hand. This one does not, and there is no interceptor to add it, so the backend will reject it as unauthenticated and the user sees 'Failed to update email.' after passing the passkey prompt. Separately, when it is fixed: the backend changes the email immediately with no verification of the new address (UserController.java:895-896). Account recovery is email-OTP based, so a typo in the new email would lock the user out of recovery.
- Suggested fix: Add the auth header (or better, add one HTTP interceptor for all calls). Decide whether to require an OTP sent to the new address before the change is applied.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. frontend/src/app/pages/change-email/change-email.page.ts:164 posts to /user/update-email with the payload only. frontend/src/main.ts:30 is provideHttpClient() with no interceptors, and a grep for Interceptor/withInterceptors in frontend/src returns nothing. SecurityConfig.java:112-119 does not permit /api/user/update-email, so the request is rejected before the controller. The page is reachable from security-settings.page.ts:314. The second point is also true: UserController.java:895-896 sets the new email at once with no verification of the new address. I did not run it, but there is no code path that could attach the token.

### [high] No HTTP interceptor and no global 401 handling: a server-rejected session leaves the user stuck with generic failure toasts

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/main.ts:30 (provideHttpClient() only); grep for Interceptor/withInterceptors/HTTP_INTERCEPTORS/ErrorHandler in frontend/src returns nothing; only frontend/src/app/pages/goal/goal.page.ts:431-433 and :1183-1185 handle 401 (toast and logout); frontend/src/app/services/app-lock.service.ts:148-154 (lock is triggered only by the client-side JWT expiry check, every 60s); frontend/src/app/pages/security-settings/security-settings.page.ts:291-301 (sessions can be revoked from another device)
- Detail: Session expiry is detected only by the client reading the token's own expiry. If the server rejects a token that the client still thinks is valid (session revoked from Trusted Devices, signing key rotated, clock skew, account closed), every screen just shows 'Failed to load...' toasts. Nothing sends the user to re-authenticate, except on the goal page. Each service and page also builds its own 'Bearer ${token}' header, which is how the Change Email bug happened, and several send 'Bearer null' when the token is missing (for example lump-sum-investment.page.ts:304-309, push-notification.service.ts:37-40).
- Suggested fix: Add one functional interceptor that attaches the token and, on 401 from a non-auth endpoint, triggers the existing lock/reauth flow (AppLockService.lockApp(true)) once. Remove the per-call header building over time.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. main.ts:30 has no interceptors. The only 401 handling in app code is goal.page.ts:1183-1185 (and :433), plus pin-prompt.component.ts:110, which is about a wrong PIN, not session loss. app-lock.service.ts:151-157 locks only when the client's own token check fails. 'Bearer null' is real: lump-sum-investment.page.ts:304-309 and push-notification.service.ts:36-40 build the header without a null check (portfolio-customize.component.ts:265-268 does check). Sessions can be revoked from another device (security-settings.page.ts:291-301), so a server-side rejection of a token the client still trusts is a reachable state.

### [high] All Upgrade and Reactivate buttons are stubs that do nothing

- Type: decision, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:403-405 (reactivateSubscription: console.log only), :889-898 (upgradeToPlusClicked and upgradeToProClicked: TODO + console.log); frontend/src/app/components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.ts:299-302 (TODO: trigger Plus subscription IAP), called from :119, :255, :261, :327; frontend/src/app/pages/goal/goal.page.ts:1175-1180 (TIER_LIMIT path, 'No IAP flow yet'); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:24 (Reactivate button), :156 (TLH CTA); frontend/src/app/components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.html:105-111 (Monte Carlo lock overlay Upgrade button); frontend/src/app/tabs/tabs.page.ts:84-90 (isSubExpired gates the tabs)
- Detail: A core-tier user who taps Upgrade on the Monte Carlo lock, the scenario chips, a strategy deck, the goals nudge, the rebalancing nudge or the TLH nudge gets no response at all. A user whose tier is empty (and who is not flagged privateBeta) has the tab bar gated as 'subscription inactive' and the only way out, the Reactivate button, is also a no-op. This is blocked on the billing model decision (per-paycheck plan, pending counsel), but the app work is to give every one of these buttons a real destination.
- Suggested fix: Once the billing model is decided, route all of these through one upgrade service. Until then, for the private beta either hide the gates for privateBeta users or have the buttons open a single plain 'Paid plans are not open yet' sheet so no button is dead.
- Verifier (adjusted): The facts are true: my-profile.page.ts:403-405, :889-898, retirement-planning.component.ts:299-302 and goal.page.ts:1175-1180 are console.log only, and they are wired to live buttons (my-profile.page.html:24, retirement-planning.component.html:106). Severity is inflated for a private beta. Dead Upgrade buttons do not put money at risk. The lockout part (tabs gated, Reactivate dead) is not reachable today: tabs.page.ts:84-89 skips the gate for privateBeta users, and the tier only becomes null through SubscriptionService.expireSubscription (SubscriptionService.java:37-47), which nothing calls (only confirmSubscription is called, UserController.java:631), or through a client PUT of selectedTier null (UserController.java:528-533). So: high for the beta (no dead buttons in front of real users), and it becomes a blocker for the public launch as part of the billing work. Type 'decision' is right.

No code in tab3.page.ts touches a greeting or first name. It is cosmetic, but it sits in the header of a main tab for every user and is a few minutes to fix, so it should be done before any real user sees the app.

### [high] One-time invest, sell and withdraw execute on a single tap with no confirmation step

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/lump-sum-investment/lump-sum-investment.page.ts:353-400 (makeInvestment goes from validation straight to POST /investments/execute; PIN only if the user turned it on); frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.ts:311-335 (sellPosition), :383-408 (withdrawCash); frontend/src/app/pages/security-settings/security-settings.page.ts:65 (step-up PIN is off by default). Compare with close account, which does use a confirm sheet (sell-withdraw.page.ts:591-600)
- Detail: With the optional PIN off (the default), tapping 'Make Investment' pulls money from the bank, tapping 'Sell VTI' places a market sell, and tapping withdraw starts an ACH out, all with no 'You are about to...' review. A slip of the thumb moves real money and a sale can be a taxable event. The app already has presentConfirmSheet with a busy state for exactly this.
- Suggested fix: Put a presentConfirmSheet in front of all three, stating amount, source or destination, and (for sells) that it is a market order that may create a taxable gain.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. lump-sum-investment.page.ts:353-400 goes from validateInvestment straight to POST /investments/execute. sell-withdraw.page.ts:311-335 and :383-408 go straight to the trading service. The PIN prompt runs only when pinService.hasPin() is true, and security-settings.page.ts:65 defaults sensitiveAuthEnabled to false. The templates call these directly from the CTA (lump-sum-investment.page.html:162, sell-withdraw.page.html:218 and :301). Fair context, which does not change the verdict: sell needs a position picked and a percentage set first, the buttons are disabled while busy, and the lump sum carries a clientUuid so a retry cannot double-invest. None of that is a review step. Close account already uses presentConfirmSheet (sell-withdraw.page.ts:591-600).

### [high] Failed loads are shown as real 'empty' states on several screens, and on Sell & Withdraw this enables Close My Account

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: Sell & Withdraw: frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.ts:207-240 (on error: toast only, positions stays []), :562-564 (canCloseAccount = positions.length === 0 && withdrawable cash <= 0), sell-withdraw.page.html:115-146 ('No Holdings Found', both checklist items green, Close My Account enabled). Recurring: frontend/src/app/recurring-investments/recurring-investments.page.ts:160-178 and recurring-investments.page.html:213-222 ('No Schedule Set' after a non-404 failure). Tax documents: frontend/src/app/pages/tax-documents/tax-documents.page.ts:118-121 (error: console only, no toast) and tax-documents.page.html:65-69 ('No Documents Yet'). Beneficiaries: frontend/src/app/beneficiaries/beneficiaries.page.ts:103-116. Change bank: frontend/src/app/change-bank-account/change-bank-account.page.ts:86-94 (silent) and change-bank-account.page.html ('No bank linked yet'). Security sessions: frontend/src/app/pages/security-settings/security-settings.page.ts:145-147 (silent, empty list)
- Detail: When the network or backend fails, these pages tell the user something untrue: you have no holdings, no schedule, no documents, no beneficiaries, no bank. The worst case is Sell & Withdraw: after a failed load the page says there are no holdings and no cash, ticks both close-account steps and enables the Close My Account button. The backend should still refuse, but the screen is wrong and alarming for someone with money in the account. None of these pages has a retry control.
- Suggested fix: Give each page a distinct loadFailed state with a 'Could not load. Try again' card and a retry button, and never render the empty state or the close-account box unless the load actually succeeded.
- Verifier (confirmed): True on every page cited. Sell & Withdraw: sell-withdraw.page.ts:228-238 only toasts on error, positions stays [] and balance null, getWithdrawableCash() returns 0 (:505-510), so canCloseAccount (:562-564) is true and the template shows 'No Holdings Found', two green ticks and an enabled Close My Account (sell-withdraw.page.html:115-146). This applies when the first load fails; a later failed reload keeps the earlier data. Recurring: recurring-investments.page.ts:160-178 plus html:213-222. Tax documents: tax-documents.page.ts:118-121 logs only, no toast. Beneficiaries: beneficiaries.page.ts:103-116. Change bank: change-bank-account.page.ts:86-94 is silent. Sessions: security-settings.page.ts:145-147 is silent. One correction that makes the close-account case more serious than stated: 'the backend should still refuse' is not something FRED's own code does. TradingController.java:311-336 checks only the in-flight funding deferral, then calls alpacaService.closeAccount, which is a bare POST to /actions/close (AlpacaService.java:925-940). Refusal for an account that still holds positions or cash depends entirely on Alpaca rejecting it, which I could not confirm. The passkey prompt and confirm sheet still stand in front (sell-withdraw.page.ts:566-600). Worth adding a server-side positions and cash check.

### [high] Portfolio Allocation silently shows the default 75/20/5 portfolio when the load fails, and saving then overwrites the user's real allocation

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/components/portfolio-customize/portfolio-customize.component.ts:274-303 (catch: console.error then setDefaultPortfolio(), no toast), :306-336 (hardcoded VTI 75 / VXUS 20 / VBR 5), :613-628 (savePortfolio PUTs whatever is on screen to /portfolio/update); entry from frontend/src/app/tab3/tab3.page.ts:292-294
- Detail: If GET /portfolio/current fails, the page shows the default portfolio as if it were the user's own, with no error. A user with a custom allocation who then adjusts a slider and saves replaces their real allocation with a variation of the default. This drives where recurring money is invested.
- Suggested fix: On load failure show an error card with retry and disable Save. Only use the default list when the server says the user has no custom portfolio.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. portfolio-customize.component.ts:297-300 catches any load error, logs it and calls setDefaultPortfolio() with no toast. :306-336 hardcodes VTI 75 / VXUS 20 / VBR 5 and stores it as originalPortfolio. The save path (:613-628) PUTs whatever is on screen to /portfolio/update. Since the default becomes the 'original', the user has to change something before saving, but then their real custom allocation is replaced. The page is reachable from tab3.page.ts:293. I did not check whether the backend adds any guard on /portfolio/update, but it has no way to know the client never loaded the real allocation.

### [high] Rebalancing section promises automatic rebalancing and a 'Next rebalance' date, but the schedule is saved only on the phone and no rebalancing engine exists

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:172-203 ('FRED keeps your portfolio on target. When your allocation drifts, FRED automatically realigns it.', 'Next rebalance: {{ nextRebalanceDate }}'); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:67-81 (date computed locally from the calendar), :727-746 (selectSchedule calls goalsService.savePrefs, comment: 'a synchronous localStorage write'); frontend/src/app/services/goals.service.ts:192-195 (localStorage only); grep for 'rebalanc' in backend/src/main/java finds only an enum value and comments (WashSaleGuardService.java:26,70,91,107 'any future rebalancer'), no job or service
- Detail: The chosen schedule (Quarterly / Semi-annual / Annual) never reaches the server, is lost on reinstall or a new phone, and nothing on the backend rebalances a portfolio. The screen shows a specific next rebalance date for something that will not happen. This is both a setting that is not persisted and a statement to customers about account activity that is not true. It is also a Plus-tier selling point.
- Suggested fix: Either build the rebalancer and persist the schedule server-side, or remove the claims and the date and mark the section 'Coming soon' like Tax Loss Harvesting.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. my-profile.page.html:182-183 says 'FRED keeps your portfolio on target. When your allocation drifts, FRED automatically realigns it. No trade approvals are needed.' and :198-200 shows 'Next rebalance: {{ nextRebalanceDate }}' for Plus and Pro. The date is pure calendar math (my-profile.page.ts:67-81). selectSchedule (:729-735) calls goalsService.savePrefs, which is localStorage only (goals.service.ts:192-199). A case-insensitive grep for 'rebalanc' in backend/src/main/java finds only an enum value and comments in WashSaleGuardService.java:26, :70, :91, :107, a narrative validator phrase and an analytics topic label. There is no rebalancing job or service. Core users are also told 'FRED uses the default quarterly schedule' (html:202), which is equally untrue. Because this is a false statement about account activity in a brokerage app, treat it as must-fix before real users see it; removing the claims is small, building the engine is large.

### [high] No crash or error reporting in the client

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: grep for ErrorHandler and Sentry in frontend/src and frontend/package.json returns nothing; frontend/src/main.ts:21-33 (no error handler provider); errors go to console.error only (122 console.warn/error calls)
- Detail: If a beta user hits a JavaScript error or a blank screen, there is no record of it anywhere. With real money involved you need to know about failures before users report them. The analytics layer notes mention Sentry as planned, but nothing is installed.
- Suggested fix: Add a global ErrorHandler that reports to an error service (Sentry or similar), scrubbed of PII, and report unhandled promise rejections too.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. A grep for ErrorHandler, Sentry and sentry across frontend/src and frontend/package.json returns nothing, and main.ts:21-33 registers no error handler. There is no window.onerror or unhandledrejection listener in app code. One piece of context: handled failures that show a toast or banner are sent to PostHog through analytics.trackErrorShown (for example sell-withdraw.page.ts:230), so known error paths are counted, and only from dev and prod builds (analytics.service.ts:273). Uncaught JavaScript errors, blank screens and stack traces are recorded nowhere. I did not check whether PostHog exception autocapture is turned on in the SDK init options; nothing in analytics.service.ts mentions it.

### [medium] Change Bank Account shows errors in a green success card, exposes raw error text, and dead-ends when the link token fails

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/change-bank-account/change-bank-account.page.html: success card shown unless the message contains lowercase 'error', 'Could not' or 'Failed' (the two *ngIf conditions on the status cards); frontend/src/app/change-bank-account/change-bank-account.page.ts:171 ('Link flow exited: ...'), :174 ('Link flow closed by user.'), :202 ('Error: ${error.message ...}' where error.message is Angular's raw 'Http failure response for <url>'), :229 ('Plaid is not ready'), :275; :106-112 (script load failure leaves isPlaidReady false, so the Try again button stays disabled, as the code comment admits)
- Detail: The page decides success versus error by searching the message text. Messages that start with a capital 'Error:' or 'Link flow exited' do not match, so they render with the green check icon. The raw HTTP failure string, including the API URL, can be shown to the user. If the link token request or the Plaid script fails, the main button stays on 'Preparing secure connection...' forever and the retry button is disabled, so the only way out is to leave the page. Closing Plaid normally shows 'Link flow closed by user.' as a success.
- Suggested fix: Track status as a typed state (idle, ready, success, error) instead of string matching. Use plain copy for errors. Make Try again re-run initializePlaidLink. Show nothing when the user simply closes Plaid.

### [medium] Raw backend or Angular error strings are shown to users in several places

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/lump-sum-investment/lump-sum-investment.page.ts:435-442 (error.error.message, else error.message); frontend/src/app/beneficiaries/beneficiaries.page.ts:235-242 and :282-289 ('Submission failed: ${error.message}'); frontend/src/app/components/document-upload/document-upload.component.ts:123-124; frontend/src/app/components/portfolio-dashboard/portfolio-dashboard.component.ts:145 and portfolio-dashboard.component.html (error card prints {{ error }}); frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.ts:413 and :629 (response.error); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.ts:694-695; frontend/src/app/pages/change-email/change-email.page.ts:185
- Detail: When there is no server message, error.message from HttpClient reads like 'Http failure response for https://api.../investments/execute: 0 Unknown Error'. That is what a user sees in a red toast when they are offline. Server messages are also passed through unreviewed, so any internal wording reaches the user.
- Suggested fix: Add one helper that maps an HttpErrorResponse to user copy (offline, timeout, server problem, known business codes) and use it everywhere. Only show server messages that come from a known list of codes.

### [medium] No offline handling anywhere

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: grep for navigator.onLine, @capacitor/network, 'offline' in frontend/src/app (non-spec) returns nothing; @capacitor/network is not in frontend/package.json dependencies (lines 27-36); the one good case is the dashboard snapshot marker, frontend/src/app/components/portfolio-dashboard/portfolio-dashboard.component.ts:147-151
- Detail: There is no connectivity check, no offline banner and no retry when the connection returns. Offline, each screen fails in its own way: a toast, a false empty state (see the separate finding), a raw 'Http failure response' string, or silence. The AI chat has a retry button and the dashboard keeps its last snapshot, which is the right pattern but is the exception.
- Suggested fix: Add a small connectivity service (Capacitor Network plugin), a global 'You are offline' banner, and have the shared error helper return an offline message when status is 0.

### [medium] 182 console.log calls ship in the bundle, some printing sensitive values

- Detail: Nothing removes these in a production build. On iOS they are readable through Safari Web Inspector on a debuggable build and add noise and a little cost everywhere. Tokens and account numbers should never be logged.

### [medium] Tax Loss Harvesting card shows a made-up savings number and a hardcoded '$0.00 saved' for Pro

### [medium] Push permission is requested at cold launch and the token is only saved if the user is already signed in

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/app.component.ts:103 (registerDeviceToken called on platform ready for every launch, before any screen); frontend/src/app/services/push-notification.service.ts:14-32 (requestPermissions immediately), :35-44 (PATCH /user/push-token with whatever token is in storage, 'Bearer null' if none); no listener for pushNotificationReceived or pushNotificationActionPerformed anywhere in frontend/src/app; grep of ios/App/App/*.entitlements for aps-environment returned nothing (not confirmed beyond that grep)
- Detail: A brand new user gets the iOS notification permission prompt before they have seen the first onboarding screen, which usually means a 'Don't Allow'. If they do allow, the token save fails because they are not signed in, and it is not retried after sign-in, only on the next cold launch. Tapping a notification does nothing specific. There is no in-app notification setting. The native push entitlement appears to be missing, so push may not work at all yet; the iOS release auditor should confirm.
- Suggested fix: Ask for permission after onboarding at a moment with context, save the token after authentication succeeds, and add a tap handler. Confirm APNs entitlement and provider setup.

### [medium] Close account clears storage but does not reset in-memory auth state

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.ts:616-626 (analytics.reset, localStorage.clear, sessionStorage.clear, navigateByUrl('/'), no AuthService.logout()); frontend/src/app/services/auth.service.ts:197-206 (logout is what clears isLoggedIn, userProgress and in-flight state); frontend/src/app/app.component.ts:208-210 and :493-510 (on resume, checkSurveyStatusAndNavigate routes by the in-memory progress)
- Detail: After a successful close, the in-memory session still says 'logged in, fully onboarded'. Reading the code, the next app resume would route by that stale progress and could send the closed account back to the tabs, where every call fails. I did not run this, so treat the exact symptom as likely rather than proven. The portfolio, goals and MFU caches are also not cleared.
- Suggested fix: Call AuthService.logout() (which already wipes the stores) before clearing storage and navigating.

### [medium] Close account is hard to find: it only appears inside Sell & Withdraw once there are no holdings

They would have to guess that selling everything inside 'Sell & Withdraw' reveals the option. The flow itself (passkey, confirm sheet, 60-day deferral message) is solid.

### [medium] AI chat has no on-screen disclaimer, sends the message text in the URL, and has no stream timeout

- Type: decision, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/ai-chat/ai-chat.page.html:1-129 (no disclaimer text; the only 'not investment advice' copy in the app is in mc-info-sheet.component.html:33 and strategy-deck.component.html:72); frontend/src/app/services/chat.service.ts:54-66 (streamChat builds URLSearchParams with message and userId and calls fetch GET /stream?message=...); frontend/src/app/pages/ai-chat/ai-chat.page.ts:524-575 (isLoading is cleared only by a token, done, error or complete event; no timeout) and ai-chat.page.html:122-123 (send disabled while isLoading)
- Detail: Three separate points. (1) Whether the chat needs a visible 'educational, not advice' line is a counsel question; today there is none. (2) The user's question travels as a query string, so personal financial questions end up in access logs and proxy logs on the way to the server. (3) If the stream connects but stalls, the thinking indicator stays and the send button stays disabled until the user starts a new chat or restarts.
- Suggested fix: Ask counsel for the disclaimer wording and placement. Move the stream to POST with a JSON body (fetch supports streaming a POST response). Add an inactivity timeout that fails the message into the existing retry state.

### [medium] No route guards: an authenticated user whose progress fails to load is sent to the tabs even if onboarding is not finished

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: grep for canActivate / CanActivateFn in frontend/src/app returns nothing; frontend/src/app/app.component.ts:626-639 (holdAuthenticatedUserOnFallback: from any onboarding route, navigate to /tabs/tab1); :148-152 (?testing=true skips all navigation logic in every build)
- Detail: Routing is done by one subscription in AppComponent, not by guards. The fallback for 'progress could not be loaded' assumes a valid token means a fully onboarded user. A user who is signed in but still at KYC or bank linking, and whose /user/progress call fails three times, lands on the main tabs with an account that is not open. The backend should still block money actions, so the impact is confusion rather than loss. Lower priority on native since there is no address bar.
- Suggested fix: In the fallback, use the JWT next-step hint (already available via JwtTokenUtils.getNextStepHint()) to decide: only go to tabs when the hint is 'complete', otherwise stay put and show a retry.

**Verified solid:**

- No alert(), window.confirm or Ionic AlertController anywhere in app code. Confirmations use presentConfirmSheet and all notifications go through the single ToastService (frontend/src/app/services/toast.service.ts). Error toasts stay up 10 seconds.
- No FIXME or HACK comments. The only meaningful TODOs are the eight listed in the summary.
- Dev mocks (portfolio XLARGE, tier 'plus', FORCE_MFU true, goals) are switched on in the working tree but are gated on environment.local, which is false in the dev, test and prod environment files (services/portfolio.service.ts:89, monthly-freedom-update.service.ts:103-108, goals.service.ts:205, auth.service.ts:233). They cannot activate in a dev or prod configuration build.
- Dev login helpers in app.component.ts (devPage, simulateUserLogin) are gated on !environment.production and the dev tools key is empty in every committed environment file.
- Tab 1 portfolio dashboard: skeleton loading state, error card with a Try Again button, pull to refresh, and a last-known snapshot with an 'as of' marker when revalidation fails (portfolio-dashboard.component.ts:128-158 and the template).
- Cold start and resume handling: progress load has a 10s timeout, two retries on transient errors, and a tagged fallback that never ejects a signed-in user to get-started (auth.service.ts:339-378, app.component.ts:298-307). A 40s safety net re-drives navigation so the launch cover cannot hang.
- Token refresh while the app is in the foreground and the lock screen on client-side JWT expiry are implemented with a self-clearing in-progress latch (app-lock.service.ts:115-154, auth.service.ts:455-470).
- Goal page and goal quiz: load and save failures are handled with toasts, 401 signs the user out with a clear message, and a 403 TIER_LIMIT is recognised (goal.page.ts:430-436, 1171-1188). The one-goal limit is enforced in the UI and, per the code comment, on the backend.
- Recurring investments: skeleton, 404 treated as 'no schedule', validation of amount, pause confirm sheet when a streak would be lost, step-up PIN, account-not-active gate both client side and on 403 (recurring-investments.page.ts:436-529).
- One-time investment sends a clientUuid idempotency key per tap, so a retried request does not double invest (lump-sum-investment.page.ts:380-388).
- Close account flow itself: passkey re-auth, destructive confirm sheet with a busy state, and a specific message for the 60-day DELETION_DEFERRED case (sell-withdraw.page.ts:566-651).
- DRIP toggle and the 'Share anonymous usage data' toggle are both saved to the server and revert on failure with a toast (sell-withdraw.page.ts:431-476, security-settings.page.ts:184-229). App Lock is a device setting by design.
- Tax document open path: empty file check, PDF header check, error toast, in-app PDF viewer (tax-documents.page.ts:134-205).
- Recovery page: every failure branch (send code, verify, passkey start, passkey cancel, passkey finish) ends with a toast and clears the loading state (recovery.page.ts:58-126).
- AI chat: a failed send marks the message as failed, shows an apology bubble and offers a retry button; partial streams are still saved (ai-chat.page.ts:552-575).
- Monte Carlo and strategy decks carry an 'educational, not investment advice' line (mc-info-sheet.component.html:33, strategy-deck.component.html:72).
- All tab3 rows that are actually displayed route to a real, registered page except Legal Information (reported). FAQ, Documents, Beneficiaries, Security, Change Bank, Recurring, One-Time, Allocation and Sell & Withdraw all resolve to routes in app.routes.ts.
- Every navigation target used in code matches a registered route (checked by listing all navigate/navigateByUrl/navigateBack/navigateForward calls against app.routes.ts and tabs.routes.ts).

## tests-ci-observability

Tests, CI and observability audit (read-only; working tree treated as current state).  Tests: the backend has 112 test files with about 829 @Test methods against 249 main classes. Coverage is strong on the scheduler, ACH relationship, analytics outbox, TLH, Plaid webhooks, user deletion and broker environment guard. The frontend has 121 spec files (about 1,800 it() blocks). "npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json" passes clean on the working tree (ran it, about 2s, no errors). The gaps are concentrated in the places that hurt most: sell, liquidate and withdraw have zero tests; JWT filter, login, passkey ceremony, recovery and PIN have no behaviour tests; subscription/tier code has no tests.  CI: one workflow (verify.yml) gates pull requests into develop only. It runs the full backend Gradle suite against MySQL, boots the backend under the ci profile, runs a read-only curl contract suite, then frontend tsc + lint + two doc/lint checks and a small scoped slice of karma (jwt-token util, analytics specs, dev-tools-key util). The other roughly 100 frontend specs are not gated, there is no production build gate, no iOS build, nothing on push, nothing for main. Local develop is 82 commits ahead of origin/develop plus 86 uncommitted paths (76 modified files, +4049/-1246, 10 untracked), so none of the last 2.5 weeks of work has been through CI or is backed up off this laptop.  Observability: essentially none beyond log lines. No Sentry or any crash/error reporter in frontend, iOS or backend; no Spring Actuator (health check is a static /hello string); no logging config (no logback, no JSON logs, no log level properties); no global exception handler on either side; every scheduler catches exceptions and only logs them, so a failed nightly investment run is silent. No alerts exist (roadmap confirms). No admin role, no admin/support endpoints, no runbooks. Money audit trail is partial: scheduled and lump-sum buys have InvestmentExecution/InvestmentTrade rows, but withdrawals and manual sells write nothing locally. The analytics outbox itself is well built and has a reconciliation view, but checking it is a manual weekly SQL query.


### [blocker] Scheduler failures are swallowed and only logged, with no alerting

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:45-63 (nightly investment job: catch Exception -> logger.error only; lock-not-acquired path at 48-51 only logs warn and returns), 70-80, 87-97, 105-115, 121-131; scheduler/AnalyticsPublisherScheduler.java:27-44; FREDdocs/analytics-roadmap.md 3(d)1: 'Nothing currently tells anyone that activation fell, that rejections spiked, or that the publisher has stopped.'
- Detail: There are about 25 @Scheduled jobs (investments 23:55 ET, funding checks, trade checks, DRIP, TLH, account status, Plaid lifecycle, market breakdown). Every one catches Exception and writes a log line. If the nightly investment run throws, skips because the job lock was not acquired, or simply does not run because the service was down at 23:55, users' paycheck investments silently do not happen and nothing notifies Andy. There is also no 'did the job run today' heartbeat.
- Suggested fix: Add a minimal alert path before beta: on any scheduler exception or skipped lock, send an alert (Sentry event, or email/Slack webhook). Add a daily heartbeat check for the money jobs (for example a job_runs table row per run with status and counts, and an alert if the 23:55 run has no success row by morning). Also alert on executions stuck in a non-terminal status for more than N hours.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed in backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:46-63: lock-not-acquired logs warn and returns (48-51), catch Exception logs error only (58-59). Same catch-and-log shape at 70-80, 87-97, 105-115, 121-131. No alert, webhook, Slack or heartbeat code exists in backend/src/main/java (grep hits for 'alert|webhook' are all Plaid webhook code and unrelated). No job_runs table or run record. Two small corrections: the count is 24 @Scheduled annotations across 12 files, not 25, and I did not open every one to prove each catches Exception. Also relevant: InvestmentExecutionService.java:421-432 catches per-schedule failures and only logs a tally, so a night where every user FAILED also looks like a successful run. I did not read getSchedulesReadyForInvestment, so whether a missed night is picked up the next day is unconfirmed.

### [blocker] 82 commits on develop are not pushed, plus 86 uncommitted paths; none of it has run through CI or exists off this laptop

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: git status -sb: '## develop...origin/develop [ahead 82]'; git log origin/develop..develop | wc -l = 82; origin/develop last commit 2026-11-02, local develop 2026-11-18; git status --short | wc -l = 86 (10 untracked); git diff --shortstat HEAD: 76 files changed, 4049 insertions, 1246 deletions; untracked includes frontend/src/app/components/fred-date-sheet/, components/skeleton/, utils/business-days.ts, utils/local-date.ts, utils/quick-picks.ts
- Detail: The whole of Goals v3, plus the amount tray, FRED sheets, skeleton loading and date sheet work, lives only in this checkout. verify.yml only runs on pull requests into develop, so the backend suite and frontend gates have not run on any of these 82 commits in CI. A disk failure loses weeks of work. The uncommitted set also touches money-adjacent screens (investment-schedule, kyc-verification, application.properties).
- Suggested fix: Andy's call on when (standing rule: sessions never push without asking), but before beta: commit the working tree in reviewed slices, run the backend suite and karma locally, then push develop (ideally through a PR so verify.yml runs). Treat 'develop pushed and green in CI' as a release gate.
- Verifier (confirmed): Reproduced exactly: git status -sb shows 'develop...origin/develop [ahead 82]', git status --short has 86 lines, origin/develop last commit 2026-11-02, local develop 2026-11-18. verify.yml triggers only on pull_request into develop, so none of this has run in CI. This is a process and release-gate item more than an app defect, and the timing is Andy's decision (standing rule: never push without asking), so type is closer to 'decision'. Blocker stands because nothing can be deployed to beta users until it is committed, pushed and green, and the only copy is one laptop.

### [high] No crash or error reporting anywhere (frontend, iOS, backend)

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: grep for sentry|crashlytics|bugsnag|datadog|rollbar across backend/src/main, backend/build.gradle, frontend/src/app, frontend/src/main.ts, frontend/src/environments, frontend/package.json, frontend/ios/App/Podfile returned zero real hits; grep for ErrorHandler|onunhandledrejection|window.onerror in frontend/src returned nothing; FREDdocs/analytics-roadmap.md section 3(d) item 2: 'Sentry. Not integrated.'
- Detail: If a beta user's app crashes, a request 500s, or a money job throws, nobody is told. There is no Angular ErrorHandler, no native crash reporter and no backend error sink. The roadmap schedules Sentry for 'month 1 of beta', which is after real money is already moving. With real users and real money, finding out about failures from a user's text message is not acceptable.
- Suggested fix: Move Sentry (or equivalent) ahead of the first beta user: backend SDK + Angular ErrorHandler + Capacitor native crash capture, DSN per environment via env vars, keyed to public_id only, PII scrubbing on. Write its row into FREDdocs/what-we-send.md first (the roadmap's own rule) and add it to the privacy manifest answers.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts are true. No sentry/crashlytics/bugsnag in frontend/package.json, frontend/ios/App/Podfile or backend/build.gradle (the only grep hits are SCSS 'scrollbar' matches). No ErrorHandler, window.onerror or onunhandledrejection in non-spec frontend TS. No @ControllerAdvice on the backend either (only two local @ExceptionHandler methods, GoalController:87 and TradingController:382). FREDdocs/analytics-roadmap.md:120 says 'Sentry. Not integrated.' Severity lowered to high for the client crash-reporting part: for a small invited beta, a missing crash reporter does not by itself stop money from moving safely. The part that is a true blocker (nobody is told when a backend money job fails) is the same gap as the scheduler alerting finding, so count the blocker once there. One Sentry backend integration would close both.

### [high] Sell, liquidate and withdraw have zero automated tests

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/TradingController.java:108 (POST /sell/percentage), :164 (POST /liquidate), :206-259 (POST /withdraw); grep -rlE 'sell/percentage|sellPercentage|/withdraw|withdrawFunds|liquidate' backend/src/test returned no files; test/api/trading.sh covers only GET /positions and GET /account/balance (file header says 'read-only'); frontend has sell-withdraw.page.spec.ts but it is not in the CI gate
- Detail: These are the three endpoints that take money out of a user's brokerage account. No unit test, no controller test, no contract test touches them. TradingController tests that exist cover only account close and schedule cancellation. The withdraw handler validates only amount > 0 (line 211) before calling Alpaca; I did not see a balance check, limit or idempotency key in the lines read, but I did not read AlpacaService.initiateWithdrawal in full, so treat that part as unconfirmed.
- Suggested fix: Add controller/service tests for sell percentage, liquidate and withdraw: auth required, amount validation, no ACH relationship, Alpaca error mapping, double-submit behaviour. Have the money-flow auditor confirm whether an idempotency key and settled-cash check exist.
- Verifier (adjusted): The test gap is true: the only backend tests touching TradingController are TradingControllerScheduleCancelledTest, CloseAccountRollbackTest and TradingControllerCloseAccountTest; nothing references sell/percentage, liquidate, withdraw or initiateWithdrawal. Endpoints are at TradingController.java:108, :164, :206. I read the part the auditor left unconfirmed: AlpacaService.initiateWithdrawal (AlpacaService.java:1401-1435) sends transfer_type, relationship_id, amount and direction only. There is no idempotency key, no balance or settled-cash check and no limit on FRED's side; it relies on Alpaca to reject. Also, the withdraw handler returns HTTP 200 with success=false when Alpaca fails (TradingController.java:239-252, AlpacaService.java:1427-1434). The frontend calls sell/percentage and withdraw (frontend/src/app/services/trading.service.ts:125, :138). frontend/src/app/sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.spec.ts has 36 tests but is not in the CI include list. Severity lowered to high for 'no tests' as such: missing tests alone do not block a small beta. The missing idempotency and double-submit protection is the real money risk and should be judged as its own finding by the money-flow audit, possibly at blocker.

### [high] Withdrawals and manual sells leave no local record (no audit trail)

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/TradingController.java:206-259 (withdraw: calls alpacaService.initiateWithdrawal and returns the response; no repository save); grep of save(|Repository. in TradingController shows only userRepository.findByEmail and investmentScheduleRepository.existsByUser:415; FREDdocs/analytics-roadmap.md 3(d)3: transfer table, contribution_attempt table and withdrawal record 'do not exist'; model directory has InvestmentExecution, InvestmentTrade, DripExecution, TlhHarvestEvent, ChatActionAudit, EmailLog but no transfer/withdrawal/audit entity
- Detail: Buys are recorded (InvestmentExecution, InvestmentTrade), TLH and DRIP have their own tables, but a user-initiated withdrawal or sell exists only at Alpaca and in a log line. For support ('where is my money'), disputes and any regulatory question, FRED cannot answer from its own database who requested what, when, and what the outcome was. There is also no record of failed or returned deposits.
- Suggested fix: Add a transfer/withdrawal table with status history (the roadmap already names it) and write a row for every withdraw, sell and liquidate request before calling the broker, updated with the result. Consider a general money_action_audit row (user, action, amount, broker id, request id, outcome).
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. TradingController.java:206-259 (withdraw) and :164-201 (liquidate) call Alpaca and return the response with no repository write. The only repository calls in the controller are userRepository.findByEmail (lines 76, 128, 172, 220, 271, 318) and investmentScheduleRepository.existsByUser (415). FREDdocs/analytics-roadmap.md:123-126 states the transfer table, contribution_attempt table and withdrawal record do not exist. The only trace is one log line in AlpacaService.java:1414 with the Alpaca account id and amount; Alpaca itself keeps the transfer record. High is right for beta with real money.

### [high] No health endpoint, metrics or logging configuration on the backend

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/HealthCheckController.java:11-14 (static 'Hello' string, no DB or dependency check); backend/build.gradle:27-55 has no spring-boot-starter-actuator, micrometer or log encoder; no logback*.xml under backend/src/main/resources; grep for 'logging\.' and 'management\.' in all application*.properties returned nothing; .github/workflows/verify.yml comment confirms 'The backend has no spring-boot-actuator dependency'
- Detail: The only liveness signal is /hello, which returns 200 even if MySQL, Alpaca or Plaid are unreachable. Logs are default Spring console text with no levels configured per profile, no request or user correlation id, and no JSON structure, so searching Railway logs for one user's failed investment is manual. There are no metrics at all.
- Suggested fix: Add Actuator with a health endpoint that checks the datasource (expose only health, keep it locked down), point the Railway health check and an external uptime monitor at it, add a logback config for prod (JSON, a request id and public_id in MDC), and set explicit log levels per profile.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. HealthCheckController.java:11-14 returns a static string with no dependency check. backend/build.gradle has no actuator, micrometer, logstash or sentry dependency. No logback*.xml in backend/src/main/resources (only the application*.properties files, db and templates). No 'logging.' or 'management.' keys in any application*.properties, including application-prod.properties. verify.yml's boot step comment confirms no actuator. Note there is technically a liveness endpoint (/hello), so the title slightly overstates; the accurate claim is 'no real health check'.

### [high] Auth core has no behaviour tests (JWT filter, login, passkey ceremony, recovery, PIN)

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: No test references AuthTokenFilter (105 lines), AuthEntryPointJwt, RecoveryController (100 lines), PinController (135 lines) or YubicoCredentialRepository (per-class grep of backend/src/test); JwtUtils/SecurityConfig appear only in DevToolsGatingTest, PublicIdClaimTest, SimulatedAttestationGatingTest, AuthControllerRefreshPublicIdTest (2 tests) and WebAuthnServiceAnalyticsTest (12 tests, analytics-focused); test/api/auth.sh is 15 lines; backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:52-56 prints the recovery OTP to stdout as a 'MOCK EMAIL'
- Detail: Nothing tests that an expired or tampered JWT is rejected, that protected routes require auth, that recovery OTPs expire or rate-limit, or that PIN verify locks out. The existing auth tests check the analytics side effects and the public_id claim. Separately, account recovery still prints the OTP to the server log instead of sending an email, which means recovery does not work for a real user and the code sits in logs (the auth auditor should own the fix; flagged here because it is a logging problem too).
- Suggested fix: Add a SecurityConfig slice test (every non-public route returns 401 without a token; dev routes are absent under prod), JwtUtils tests (expiry, bad signature), RecoveryController tests (OTP expiry, attempts, passkey clearing) and PinController tests. Replace the System.out mock email with the real EmailSender port.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. Zero test files reference AuthTokenFilter, AuthEntryPointJwt, RecoveryController, PinController, YubicoCredentialRepository or SecurityConfig. JwtUtils appears only in the five tests the auditor listed. test/api/auth.sh is 15 lines and calls two endpoints with a valid token. Small correction: a few per-controller tests do assert 401 without auth (GoalControllerTest, TlhControllerTest, AnalyticsConsentEndpointTest), but there is no app-wide SecurityConfig route test. The recovery OTP part is true and is worse than stated: RecoveryController.java:52-56 prints the OTP with System.out and has no profile gate, so it would run in prod; no email is ever sent, so account recovery cannot work for a real user. The verify handler (lines 61-99) has no attempt counter or lockout on a 6-digit code, and a correct code deletes all passkeys and issues a JWT. That recovery defect deserves its own blocker-level finding in the auth domain; the 'no tests' part is high.

### [high] Subscription and tier code has no tests, and the confirm endpoint trusts the client

- Type: decision, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/SubscriptionService.java:21-35 (sets selectedTier and billingPeriod from arguments, no verification); controller/UserController.java:618-632 (POST /user/subscription/confirm takes tier from request body, comment says 'post Apple IAP / RevenueCat'); no test file references SubscriptionService; no backend test asserts tier gating except TLH/market-breakdown/goal tests that mention tier
- Detail: Whatever billing model counsel approves, today any logged-in user can POST a tier string and be recorded as on that tier, and nothing tests either the endpoint or that a lower tier is refused premium features. This is expected given billing is not built, but it must not ship as is. BillingAnalyticsEmitter exists and is tested, so the analytics hook for billing is ready.
- Suggested fix: Once the billing model is decided: make tier a server-set value only (written by the billing integration, not the client), add a single entitlement check used by every gated feature, and add tests per tier per feature. Until then, treat tier as unenforced.
- Verifier (adjusted): Core claim is true: SubscriptionService.java:21-35 writes tier and billingPeriod straight from arguments, UserController.java:618-632 takes tier from the request body with no validation of allowed values, and no test references SubscriptionService or subscription/confirm. Three corrections. (1) There is a second client-trusted write path the auditor missed: the profile update handler at UserController.java:528-539 also sets selectedTier from the request body. (2) The frontend does not call /subscription/confirm at all (no match in frontend/src/app), so that endpoint is currently dead code and the profile update path is the live one to check. (3) 'Nothing tests that a lower tier is refused' is too strong: TlhControllerTest.java:79 (nonProTiersGet403OnWriteEndpoints) and :92 (exact, case-sensitive tier match) do test gating for TLH, and tier appears in GoalServiceTest and MarketBreakdownSchedulerTest. There is no single entitlement check; gating is per feature. Type 'decision' is right since the fix depends on the billing model.

### [high] No production build gate, no iOS build, and nothing runs on push or on main

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: .github/workflows/verify.yml: 'on: pull_request: branches: [develop]' only; 'ng build --configuration=ci' runs only inside the design-fidelity step, which is conditional on verify/* branches with a design-ref; no workflow references xcodebuild, cap sync, fastlane or TestFlight; .github/workflows contains only auto-pr.yml and verify.yml; origin/main last commit 2025-09-22 and origin/develop is 871 commits ahead of it
- Detail: CI type-checks with 'npx tsc --noEmit' against the root tsconfig, which does not compile Angular templates, so a template error that breaks 'ng build --configuration=production' (AOT, strictTemplates) passes CI. There is no automated iOS build or archive, no release workflow and no tagging. main is over a year stale, so there is no defined release branch. Direct pushes to develop are not checked by any workflow (branch protection is referenced in a comment but cannot be confirmed from the repo).
- Suggested fix: Add 'ng build --configuration=production' to verify.yml as a required step. Decide the release branch model (main as the release branch, fast-forwarded from develop) and run verify on it. Add a release checklist or workflow for the iOS archive and TestFlight upload, even if manual at first. Confirm branch protection on develop in GitHub settings.
- Verifier (confirmed): Confirmed. verify.yml trigger is pull_request into develop only. 'ng build --configuration=ci' appears only inside the design-fidelity step, which runs only for verify/* branches and exits early without a design-ref, route file or API key. The frontend gate is 'npx tsc --noEmit' against frontend/tsconfig.json, which does not compile Angular templates (strictTemplates is an Angular compiler option, not a tsc one), so template errors pass CI; ng lint does not replace an AOT build. .github/workflows holds only auto-pr.yml and verify.yml, with no xcodebuild, cap sync or fastlane. origin/main last commit is 2025-09-22 and origin/develop is 871 commits ahead. Branch protection is mentioned in a workflow comment but cannot be checked from the repo. A 'production' build configuration does exist in frontend/angular.json, so adding the gate is a one-line change.

### [medium] No admin or support tooling, and no admin role

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/security/services/UserDetailsImpl.java:57-59 ('every user gets ROLE_USER'); grep for hasRole|hasAuthority|PreAuthorize in backend/src/main shows no admin authorization; controller directory listing has no Admin/Support controller (only DevAuthController, DevPlaidController, DevUserController, which are dev-tools gated per DevToolsGatingTest); FREDdocs/metrics.md:371 documents a read-only SQL role only
- Detail: There is no way to look up a user, see their schedule/hold state, view their executions, retry a stuck execution, clear a hold or resend an email without raw SQL against the database. The only tooling is the dev controllers, which are (correctly) gated off outside dev. For a private beta of a handful of users SQL is survivable, but there is no written procedure for it either.
- Suggested fix: For beta: write a support runbook with the exact read-only SQL for 'look up user', 'why did the investment not run', 'stuck execution', 'KYC stuck', and safe fix procedures. Before public launch: a small authenticated admin surface (separate role, audit-logged) for lookup and the two or three most common fixes.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts are true: UserDetailsImpl.java:57-59 gives every user ROLE_USER only; no hasRole/hasAuthority/@PreAuthorize use in backend/src/main (the single import in AuthController.java:24 is not an admin check); the controller directory has no admin or support controller, only the three Dev* controllers. Severity lowered: an admin surface is not required to let beta users in or to pass App Store review. With a handful of beta users, read-only SQL plus a written runbook is workable. The beta-sized piece (write the support runbook) is small; the admin UI is a later, larger item. Split it that way in the checklist.

### [medium] No global exception handling on backend or frontend

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: grep for ControllerAdvice|@ExceptionHandler in backend/src/main found only TradingController.java:382 and GoalController.java:87 (controller-local); no @ControllerAdvice class; no ErrorHandler provider in frontend/src
- Detail: Each controller hand-rolls try/catch and frequently logs only e.getMessage() without the stack trace (for example TradingController.java:255 and :300). Unhandled exceptions fall through to Spring's default error response. On the frontend, uncaught errors and rejected promises go to the console only. This makes failures both inconsistent for the user and hard to diagnose.
- Suggested fix: Add one @RestControllerAdvice that logs with stack trace and request id and returns a consistent error body; add an Angular ErrorHandler that reports to the crash reporter. Log the exception object, not just the message, in money paths.

### [medium] API contract suite is read-only and thin; no end-to-end test of the money path

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: test/api/*.sh total 214 lines; test/api/trading.sh (2 GETs), test/api/investments.sh (4 GETs, header says 'read-only'); test/api/run-all.sh runs 7 suites (chat.sh exists but is not in run-all); backend/src/test/java/com/investingapp/backend/support/RequiresCiDatabase.java:17-31 (DB-backed tests skip silently when MySQL is absent; 18 test files use it)
- Detail: The 'Phase 2 API contract' gate proves the app boots and GET endpoints respond for a seeded user. It does not create a schedule, run an investment, or exercise KYC, Plaid link or withdraw, even against stubs. Also, 18 DB-backed backend test classes skip rather than fail on a machine without the ci MySQL, so a local 'green' run is weaker than a CI run; the 82 unpushed commits have only had the weaker version.
- Suggested fix: Add a stubbed end-to-end test under the ci profile (CiStubConfig already exists): create schedule, trigger processScheduledInvestments, assert execution rows and status transitions. Run the backend suite locally against a MySQL container before pushing develop so the RequiresCiDatabase tests actually execute.

### [medium] Analytics outbox health is a manual weekly SQL check; publisher is off by default and prod frontend key is empty

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:33 (app.analytics.publisher.enabled defaults false); frontend/src/environments/environment.prod.ts:11 (posthogKey: ''); FREDdocs/metrics.md:308-366 (v_outbox_reconciliation and 'The weekly reconciliation' query); FREDdocs/analytics-roadmap.md 3(b) steps 1-4 and 3(d)1; infra/posthog/sync-dashboards.mjs contains no alert definitions (grep 'alert' returned nothing); FREDdocs/beta-qa-checklist.md:16-27 (four server events never observed on a real account)
- Detail: The outbox design is solid (transactional, rollback-tested, reconciliation view). What is missing is operational: no production PostHog project or keys, the publisher silently fills the outbox if the env var is not set, nothing alerts when the publisher stops or rows fail, and four money events have never been seen end to end. All of this is documented and planned, not forgotten.
- Suggested fix: Follow roadmap 3(b) at beta start (prod PostHog project, Railway vars, dashboard sync, tick the four events in beta-qa-checklist.md). Pull the 'publisher stopped' alert forward from month 1 to beta week, since it is the alert that tells you the others are blind.

### [medium] No runbooks for incidents, deploy, rollback or stuck accounts

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: grep -rliE 'runbook|incident|rollback plan|on-call' FREDdocs matched only plaid-sandbox-secret-rotation.md, analytics-implementation-review.md, a Paycheck Autopilot plan/spec and .stories/FRED-113.md; FREDdocs listing has no deploy, incident or support document; FREDdocs/flyway-baseline.md covers the migration repair only
- Detail: There is good documentation for narrow procedures (Plaid secret rotation, Flyway renumbering repair, PostHog proxy deploy) but nothing that says what to do when the nightly investment job fails, when an ACH is returned, when a user is stuck in KYC, how to deploy and roll back the backend, or how to pause all money movement. INVESTMENT_SCHEDULER_ENABLED (application.properties:55) is effectively the kill switch but is not documented as one.
- Suggested fix: Write a short ops runbook before beta: kill switch (INVESTMENT_SCHEDULER_ENABLED=false), deploy and rollback steps for Railway, the top five 'user is stuck' cases with SQL, who to contact at Alpaca and Plaid, and how to communicate with affected users.

### [medium] Logs contain user emails and stray System.out prints; no PII policy for logs

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: grep -rnE 'logger\.(info|warn|error)\(.*(email|Email)' backend/src/main/java counted 149 lines (for example service/SubscriptionService.java:34 and :46, controller/UserController.java:600); 7 System.out.println/printStackTrace in main code: controller/AuthController.java:79, controller/RecoveryController.java:52-56, model/InvestmentSchedule.java:195; about 301 console.log/warn/error calls in non-spec frontend TypeScript
- Detail: The analytics pipeline has a strict PII guard, but application logs do not: emails are logged routinely, and the recovery OTP is printed. Once logs are shipped to a third party (Railway today, Sentry later) this becomes a disclosure question. I counted lines by pattern and did not review each one, so some may log a variable named email that is already masked.
- Suggested fix: Log public_id or user id instead of email, remove the System.out calls, and strip or gate frontend console logging in the production build. Decide the log PII rule once and note it next to the analytics rules.

**Verified solid:**

- Frontend type-check passes on the current working tree: 'npx tsc --noEmit -p tsconfig.app.json' exited clean in about 2 seconds with no errors.
- verify.yml runs the full backend Gradle suite against a real MySQL 8 service container on every PR into develop, so the 18 RequiresCiDatabase context/migration/view tests do execute in CI.
