- Auto-merge in verify.yml is limited to verify/* routine branches; PRs Andy opens himself are not auto-merged. auto-pr.yml triggers only on verify/** pushes.
- Scheduler has meaningful unit coverage: InvestmentSchedulerTest (7), ScheduledInvestmentProcessorTest (12), InvestmentExecutionService EOD batch/gate/coordinator/TodayEt tests, InvestmentScheduleEligibilityTest, JobLockServiceTest (7), SchedulerGateTest, AccountStatusSchedulerTest, TlhSchedulerTest, MarketBreakdownSchedulerTest.
- Nightly investment job takes a DB job lock (JobLocks.PROCESS_SCHEDULED, 10 minute lease) and is gated by SchedulerGate (investment.scheduler.enabled, env INVESTMENT_SCHEDULER_ENABLED), which doubles as a kill switch (InvestmentScheduler.java:45-63, SchedulerGate.java:19-20, application.properties:55).
- ACH relationship handling is well tested (AchRelationshipServiceTest 20 tests, AlpacaApiServiceAchRelationshipTest), as are lump-sum execution (InvestmentControllerLumpSumTest 5), account close and rollback (TradingControllerCloseAccountTest, CloseAccountRollbackTest), user deletion (UserDeletionServiceTest 15) and KYC submission (KycSubmissionServiceTest 6 plus decided/rollback tests).
- Broker environment and dev-tool safety have dedicated tests: BrokerEnvironmentGuardTest (28), DevToolsGatingTest (9), DevToolsKeyFilterTest, ProdPlaidPropertiesTest, SimulatedAttestationGatingTest.
- Plaid webhook verification and inbox are tested (JwtPlaidWebhookVerifierTest, PlaidWebhookKeyCacheTest, PlaidWebhookVerificationFilterTest, PlaidWebhookInboxServiceTest, PlaidWebhookControllerTest, PlaidItemLifecycleServiceTest).
- Analytics outbox is transactional with rollback tests per event, a client/server parity-tested privacy guard, a publisher scheduler every 2 minutes that never deletes rows, and a v_outbox_reconciliation view with a documented weekly query (FREDdocs/metrics.md:308-366). Seven metrics views each have a test.
- TLH has broad coverage (catalog, eligibility, execution, harvest cycle, summary, wash-sale guard, tax lot ledger, replacement selection, controller, scheduler).
- No focused or skipped tests found: grep for xit/xdescribe/fit/fdescribe and @Disabled across frontend/src and backend/src/test returned nothing.
- Money-moving buys do have a local audit trail: InvestmentExecution, InvestmentTrade, DripExecution, TlhHarvestEvent, TlhCycleRun, LotDisposal and ChatActionAudit entities exist; EmailLog records email sends.
- Flyway migrations V1 to V9 plus seven repeatable view migrations are present; migration procedure and the renumbering repair are documented in FREDdocs/flyway-baseline.md.
- Fully merged local branches that can be deleted safely: feature/goals-v3-polish, feature/goals-v3-wt, feature/instagram-floating-tab-bar (each 0 commits ahead of develop).

## docs-backlog-mining

Document sweep of FRED's own planning docs (backlog.md, stories_in_progress.md, beta-qa-checklist.md, analytics-roadmap.md, analytics-implementation-review.md, handoff-paycheck-autopilot.md, privacy-manifest-and-label.md, plaid-sandbox-secret-rotation.md, flyway-baseline.md, posthog-proxy-deploy.md, the Paycheck Autopilot spec, docs/superpowers plans/specs from November, ITPM memory). I did not re-audit code, but I ran a handful of read-only greps to check whether backlog items were stale.  Headline: the docs already name most of the production gates. They fall into five groups. 1. Security and money-safety stories filed on 2026-09-16 that say "must fix before any real user": FRED-219 (recovery OTP printed to stdout, no rate limit) is still open in the code; FRED-221 (SSN in a localStorage draft), FRED-222 (stuck orders), FRED-223 (no idempotency on money paths) and FRED-228 are still listed as READY. FRED-218 and most of FRED-220 look already fixed in the tree but are still listed as open, so the backlog is stale there. 2. Billing does not exist. FRED-174 and FRED-112 are check-marked done, but the analytics review records that the subscription webhook was deleted and all StoreKit work was withdrawn ("the advisory fee is collected outside Apple and a separate session owns billing"). FRED-169, FRED-173, FRED-143 depend on it. This is a decision for counsel plus later app work. 3. Email does not send. FRED-101 (transactional emailer) is sleeping; EmailService is MOCKED; recovery OTP, ACH authorization email, payday emails and the market breakdown all depend on a real provider. 4. App Store and legal gates blocked on counsel or Apple: privacy manifest covers analytics only (KYC, auth, banking, payroll data undeclared), SSN retention for rejected applicants, legal/TOS/privacy policy in app (FRED-138), securities attorney and Alpaca review (FRED-139), Release entitlement and the Capacitor dev-host default (review item X7). 5. Environment and release hygiene: no production backend, no production PostHog project (environment.prod.ts key empty), api-dev may need a Flyway history repair, Plaid sandbox secret rotation is a manual step still owed, Sentry and alerts are not built, local develop is 82 commits ahead of origin with 86 dirty files, Paycheck Autopilot Phase 1 (29 commits) and several UI workstreams are unmerged or uncommitted, and a long list of features are waiting on a device pass.  Items explicitly blocked on outside parties: counsel (SSN retention; privacy manifest purposes; privacy policy text; billing model; FRED-139 securities review; FRED-122 compliance copy; Autopilot payroll data disclosure and Phase 2 privacy policy), Alpaca (FRED-139 review; FRED-175 ACATS in onboarding is in the BLOCKED section; FRED-232 custodial accounts unverified), Apple (App Store Connect label; FRED-144 Verify with Wallet; FRED-169 trial length if Apple IAP is used), Plaid (production plan switch at Autopilot Phase 2; dashboard secret rotation), designer (celebratory MFU mascot, per memory).  ITPM/: there is no current priorities list. ITPM/pending is empty, the newest archived dashboard is 2026-07-06, and routine_memory.md only has summaries from May to July, so ITPM is not a live source of launch priorities.


### [blocker] FRED-219: account recovery code is printed to stdout and never delivered; verify endpoint has no attempt limit

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: FREDdocs/backlog.md:390-392 (FRED-219, 'must fix before production or before any real user'); spot check backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:52-56 still has System.out.println of email and OTP; :61-79 verify has no attempt/lockout code
- Detail: The backlog says the recovery OTP and the user's email are printed with System.out.println in every profile, so no real user can complete account recovery and any log drain captures live OTPs. POST /api/auth/recovery/verify is permitAll and has no throttle on a 6-digit code. My grep confirms the println block is still there and found no attempt or lockout logic in the controller.
- Suggested fix: Route the OTP through EmailService (needs the real provider, see FRED-101 finding), remove the println, add attempt counting and lockout on verify.
- Verifier (confirmed): True as stated. backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/RecoveryController.java:51-56 prints the email and the OTP with System.out.println in every profile (no profile gate, no EmailService call). verify (:61-99) has no attempt counter or lockout; a wrong guess just returns 401 and the OTP stays valid for 15 minutes (:48). Success deletes all passkeys and issues a JWT (:91-96), so guessing a 6-digit code is a full account takeover on a brokerage account. Two extras the auditor missed: (1) :46 uses nextInt(999999), so code 999999 is never generated (minor); (2) the class has @CrossOrigin(origins="*") at :20. I did not find any global rate-limit filter (grep for ratelimit/bucket4j/throttle found nothing relevant to recovery).

### [blocker] FRED-101: no real email provider. Every transactional email is mocked

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: FREDdocs/backlog.md:181-193 (FRED-101, sleeping; AC1 'Founder unblocks: choose email provider'); FREDdocs/analytics-implementation-review.md:374 and :554 (Resend pipeline deferred, 'gated on a go-ahead that never came'); FREDdocs/specs/2026-10-19-paycheck-autopilot-design.md:371 and :682 ('Email is MOCKED until a provider ships'; real EmailSender is a Phase 2 gate); spot check backend/.../service/EmailService.java:16 ('with app.email.enabled=false (current state) sends are MOCKED') and LoggingEmailSender.java exists as the only sender
- Detail: FRED-101 covers ACH deposit/withdraw confirmations, trade confirmations, account verification, recovery OTP, ACATS emails and an 'email not verified' warning in My Profile. None can send today. FRED-171 (payday lifecycle emails) and FRED-176 (marketing opt-in, which the backlog says is a TODO stub with 'provider TBD', backlog.md:799-807) and the Monthly Market Breakdown all sit on the same missing provider. Picking the provider and adding credentials is Andy's step; the wiring is app work.
- Suggested fix: Andy picks the provider (Resend is named in the analytics spec), then implement a real EmailSender behind the existing port, wire recovery OTP first, then the money notifications. Decide which emails are required for beta versus later.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. The only EmailSender implementation is backend/.../service/LoggingEmailSender.java:15. application.properties:47-49 defaults EMAIL_ENABLED=false and EMAIL_PROVIDER=logging, and the from address is still the placeholder noreply@investingapp.com (:48). EmailService.java:14-18 states sends are mocked. Blocker status holds mainly because recovery OTP depends on it (and RecoveryController does not even route through EmailService). Deposit/trade confirmation emails are high rather than blocker on their own for a private beta, since push notifications exist, but as one item the blocker rating is fair. Provider choice is an owner decision; the wiring is app work.

### [blocker] Subscription billing does not exist, although FRED-174 and FRED-112 are check-marked done

- Type: decision, effort XL, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: FREDdocs/backlog.md:789-797 (FRED-174 marked done: App Store Connect products, /subscription/confirm, expiry webhook); backlog.md:520-530 (FRED-112: IAP steps are 'TODO pseudocode until FRED-174'); backlog.md:514 (FRED-111 Upgrade button 'Leave as // TODO: trigger Plus subscription IAP'); FREDdocs/analytics-implementation-review.md:251 (Phase 6 withdrawn: 'the advisory fee is collected outside Apple and a separate session owns billing'), :381 (no Swift plugin, no paywall, no App Store webhook, no entitlement or ledger tables), :388 (the unauthenticated RevenueCat-shaped webhook was deleted), :382 (trial_started, subscription_billed, subscription_cancelled are defined and never emitted); FREDdocs/privacy-manifest-and-label.md:45-48
- Detail: The backlog reads as if Apple subscriptions are wired. The later analytics review is the accurate record: all StoreKit/RevenueCat work was withdrawn and the webhook removed. What exists per the docs is only selected_tier and billing_period on the user and tier gates that read them (FRED-99, backlog.md:462-472). Nothing charges anyone, nothing sets or clears a tier on payment or lapse, and the FRED-112 'expired' gate has nothing that triggers it. The billing model itself is waiting on counsel (per-paycheck payment). Not designing it here.
- Suggested fix: After counsel answers on the payment model, open a billing story. Until then, correct the backlog so FRED-174/FRED-112 no longer read as done, and decide what tier a private beta user gets with no billing (FRED-113 privateBeta bypass exists per backlog.md:532-543).
- Verifier (adjusted): Core claim is true: FREDdocs/backlog.md:520 and :789 mark FRED-112 and FRED-174 done, and backend/.../service/BillingAnalyticsEmitter.java:14-28 says 'Billing does not exist in this repository'. Correction: the auditor says nothing sets or clears a tier. There is a live endpoint, POST /user/subscription/confirm (UserController.java:618-633), that calls SubscriptionService.confirmSubscription (SubscriptionService.java:21-34) and sets selectedTier and billingPeriod from the request body with no receipt, payment or tier-name validation. Any signed-in user can grant themselves any tier for free. That is an app-work security gap that should be listed on its own (close or guard the endpoint before beta), separate from the counsel decision. expireSubscription (SubscriptionService.java:37) has no caller anywhere in backend or frontend, so the expired gate is indeed never triggered. Type should be split: decision for the billing model, app-work for the open confirm endpoint and the backlog correction.

### [blocker] Release entitlement and Capacitor dev-host default are known-bad and unfixed

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: FREDdocs/analytics-implementation-review.md:387 (X7: 'The Release entitlement and dev-host issues ... are still unfixed') and :543 ('A separate story for each. Both are still unfixed'); spot check frontend/ios/App/App/Release.entitlements:5-6 (associated-domains is a single <string>, where Debug and Test use an <array>); frontend/capacitor.config.*:7-8 (server.url 'https://local.fredvested.com')
- Detail: The analytics review flags both as real problems reported at Checkpoint A and says no backlog story exists for either. The grep shows the Release entitlements file gives associated-domains as a string, not an array, which would break passkey webcredentials for a Release build, and the Capacitor config points the app at the local dev server. I did not check whether a build configuration overrides server.url, so treat the second as unconfirmed in detail.
- Suggested fix: File two stories. Fix Release.entitlements to an array of webcredentials domains, and make the release build load the bundled app, not local.fredvested.com. Verify with a Release archive on a device.
- Verifier (adjusted): Both halves are true, and I confirmed the part the auditor left unconfirmed. (1) frontend/ios/App/App/Release.entitlements:5-6 gives com.apple.developer.associated-domains as a single <string>; Debug.entitlements uses an <array>. The Xcode project uses this file for the Release config (frontend/ios/App/App.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:413). The domain is api.fredvested.com, which does not match the prod rpId app.fredvested.com (environment.prod.ts:8). (2) frontend/capacitor.config.ts:7-9 hardcodes server.url 'https://local.fredvested.com' with no environment switch, and the generated frontend/ios/App/App/capacitor.config.json carries the same server.url. I found no script in frontend/package.json or frontend/scripts that swaps it for release. So a Release/TestFlight build as configured would load the web app from Andy's local dev tunnel, not the bundled app, and passkey sign-in would not work. Since passkeys are the only sign-in and no beta tester can use a build that depends on a laptop tunnel, this is a blocker for the first TestFlight build, not just high. Effort stays small. Confidence high.

### [blocker] FRED-221: full SSN kept in a plaintext localStorage KYC draft

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: FREDdocs/backlog.md:398-400 (FRED-221, 'must fix before production or before any real user'); spot check frontend/src/app/components/kyc-verification/kyc-verification.component.ts:267 still builds the key fred_kyc_step1_<userId>
- Detail: Backlog says the whole step-1 form, including taxId (SSN), DOB, name, address and phone, is written unencrypted and only cleared after a successful create-account. The draft key still exists in the working tree. That file has uncommitted changes, and I did not re-read what is written into the draft now, so the exact current contents are unconfirmed.
- Suggested fix: Exclude taxId and DOB from the draft or drop the draft, and clear it on failure, logout and edit-mode exit.
- Verifier (adjusted): Confirmed in the current working tree, with the part the auditor could not confirm now verified. frontend/src/app/components/kyc-verification/kyc-verification.component.ts:270-272 writes JSON.stringify(this.step1Form.value) to localStorage under fred_kyc_step1_<userId>, and the form includes taxId (:144, SSN pattern), so the full SSN is stored in plain text. It is saved at :453 and only removed at :519 after a successful submit; there is no other caller of clearStep1Draft, so a failed or abandoned KYC leaves the SSN on the device indefinitely, and with userId missing the key is fred_kyc_step1_anon (:266). Also note capacitor.config.ts serves the app from a remote origin today, which widens exposure of localStorage. The backlog itself says 'must fix before any real user'. For real people entering real SSNs this should be a blocker for beta, and the fix is small (drop taxId and DOB from the draft, clear on failure and logout). Confidence high.

Nothing for name, email, phone, address, SSN/sensitive info, financial info or bank details, which the app clearly collects in KYC. ### [high] FRED-222: partially filled and expired orders are never re-polled, so executions can stick forever

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: FREDdocs/backlog.md:402-404 (FRED-222); spot check InvestmentExecutionService.java:1101 sets PARTIALLY_FILLED and grep found no 'expired' handling in that file
- Detail: Backlog: only SUBMITTED trades are re-checked. A PARTIALLY_FILLED trade is never polled again, an expired day order is not handled, and when one placement fails the execution is marked TRADING_FAILED while sibling trades that did submit never get fills recorded. User-visible result is money pulled and an execution that never completes or fails.
- Suggested fix: Keep polling PARTIALLY_FILLED, handle expired/canceled, finish recording siblings, add an alert for executions stuck beyond a time limit.
- Verifier (confirmed): True. backend/.../service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:1080 only checks orders whose trade status is SUBMITTED. Once a trade is set to PARTIALLY_FILLED (:1100-1105) it is never polled again, and because it is not FILLED (:1109-1111) the execution can never reach COMPLETED. Only 'rejected' and 'canceled' are treated as failures (:1092-1093); 'expired' (a real end state for time_in_force 'day' orders, AlpacaService.java:303) falls through every branch, stays SUBMITTED, and is polled forever. Mitigation that lowers likelihood but not impact: orders are notional/fractional market buys on liquid ETFs, which normally fill in full at once. I did not verify the sibling-trades claim beyond seeing TRADING_FAILED set at :1009. High is right for real money; pair it with a stuck-execution alert.

### [high] No crash reporting and no alerts. Nothing tells anyone when money flows or the publisher break

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: FREDdocs/analytics-roadmap.md:112-126 (month 1 of beta: alerts, Sentry, transfer tables); FREDdocs/analytics-implementation-review.md:558 (9.1e 'No alerts'), :560 (9.3 Sentry not integrated), :666-667 ('Returns are invisible'), :550 and :553 (no transfer, contribution_attempt or withdrawal tables); FREDdocs/specs/2026-10-19-paycheck-autopilot-design.md:640 (ops alert channel is a Phase 1 gate)
- Detail: The roadmap schedules these for month 1 of beta, after launch. For real money that ordering is a decision worth revisiting: a returned ACH transfer still counts as funded and is recorded nowhere, a crash during Plaid linking is indistinguishable from a user leaving, and there is no ops alert channel on develop (it is part of the unmerged Autopilot Phase 1). Sentry needs its disclosure row written into what-we-send.md and the privacy label before a key is configured.
- Suggested fix: Decide whether Sentry plus a minimal ops alert (stuck executions, failed transfers, publisher stopped) moves ahead of beta. Then the transfer/return tables.
- Verifier (confirmed): True for develop. No Sentry dependency in frontend/package.json or the backend build file (grep returned nothing), and no ops alert code or app.ops.alert property exists under backend/src/main/java or resources (grep returned nothing). I did not re-verify the 'returned ACH still counts as funded' statement; note that checkFundingStatus does handle REJECTED, CANCELED and RETURNED transfer statuses while funding is pending (InvestmentExecutionService.java:619-628), so that sentence may only apply to returns that arrive after settlement. High is reasonable; for a small private beta a minimal alert on stuck executions and failed transfers is arguably needed before real money, so the lead should treat the 'before or after beta' ordering as a decision.

Pushing is Andy's call; sessions must not push unasked.

### [medium] FRED-223: no idempotency on money paths (orders, ACH transfers, scheduler lock)

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: FREDdocs/backlog.md:406-408 (FRED-223); FREDdocs/specs/2026-10-19-paycheck-autopilot-design.md:275 ('The Alpaca POST has no idempot...' and the claim-before-POST design) and :610; spot check: client_order_id/ShedLock/Idempotency-Key only matched in TlhHarvestEvent.java, TlhExecutionService.java and AlpacaService.java
- Detail: Backlog says buy orders carry no client_order_id outside TLH, the ACH transfer POST has no idempotency key, there are no unique constraints on investment_executions or investment_trades, no scheduler lock, and the 23:59 batch runs all users in one transaction so a late failure can lose transfer id stamps after pulls went out. A retry, double tap, or second backend instance could duplicate a real transfer or order. The backlog rates it MEDIUM; with real money I rate it higher. Paycheck Autopilot's design includes a claim-before-POST stamp, so part of this may be addressed on the unmerged Phase 1 branch; I did not verify that.
- Suggested fix: Deterministic client_order_id, unique constraints, per-user transaction in the batch, and a scheduler lock (or a hard single-instance rule in the runbook). Check what Phase 0/1 already covers before scoping.
- Verifier (adjusted): The backlog text is out of date and the finding overstates what is missing. On develop the Phase 0 idempotency work is already in: investment_executions has UNIQUE source_key and night_key (backend/.../model/InvestmentExecution.java:76 and :80, keys in util/ExecutionKeys.java:7-30); lump-sum taps are deduplicated by clientUuid (controller/InvestmentController.java:271-289, and the frontend sends it from lump-sum-investment.page.ts and investment.service.ts); the nightly batch no longer runs in one transaction, it uses per-step REQUIRES_NEW transactions and a compare-and-set claim (transferAttemptedAt) before the Alpaca POST (service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:188-189, :246-254, :282, :352-353, :387-388, :406-410; repository/InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:139-165). What is still true: regular buy orders carry no client_order_id (service/AlpacaService.java:295-303, only the TLH paths at :567/:600/:633 do), the ACH transfer POST sends no idempotency key (AlpacaService.java:111-121), and there is no ShedLock or scheduler lock (grep found none), so a second backend instance would double-run the schedulers, although the claim step limits the damage on transfers. I did not check unique constraints on investment_trades. Remaining work is smaller: client_order_id on buys plus a single-instance rule or lock. Medium, effort M.

## ai-chat

All paths below are relative to /Users/andrewtimmer/PersonalTypeshit/FRED. "BE/" means backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/.  Ask FRED chat works end to end and the core security design is sound: every chat endpoint binds to the authenticated principal, the model's tools are read-only and bound to that user, and account changes only run on a Confirm tap through an allowlisted, rate-limited, audited endpoint. The Monthly Market Breakdown has a real deterministic compliance gate and a grounding check.  What is not production ready: 1. There is no spend control on chat at all. No per-user rate limit, no daily cap, no message length cap, and each message triggers up to 4 Claude rounds at 16k max tokens with adaptive thinking, plus a classification call, an embedding call and sometimes a title call. 2. The privacy story is wrong on paper and absent in the app. Chat sends free text, age, and exact portfolio dollar figures to Anthropic, and every message verbatim to OpenAI, while FREDdocs/what-we-send.md says PostHog is the only destination and that free text and dollar amounts never leave. There is no in-app disclosure or consent, and no chat disclaimer. 3. Chat data is never deleted: chat_messages has no retention, and account deletion does not purge chat_messages, conversation_insights or chat_action_audit. 4. The regex "safety" gate blocks legitimate core questions ("How much is my portfolio worth?", "What is a recession?", "Is FRED a scam?") and there is no output-side compliance check on chat replies. 5. The knowledge base the model is told to trust above all else contains stale facts (flat $8/month pricing, Persona biometric recovery, emails that are mocked). 6. Session history is loaded by sessionId only, with a shared "default-session" fallback, so one user's messages can enter another user's prompt. 7. Chat is not listed in any tier and is not gated, so it is a free unlimited cost centre until a decision is made. The Monthly Market Breakdown (a Plus feature) reaches no user today: email is mocked and there is no in-app surface.  I did not run the backend tests or the app, and did not call any provider. The model id claude-sonnet-5 is a valid current id per the bundled Claude API reference (cached 2026-11-03).


| Tier | Feature | Status | Tier gate | Gaps |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| plus | Monthly market breakdown | partial | yes-backend | Generation, compliance gate, personal numbers and template are built and tested. Delivery is not: the email sender only logs, the unsubscribe link is a placeholder, email preference is not checked, the job has no lock, and there is no in-app screen. Eligibility is selectedTier in plus/pro, which has no billing behind it. |
| none | Ask FRED AI chat | implemented | no | Works end to end but has no tier gate, no usage cap, no disclaimer or AI consent, and no data deletion. Needs a decision on which tiers include it. |


### [high] No rate limit, daily cap, or message size cap on chat endpoints

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/controller/ChatController.java:37-59 (no limit, no length check on message); BE/service/LLMService.java:54 (CHAT_MAX_TOKENS 16000), :59 (MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS 4), :444-445 (adaptive thinking on every streamed chat); BE/service/ConversationAnalyticsService.java:45-49,123 (extra Claude call per message for topic); BE/service/RAGService.java:46 (OpenAI embedding per message); BE/service/ChatService.java:403-405 (title call). Grep for ratelimit/bucket across BE/ only hits FredChatActionService (actions), WebAuthnService, AnalyticsBands, FredKnowledge.
- Detail: Any authenticated account can call GET /api/chat/stream or POST /api/chat in a loop. Each message costs: 1 OpenAI embedding, up to 4 streamed Claude rounds with adaptive thinking and a 16k output ceiling, 1 extra Claude call for topic classification, and 1 more for a title when requested. The only rate limit in the chat domain is on the action endpoint (6 per minute). There is also no maximum message length, and no per-user or global daily token or dollar budget. With real users this is unbounded provider spend from a single scripted account. The system prompt (constitution plus two story texts) is resent on every request with no prompt caching, which raises the per-message cost further.
- Suggested fix: Add a per-user limiter on /api/chat and /api/chat/stream (for example Caffeine sliding window like FredChatActionService: N messages per minute and N per day), a hard message length cap (for example 2,000 chars), and a global daily kill switch property. Return a friendly in-character message when limited. Set a monthly spend limit on the Anthropic and OpenAI workspaces as a backstop. Consider dropping the per-message LLM topic classification or moving it to a cheaper model, and add cache_control on the system prompt.
- Verifier (adjusted): True as stated. ChatController.java:37-59 has no limiter or length check, a grep of backend/src/main for rate limiting finds only FredChatActionService.java:80-85 (actions) and PinController, and LLMService.java:54,59,442-445 confirm the 16k ceiling, 4 tool rounds and adaptive thinking. The extra topic call is real (ConversationAnalyticsService.java:45-49,123) and so is the embedding per message (RAGService.java:46). Two things the auditor missed: (1) chat is not public, SecurityConfig.java:113-119 puts /api/chat/** under anyRequest().authenticated(), so the abuser must be a registered beta user; (2) the stream endpoint takes the message as a GET query param (ChatController.java:49), so the server's default header limit caps it in practice, while POST /api/chat has no such cap. I did not confirm the 'no prompt caching' claim beyond a grep that found no cache_control in LLMService. For an invite-only private beta this is a cost exposure that a provider-side spend limit can backstop, not something that stops real money going in, so high rather than blocker. Must be fixed before public launch.

### [high] what-we-send.md and the privacy label omit Anthropic, OpenAI and Pinecone, and contradict what chat sends

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: FREDdocs/what-we-send.md:18-22 ("product analytics to one destination, PostHog, and nothing else today"; never free text; never a dollar amount), :32-38 (destinations table has only PostHog and Sentry), :207 (chat insights "not sent to any third party"). Actual sends: BE/service/ChatService.java:523-551 (age, pay frequency, time horizon, risk tolerance in the prompt), :218-226 and :333-352 (user free text and history to Claude); BE/service/FredChatToolsService.java:132-145,187-226 (portfolio value, positions, gain/loss, schedule amounts, next investment date returned to Claude as tool results); BE/service/RAGService.java:46 with BE/service/LLMService.java:111-128 (every user message sent verbatim to OpenAI embeddings); BE/service/ConversationAnalyticsService.java:92-123 (question sent to Claude again). FREDdocs/privacy-manifest-and-label.md:60-72 lists undeclared data types and has no row for AI chat or AI providers.
- Detail: The document meant for counsel and App Store review says no free text and no dollar amount ever leaves the system and that PostHog is the only third party. Chat sends user-typed free text to both Anthropic and OpenAI, sends exact age (the prompt labels it "Age range" but passes user.getAge()), and sends exact portfolio dollar values, holdings and schedule amounts to Anthropic through tool results. The retention table says chat insight records go to no third party, but the question text in them was already sent to Anthropic for classification. The analytics opt-out toggle does not cover any of this.
- Suggested fix: Add an "AI chat" section to what-we-send.md with rows for Anthropic (prompts, profile fields, tool results with dollar figures), OpenAI (message text for embeddings) and Pinecone (query vectors only), plus each provider's retention terms. Add the same to privacy-manifest-and-label.md. Decide whether to stop sending the raw user message to OpenAI (for example skip RAG for short or personal queries) and whether to send an age band instead of exact age. Confirm provider data-retention settings on both accounts.
- Verifier (adjusted): Facts confirmed. FREDdocs/what-we-send.md:1-6 says the page covers 'every piece of data FRED sends to a third party' and is the document for counsel and App Store review, then :18-22 says PostHog 'and nothing else today', no free text, no dollar amount. A grep for anthropic/openai/pinecone/claude in what-we-send.md and privacy-manifest-and-label.md returns only line 207 ('not sent to any third party'). The code does send user free text to Claude and OpenAI (ChatService.java:317,333-335; RAGService.java:46; ConversationAnalyticsService.java:113-123) and exact age labelled 'Age range' (ChatService.java:529,540). I did not re-open FredChatToolsService for the dollar figures in tool results, so that part is unverified by me. Severity: this is a documentation and disclosure error, quick to fix, and it must be corrected before the doc goes to counsel or App Review. It does not by itself stop a private beta, so high, not blocker. Treat it as a blocker for the App Store submission step.

### [high] No in-app AI disclosure, consent, or disclaimer in the chat UI

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/ai-chat/ai-chat.page.html:1-129 (whole template: no disclaimer, no AI label, no provider notice). grep for "not (financial|investment) advice|educational|disclaimer|AI-generated" in frontend/src/app/pages/ai-chat/ returned nothing. grep for "anthropic|third.party AI|shared with" in frontend/src/app (non-spec .ts/.html) returned nothing.
- Detail: The chat screen shows "Ask FRED", the messages and an input. Nothing tells the user that replies are AI generated, may be wrong, are education and not personalised advice, or that their messages and portfolio data are sent to a third-party AI provider. The only disclaimers are inside the two canned story texts. For an investing app this is a compliance gap, and Apple's App Review guideline 5.1.2(i) (updated November 2025, from my own knowledge and not verified in this repo) requires clear disclosure and explicit permission before sharing personal data with third-party AI.
- Suggested fix: Add a persistent one-line footer under the composer (for example "FRED is an AI guide. Education only, not personal advice. It can be wrong.") and a first-use consent sheet naming the AI provider and what is shared, with the consent stored server-side. Wording needs counsel sign-off.
- Verifier (confirmed): Grep across frontend/src/app/pages/ai-chat (.html and non-spec .ts) for advice, educational, disclaimer, AI-generated, anthropic, artificial, consent, disclos returns only an unrelated comment in chat-action-card.component.ts:32. The footer at ai-chat.page.html:120-129 is just the composer and send button. No file under frontend/src/app mentions Anthropic, OpenAI or Pinecone. The Apple guideline 5.1.2(i) reference is the auditor's own knowledge and is not verifiable from the repo. The exact wording is a counsel item, but building the footer and consent sheet is app work.

### [high] Chat data is never deleted: no retention on chat_messages and account deletion skips all chat tables

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/service/UserDeletionService.java:142-165 (deletes plaid events, trades, executions, drip, beneficiaries, schedules, sessions, portfolio, goals, user; no chat repository is injected at lines 48-61). backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__baseline.sql:42-78 (chat_action_audit, chat_messages, conversation_insights have user_id with no foreign key, so nothing cascades). BE/repository/ChatMessageRepository.java:12-28 (no delete method). BE/service/ConversationAnalyticsService.java:216-229 (90-day cleanup exists only for conversation_insights). FREDdocs/what-we-send.md:214-215 claims "the user row and its personal data are deleted from our database".
- Detail: When an account is closed, every chat message the user typed, FRED's replies (which can contain their portfolio figures), the raw question text in conversation_insights (until the 90-day job) and the action audit rows stay in the database keyed by the old user id. chat_messages has no retention job at all, so it grows forever. There is also no way for a user to delete a conversation: ChatService.deleteSession in the frontend only touches localStorage and is never called from any page.
- Suggested fix: Add deleteAllByUserId for chat_messages and conversation_insights to UserDeletionService inside the same transaction. Decide with counsel whether chat_action_audit and chat transcripts must be retained as adviser records (that would change the fix to anonymise rather than delete). Add a retention job or documented policy for chat_messages, and update what-we-send.md retention table to include it.
- Verifier (confirmed): UserDeletionService.java:6-15 and :48-89 import and inject no chat, insight or audit repository. ChatMessageRepository.java:12-30 has only finders, no delete. V1__baseline.sql:42-62 defines chat_action_audit, chat_messages and conversation_insights, and the foreign keys to users in that file sit on other tables. The only cleanup job is the 90-day one for insights (ConversationAnalyticsService.java:216-229). Frontend deleteSession exists only at chat.service.ts:183 with no caller in any non-spec .ts or .html. This contradicts what-we-send.md:214-215. Note the Safety Check log lines also write the raw user message to application logs (ChatService.java:282-283, :397-398), which is a further retention surface. Whether transcripts must be kept as adviser records is a counsel question.

### [high] Regex safety gate blocks core legitimate questions and is trivially bypassed

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/service/SafetyService.java:28-30 (AMOUNT_PATTERN matches "how much" and "invest $<digit>"), :23-25 (TIMING_PATTERN matches the single words recession, correction, peak, bottom, timing), :44-46 (TOXIC_PATTERN matches hate, kill, stupid, scam), :54-69 (first match returns a block). BE/config/FredRedirections.java:42-48 (canned "I can't tell you exactly how much to invest"), :67-68 ("I cannot continue this conversation."). BE/service/ChatService.java:281-297 (blocked messages never reach the model or the tools).
- Detail: The pre-filter runs on the raw user message before the model. Reading the patterns: "How much is my portfolio worth?" and "How much have I invested?" match AMOUNT_PATTERN and get a canned refusal about how much to invest, so the read-only portfolio tools never run. "What if I invest $200 a month?" is blocked the same way. "What is a recession?" and "What is a market correction?" are blocked as market timing. "Is FRED a scam?" or "I hate my job" gets the hard stop "I cannot continue this conversation." Meanwhile real advice requests that avoid the keyword pairs (for example "should I move everything into Nvidia") pass straight through, so the gate gives little real protection. I confirmed this by reading the regexes, not by running them. Only FredRedirectionsTest covers this class.
- Suggested fix: Narrow or remove the regex gate and rely on the constitution plus an output-side check. At minimum drop "how much", the single-word timing terms and "scam/hate/stupid" from the hard blocks, and add a SafetyService test table with must-pass questions (portfolio questions, the daily suggestion pool, education basics) and must-block ones.
- Verifier (adjusted): Confirmed, and stronger than stated: I ran the exact patterns from SafetyService.java:18-46 in a regex test. 'How much is my portfolio worth?', 'How much have I invested?' and 'What if I invest $200 a month?' hit AMOUNT; 'What is a recession?' and 'What is a market correction?' hit TIMING; 'Is FRED a scam?' and 'I hate my job' hit TOXIC; 'should I move everything into Nvidia' matches nothing (the ticker list has nvda, not nvidia). Both paths gate on it before the model (ChatService.java:143-160 and :281-297). The built-in suggestion chips I tested ('Help me bump up my monthly investment a little', 'What's your story FRED?') pass. Correction to detail: there is no SafetyService test at all in backend/src/test/.../service (only ChatServiceStoryReplyTest matches chat or safety there). The daily LLM-written chips are told to use 'concrete numbers (monthly amounts, years)' (DailyQuestionService.java:174-176), so a generated chip containing 'invest $500' or 'how much' would be blocked by the gate when tapped; I did not observe a real instance. Severity high stands: it is a product-quality failure on a headline feature, not a money-safety issue.

### [high] No output-side compliance check on chat replies; guardrails are prompt-only

- Type: decision, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: BE/service/ChatService.java:352-385 (streamed tokens go to the client and are persisted with only the trigger-token intercept); BE/config/FredConstitution.java:209 (model told to default every projection to a 10% average annual return), :218-234 (model may propose changing the user's contribution amount), :271-285 (the prohibitions are instructions only). Contrast BE/util/MarketNarrativeValidator.java:29-35 and BE/service/MarketBreakdownService.java:250-255 where generated text is validated before use. BE/service/DailyQuestionService.java:170-187 (LLM-written suggestion chips are saved and shown to all users with no validation).
- Detail: The Monthly Market Breakdown is gated by a deterministic validator, but chat replies and the daily LLM-written suggestion chips are not checked at all. Chat can also read the user's real numbers and then propose a specific new contribution amount as an action card (a daily suggestion chip literally says "Help me bump up my monthly investment a little" at DailyQuestionService.java:208). Whether that counts as personalised advice, and whether the standing 10% projection default is acceptable, is a question for counsel. what-we-send.md:230-231 refers to a registered adviser, while BE/config/FredKnowledge.java:54 and :98 tell users FRED is "Not a financial advisor" and "provides education only". Those two positions need to be reconciled.
- Suggested fix: Ask counsel: (1) is FRED an adviser or education-only, (2) may chat propose specific contribution changes, (3) is a 10% default projection with a hypothetical note acceptable. Then add a light post-generation check on chat output (forbidden phrases, guarantee language) with logging for review, and run the daily suggestion chips through the same check plus a length cap before saving.
- Verifier (confirmed): ChatService.java:352-385 streams and persists the reply with only the story-trigger intercept. FredConstitution.java:209 sets the 10% default and :218-219 allows proposed account changes. MarketBreakdownService.java:250-251 does validate with MarketNarrativeValidator, so the contrast holds. DailyQuestionService.java:182-187 splits the model output on '|' and returns it with no validation or enforced length cap (the 90 character limit is only an instruction at :177). what-we-send.md:230-231 does say 'a registered adviser' while FredKnowledge.java:54-55 and :98 say not a financial advisor and education only. Mitigations the auditor understated: actions only execute on an explicit Confirm tap and are audited and rate limited (ChatController.java:61-64, FredChatActionService.java:80-95). Type 'decision' is right: the adviser versus education question goes to counsel first, and the size of the app work depends on the answer.

### [high] Knowledge base contains stale or wrong facts the model is told to prioritise above all else

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/config/FredKnowledge.java:109-110 ("A flat $8/month subscription", "First 2 weeks are free") versus frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:72-74,115-117,170-172 (three tiers at $8, $15, $40 with yearly prices); FredKnowledge.java:193 and :338-352 (identity verification and account recovery "via Persona" with a live selfie), while grep for withpersona/PersonaService/persona.api in backend/src/main/java finds no code; :371 ("For development, the email sending service is mocked (logs the OTP to the console)"); :438-439 (transfer and trade confirmation emails) versus BE/service/LoggingEmailSender.java:13-25 (default sender only logs); :48 ("A banking + automation app"); BE/config/FredConstitution.java:311 (model MUST prioritise this knowledge above all else).
- Detail: FRED will tell real users the price is a flat $8 a month with a two-week trial, that account recovery uses Persona biometric selfie checks, that they will receive transfer and trade emails, and can surface an internal dev note about mocked email. The pricing claim does not match the tier screen, I found no Persona integration in backend code (project notes say recovery is email plus OTP), and email sending is mocked. I did not audit all 706 lines; the freedom-calculation chunk at :648-690 describes two different calculations and should be checked against the current unified estimate.
- Suggested fix: Do a line-by-line review of FredKnowledge.CANONICAL_CHUNKS against the shipped app, remove pricing until billing is decided, rewrite the recovery chunk to the real flow, strip dev notes, and add a short owner checklist so the chunks are reviewed on each release. Note that removed chunks are never deleted from Pinecone (see the RAG finding), so old vectors must be purged too.
- Verifier (confirmed): FredKnowledge.java:109-110 says flat $8/month and first 2 weeks free, while investmentconfirmation.component.ts:72-74,115-117,170-172 has $8, $15 and $40 tiers. FredKnowledge.java:91-94, :193, :338-349, :368 describe Persona KYC and selfie recovery; 'persona' appears in no integration class under backend/src/main/java (hits are the word 'personal' or unrelated, plus this file). Line :371 exposes the mocked-email dev note. :48 says 'A banking + automation app'. FredConstitution.java:311 tells the model it MUST prioritise this context. I did not verify the transfer and trade email lines at :438-439 or the freedom calculation chunk. Telling real customers wrong pricing and a non-existent biometric recovery flow in a regulated app is a real misstatement risk; I would fix this before the beta, though it is a content edit rather than a structural blocker.

### [high] Chat history is loaded by sessionId only, with a shared "default-session" fallback

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/service/ChatService.java:320-321 and :189-190 (findTop10BySessionIdOrderByCreatedAtDesc with no user filter feeds the prompt), :455-460 (titleSource reads the first user message of the session, also unscoped), :127-133 (action outcome is saved into any client-supplied sessionId); BE/controller/ChatController.java:56-57 (null sessionId becomes "default-session"); frontend/src/app/pages/ai-chat/ai-chat.page.ts:509 and :841 (client also falls back to 'default-session'); frontend/src/app/services/chat.service.ts:260-262 (session ids are Date.now plus Math.random). The history endpoint itself is scoped correctly at ChatService.java:499-506.
- Detail: The GET /history endpoint filters by user, but the prompt-building path does not. If two users ever send with the same sessionId, the last 10 messages of that session, from any user, are put into the model context and can be echoed back, and the session title can be generated from another user's first message. Guessing a random session id is impractical, but "default-session" is a fixed string used by both the controller and the client whenever no session exists, and any API client can pass it. In normal app use a session is created before sending, so I rate real-world likelihood as low, but the impact is a cross-user disclosure of financial conversations.
- Suggested fix: Add userId to every session query (findTop10ByUserIdAndSessionId..., findTopByUserIdAndSessionIdAndRole...), remove the "default-session" fallback on both sides (reject a missing sessionId or generate a server-side UUID), and verify session ownership before saving an action outcome row.
- Verifier (adjusted): Confirmed, and there is one more leak path the auditor missed. Prompt history is unscoped at ChatService.java:189-190 and :320-321, the title source at :455-460, and the action outcome save at :127-133. The controller falls back to 'default-session' at ChatController.java:57 and the client at ai-chat.page.ts:509 and :841. Additional path: GET /api/chat/history with no sessionId calls getRecentSessionHistory (ChatController.java:102), which finds the user's latest sessionId and then calls the unscoped getChatHistory (ChatService.java:508-518, :485-492). If a user's latest message is in 'default-session', that endpoint returns every user's messages in that session directly to the client, with no model in between. So the comment at ChatController.java:91-94 that history is 'always scoped' is only true for the sessionId branch. Also POST /api/chat passes request.sessionId() through with no null fallback (ChatController.java:41-42), and session_id is NOT NULL (V1__baseline.sql:58), so a null there would error rather than leak. Normal app use creates a session first, so likelihood is low, but the fix is small and the impact is cross-user disclosure of financial chat. Keep high and do it before beta.

### [high] Monthly Market Breakdown (Plus feature) is generated but reaches no user

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/service/LoggingEmailSender.java:13-25 (default EmailSender logs "MOCK EMAIL"); backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:47-49 (EMAIL_ENABLED false, provider logging by default); BE/service/MarketBreakdownEmailComposer.java:123 (unsubscribeUrl is "#"); BE/scheduler/MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:31,68 (eligibility is selectedTier in plus/pro only, no check of agreedToMarketing or any email preference; BE/model/User.java:315-319 has the field, and its only other use is UserController.java:550-551); :50 (no JobLock, unlike AccountStatusScheduler.java:59 and InvestmentScheduler.java:48). grep for market-breakdown in frontend/src/app finds only the tier card (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:159-161). BE/controller/MarketBreakdownController.java:65 (preview endpoint exists but no app screen calls it).
- Detail: The tier screen promises Plus users a monthly plain-English market breakdown. Generation, the compliance gate, the personal numbers and the email template exist and are tested, but the only delivery channel is email, which is known to be mocked, and there is no in-app place to read it. When a real provider is wired, three gaps remain: the unsubscribe link is a "#" placeholder, the send loop ignores the user's email preference, and the job has no lock so two backend instances could double send (the hasBeenSent check narrows but does not close that race). The narrative also auto-sends with no human review step. Eligibility keys off selectedTier, which is self-selected with no billing behind it.
- Suggested fix: Decide the delivery channel for beta: either wire a real email provider with a working unsubscribe link and preference check, or add an in-app "Monthly breakdown" screen backed by the existing preview endpoint (returning JSON). Add a JobLock, and decide with counsel whether a human approval step is needed before each month's send.
- Verifier (adjusted): Confirmed on every cited point: LoggingEmailSender.java:13-25 is the default sender and the only EmailSender implementation in the service folder; application.properties:47-49 defaults to disabled and 'logging'; MarketBreakdownEmailComposer.java:123 has unsubscribeUrl '#'; MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:31,68 selects by selectedTier only, with no agreedToMarketing check (the field is only touched at UserController.java:550-551 and User.java:316-319) and no lock on the @Scheduled method at :50; the frontend mentions the feature only on the tier card (investmentconfirmation.component.ts:159-160); the preview endpoint returns HTML at MarketBreakdownController.java:65. Adjustment is to type, not severity: the choice of delivery channel, real email provider and whether a human approves each send are decisions and partly external (provider account, counsel), and the project notes list go-live gates for this on purpose. The mocked send itself is known context. The app-work pieces are the unsubscribe link, the preference check, the job lock and, if chosen, an in-app screen. It matters because the tier card sells this to Plus users, so it is tied to the subscription tier work.

### [medium] Chat has no tier gating and is not part of any tier's promise

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/controller/ChatController.java:23-114 (no tier check anywhere); frontend/src/app/components/investmentconfirmation/investmentconfirmation.component.ts:64-200 (feature ids: auto-invest, portfolio, rebalance, education, montecarlo, multi-goal, external-accounts, custom-rebalance, market-breakdown, drawdown, deeper-projections; none mention Ask FRED); grep for tier/plus/paywall in frontend/src/app/pages/ai-chat/ai-chat.page.ts finds nothing; FREDdocs/backlog.md:526 describes a frontend-only expired gate that greys out the Chat tab when selectedTier is null.
- Detail: Ask FRED is the most expensive feature per use and is available to every authenticated user, including users with no tier, as far as the backend is concerned. It is not listed as a benefit of any plan. This is a product and pricing decision: free for all with a cap, or included from a given tier.
- Suggested fix: Decide which tiers get chat and what the usage allowance is, then enforce it in ChatController (backend), not only in the tab bar. Add it to the tier cards if it is a selling point.

### [medium] Provider keys are required at boot but missing from the prod checklist; prod profile has no AI config

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:61-68 (anthropic.api-key=${ANTHROPIC_API_KEY}, openai.api-key=${OPENAI_API_KEY}, pinecone.api-key=${PINECONE_API_KEY}, pinecone.index-name=${PINECONE_INDEX_NAME}, all with no default; :62 model fixed to claude-sonnet-5; :64 unused openai.chat-model=gpt-4o); backend/src/main/resources/application-ci.properties:38-45 (comment confirms the context fails to start without the Anthropic key); backend/src/main/resources/application-prod.properties:1-6 (required-variable list does not mention any of the four); BE/service/LLMService.java:92-99 (comment says the app can boot without the key, which the placeholder contradicts).
- Detail: A first prod deploy that follows the documented variable list will fail to start because four AI variables have no default. There is also no prod-specific setting for the Pinecone index, so dev and prod will share one index unless someone sets a different name by hand, and RAGService rewrites that index on every boot. The model id is valid (claude-sonnet-5 is a listed current model) but is a previous-generation id; note that moving to claude-sonnet-5-5 would break the utility calls because LLMService.java:451 sends thinking disabled, which that model rejects.
- Suggested fix: Add the four variables to the application-prod.properties header and the deploy runbook, use a separate Pinecone index and separate Anthropic and OpenAI keys for prod, give the keys empty defaults if the intent is that the app boots without chat, and delete the unused openai.chat-model line.

### [medium] User chat text is written to application logs at INFO

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/service/ChatService.java:146-147, :282-283 (full query with user id in the safety log), :256-257, :397-398 (full query again in the interaction log); :140 in BE/service/DailyQuestionService.java is unrelated. BE/controller/ChatController.java:47-52 and frontend/src/app/services/chat.service.ts:58,67 (the message travels as a GET query string).
- Detail: Every chat message is logged two to three times in full next to the numeric user id, so Railway log storage holds users' free-text financial questions with no retention control. Because streaming uses GET with the message in the URL, the text can also land in proxy, tunnel and access logs, and long messages can exceed URL or header size limits and fail before reaching the app (I did not test the limit).
- Suggested fix: Log only message length, verdict and reason. Change the stream endpoint to POST with a JSON body (fetch already supports reading an SSE body from a POST) and keep the GET only until the app update ships.

### [medium] Chat stream setup blocks the request thread and runs on the shared common pool with no timeouts on OpenAI

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: BE/service/ChatService.java:281-321 (safety check, DB writes, embedding and Pinecone query all run before the emitter is returned), :344 (CompletableFuture.runAsync with no executor, so the JVM common pool); BE/service/LLMService.java:86-90 and :122-128 (RestClient to OpenAI built with no connect or read timeout), :101-103 (Anthropic client built with SDK defaults); BE/service/RAGService.java:83-86 (errors are caught, but a hang is not an error). No spring.task.execution or async settings in any backend/src/main/resources/*.properties.
- Detail: If OpenAI or Pinecone is slow, every chat request waits on the servlet thread before the user sees anything, and nothing times it out. Each active stream then holds one common-pool thread for the whole generation, which can run for minutes. On a small container the common pool is tiny and is shared with any other CompletableFuture or parallel stream work in the app, so a handful of concurrent chats can stall each other. I did not load test this; it is from reading the code.
- Suggested fix: Give chat its own bounded ThreadPoolTaskExecutor with a queue limit and a clear "FRED is busy" reply when full, set short connect and read timeouts on the embedding call (for example 3 seconds, then continue without RAG), and move the RAG lookup inside the async task.

### [medium] Provider-down experience is a generic error with no status, and a failed first round leaves orphan rows

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/service/ChatService.java:300 (user message persisted before generation), :424-429 (any exception ends the stream with completeWithError, no in-character message); BE/service/LLMService.java:293-303 (graceful text only when a later round fails after text was streamed), :97-100 (missing key throws IllegalStateException); frontend/src/app/pages/ai-chat/ai-chat.page.ts:552-561 (every failure shows "I'm sorry, I'm having trouble connecting right now. Please try again."), :578-596 (retry resends, creating a second user row); frontend/src/app/services/chat.service.ts:67-71 (raw fetch, so a 401 from an expired JWT is treated as the same connection error and does not go through the app's auth handling).
- Detail: When Anthropic is down, rate limited, or the key is missing, the user sees the same message as a network drop, with a retry button that will keep failing. There is no distinction between "you are offline", "FRED is temporarily unavailable" and "your session expired". The backend keeps the user message on every failed attempt, so retries pile up duplicate user rows (LLMService coalesces them, so it does not break the next call). No alerting exists for repeated provider failures.
- Suggested fix: Send a typed SSE error event (provider_unavailable, rate_limited, unauthorized) and show matching copy, route 401 through the normal re-auth path, and add an error-rate log line or metric that can be alerted on.

### [medium] Local chat history is not user-scoped and is not cleared on logout

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/services/chat.service.ts:27 (single key 'fred_chat_history'), :153-181 (sessions with full message text stored in localStorage); frontend/src/app/utils/jwt-token.utils.ts:100-116 (clearJwtData removes the per-user stat strip and portfolio snapshots but not chat history). grep for fred_chat_history outside chat.service.ts finds no other reference.
- Detail: Chat transcripts, which can include portfolio figures, are stored unencrypted in localStorage under one device-wide key. After logout, or when a second person signs in on the same device, the History menu still lists the previous user's conversations. The same file already clears other per-user caches for exactly this reason. The backend history endpoint would correctly refuse the other user's sessions, but the local copy is shown first.
- Suggested fix: Key the chat cache by userId and remove it in clearJwtData, the same way the stat strip and portfolio snapshots are handled.

No test references ChatController, FredChatActionService, FredChatToolsService, LLMService, RAGService, DailyQuestionService or ConversationAnalyticsService.
- Detail: The market breakdown is well covered. The chat controller's principal binding, the action allowlist and rate limit, the tool executor, the trigger-token holdback in streaming, and the message bridge in LLMService have no tests, so the security properties listed under verifiedOk are held only by code review. I did not run the existing tests.
- Suggested fix: Add tests for: ChatController ignores client userId; history scoped to user; FredChatActionService rejects unknown actions, enforces the amount range and the 6 per minute limit, and writes audit rows; drainSendable never leaks a trigger tag; SafetyService must-pass and must-block tables.

### [low] RAG bootstrap re-embeds everything on every boot and never removes retired chunks

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/service/RAGService.java:92-143 (on ApplicationReadyEvent, one OpenAI embedding call per chunk then upsert; failures only logged), :100-102 (inactive chunks are skipped, not deleted), :60-71 (retrieval filters on an "active" metadata flag that a skipped chunk never gets updated to false).
- Detail: Each backend start makes one OpenAI call per knowledge chunk, synchronously, and with multiple instances they all do it. A chunk that is deleted or marked inactive in code stays in Pinecone with active=true and keeps being retrieved, so fixing the stale knowledge in code alone will not stop the model seeing the old text. Without the OpenAI key at runtime, chat still works but with no knowledge context, and only an error log says so.
- Suggested fix: Delete vectors whose ids are not in the active set during bootstrap, skip re-embedding when a content hash is unchanged, and log a single clear warning (or health flag) when RAG is unavailable.

### [low] /api/chat/suggestions trusts a client-supplied userId and can 500

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: BE/controller/ChatController.java:110-113 (@RequestParam Long userId passed straight through, unlike the other endpoints which use authenticatedUserId()); BE/service/DailyQuestionService.java:80-88 (existing.get() after a generation that may not have saved), :91-98 (per-user branch based on that userId); frontend/src/app/services/chat.service.ts:147-149 (omits the param when localStorage has no userId, which makes the required param fail with 400).
- Detail: The leak is tiny (it reveals only whether another user has asked for the FRED story), but it is the one chat endpoint that does not bind to the principal. If saving the day's questions fails, the method throws on an empty Optional instead of returning the fallback list. Two first requests of the day can also both call Claude to generate the same row.
- Suggested fix: Use authenticatedUserId(), make the param optional and ignored, and return the fallback list when no row exists.

### [low] Action confirm PIN step-up is enforced only in the frontend

- Evidence: frontend/src/app/pages/ai-chat/ai-chat.page.ts:822-836 (PIN prompt before confirm); BE/controller/ChatController.java:65-77 and BE/service/FredChatActionService.java:71-128 (no step-up check server-side).

This matches the frontend comment that the in-app flows gate the same mutations with a PIN, but I did not check whether the non-chat schedule endpoints enforce step-up on the server, so this may be a wider pattern rather than a chat-only gap.
