### [low] Market breakdown compliance gate has a narrow phrase list and uses the older web search tool version

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: BE/util/MarketNarrativeValidator.java:29-35 (advice phrases only; no forward-looking terms such as "will likely", "expect", "is poised"); BE/service/LLMService.java:173-175 (WebSearchTool20250305); BE/service/MarketBreakdownService.java:244-248 (requires at least one source but does not check that sources are reputable or store which claims they support); backend/src/main/resources/application-dev.properties:72 (manual generate enabled on the shared dev server for any authenticated user).
- Detail: The gate is a good backstop for direct advice and for unsafe HTML, but the prompt's "never predict" rule has no matching check, and the prompt itself asks the model to explain why funds "likely" moved. Web content from search is the one place untrusted text enters an LLM call; the tag allowlist limits the damage to wording. The basic web search tool version still works on this model; a newer version with dynamic filtering exists. On api-dev any logged-in user can trigger generation for a failed month repeatedly.
- Suggested fix: Add a small set of prediction phrases to the validator with tests, consider an allowed-domains list for the search tool, and restrict the dev manual-generate endpoint to the dev tools key.

**Verified solid:**

- Chat endpoints require authentication: SecurityConfig.java:111-119 permits only auth, passkey, Plaid webhook, dev and hello paths; everything else including /api/chat/** and /api/market-breakdown/** is authenticated.
- Chat binds to the authenticated principal, not client input: ChatController.java:41-42, 56-57, 68-73, 95-102 ignore the client userId for chat, stream, action and history.
- History endpoint is scoped to the requesting user: ChatService.java:499-506 filters session history by userId.
- Model tools are read-only and bound to one user: FredChatToolsService.java:50-62 closes over the authenticated User; the four tools (lines 64-104) take no user identifier and none mutate state; unknown tool names return an error (line 115).
- The model cannot execute account changes: actions run only via POST /api/chat/action after a Confirm tap, with an allowlist of three actions (FredChatActionService.java:40-43, 75-78), a 6 per minute per-user limit (45, 80-85), amount range validation (139-144), an intent-first audit row that blocks execution if it cannot be written (87-97), and an outcome update (118-126).
- Tool loop is bounded and degrades gracefully: LLMService.java:59 (4 rounds), 309-315 (exhaustion message), 354-358 (final round forces a text answer), 293-303 (later-round failure after text streamed closes politely), 365-376 (refusal and max_tokens handled in character).
- SSE keepalive and client-disconnect handling exist: LLMService.java:265-272 heartbeat every 5 to 10 seconds; ChatService.java:276-278 and 439-449 stop writes but finish and persist the reply when the client goes away; frontend chat.service.ts:75-98 buffers partial SSE lines.
- Raw trigger tokens cannot reach the user: ChatService.java:50-84 holdback logic and 370-382 swap to the canonical story text.
- Chat markdown rendering is sanitized: frontend/src/app/pipes/markdown.pipe.ts:25-27 runs DOMPurify and forbids img tags; user messages render as plain text (ai-chat.page.html:80-82).
- Action cards are only tappable on the latest message and gated by PIN when the user has one: ai-chat.page.ts:815-836.
- Model id claude-sonnet-5 is a valid current Anthropic model id, and the request shapes used (adaptive thinking for streaming, explicit disabled thinking for utility calls, no prefill, no sampling params) are accepted on that model per the bundled Claude API reference.
- Anthropic SDK is the official Java SDK: backend/build.gradle:34 (com.anthropic:anthropic-java:2.48.0).
- Real API keys are not in git: application-local.properties holds live-looking keys but is gitignored (.gitignore:44), is not in git ls-files, and has no commit history; tracked profiles use environment placeholders or ci-disabled.
- CI boots offline: application-ci.properties:35-45 uses placeholder keys and CiStubConfig provides a stub Pinecone client.
- RAG failure does not break chat: RAGService.java:83-86 returns an empty list on any exception and ChatService continues without knowledge context.
- Conversation insight records have a working 90-day cleanup job: ConversationAnalyticsService.java:216-229.
- Raw questions from one user are not shown to others today: getPopularQuestions and getPersonalizedQuestions (ConversationAnalyticsService.java:270, 310) have no callers; daily suggestions come from a fixed pool plus one LLM call (DailyQuestionService.java:123-213).
- Daily question generation has safe fallbacks when the provider fails: DailyQuestionService.java:127-132, 144-151, 192-195.
- Market breakdown generation is gated and idempotent: completed months only (MarketBreakdownService.java:223-226), existing GENERATED row reused (228-231), numbers from Alpaca with a hard failure on any missing bar (117-134), must have web sources (244-248), must pass MarketNarrativeValidator (250-255), failed narrative is nulled so it cannot be sent (266-267).
- Market breakdown validator enforces a strict tag allowlist with no attributes, no href, no event handlers, no javascript: URIs, and length bounds: MarketNarrativeValidator.java:25-104.
- Market breakdown email carries a not-investment-advice and past-performance disclaimer: templates/email/market-breakdown.html:32-33 and MarketBreakdownEmailComposer.java:128-129.
- Manual market breakdown generation is off by default and off in prod: application.properties:52 defaults false; application-prod.properties does not enable it; MarketBreakdownController.java:109-112 returns 403.
- Per-user market breakdown sends are idempotent per period and one user's failure does not stop the batch: MarketBreakdownScheduler.java:77-94.

## data-model-migrations

The schema foundation is in good shape: Flyway owns the schema (V1 to V9 plus 7 repeatable views), ddl-auto is validate in every profile, a static entity-versus-migration comparison found no drift, there is no V-number collision with the unmerged Paycheck Autopilot branch (it carries V10, develop ends at V9), money is DECIMAL on every ledger table, and the nightly ACH batch has real idempotency (unique source_key and night_key, a committed claim before the Alpaca POST, job locks). The production risks are concentrated in four places. (1) Beneficiary SSNs are stored in plaintext and returned in full by the API. (2) The funding-check and trading-check jobs run one database transaction across all users with Alpaca calls and buy orders inside it, and buy orders carry no client_order_id, so a rollback or a restart mid-pass can place duplicate buys. (3) Account deletion hard-deletes the money ledger (executions, trades, DRIP) while leaving chat, tax-lot, TLH and email-log rows orphaned, cannot be used by a user with no brokerage account, and has no pending-deletion state. (4) There is no reconciler for a batch row that was claimed but never got its transfer id. Below those: an unsalted SSN hash that nothing reads, a weak key policy in prod, no optimistic locking on the wide users row, most recurring jobs unlocked across instances, missing indexes on scheduler lookups, fill precision narrower than what Alpaca returns, and mixed clocks. I could not reach any live database, so the state of api-dev's flyway_schema_history and whether backups exist are unconfirmed.


### [blocker] Beneficiary SSN is stored in plaintext and returned in full by the API

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/resources/db/migration/V1__baseline.sql:32 (social_security_number varchar(11) NOT NULL); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/Beneficiary.java:68-71 (length 11, pattern XXX-XX-XXXX, no @JsonIgnore); service/BeneficiaryService.java:426 (copied as is, no encrypt call anywhere in the file); controller/BeneficiaryController.java:37-38, 55-56, 211-212, 229-230 (endpoints return List<Beneficiary> entities); service/AlpacaService.java:1176 (read raw as tax_id)
- Detail: The user's own SSN is encrypted with AES-GCM (KycSubmissionService.java:89), but a beneficiary's SSN is a plain 11 character column. It cannot hold ciphertext at that width, and no code encrypts it. The entity is serialized directly by five GET endpoints and the SSN field is not ignored, so every list call sends each beneficiary's full SSN back to the device. Beneficiary date of birth, address, phone and email are also plaintext. These are third parties who never agreed to anything.
- Suggested fix: New migration widening social_security_number to varchar(512); encrypt on write and decrypt only when submitting to Alpaca; re-encrypt any existing rows in the same migration window; mark the field write-only in JSON and return a masked last-4 instead. Consider not keeping it at all after Alpaca accepts the beneficiary.
- Verifier (confirmed): True in the working tree. V1__baseline.sql:32 is varchar(11) NOT NULL; Beneficiary.java:68-71 has no @JsonIgnore on socialSecurityNumber (the only @JsonIgnore is at :33, the only @JsonProperty is on getFullAddress at :232). BeneficiaryService.java injects EncryptionService but uses it only to decrypt the Alpaca account id (:188, :219, :299); the SSN is copied raw at :426. BeneficiaryController.java:37-38, 55-56, 211-212, 229-230 return List<Beneficiary> entities. One nuance: these endpoints are authenticated and return only the caller's own beneficiaries, so the over-the-wire exposure is to the owning user's device, not to other users. The plaintext-at-rest part alone justifies blocker for real users.

### [blocker] Funding and trading status jobs can place duplicate buy orders after a rollback or restart

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:36 (class-level @Transactional); :536-555 checkFundingStatus and :1021-1045 checkTradingStatus (public, no propagation override, loop over all executions); :596-611 (status set then initiateTradingForExecution inside the same transaction); :907-912 and :976-981 (trade row saved, then Alpaca order placed); service/AlpacaService.java:295-304 (placeBuyOrder request has no client_order_id); scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:70-96 (neither job takes a job lock); service/JobLocks.java:6-9
- Detail: The nightly ACH batch was hardened to keep Alpaca calls out of database transactions, but the two daytime jobs were not. Each run is one transaction covering every user: it reads transfer status from Alpaca, flips rows to FUNDING_COMPLETED or TRADING_INITIATED, saves trade rows and places market buy orders. Nothing commits until the last user is done. If the process is redeployed or crashes mid-pass, or any nested transactional call throws and marks the transaction rollback-only, every status write and trade row from that pass is lost while the Alpaca orders stay live. The next pass (15 minutes later) sees the same rows as FUNDING_INITIATED or FUNDING_COMPLETED and buys again. Buy orders have no idempotency key, so Alpaca cannot reject the repeat. TLH orders already use client_order_id (TlhExecutionService.java:452), so the pattern exists in the repo. The jobs also have no job lock, so two instances overlapping during a deploy can both act on the same rows. I did not reproduce this; it is read from the code.
- Suggested fix: Mark both methods NOT_SUPPORTED and process each execution in its own REQUIRES_NEW unit, as the 11:59 batch does. Commit the trade row first, send client_order_id derived from the trade id, and on restart look the order up by that id before placing. Add job locks for both jobs and for the DRIP jobs.
- Verifier (confirmed): Code matches the claim. InvestmentExecutionService.java:36 is class-level @Transactional; checkFundingStatus (:536) and checkTradingStatus (:1021) have no propagation override, unlike processEndOfDayBatchTransfers (:282) and processScheduledInvestments (:410), which are NOT_SUPPORTED. Status flips, trade rows and Alpaca orders (:596-611, :848-1015) all happen inside the one outer transaction. AlpacaService.placeBuyOrder (:295-304) sends no client_order_id, while the TLH order methods do (AlpacaService.java:553-567). InvestmentScheduler.java:70-96 takes no job lock for either job; JobLocks.java has only four names, none for these. The per-execution try/catch in the loops does not help: a RuntimeException crossing a nested @Transactional proxy marks the shared transaction rollback-only, and the commit then fails for every user in the pass. Not reproduced by me either; I found no guard that prevents it. Extra detail: both jobs fire at the same instant at :00 and :30 on a thread pool scheduler (SchedulingConfig.java:15-16), so they can run concurrently in one instance too.

### [blocker] Closing an account hard-deletes the money ledger and leaves other personal data orphaned

- Type: decision, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/UserDeletionService.java:142-165 (deletes trades, executions, drip executions, beneficiaries, schedules, sessions, portfolio, goals, user); V1__baseline.sql:42-61 (chat_action_audit, chat_messages have user_id and no FK), :62-78 conversation_insights, :110-123 email_log (recipient email), :242-283 tax_lot_disposals and tax_lots, :300-374 tlh_harvest_events, tlh_user_states, tlh_wash_windows; grep of repository/ shows no delete-by-user method for any of those tables
- Detail: Two opposite problems in one path. First, account closure permanently removes every investment_executions, investment_trades and drip_executions row. For a registered adviser those are the records of what was done with client money, and the ACH authorization trail goes with them. Whether FRED must keep them (and for how long) is a counsel question, but today the code makes keeping them impossible. The KYC rejection path was already changed to keep the row for this reason (AccountStatusService.java:132-181). Second, the tables with no foreign key are never touched: chat history, chat action audit, conversation insights, email log with the email address, tax lots, disposals and TLH events all survive under a user id that no longer exists. So the app tells the user the account is permanently closed while their chat and email address remain.
- Suggested fix: Get counsel's retention answer, then replace hard delete with a closed state on the user row: scrub or encrypt direct identifiers, keep the ledger rows for the retention period, and explicitly delete chat, insights and email_log rows. Add the missing delete-by-user repository methods and a test that lists every table with a user_id column.
- Verifier (confirmed): UserDeletionService.java:142-165 hard-deletes trades, executions, drip executions, beneficiaries, schedules, sessions, portfolio, goals and the user. In V1 the tables chat_action_audit, chat_messages, conversation_insights, email_log, tax_lots, tax_lot_disposals, tlh_harvest_events, tlh_user_states and tlh_wash_windows have a user_id column and no foreign key, and the repository package has no delete-by-user method for any of them (the only deleteBy/deleteAll methods are for schedules, trades, sessions, beneficiaries, executions, plaid events/removals, drip and goals). passkey_credentials and user_progress are removed through JPA cascade on User.java:135-141, so those are fine. The retention half is correctly typed as a counsel decision; the orphaned chat/email rows half is plain app work. AccountStatusService.java:132-181 confirms the rejection path was already changed to keep the row for recordkeeping reasons.

### [high] Account deletion is unavailable to users without a brokerage account or with a recent transfer

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: controller/TradingController.java:310-321 (returns 404 when alpacaAccountId is null); :326-330 and service/UserDeletionService.java:44, 98-109 (refused while FUNDING_INITIATED or within 60 days of the last settled transfer); grep for DeleteMapping, deletedAt, pendingDeletion in controller/model/service finds no other account deletion path or state
- Detail: The only account deletion endpoint is POST /account/close. A user who signed up but never finished KYC gets a 404 and cannot delete their account. A funded user cannot delete for 60 days after any settled transfer, and the schema has no column to record that they asked, so the request is simply lost. Apple requires in-app account deletion for any app that allows account creation, so this is likely an App Store review issue as well as a data issue. A rejected applicant's row (with encrypted SSN) is also kept with no removal path.
- Suggested fix: Add deletion_requested_at and a closed or pending-deletion status on users via a new migration. Let pre-KYC users delete immediately. For deferred cases, record the request, stop schedules, sign the user out, and finish the deletion from a scheduled job once the window passes.
- Verifier (confirmed): TradingController.java:319-322 returns 404 when alpacaAccountId is null, and :327-331 returns 409 DELETION_DEFERRED. UserDeletionService.java:44 and :98-109 give the 60 day window. The only other caller of userDeletionService.delete is DevUserController.java:51, which is gated to local/dev/test/ci profiles (:30), so prod has no other path. No deletion-request column or state exists. The Apple point is fair: a regulated app may delay completing deletion, but must let the user start it in the app, and today a pre-KYC user cannot and a deferred request is not recorded. High is right; it is not a blocker for a private beta.

### [high] No recovery for a batch row that was claimed but never got a transfer id

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:314-325 (claim commits before the POST), :204-222 (status written after the POST in a separate transaction); repository/InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:150-165 (claimed rows are excluded from every later selection), :180-182; grep for transferAttemptedAt shows no other reader; service/AlpacaService.java:111-119 (transfer POST has no idempotency key); service/UserDeletionService.java:98-109 (deferral check looks only at FUNDING_INITIATED)
- Detail: The claim-before-POST design correctly prevents a double ACH pull. The cost is a stuck state: if the process dies, the POST times out after Alpaca accepted it, or the status write fails, the rows stay SCHEDULED with transfer_attempted_at set and no alpaca_transfer_id. Nothing ever selects them again and nothing alerts. If the transfer did go through, the user's money sits at Alpaca as cash and is never invested, and the app shows the investment as queued forever. Such a user also passes the deletion deferral check.
- Suggested fix: Add a reconciler job: for rows with transfer_attempted_at older than about 15 minutes, status SCHEDULED and no transfer id, list the account's transfers at Alpaca for that night and either adopt the matching transfer or mark the rows FUNDING_FAILED and alert. Include this state in the deletion deferral check.
- Verifier (adjusted): The stuck state is real, but one of the three stated triggers is wrong. AlpacaService.initiateAchTransfer catches every exception and returns a FAILED response (AlpacaService.java:158-160), so a POST timeout does not leave rows SCHEDULED; it marks them FUNDING_FAILED (InvestmentExecutionService.java:227-231). That is a different and arguably worse bug: if Alpaca accepted the transfer before the read timed out, the money is pulled while FRED records a failure with no transfer id, and nothing reconciles it (no idempotency key on the POST, :111-119). The SCHEDULED-with-transferAttemptedAt strand happens only on a process crash between claim and status write, or when the applyToFreshRows write fails. In that case the claim is accurate: the selection query excludes claimed rows (InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:163-164), the only readers of transferAttemptedAt are that query and the claim update (:180-181), there is no reconciler, and deferralReason checks only FUNDING_INITIATED. Keep high; the fix should cover both the stranded and the timeout-marked-failed cases.

### [high] SSN is kept after KYC with an unsalted hash that nothing reads

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/KycSubmissionService.java:89-90 (stores encrypted SSN and SHA-256 hash); service/EncryptionService.java:138-146 (plain SHA-256, no salt or key); repository/UserRepository.java:28 (findBySsnHash defined); grep shows no caller of findBySsnHash and no caller of User.getSsn outside the model; V1__baseline.sql:466-467
- Detail: A plain SHA-256 of a nine digit number can be reversed by trying all one billion values in seconds, so ssn_hash is effectively the SSN in the clear for anyone who reads the table. The column is not used: no code looks a user up by it. The encrypted ssn column is also never read back after it is written, since Alpaca holds the SSN once the account is created. FRED is carrying the most sensitive field it has for no functional reason.
- Suggested fix: Decide with counsel whether FRED needs to hold the SSN at all. If not, stop writing both columns and drop them in a migration. If a lookup is needed, replace the hash with an HMAC keyed by a secret that is not in the database.
- Verifier (confirmed): KycSubmissionService.java:89-90 writes both columns. EncryptionService.java:138-146 is a bare SHA-256 with no salt or key. UserRepository.java:28 defines findBySsnHash and a repo-wide grep finds no caller; there is also no caller of getSsn() or getSsnHash() anywhere in backend/src/main. So both columns are write-only. Whether to keep the SSN is a counsel decision, but dropping or keying the hash is cheap app work and should not wait.

### [high] Encryption key policy is too loose for production and has no rotation path

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/EncryptionService.java:41-63 (key is the raw UTF-8 bytes of a string; 16 or 24 bytes accepted in prod with only a log warning), :30 and :87 (single ENC: prefix, no key id), :148-162 (dev profile returns non-prefixed values as plaintext); application-prod.properties:29
- Detail: The cipher itself is sound (AES-GCM, random 12 byte IV). The gaps are around the key. Prod will start with a 16 character key and only log a warning. The key is typed text rather than random bytes, so its strength depends on how it was generated. Ciphertexts carry no key version, so rotating the key means re-encrypting every row in one step with no way to tell old from new. There is no associated data binding a ciphertext to its row or column, so a value can be copied between users by anyone with write access to the database. On api-dev, a value without the prefix is returned as plaintext, which hides rows that were never encrypted.
- Suggested fix: Fail startup in prod unless the key decodes to 32 random bytes (base64 env var). Add a key id to the prefix (ENC:v1:) and support decrypting with a previous key. Write the key generation and rotation steps down before the prod database is created.
- Verifier (confirmed): EncryptionService.java:41-63 uses the UTF-8 bytes of the configured string, accepts 16 or 24 bytes outside the local profile with only a warning, and I found no startup guard in the config package that checks the encryption key (BrokerEnvironmentGuard covers Plaid/broker settings only as far as grep shows). Single ENC: prefix with no key id (:30, :87); no AAD passed to GCM. The plaintext fallback applies only to dev and local profiles (:148-162) and throws in prod/test, so that sub-point is an api-dev hygiene note, not a prod risk. High is fair mainly because the ciphertext format and key generation must be settled before the prod database holds data; the missing AAD is a lower-grade concern.

### [high] No backup, restore or failed-migration runbook for the future production database

- Type: external, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: grep for backup, mysqldump, restore, PITR across FREDdocs/*.md finds only ad hoc 'take a mysqldump first' notes (flyway-baseline.md:123, handoff-paycheck-autopilot.md:205, analytics-roadmap.md:181); application-prod.properties:1-11 (datasource comes entirely from env, no TLS requirement enforced in the repo); flyway-baseline.md 'A failed migration needs a manual repair'
- Detail: There is no production database yet, so nothing is wrong today. But nothing in the repo describes how the prod database will be backed up, how a restore is tested, or what to do when a migration fails halfway (MySQL has no transactional DDL, and the doc notes this already happened once in development). With real money records and encrypted SSNs, losing the database or the encryption key is unrecoverable. The prod JDBC URL is whatever the env var says, so TLS to the database is not guaranteed by the code.
- Suggested fix: When provisioning prod: enable automated daily backups with point-in-time recovery, do one test restore, store the encryption key separately from the database backups, require sslMode=VERIFY_IDENTITY (or at least REQUIRED) in the URL, and write a one page runbook covering backup, restore and failed-migration recovery.
- Verifier (confirmed): Grep of FREDdocs for backup, mysqldump, restore and PITR hits only flyway-baseline.md, handoff-paycheck-autopilot.md, analytics-roadmap.md, analytics-implementation-review.md and backlog.md, all ad hoc mentions, no runbook. application-prod.properties:7-9 takes the datasource URL, user and password wholly from env vars with nothing enforcing TLS. flyway-baseline.md:63-66 confirms the manual-repair-on-failed-migration caveat and that it already happened once. Nothing is broken today because prod does not exist; this is a must-do at provisioning time, correctly typed external, and high rather than blocker only because it is sequenced with creating the prod database.

### [medium] Queue state is stored as text inside error_message and matched by substring

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:152-153, :362-363, :759-761, :808-826 (amount parsed back out of the message text); controller/InvestmentController.java:320-323; service/ScheduledInvestmentProcessor.java:109
- Detail: Whether a SCHEDULED row belongs to tonight's batch is decided by error_message containing the phrase 'Queued for end-of-day'. Another path restores an original dollar amount by parsing it out of the error message string. Any edit to that copy, or any code that clears or overwrites error_message, silently drops rows from the batch or changes an amount. A money path should not depend on free text.
- Suggested fix: Add a real column (for example queue_state or a QUEUED status value, and original_amount if still needed) in a new migration, backfill from the marker, and switch the three readers to it.

### [medium] No optimistic locking on any entity, including the wide users row

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: grep for @Version across backend/src/main/java returns nothing; grep for DynamicUpdate returns nothing; model/User.java:24-360 (about 90 columns: Plaid token, Alpaca ids, account status, tier, streaks, MFU state); userRepository.save call counts: controller/UserController.java 8, service/UserService.java 5, service/ReferralService.java 5, controller/PinController.java 5, service/AccountStatusService.java 4; repository/UserRepository.java:81-93 (row locks exist for only two paths)
- Detail: Hibernate writes every column on update. Many request handlers and schedulers load a User, change one field and save. When two of them overlap (the 5 minute status poller setting ACTIVE while a profile save is in flight, a Plaid exchange while the ACH retry job runs), the later save writes back the stale values it read and silently undoes the other change. Fields at risk include account_status, alpaca_ach_relationship_id and plaid_access_token. Two paths were already given SELECT FOR UPDATE because this bit them; the rest are unprotected. The same applies to investment_executions, where status transitions are read-then-write with no guard.
- Suggested fix: Add a version column to users, investment_schedules and investment_executions (one migration, default 0) with @Version, and handle the optimistic lock exception with a retry in the schedulers. As a cheaper first step add @DynamicUpdate on User so a save only writes the columns it changed.

### [medium] Most recurring jobs are not safe to run on two instances

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: service/JobLocks.java:6-9 (four lock names only); scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java:70-131, 147-181 (funding check, trading check, DRIP, three payday notification jobs take no lock); scheduler/AccountStatusScheduler.java:39-48; scheduler/TlhScheduler.java:43-75; service/StreakService.java:41; service/DripService.java:100 and :69-80 (@Transactional on processDividendsForUser is bypassed because it is called from the same class), :140-178 (order placed with no client_order_id)
- Detail: Only four jobs take a database lock. Everything else assumes exactly one backend instance. Railway starts the new container before stopping the old one during a deploy, and any future scale-out makes it permanent. Effects range from duplicate push notifications and double streak increments to duplicate DRIP buys. DRIP is partly protected by the unique alpaca_activity_id, but two instances can both pass the exists check, and the loser's insert error is caught per user rather than per dividend. I did not confirm how Railway is configured for the future prod service.
- Suggested fix: Wrap every @Scheduled method in the existing JobLockService with a lease longer than its worst run time. Document single-instance as a hard requirement until that is done.

### [medium] Account status poller holds one transaction across an Alpaca call per pending user

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/AccountStatusService.java:64-113 (@Transactional method loops all pending users and calls alpacaApiService.getAccountStatus inside); scheduler/AccountStatusScheduler.java:39 (every 5 minutes, no job lock); application.properties:10-17 (pool of 10, max lifetime 10 minutes)
- Detail: Every five minutes one transaction stays open while the job calls Alpaca once per user with a non-final status, then runs ACH setup and emails for any that changed. A slow Alpaca response holds a connection for the whole pass, and a failure near the end rolls back status changes for users whose emails were already sent. At private beta volume this is fine. It becomes a pool and consistency problem as sign-ups grow.
- Suggested fix: Fetch the id list in a short read, then handle each user in its own transaction with the HTTP call made before the transaction opens.

### [medium] Missing indexes on scheduler and history lookups

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: V1__baseline.sql:124-144 (investment_executions has only the user_id key; V6 adds unique source_key and night_key), :166-187 (investment_trades has no index on user_id or alpaca_order_id), :42-61 (chat_messages and chat_action_audit have no secondary index), :394-408 (user_sessions has no device_id index), :409-481 (users has no index on account_status, selected_tier, device_id, ssn_hash); queries: repository/InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:34-51, :91, :96, :128-129, :163-165; repository/ChatMessageRepository.java:16-24; repository/UserRepository.java:56-75
- Detail: The status jobs query investment_executions by status every 15 and 30 minutes and by alpaca_transfer_id per row; both are full table scans. Chat history is read by session_id on every message with no index, and chat_messages only grows. findByScheduledDate wraps the column in DATE(), which no index can serve. None of this hurts at beta size. It will show up as slow chat and slow jobs within the first few thousand users, and adding an index to a large table later is slower than adding it now.
- Suggested fix: One migration adding: investment_executions (status, execution_date), (alpaca_transfer_id), (user_id, status); investment_trades (user_id), (alpaca_order_id); chat_messages (session_id, created_at), (user_id, created_at); chat_action_audit (user_id); users (account_status), (selected_tier), (device_id). Rewrite the DATE() queries as ranges.

### [medium] Trade fill precision is narrower than what Alpaca returns

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: V1__baseline.sql:174-177 (filled_avg_price decimal(10,2), filled_quantity decimal(10,6), quantity decimal(10,6)); model/InvestmentTrade.java:25-35; service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:1087-1088 (Alpaca values stored directly); compare V1__baseline.sql:248-249 and :266-270 (tax lots use decimal(18,6) price and decimal(18,9) quantity)
- Detail: Alpaca reports fractional quantities to nine decimal places and average fill prices beyond cents. investment_trades rounds quantity to six places and price to two on insert, with no error. The TLH tables were built with the wider types, so the two ledgers will disagree on the same fill. Any statement, cost basis or reconciliation built from investment_trades will be off by small amounts that add up across many small recurring buys.
- Suggested fix: Migration altering filled_quantity and quantity to decimal(18,9) and filled_avg_price to decimal(18,6), with matching precision on the entity.

### [medium] KYC submission can create an Alpaca account that FRED never records

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: controller/AlpacaController.java:100-135 (broker createAccount, then persistSubmission; a false return only logs a warning and still returns 200; an exception returns 500); service/KycSubmissionService.java:65-110
- Detail: The brokerage account is created at Alpaca first and the account id is saved second, in a separate step. If the save fails (database blip, deploy), the Alpaca account exists but FRED has no id for it. The duplicate guard at line 103 checks the saved id, so the user's retry creates a second application for the same person. There is no record from which to find the orphan.
- Suggested fix: Before calling Alpaca, write a pending row or status (KYC_SUBMITTING with a client-generated reference). After a failure, look the account up at Alpaca by email before creating another. Return an error, not 200, when the save did not happen.

### [medium] api-dev likely needs a manual Flyway history fix before it can boot current develop

- Type: external, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: FREDdocs/flyway-baseline.md sections 'If a migration was renumbered after it had already run somewhere' and 'Before the next deploy to Railway dev'; git: local develop is 82 commits ahead of origin/develop (origin/develop has V1 to V8 only, V9__goals.sql exists only locally, commit c950053)
- Detail: The doc says api-dev very likely recorded the analytics migrations under the old numbers 6 and 7, so the next deploy will fail with a checksum mismatch until two rows are deleted from flyway_schema_history by hand (not flyway repair, which would mark Autopilot's V6 as applied without creating its tables). I could not reach that database, so this is unconfirmed. Separately, the only copy of 82 commits including V9 is this laptop. This is the only rehearsal available for the first production migration run.
- Suggested fix: Push develop. Take a dump of the api-dev database, check flyway_schema_history, apply the two documented deletes if version 6 reads 'investment schedule status', deploy, and confirm history reads 1 to 9 and validate passes. Record the result in flyway-baseline.md.
- Verifier (adjusted): The repo facts check out: FREDdocs/flyway-baseline.md:94-134 describes the renumbering and says api-dev very likely needs the two manual deletes (:119-120) and not flyway repair (:110); origin/develop has V1 to V8 only, V9__goals.sql is local only, and local develop is 82 commits ahead of origin/develop (git rev-list --count). The api-dev database state itself cannot be confirmed from here. I lowered the Flyway part to medium: it affects only the dev environment, and a fresh prod database would apply V1 to V9 cleanly with no history conflict. The second point bundled into this finding, 82 unpushed commits living only on one laptop, is a separate and more serious risk (high, trivial to fix by pushing, which needs Andy's go-ahead) and should be its own checklist item.

### [medium] Autopilot branch is 81 commits behind develop and owns V10

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: git: feature/paycheck-autopilot-phase-1 @ 15b95dd has V10__ops_alerts_and_admin_actions.sql and lacks V9__goals.sql; develop @ 89f7ad3 ends at V9; rev-list shows 81 commits only on develop and 29 only on the branch; V6__paycheck_autopilot.sql creates paycheck_streams, paycheck_events, paycheck_cycles, ach_authorizations with no entity on develop, plus 14 unused columns on investment_schedules, 6 on investment_executions, 7 on users
- Detail: There is no number collision today: V9 is Goals on develop and the branch uses V10. The risk is ordering. If any new migration lands on develop first it will take V10 and the branch must be renumbered before it has run anywhere, which is exactly the mistake the doc records from October. Meanwhile develop ships about 30 columns and four tables that no code on develop reads or writes. They are harmless (all nullable or defaulted) but they are schema for a feature whose merge is undecided and whose payment model is with counsel.
- Suggested fix: Decide the merge order now. Either merge or rebase the Autopilot branch before writing the next migration, or reserve V10 for it in flyway-baseline.md and start new work at V11. Do not enable Flyway out-of-order.

### [low] users.public_id is still nullable in the database

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: V2__user_public_id.sql:17 (VARCHAR(36) NULL); model/User.java:42 (nullable = false); FREDdocs/flyway-baseline.md 'Two follow-ups this leaves open'
- Detail: The doc lists tightening this column as an open follow-up. It was left nullable only to survive a rolling deploy that is long past. A row inserted outside the app would have no public id and its analytics events would be parked.
- Suggested fix: New migration: backfill any NULLs, then ALTER to NOT NULL. Do it before the prod database is first created so prod never has the loose version.

### [low] Mixed clocks: Eastern wall time in most columns, UTC in job locks, server time in views

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: BackendApplication.java:17-18 (JVM default pinned to America/New_York); application-dev.properties:8 (serverTimezone=UTC); model/InvestmentTrade.java:77-89 and service/KycSubmissionService.java:107 (LocalDateTime.now() with the default zone); service/JobLockService.java:48 (UTC); R__v_contribution_retention.sql:65-68 (compares stored values to MySQL NOW()); V1__baseline.sql:429-434 (last_logged_in_month, last_mfu_period_start, last_mfu_period_end, last_streak_update stored as varchar(255))
- Detail: Timestamps are stored as DATETIME with no zone, holding Eastern wall-clock time. That works while everything is written by this one app, but the one hour repeated at the autumn clock change is ambiguous, and anything comparing to the database clock (the retention view uses NOW(), which is UTC on a typical managed MySQL) is off by four or five hours. Several dates are stored as free strings. None of this moves money incorrectly today; it will confuse support queries and the metrics views.
- Suggested fix: Pick one rule and write it in CONTEXT.md. Cheapest: keep Eastern, route every now() through EasternTime, and replace NOW() in the views with CONVERT_TZ(UTC_TIMESTAMP(), 'UTC', 'America/New_York'). Longer term store UTC instants.

### [low] Planning amounts on users and user_progress are stored as double

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: V1__baseline.sql:385-386 (user_progress.monthly_investment, retirement_income double), :437, :463, :470 (users.monthly_investment, retirement_income, target_portfolio double); model/User.java:231-233; model/UserProgress.java:58-62
- Detail: Every ledger amount (transfers, trades, schedules, goals, tax lots) is DECIMAL, which is correct. The plan inputs are doubles. They feed the Freedom estimate and, per the goal-first contribution rule, can drive what the user is shown as their monthly amount. They are not sent to Alpaca directly, so this is a display and parity risk rather than a money risk.
- Suggested fix: Migrate the five columns to DECIMAL(12,2) and the fields to BigDecimal when next touching that code.

### [low] Unbounded reads and tables with no retention

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: service/DripService.java:70, service/StreakService.java:47, service/ReferralService.java:31 (userRepository.findAll()); service/InvestmentScheduleService.java:291-296 (all schedules of ACTIVE users loaded then filtered in memory); repository/InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:91 (findByStatus unpaged); V5__analytics_outbox.sql header ('Rows are NEVER deleted'); V6__paycheck_autopilot.sql plaid_webhook_events.payload_json (no purge other than at user deletion)
- Detail: Several jobs load every user row, including the encrypted SSN and Plaid token columns, to filter a few in Java. plaid_webhook_events keeps full payloads indefinitely and chat_messages has no cap. Fine for a private beta; worth a ticket before public launch.
- Suggested fix: Replace findAll with targeted queries (for example drip_enabled and ACTIVE), page the status jobs, and add a retention job for processed webhook payloads.

**Verified solid:**

- ddl-auto is validate in every profile (application-ci.properties:20, application-dev.properties:12, application-local.properties:4, application-prod.properties:10, application-test.properties:18) and Flyway is enabled with baseline-on-migrate at version 1 (application.properties:118-121).
- No entity versus migration drift found by a static comparison of all 29 entities against V1 to V9: every mapped column exists in the migrations. The only database columns without an entity field are Autopilot Phase 2 columns from V6, and all of them are nullable or have defaults, so inserts are not broken. I did not boot the app; CI does boot it against MySQL 8 with validate (.github/workflows/verify.yml:24-71).
- No V-number collision: develop has V1 to V9, the unmerged feature/paycheck-autopilot-phase-1 branch adds V10, and the goals branches carry the same V9 as develop.
- Recurring investment idempotency is solid: unique source_key and night_key on investment_executions (V6, uk_investment_executions_source_key and uk_investment_executions_night_key), pre-checked in ScheduledInvestmentProcessor.java:70-79, each schedule in its own REQUIRES_NEW transaction (:44), and a missed night catches up once because readiness uses next date on or before today (InvestmentSchedule.java:356-365).
- The 11:59 PM ACH batch keeps the Alpaca POST outside any database transaction, commits a compare-and-set claim first (InvestmentExecutionRepository.java:179-182), writes each user's result in its own transaction, and runs under a database job lock (InvestmentExecutionService.java:281-295).
- Lump-sum double-tap protection works end to end: the frontend sends clientUuid (frontend/src/app/services/investment.service.ts, lump-sum-investment.page.ts) and the backend short-circuits on the unique source_key (InvestmentController.java:275-291).
- One schedule and one portfolio per user are enforced by unique keys on user_id (V1__baseline.sql:163, :239); user_progress likewise (:391).
- DRIP dedupe has a unique key on alpaca_activity_id (V1__baseline.sql:106); TLH has unique claim and client order id keys (:342-343) and sends client_order_id on every order (TlhExecutionService.java:452, :646); tax lots are unique on source_activity_id (:280); Plaid webhooks are unique on dedupe_key (V6); analytics outbox is unique on event_id (V5); goals are unique on (user_id, client_id) (V9).
- The user's own sensitive identifiers are encrypted with AES-GCM and a random IV at every write site I found: SSN, Alpaca account id and number (KycSubmissionService.java:81-89), ACH relationship id (AchRelationshipService.java:100, AccountStatusService.java:266), Plaid access token (PlaidService.java:214-217), recovery OTP (RecoveryController.java:47), and the account id snapshots on executions and TLH events (InvestmentExecutionService.java:211, 465, 505; TlhHarvestService.java:325). The PIN is hashed with the password encoder (PinController.java:41).
- All ledger money columns are DECIMAL: investment_executions.amount, investment_schedules amounts, trade notional, drip net_amount, goals balances, tax lots and TLH amounts (V1, V6, V9).
- KYC rejection no longer deletes the user; the row is kept and marked REJECTED with the schedule held (AccountStatusService.java:132-181).
- A second create-account for a user who already has a saved Alpaca account id is refused (AlpacaController.java:100-112).
- User deletion queues the PostHog person deletion and the Plaid item removal in the same transaction as the row deletes, and refuses while a transfer is FUNDING_INITIATED (UserDeletionService.java:98-165).
- Chat history reads through the HTTP endpoint are scoped to the authenticated user (ChatController.java:89-100).
- Scheduler crons for money jobs are pinned to America/New_York and compute one todayET per run (InvestmentScheduler.java:45-63, EasternTime.java).

## critic (gaps the others missed)

Completeness pass over what the sixteen auditors did not report. I checked each suggested candidate in code and kept only what I could confirm and that is not already in the list. Eleven new findings. The most important are operational: the app has no minimum-version or maintenance gate, no way to freeze one user or stop user-initiated money movement without a redeploy, and system holds that only a database edit can clear. Product gaps that matter for a brokerage app: there is no in-app transaction history at all (the two frontend methods that would feed one are never called), an ACATS transfer form is live but half built, and one-time purchases of any single stock ship in an app positioned as an automated portfolio. Money-path gaps: no bank balance check before an ACH pull, no bank re-link flow in the app, and Plaid webhooks are stored but never processed. Reliability: two of the three Alpaca HTTP clients have no timeouts. Smaller items: referral crediting bugs, no duplicate-person check, accessibility, and a streak that counts months where nothing was invested. Candidates I checked and found either fine or already covered: idle/session handling, goals tier limit, portfolio customise validation, age checks, market-closed logic, cost basis display, dividends/DRIP, paycheck autopilot branch state, support tooling.


### [high] No minimum app version check, forced update, or maintenance mode

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: Repo-wide grep for minVersion, forceUpdate, maintenance, appVersion, X-App-Version, killSwitch across frontend/src, backend/src/main and ios/App/App returned only unrelated TLH comments and backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/model/HoldReason.java:9. No feature flag or remote config code either (grep for featureFlag, isFeatureEnabled, remoteConfig returned nothing). ios/App/App.xcodeproj/project.pbxproj:351-355 (version 1.0 build 1, never sent to the server).
- Detail: Once the app ships as a bundled build, old copies stay on phones. The client sends no version header and the backend has no check, so there is no way to make an old build stop and ask the user to update when a money-path bug or a breaking API change is found. There is also no maintenance switch to pause the app during a migration or an Alpaca outage, and no remote flags to turn a single feature off. For an app that moves money this is the standard safety net.
- Suggested fix: Send the app version and build on every request (one HTTP interceptor). Add a small public endpoint or response header that returns minimum supported build and a maintenance flag, driven by config that can change without a release. Show a blocking 'Update required' or 'Back soon' screen in the app shell. Consider a simple server-driven flag map for risky features (TLH, ACATS, single-stock buys).

### [high] No way to freeze one user or stop user-initiated money movement, and system holds cannot be cleared

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/security/services/UserDetailsImpl.java:86-104 (isAccountNonLocked and isEnabled hard-coded true with 'You can add logic' comments); model/HoldReason.java:9 (KILL_SWITCH and DORMANT defined) with grep showing setHoldReason is only ever called with BANK_CHANGE, MANUAL_REVIEW or null (service/InvestmentScheduleService.java:254, service/AccountStatusService.java:204, service/AchRelationshipService.java:111 and :172); AchRelationshipService.java:109-111 (only a BANK_CHANGE hold is ever cleared); config/SchedulerGate.java:19 (boot-time property); scheduler/TlhScheduler.java, service/StreakService.java, scheduler/ReferralValidationScheduler.java have zero schedulerGate checks; controller/TradingController.java:108-262 and controller/InvestmentController.java:194-372 (sell, liquidate, withdraw, one-time invest have no gate).
- Detail: If an account is compromised or a user is suspected of fraud, there is no flag to lock that user out: the User model has no disabled or locked field and the Spring user details always report enabled. The KILL_SWITCH and DORMANT hold reasons exist in the enum but nothing sets them. The only global stop is investment.scheduler.enabled, which is read at boot (needs a restart) and only covers the scheduled investment, ACH and Plaid jobs. It does not cover TLH jobs or any user-initiated endpoint (one-time invest, sell, liquidate, withdraw). Separately, a MANUAL_REVIEW hold (set on KYC rejection or an ACH relationship problem) has no code path that clears it, so the only way to release a held user is a manual database edit. This overlaps with the known 'no admin tooling' item but these specific controls are not listed there.
- Suggested fix: Add a per-user 'frozen' flag checked in the JWT filter (read-only or full block) and in every money endpoint. Add a runtime kill switch (database row, not a boot property) that all schedulers and money endpoints check. Add a guarded admin action to set and clear holds, with an audit row.

### [high] No in-app transaction or activity history

- Type: app-work, effort L, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/services/investment.service.ts:125 and :134 (getInvestmentHistory, getExecutionDetails) have no callers anywhere in frontend/src/app (grep shows only the definitions). backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/InvestmentController.java:51-83 (history is last 6 months of executions only). Grep of all templates for 'activity', 'transactions', 'pending' finds only frontend/src/app/pages/tax-documents/tax-documents.page.html:102 (monthly statement card copy) and the lump-sum page title.
- Detail: A user cannot see a list of what happened to their money: bank pulls, buys, sells, withdrawals, dividends, reinvestments, or anything pending. The backend has an executions history endpoint (6 months, deposits and buys only) but no screen uses it, and withdrawals and manual sells are not recorded locally at all (already reported). The existing finding 'no screen shows execution status' covers failures; this is the wider gap that there is no ledger view of any kind. Users of a brokerage app expect this, support cannot answer 'where is my money' without it, and with trade confirmations also hidden the monthly PDF statement is the only record a user can see.
- Suggested fix: Build one Activity screen fed by Alpaca account activities (transfers, fills, dividends) merged with FRED executions, with pending items at the top. Link it from the portfolio tab and from the One-Time Transactions and Sell and Withdraw pages.

### [high] ACATS transfer form is live in the app but half built

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/lump-sum-investment/lump-sum-investment.page.html:170-223 (Transfer Existing Assets card, visible to all users); lump-sum-investment.page.ts:312-349 (single tap submit, logs the account number to console at :327, shows only a toast with a reference id); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/AlpacaController.java:266-296 (no validation, no local record, raw exception text returned at :294); service/AlpacaService.java:1440-1474 (returns a made-up id 'acat_initiated_<uuid>' at :1463 when Alpaca returns no id).
- Detail: Any user can start a full incoming account transfer from another brokerage with two fields and one tap. There is no confirmation step, no transfer authorisation or disclosure text, no check that account titles match, no partial transfer option, no saved record in FRED's database, and no way to see status or a rejection afterwards. If Alpaca's response has no id the server invents one and the user is shown it as a reference. Transferred positions will be whatever the user held elsewhere, which the portfolio model, the allocation screen and scheduled buys do not account for. The backlog lists ACATS (FRED-175) as blocked, yet this form is reachable today. I could not confirm whether the Alpaca production agreement enables ACATS for FRED.
- Suggested fix: Hide the card for beta unless Alpaca and counsel confirm ACATS is in scope. If it stays: add a review and authorisation step, store the transfer with status, poll or subscribe for status, show it in the app, remove the fake id fallback, and decide how non-portfolio positions are displayed and handled.

### [medium] One-time purchase of any single stock is live with no asset checks

- Type: decision, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: frontend/src/app/lump-sum-investment/lump-sum-investment.page.html:22, :32-37, :58-110, :159 (Specific stock tab, search of all stocks and ETFs); backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/InvestmentController.java:245-264 (type 'stock' accepts any non-empty symbol string); controller/AlpacaController.java:209-218 (asset search passes through all active us_equity assets); service/InvestmentExecutionService.java:870-893 (symbol is only used at trade time, after the bank pull) and :1223-1245 (non-fractionable assets are bought in whole shares using a quoted price).
- Detail: The One-Time Transactions page lets a user buy any single US-listed stock or ETF, funded by a bank pull. This turns FRED into a self-directed trading app as well as an automated portfolio, which affects how the product is described to Alpaca, counsel and App Review. The server does not check that the symbol exists, is tradable, or is suitable (leveraged and inverse ETFs are included) before pulling money; a bad symbol only fails days later at trade time. For assets that cannot be bought fractionally, an amount smaller than one share buys nothing and leaves the cash sitting in the account. These holdings sit outside the target allocation and nothing manages them afterwards.
- Suggested fix: Decide with counsel whether single-stock buying belongs in the beta. If not, hide the tab and reject type 'stock' on the server. If yes, validate the symbol against Alpaca (active, tradable, fractionable or price below amount) before creating the execution, and filter the search list.

### [medium] No bank balance check before an ACH pull

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PlaidService.java:138-139 (Link requests only AUTH and IDENTITY, plus Transactions consent); grep for balance/get, accountsBalanceGet, insufficient, NSF across backend/src/main/java returns no Plaid balance call; PlaidService public surface (lines 117-338) is link token, token exchange, accounts get and institution lookup only.
- Detail: Every scheduled and one-time pull is sent to the bank blind. FRED never asks Plaid for the available balance first, so a user with too little money gets an ACH return (bank fee for the user, return cost and return-rate exposure for FRED with Alpaca). Combined with the already reported issues that returns do not stop the schedule and the user is not told, an under-funded user can be hit repeatedly. Whether a balance check is required is partly an Alpaca and Plaid contract question, which I could not confirm from the repo.
- Suggested fix: Add the Plaid Balance product (or Signal) and check available balance shortly before the nightly batch creates a transfer. On a shortfall, skip that pull, tell the user, and do not count it as a failure of the schedule. Confirm with Alpaca what return-rate limits apply.

### [medium] No bank re-link flow in the app, Plaid webhooks are stored but never processed, and Transactions consent is already being collected

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PlaidWebhookInboxService.java:26-32 ('nothing is processed here (the processor is Phase 1)') and grep showing PlaidWebhookEvent is only referenced by the inbox service, its repository and UserDeletionService; service/PlaidService.java:76-90 and :129-136 (UPDATE mode exists on the server); frontend/src/app/change-bank-account/change-bank-account.page.ts:234 is the only caller that sets a mode and it sends 'link' (grep for update mode, relink, reconnect, ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED in frontend/src/app returns nothing); PlaidService.java:138-139 (additionalConsentedProducts TRANSACTIONS on every new link); service/PlaidItemLifecycleService.java:106-143 (consent flag stored).
- Detail: When a bank login expires or the user revokes access, Plaid sends a webhook. FRED verifies and stores it, but no code reads the stored rows, so nothing reacts. The server can issue an update-mode Link token, but the app never asks for one, so there is no 'reconnect your bank' flow: the only fix a user has is the full Change Bank Account path. The known notification finding covers the missing notice; the missing processor and missing re-link flow are the app work behind it. Separately, every user linking a bank on develop today is asked to consent to Plaid Transactions data for Paycheck Autopilot, a feature that is not merged. That consent needs to match what the privacy policy and Plaid application say at launch.
- Suggested fix: Build the webhook processor for at least ITEM_LOGIN_REQUIRED, PENDING_EXPIRATION, PENDING_DISCONNECT and USER_PERMISSION_REVOKED: put the schedule on hold, notify the user, and surface a reconnect button that opens Link in update mode. Decide whether to keep requesting Transactions consent before Autopilot ships, and make the privacy policy match.

### [medium] Two of the three Alpaca HTTP clients have no timeouts, and there is no rate-limit handling

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/PortfolioDashboardService.java:96-99 (plain new RestTemplate(), used for 10 outbound calls); service/AlpacaApiService.java:46 (HttpComponentsClientHttpRequestFactory with no timeout set, used for 14 calls); service/MarketBreakdownService.java:44 (plain RestTemplate). Compare service/AlpacaService.java:65-69 where 5s connect and 30s read timeouts were added. Grep for 429, Retry, backoff in the three Alpaca services returns no handling. backend/src/main/resources/application.properties:17 (database pool of 10).
- Detail: The main Alpaca client has timeouts, but the client behind the portfolio dashboard, positions, history and performance screens, and the one behind account creation, KYC and documents, do not. If Alpaca is slow or hangs, those request threads wait forever. With a database pool of 10 and connections held during the request, a short Alpaca brownout can take the whole API down, including login. There is also no handling of Alpaca rate-limit (429) responses anywhere, so a burst (many users opening the dashboard, or the nightly batch) fails as generic errors with no retry.
- Suggested fix: Build one shared RestTemplate bean with connect and read timeouts and use it in all three services. Add a small retry with backoff for 429 and 5xx on read calls only. Serve the last cached dashboard with a 'prices may be delayed' note when Alpaca is unreachable.

### [medium] Referral crediting has bugs: beta referrals can be counted twice, the wrong user is flagged, and the code reveals the user's email name

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/UserService.java:139-147 and :154-160 (sets referrer.setReferralCounted(true) instead of marking the referred user); service/ReferralService.java:31-37 (daily job picks every referred user whose own referralCounted is false and whose subscriptionStartedAt is 30+ days old) and :46-55 (credits the referrer again); UserService.java:73-99 (code is the email local part plus two digits); grep for setPrivateBeta returns nothing (model/User.java:293 is the only definition); controller/UserController.java:301-309 (validate endpoint tells any signed-in user whether a code exists); frontend/src/app/pages/my-profile/my-profile.page.html:224 (merch reward copy).
- Detail: When a private beta user applies a code, the referrer is credited at once, but the 'counted' flag is written on the referrer's row, not on the referred user's. Thirty days after that referred user picks a tier, the daily job sees them as not yet counted and credits the referrer a second time. Writing the flag on the referrer also means the referrer's own referral (if they used someone's code) will never be credited. Referral codes are built from the first part of the user's email, so sharing a code shares part of the email address. The privateBeta flag that drives founder rewards and the subscription bypass has no setter in code, so beta users can only be marked by hand in the database. Reward delivery being a stub is already reported; these are separate defects.
- Suggested fix: Set referralCounted on the referred user in both immediate paths. Add a unique guard so one referred user can credit only once. Generate random codes not derived from email. Add a supported way to mark beta users (invite list or admin action).

### [medium] No duplicate-person check: one identity can open several FRED users

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/repository/UserRepository.java:28 (findBySsnHash defined, no callers in backend/src/main/java); service/KycSubmissionService.java:90 (hash is written); controller/AlpacaController.java:99-111 (the only duplicate guard is per FRED user, not per person).
- Detail: FRED stores a hash of the SSN 'for lookup' but never looks it up. The same person can sign up with several emails and submit KYC each time. Whether Alpaca rejects the second application in production is outside this repo and I could not confirm it. Inside FRED, nothing stops it, which also opens the referral program to self-referral through second accounts and makes account recovery ambiguous. The existing finding on the SSN hash covers how it is stored; this is the missing use of it.
- Suggested fix: Before calling Alpaca, look up the SSN hash (with a keyed hash once that fix lands) and refuse or route to recovery if another user already holds it. Confirm with Alpaca how duplicates are handled on their side.

### [medium] Accessibility: zoom is disabled, text does not scale, and icons are read aloud as their names

- Type: app-work, effort M, auditor confidence medium
- Evidence: frontend/src/index.html:11 (maximum-scale=1.0, user-scalable=no); grep counts in frontend/src: 781 font-size declarations in px against 17 in rem, and no dynamic type opt-in; 295 material-symbols-outlined icon spans in templates, 278 of them without aria-hidden; 26 of 60 templates have no aria attribute at all, including tab1.page.html, tab2.page.html, pin-prompt.component.html, recovery.page.html, change-bank-account.page.html, linkplaid.component.html, surveyinitial.component.html; 16 clickable div elements with no role or tabindex (for example faq/faq.page.html:29, beneficiaries/beneficiaries.page.html:118, components/retirement-planning/retirement-planning.component.html:38 and :125).
- Detail: The app ignores the iPhone text size setting and blocks pinch zoom, so users who need larger text cannot get it. The icon font shows icons as text ligatures, so VoiceOver will read names like 'expand_more' or 'arrow_back_ios_new' on almost every screen. Several tappable cards are plain divs that VoiceOver does not announce as buttons. Money screens (PIN prompt, bank link, recovery) have no accessibility labels. This is not a hard App Review rule, but financial apps are a common target for accessibility complaints and it is cheap to fix the systemic parts. I did not run VoiceOver on a device, so the exact spoken output is inferred from the markup.
- Suggested fix: Remove the zoom lock. Add aria-hidden to the icon span globally (one shared class or directive). Turn clickable divs into buttons. Label the PIN pad, bank link and recovery controls. Then do one VoiceOver pass through onboarding, invest, sell and withdraw. Dynamic type can follow as a later pass.

### [low] Investing streak counts months where nothing was invested

- Type: app-work, effort S, auditor confidence high
- Evidence: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/service/StreakService.java:41-66 (adds one to the streak for every user with an ACTIVE schedule on the 1st of the month); repository/InvestmentScheduleRepository.java:43-44 (the check looks at status only, not holds or executions); frontend/src/app/recurring-investments/recurring-investments.page.html:52-56 (shown as 'N-month streak').
- Detail: The streak goes up when a schedule merely exists and is not paused. It does not look at whether any investment actually completed that month, and it ignores system holds, so a user whose bank pulls have failed for months still sees a growing streak. StreakService also loads every user with findAll and has no job lock. Low impact, but it is a number shown to users that can be wrong.
- Suggested fix: Count a month only when at least one execution completed in it, and skip schedules with a blocking hold.

**Verified solid:**

- Idle and session handling exists: 30 minute physical-idle cap on token refresh in frontend/src/app/services/app-lock.service.ts:31-37 and :125-138
- One-time investments are idempotent per tap through clientUuid and a source key: backend/src/main/java/com/investingapp/backend/controller/InvestmentController.java:269-291
- A user cannot resume a schedule out of a system hold; resume only flips a user pause: backend service/InvestmentScheduleService.java:376-404
- Self-referral and applying a second code are blocked server side: backend service/UserService.java:125-132
- Goal count limit is enforced on the server for create and update: backend service/GoalService.java:70, :91, :134-138 (the import bypass is already reported)
- Simulated passkey registration is limited to local, dev, test and ci profiles and needs the dev tools key: backend service/SimulatedAttestationService.java:29-30 and :57-62
- Main Alpaca client has connect and read timeouts: backend service/AlpacaService.java:65-69
- Investment, ACH, DRIP and Plaid lifecycle jobs all check the scheduler gate first (8 checks in scheduler/InvestmentScheduler.java, 2 in AccountStatusScheduler.java)
- Plaid webhooks are signature verified, deduplicated and stored (processing is the gap): backend service/PlaidWebhookInboxService.java:54-93 and config/SecurityConfig.java:114
- Plaid update-mode link tokens are supported on the server: backend service/PlaidService.java:76-136
- Per-user duplicate brokerage application guard returns 409: backend controller/AlpacaController.java:99-111
- Cost basis and unrealized gain or loss are shown per position: frontend components/position-detail-sheet/position-detail-sheet.component.html:27-44 and sell-withdraw/sell-withdraw.page.html:172
- Reduced-motion preference is respected in global styles (frontend/src/global.scss:603) and the floating tab bar has tab roles and labels (components/floating-tab-bar html lines 5-16)
- Support contact exists as a mailto link on the menu and the recovery screen: frontend tab3/tab3.page.ts:368 and pages/recovery/recovery.page.html:40
- Beneficiary changes are sent to Alpaca, including after a delete: backend service/BeneficiaryService.java:174-190 and :204-262
- Only the anonymous routes expected are public; everything else needs a JWT: backend config/SecurityConfig.java:112-119
